## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{Setup, standing assumptions, and the two timescales}
\label{sec:theory-setup}

\paragraph{Model and expected gradient flow.}
Fix $t>0$, $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$. The RFNN score is $s_A(x)=A\phi(x)$, $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$, $W_{ij}\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$ frozen, and $A\in\R^{\dlat\times\pwidth}$ is trained from $A(0)=0$ by full-batch gradient descent on $\|\sqrt{\Delta_t}s_A(x_t^\mu)+\eta^\mu\|^2$ with a \emph{fresh} noise draw at every step. Its mean trajectory is exactly the Euler scheme of the gradient flow of $L_{\nsamp}(A)=\tfrac1{2\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta\|A\phi(x_t^\mu)-y^\mu\|^2$, $y^\mu=-\eta^\mu/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:sgdmean-p1}). With $\Umat=\tfrac1{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[\phi(x^\mu_t)\phi(x^\mu_t)^{\top}]=\sum_{i\le\pwidth}\lambda_iv_iv_i^{\top}$ and $V=\tfrac1{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[y^\mu\phi(x^\mu_t)^{\top}]$, the flow $\dot A=-(A\Umat-V)$ is diagonal in the eigenbasis of $\Umat$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gf-p1}): $a_i(T):=A(T)v_i=a_i^\star(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})$ with $a_i^\star=Vv_i/\lambda_i$. The single-draw operator has rank $\le\nsamp$ (the cliff at index $\nsamp$ in Fig.~\ref{fig:fourbulk}); the $\E_\eta$ operator that governs training is full rank, its $\pwidth-\nsamp$ tail modes sitting contiguously below the sample block (Assumption~\ref{ass:standing-p1}(A2)). The repository's rule $\eta_{\rm lr}=0.01\dlat/\Delta_t$ gives $T=0.02\,\dlat\times(\text{steps})$: \emph{ratios} of timescales are clock-free, step counts carry $\dlat^{-1}$, and the ``spectral times'' of the audit are flow-time reciprocals, not steps (Remark~\ref{rem:clock-p1}).

\paragraph{Standing assumptions.}
(A1) fixed $t$; (A2) $\pwidth\ge\nsamp$ (violated at $\dlat=5$ in the sweep, where the tail block is empty); (A3) the clustered data model of Sec.~\ref{sec:design} with $(k,s,\dint,\sigsig,\signoise,\nsamp)$ fixed and $\dlat\to\infty$ at fixed $\psi_p=\pwidth/\dlat$, so $\psi_n=\nsamp/\dlat$ is \emph{not} fixed, must exceed $1$, and every population/sample separation degrades as $\psi_n\downarrow1$; the signal block is a finite-rank spike set, not a bulk; (A4) the operating point is $q(\dlat)=\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat=\beta_t^2+\dint(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)/\dlat$, decreasing to $\beta_t^2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:q-p1}: $q=2.76\to0.33$, $a_1(q)^2=0.18\to0.63$, $a_*(q)^2=0.079\to0.0047$ over $\dlat=5\to200$), and $a_1,a_*$ are always evaluated at $q(\dlat)$; (A5) the sorted spectrum splits by index into $\mathcal S=[1,\dint]$, $\mathcal N=(\dint,\dlat]$, $\mathcal M=(\dlat,\nsamp]$, $\mathcal Z=(\nsamp,\pwidth]$ with $\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}>\lambda^{\mathcal N}_{\max}$, $\lambda^{\mathcal N}_{\min}>\lambda^{\mathcal M}_{\max}$ and no gap assumed between $\mathcal M$ and $\mathcal Z$; (A6$'$) a \emph{mass-weighted} spectral-alignment hypothesis used only for the gap (Assumption~\ref{ass:standing-p1}).

\paragraph{Residual spectral error.}
Let $\pi_i=\|A^\star v_i\|^2$ and, for a block $\mathcal B$, $E_{\mathcal B}(T)=\sum_{i\in\mathcal B}\pi_ie^{-2\lambda_iT}$, $\Pi_{\mathcal B}=E_{\mathcal B}(0)$. Exactly, $\|A(T)-A^\star\|_F^2=E_{\{1..\pwidth\}}(T)$ and $L_{\nsamp}(A(T))-L_{\nsamp}(A^\star)=\tfrac12\sum_{i\le\pwidth}\lambda_i\pi_ie^{-2\lambda_iT}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:rse-p1}); since $\Pi_{\mathcal Z}\gg\Pi_{\mathcal M}\gg\Pi_{\mathcal N}\gg\Pi_{\mathcal S}$, only block-restricted or loss-weighted functionals are observable.

\begin{definition}[Generalisation time]\label{def:taugen-p1}
$\taugen(\varepsilon)=\inf\{T:E_{\mathcal S}(T)\le\varepsilon\Pi_{\mathcal S}\}$; $\tau_{\rm pop}(\varepsilon)$ is the same with $\mathcal S\cup\mathcal N$.
\end{definition}
\begin{proposition}\label{prop:taugen-p1}
With $\theta_{\mathcal S}=\log(\Pi_{\mathcal S}/\pi_{i_\circ})$, $i_\circ$ the slowest signal mode: $\frac{\log(1/\varepsilon)-\theta_{\mathcal S}}{2\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}}\le\taugen(\varepsilon)\le\frac{\log(1/\varepsilon)}{2\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}}$, and $\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}=a_1(q(\dlat))^2(e^{-2t}\ell_{\min}+\Delta_t)/\dlat\,(1+o(1))$ with $\ell_{\min}$ the smallest signal-subspace data eigenvalue (Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-fourbulk}). Hence $\taugen$ \emph{grows} with $\dlat$ in flow time ($10.9\times$ over $\dlat=5\to200$ on the measured spectra) and \emph{falls} in repository steps ($158\to43$); it is not $\dlat$-independent in either clock.
\end{proposition}

\begin{definition}[Memorisation times]\label{def:taumem-p1}
Let $G(T)$ be the test--train gap relative to initialisation and $\gamma_i=\langle a_i^\star,\bar Vv_i\rangle/(\lambda_i\pi_i)$ the population alignment of mode $i$. Exactly (Proposition~\ref{prop:gap-p1}) $G$ is a quadratic form in $(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})_{i\le\pwidth}$, and under (A6$'$) $G=\sum_{i\in\mathcal M\cup\mathcal Z}g_i+o(G_{\rm thr})$ with $g_i=\tfrac12\pi_i[\bar u_i(1-e_i)^2-2\gamma_i\lambda_i(1-e_i)+\lambda_i(1-e_i^2)]$, which vanishes for population-aligned modes ($\bar u_i=\lambda_i$, $\gamma_i=1$). Split $\mathcal M=\mathcal M_{\rm spk}\cup\mathcal M_{\rm blk}$ by $\gamma_i>\gamma_0$: the spikes are cluster residuals whose absorption \emph{lowers} the test loss (the logged test loss still falls at $35$k--$90$k steps for $\dlat\ge40$, $20$--$60\times$ after the last population e-fold). Define $\taumem^{\rm onset}=1/\lambda^{\rm blk}_{\max}$ and $\taumem^{\rm gap}(\delta)=\inf\{T:G_{\rm blk}(T)\ge\delta G^{\rm blk}_\infty\}$; then $\frac{\log\frac1{1-\delta}}{2\lambda^{\rm blk}_{\max}}\le\taumem^{\rm gap}(\delta)\le\frac{\log\frac1{1-\sqrt\delta}}{\lambda^{\rm blk}_{\min}}$. The index-based $1/\lambda^{\mathcal M}_{\max}$ of the audit is a lower bound on the onset. The paper's primary statistic is the memorised \emph{fraction} $F(T)$ \citep{somepalli2023diffusion,bonnaire2025}; a single sample edge makes any spectral surrogate a step function, so $F$ requires the within-bulk decomposition $\pi_i=\sum_\mu\pi_{i,\mu}$ of Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-fraction}.
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Measured proxies.}
``gen-gap $>0.02$'' is the crossing $G(T)=0.01\dlat/\Delta_t$, an absolute threshold whose relative level varies with $\dlat$ through $G_\infty$ and whose single-draw noise floor $\sqrt{2/(\nsamp\dlat)}$ exceeds $0.02$ at $\dlat\le20$; ``test loss within $5\%$ of its minimum'' fires at the first evaluation for every $\dlat$ because population modes e-fold in $10^2$--$10^3$ steps, while its $1\%$ crossing ($15$k$\to50$k steps) and argmin track the slow spikes $\mathcal M_{\rm spk}$, not $\taugen$; the signal-block absorption deficit $D_{\mathcal S}(T)$, which obeys Proposition~\ref{prop:taugen-p1} at rate $\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}$, is the observable that tests Definition~\ref{def:taugen-p1} (Remark~\ref{rem:proxy-p1}).
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Theory appendix, part 1: setup and spectral timescales}
\label{app:theory-setup}

\subsection{Setting}
Data: $x^\mu=Q(m_{c(\mu)}+\xi^\mu)$, $\mu=1,\dots,\nsamp$, with $k$ centres $m_c$ of norm $s$ in $\operatorname{span}(e_1,\dots,e_{\dint})$, $\xi^\mu\sim\mathcal N(0,\diag(\sigsig^2I_{\dint},\signoise^2I_{\dlat-\dint}))$, $Q\in O(\dlat)$ Haar. Since $W$ is Gaussian and the loss is rotation-equivariant we set $Q=I$. Forward process $x_t=e^{-t}x+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$; $M_t=\tfrac1{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}]=e^{-2t}\hat\Sigma+\Delta_tI$; population block eigenvalues $\alpha_t^2=e^{-2t}(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)+\Delta_t$, $\beta_t^2=e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t$. Features $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$; the code stores $W/\sqrt{\dlat}$ as $\mathcal N(0,1/\dlat)$ entries and omits $\pwidth^{-1/2}$ in its logged $\Umat$, so code eigenvalues are $\pwidth$ times ours. Two feature operators are distinguished throughout: the single-draw $\Umat^{(1)}_k$ (one noise draw per point; rank $\le\min(\pwidth,\nsamp)$; the operator displayed in the four-bulk figure when one draw is used) and its expectation $\Umat=\E_\eta\Umat^{(1)}_k$ (full rank; the operator governing the mean training dynamics). The released eigenvalue files average $50$ draws and are of the second kind: they are full rank and show only a soft step at index $\nsamp$ ($\lambda_{\nsamp}/\lambda_{\nsamp+1}=1.00,1.7,1.7$ at $\dlat=20,60,200$). Empirical objective $L_{\nsamp}$, population objective $L(A)=\tfrac12\E\|A\phi(x_t)-y\|^2$, $\bar\Umat=\E[\phi\phi^{\top}]$, $\bar V=\E[y\phi^{\top}]=\E[s^\star(x_t)\phi(x_t)^{\top}]$ (tower property), $s^\star=\nabla\log p_t$. Gaussian-equivalence coefficients $a_1(q)=\E[\tanh'(\sqrt qz)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\tanh(\sqrt qz)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$ \citep{pennington2017nonlinear,benigni2021eigenvalue,goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality,mei2022generalization}.

Related work and scope. \citet{bonnaire2025} prove the spectral characterisation of $\Umat$ for arbitrary input spectral measure in the proportional regime; \citet{george2025dsm} give a linear pencil with block dimensions $(\pwidth,\dlat-\dint,\dint,\nsamp)$ for data on a $\dint$-dimensional subspace with $\signoise=0$. Everything in this appendix is exact finite-size algebra for the expected gradient flow given the spectrum of $\Umat$; the only asymptotic inputs are the block scales of Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-fourbulk}, which are those theorems evaluated at the two-atom spectral measure $\{\alpha_t^2,\beta_t^2\}$ with $\signoise>0$.

\subsection{Standing assumptions}
[Assumption~\ref{ass:standing-p1}, items (A1)--(A6$'$), reproduced verbatim.]

Numerical status of (A2). At $(\dlat,\pwidth,\nsamp,t)=(20,1280,500,0.01)$ with the repository's \texttt{compute\_U}: one draw gives tail eigenvalues $\le10^{-17}$; $50$ and $400$ draws give an identical tail (max $1.79\times10^{-6}$, mean $5.0\times10^{-7}$, $0.11\%$ of $\operatorname{tr}\Umat$), so this is the true $\E_\eta$ tail and not Monte-Carlo noise. The tail top equals the sample-block bottom ($1.81\times10^{-6}$; the Marchenko--Pastur lower edge $a_*^2(1-\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2/\nsamp=4.6\times10^{-6}$ lies above the realised minimum). Under the clock $T=0.02\dlat\times$steps, $\lambda_{\mathcal Z}T\in[0.06,0.2]$ at $3\times10^5$ steps, and because $a_i^\star=Vv_i/\lambda_i$ with $\|VP_{\mathcal Z}\|_F^2/\|V\|_F^2=4.3\times10^{-4}$, the tail target mass is $\Pi_{\mathcal Z}\approx6\times10^8$ against $\Pi_{\mathcal M}\approx2.4\times10^7$. The tail is therefore slow but not inert in Frobenius norm; it is inert in \emph{loss} only to the extent of Proposition~\ref{prop:gap-p1}'s bound (weights $\bar u_i,\lambda_i\lesssim2\times10^{-6}$).

\subsection{Mean dynamics, gradient flow and residual spectral error}
[Proposition~\ref{prop:sgdmean-p1} with proof.]
[Lemma~\ref{lem:gf-p1} with proof.]
[Proposition~\ref{prop:rse-p1} with proof.]

\subsection{Operating point}
[Lemma~\ref{lem:q-p1} with proof and table.] At $t=0.01$, $\signoise=0.5$: $\beta_t^2=0.2649$, $\alpha_t^2=2.7644$, $q(5,20,60,200)=2.764,0.890,0.473,0.327$ (audit: $2.76\to0.33$); $a_1(q)^2=0.180\to0.625$ and $a_*(q)^2=0.0785\to0.0047$, which is why no edge and no timescale below is $\dlat$-independent. Trace check against the released spectra: $\operatorname{tr}\Umat/\pwidth=0.545\to0.209$ versus $\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)=0.576\to0.209$; the sample-block mean is $0.72$--$0.89\times a_*^2/\nsamp$ (Jensen dispersion of the per-sample $q_\mu$); the noise-dimension medians $5.22\to9.15$ (code units) versus the leading-order $a_1^2\beta_t^2\psi_p=4.76\to10.6$ overshoot by $10$--$15\%$ at large $\dlat$ and saturate from $\dlat=100$.

\subsection{Clocks}
[Remark~\ref{rem:clock-p1} with table.]

\subsection{Generalisation time}
[Definition~\ref{def:taugen-p1}; Proposition~\ref{prop:taugen-p1} with proof.]

Why the signal block. The noise-dimension target is exactly linear, $s^\star_{\mathcal N}(x_t)=-x_{t,\mathcal N}/\beta_t^2$ (cluster means have no $\mathcal N$-component), with per-coordinate second moment $1/\beta_t^2=3.8$, whereas the signal-subspace target has per-coordinate second moment at most $1/(e^{-2t}\sigsig^2+\Delta_t)=1.0$ (convexity of Fisher information for the Gaussian mixture); hence for $\dlat\gg\dint$ the reducible population loss is dominated by $\mathcal N$ and a test-loss plateau measures $\tau_{\rm pop}$ (rate $\lambda^{\mathcal N}_{\min}\simeq a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$) rather than $\taugen$ (rate $\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}$). Sample quality (score error in the signal subspace, FID after decoding) is a functional of the $\mathcal S$-block score only, so $\taugen$ is defined through $\mathcal S$. The block $\mathcal N$ is the buffer: its absorption costs time and buys no sample quality.

\subsection{Generalisation gap and memorisation times}
[Proposition~\ref{prop:gap-p1} with proof.]
[Definition~\ref{def:taumem-p1}.]

Consequences. (i) Under (A6$'$) the population blocks contribute nothing to the gap at the level $G_{\rm thr}$: the model's coefficient on a population direction converges to the population-optimal one, so train and test loss fall together on $\mathcal S\cup\mathcal N$ (the ``both curves drop'' phase of Sec.~\ref{sec:rfnn}). (ii) A pure sample mode ($\gamma_i=0$) raises the test loss by $\tfrac12\bar u_i\pi_i(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})^2$ and lowers the train loss by $\tfrac12\lambda_i\pi_i(1-e^{-2\lambda_iT})$; a population-aligned sample mode ($\gamma_i\approx1$, $\bar u_i\approx\lambda_i$) lowers both. The repository logs show the latter: the test loss keeps falling until $35$k ($\dlat=40$), $55$k ($100$), $90$k ($200$) steps, $20$--$60\times$ later than the slowest population e-fold ($3200/\lambda^{\mathcal N}_{\min,\rm code}=1.5$k steps at $\dlat=200$), so the top of the index-based sample block ($\lambda_{\rm code}\sim0.1$--$0.17$) is in $\mathcal M_{\rm spk}$ at large $\dlat$. The squared form $(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})^2$ is the origin of the shape used heuristically in the released predictor (Sec.~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}), but there the eigenvalues are those of $M_t$, not of the sample block, a discrepancy addressed in Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-predictor}. (iii) $\taumem^{\rm onset}$ and $\taugen$ are single-eigenvalue functionals, so $\taumem^{\rm onset}/\taugen(\varepsilon)=2\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}/(\lambda^{\rm blk}_{\max}\log(1/\varepsilon))\,(1+O(\theta_{\mathcal S}/\log(1/\varepsilon)))$ is clock-free. On the released spectra with the \emph{index-based} sample top, $\lambda^{\mathcal S}_{\min}/\lambda^{\mathcal M}_{\max}=34,63,143,211,279,340,416,426$ for $\dlat=10,20,40,60,80,100,150,200$ (monotone; $\dlat=5$ gives $21$ but there $\pwidth<\nsamp$); since $\lambda^{\rm blk}_{\max}\le\lambda^{\mathcal M}_{\max}$ these are lower bounds on the bulk-onset ratio, whose monotonicity is to be re-established after the $\gamma$-cut (Prediction 5). No statement of the form ``$\taumem-\taugen\ge(\dlat-\dint)\times(\text{a timescale})$'' follows from a diagonal flow, since modes decay in parallel; the buffer is a statement about the ratio of two eigenvalues.

\subsection{Measured proxies}
[Remark~\ref{rem:proxy-p1}.] Concretely, from \texttt{code/v3/plot.py}: $\taugen^{\rm meas}$ = first step with test loss $\le1.05\times$ its run minimum; $\taumem^{\rm meas}$ = first step with memorised fraction $>0.01$ when available (MLP, real data), otherwise first step with gen-gap $>0.02$ (RFNN). In the RFNN logs (seed 42, evaluation every $5\,000$ steps) $\taugen^{\rm meas}\le10^4$ steps for every $\dlat$; the reducible per-coordinate loss $1-\ell_{\min}$ is $0.052$--$0.063$ for $\dlat\ge40$ against a tolerance $0.05\ell_{\min}\approx0.047$, so the proxy is informative but coarse, while the $1\%$ crossing ($15$k$\to50$k steps, $\dlat=40\to200$) and the argmin ($35$k$\to90$k) are set by $\mathcal M_{\rm spk}$. $\taumem^{\rm meas}$ moves from $2\times10^4$ ($\dlat=5$) to $8\times10^4$ ($\dlat=200$) and the final gap from $0.019$ to $0.093$; the theory attributes the modest growth to the $\dlat$-dependence of $G_\infty$ entering $\delta_{\rm eff}$ and to the single-draw noise floor $\sqrt{2/(\nsamp\dlat)}$, and predicts a much steeper growth for a relative-threshold, many-draw extraction on $\mathcal M_{\rm blk}$. The repository's \texttt{bulk\_indices} cuts the sample slice at $[\dlat,\dlat+\nsamp)$; its signal and noise-dimension slices are usable as logged, its sample and rank-null masses are not the blocks $\mathcal M,\mathcal Z$ and must be re-cut at index $\nsamp$.

\subsection{Fraction-level definition}
The fraction $F(T)$ is a per-training-point statistic. Writing $\pi_i=\sum_\mu\pi_{i,\mu}$ for a decomposition of each bulk sample mode's target mass over training points, point $\mu$ is memorised once the absorbed share $m_\mu(T)=\sum_{i\in\mathcal M_{\rm blk}}\pi_{i,\mu}(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})^2/\sum_{i\in\mathcal M_{\rm blk}}\pi_{i,\mu}$ exceeds a level $\vartheta$ set by the nearest-neighbour test; since the empirical-score reverse dynamics condense onto a training point once its weight dominates, which at $t=0.01$ holds for all $\dlat$ in the sweep ($\Delta_t=0.02\ll d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)$, \citealp{biroli2024dynamical}), memorisation at fixed $t$ is a question of how much bulk sample-mode mass has been absorbed. A single edge makes $m_\mu$ identical across $\mu$ and $\hat F$ a step; the required spread of $(\lambda_i,\pi_{i,\mu})$ over $\mathcal M_{\rm blk}$ is derived in Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-fraction}.
```



---

# Piece 2: Four spectral blocks of the RFNN feature correlation matrix: counts, d_latent-dependent edges via Gaussian-equivalent coefficients, spiked-covariance outliers, trace check, separation conditions, single-copy vs xi-averaged matrix, isotropic and sigma_perp -> 0 limits

## Statements

### `lem:fourbulk-kernel` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: W has i.i.d. N(0,1) rows (exact Gaussianity over W; no assumption on the data distribution); tanh activation (odd, so even Hermite coefficients vanish)

```latex
Fix the diffused training points $x_t^1,\dots,x_t^\nsamp$ and let $q_\mu=\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$, $\rho_{\mu\nu}=\langle x_t^\mu,x_t^\nu\rangle/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$. For $w\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat})$ the pre-activations $g_\mu=\langle w,x_t^\mu\rangle/\sqrt{\dlat}$ are jointly Gaussian with $\operatorname{Var}g_\mu=q_\mu$, $\operatorname{Corr}(g_\mu,g_\nu)=\rho_{\mu\nu}$, and $K_{\mu\nu}:=\E_w[\tanh(g_\mu)\tanh(g_\nu)]=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}}c_k(q_\mu)c_k(q_\nu)\rho_{\mu\nu}^k$ with $c_k(q)=\E[\tanh(\sqrt q z)\mathrm{He}_k(z)]/\sqrt{k!}$, $c_1(q)=\sqrt q\,a_1(q)$. Hence $K=A_1(X_tX_t^\top/\dlat)A_1+K_{\mathrm{res}}$ with $A_1=\diag(a_1(q_\mu))$, $(K_{\mathrm{res}})_{\mu\mu}=a_*(q_\mu)^2$ and $(K_{\mathrm{res}})_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{k\ge3\ \mathrm{odd}}c_k(q_\mu)c_k(q_\nu)\rho_{\mu\nu}^k=O(\rho_{\mu\nu}^3)$. For the synthetic family, $\rho_{\mu\nu}=O(\dlat^{-1/2})+O(s^2\langle m_{c(\mu)},m_{c(\nu)}\rangle/(\dlat q))$ for different clusters and $\rho_{\mu\nu}\simeq s^2/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$ for the same cluster.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
$g$ is linear in $w$, hence jointly Gaussian with the stated moments. Expand $\tanh(\sqrt q\,\cdot)$ in normalized Hermite polynomials; Mehler's identity $\E[\mathrm{He}_k(g_\mu/\sqrt{q_\mu})\mathrm{He}_l(g_\nu/\sqrt{q_\nu})]=\delta_{kl}k!\rho_{\mu\nu}^k$ gives the series; oddness of $\tanh$ kills even $k$ (in particular $c_0=0$, so no rank-one $\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^\top$ term). Stein's identity $\E[f(\sqrt q z)z]=\sqrt q\,\E[f'(\sqrt q z)]$ gives $c_1=\sqrt q\,a_1(q)$, so the $k=1$ term is $a_1(q_\mu)a_1(q_\nu)\langle x_t^\mu,x_t^\nu\rangle/\dlat$. The diagonal of $K_{\mathrm{res}}$ is $\E\tanh(\sqrt{q_\mu}z)^2-a_1(q_\mu)^2q_\mu=a_*(q_\mu)^2$ by definition. The overlap estimates follow from $\langle x^\mu,x^\nu\rangle=\langle m_{c(\mu)},m_{c(\nu)}\rangle+O(\sqrt{\dlat})$ (the generator uses $\dint$ orthogonal centres plus $k-\dint$ random unit centres, so cross-cluster centre overlaps are $O(s^2)$, not zero).
```

### `ass:fourbulk-ge` (assumption, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: proportional limit p,n,d_lat -> infinity with n/p and (d_lat-d_int)/n fixed; d_int fixed; bounded pre-activation variances q_mu (true a.s. for the synthetic family); (a) proved by import (Louart-Liao-Couillet 2018, Liao-Couillet 2018, Fan-Wang 2020); (b) and (c) conjectured

```latex
Let $\Phi\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$ have rows $\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top$ and $G=\Phi\Phi^\top/\nsamp$. (a) [Bulk; imported] In the limit $\pwidth,\nsamp,\dlat\to\infty$ with $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth$ and $\gamma_\perp=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$ fixed and $\max_\mu\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ bounded, the empirical spectral distribution of $G$ coincides with that of $G^{\mathrm{GE}}=\tfrac1\nsamp K^{1/2}(ZZ^\top/\pwidth)K^{1/2}$, $Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$ i.i.d.\ $\mathcal N(0,1)$, i.e.\ a sample covariance matrix with population $K/\nsamp$ and $\pwidth$ samples. (b) [Outliers; open] The $\dint$ largest eigenvalues (a finite-rank set at fixed $\dint$) and the $\dlat-\dint$ noise-dimension eigenvalues of $G$ are located by the same replacement, to relative accuracy $o(1)$. (c) [$\xi$-average; open] The same holds for the $\pwidth\times\pwidth$ matrix $\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi\phi^\top]$ with the Gram of the $\nsamp K$ noise draws in place of $K$. Identity in distribution: in the homogeneous case $a_1(q_\mu)\equiv a_1$, writing $L=[a_1X_t/\sqrt{\dlat},\;K_{\mathrm{res}}^{1/2}]\in\R^{\nsamp\times(\dlat+\nsamp)}$ one has $LL^\top=K$, so $L\tilde Z/\sqrt{\pwidth}\overset{d}{=}K^{1/2}Z/\sqrt{\pwidth}$ for $\tilde Z\in\R^{(\dlat+\nsamp)\times\pwidth}$ Gaussian: the $\pwidth\times\pwidth$ form $\Umat\simeq\tfrac{a_1^2}{\pwidth\dlat}W\hat M_tW^\top+\tfrac{1}{\pwidth\nsamp}\Theta^\top K_{\mathrm{res}}\Theta+\text{cross}$ and the Gram form are the same object, and the correct perturbation model for the data directions is the spiked \emph{population} covariance (Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp}), not an additive spike.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) is a theorem for deterministic bounded-norm data, Gaussian $W$ and Lipschitz activation: the deterministic equivalent of the resolvent of $\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ in \citet{louart2018random} is exactly the Silverstein equivalent of a sample covariance with population $K=\E_w[\sigma(w^\top X)^\top\sigma(w^\top X)]$ (the matrix of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-kernel}); \citet{liao2018spectrum} treat Gaussian-mixture inputs with $O(1)$ same-cluster overlaps through the same Hermite coefficients, and \citet{fanwang2020spectra} give the Marchenko--Pastur--Silverstein form for the conjugate kernel. The physics counterpart is Theorem~3.1 of \citet{bonnaire2025} (arbitrary input covariance) and the Gaussian-equivalence literature \citep{goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality,mei2022generalization}, the latter for the learning problem rather than the spectrum. (b) is not covered: the $\dint$ signal directions are a finite-rank spike set at fixed $\dint$ and need BBP-type control of isolated eigenvalues of $G$ (Liao--Couillet's isolated-eigenvalue results are stated under growth-rate conditions on the class means that our fixed $s=3$, $\dint=5$ regime does not satisfy); the noise-dimension block is a bulk of vanishing relative mass $\gamma_\perp\gamma_p$ in the $\pwidth\times\pwidth$ picture. (c) is not covered because the $\nsamp K$ rows of the averaged feature matrix are strongly dependent (same-point draws have $\rho\simeq1-\Delta_t/q$). The identity in distribution is rotational invariance of the Gaussian columns: each column of $L\tilde Z$ is $\mathcal N(0,LL^\top)=\mathcal N(0,K)$.
```

### `lem:fourbulk-counts` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: n > d_lat, p >= n (for p < n the Gram has n - p zeros and the sample block has p - d_lat modes; see the d_lat = 5 footnote); exact zeros only for the single-copy matrix; the released spectra are the K = 50 average

```latex
Let $D=A_1X_tX_t^\top A_1/(\dlat\nsamp)$ (PSD, $\operatorname{rank}D\le\dlat$, $=\dlat$ a.s.\ for $\nsamp>\dlat$) and $R=K_{\mathrm{res}}/\nsamp$. Then at least $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues of $K/\nsamp=D+R$ lie in $[\lambda_{\min}(R),\lambda_{\max}(R)]$, at most $\dlat$ lie above $\lambda_{\max}(R)$, and exactly $\nsamp-\dlat$ lie inside iff every data direction is an outlier, which holds under the margin condition of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp} (measured margin $\ge20\times$ at $\signoise=0.5$; violated at $\signoise=0.01$, Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-sigperp0}). For the single-copy matrix $\Umat^{(1)}=\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ with $\pwidth\ge\nsamp$ there are exactly $\pwidth-\nsamp$ zero eigenvalues; for the $K$-draw average $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\min(\pwidth,K\nsamp)$. Hence the sample block has $\nsamp-\dlat$ modes and the last non-residual index is $\nsamp$, not $\dlat+\nsamp$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Cauchy interlacing for a PSD perturbation of rank $r\le\dlat$: $\lambda_i(R)\le\lambda_i(D+R)\le\lambda_{i-r}(R)$, so $\lambda_i(D+R)\in[\lambda_{\min}(R),\lambda_{\max}(R)]$ for all $i>r$; the top $r$ are $\ge\lambda_{r}(R)$ and may lie inside or above $\lambda_{\max}(R)$, which is the iff. Zeros: $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}=\operatorname{rank}\Phi\le\nsamp$ since $\Phi$ is $\nsamp\times\pwidth$; for the average, $\Umat=\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}^\top\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}/(K\nsamp)$ with $\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}\in\R^{K\nsamp\times\pwidth}$. The $\nsamp$ non-trivial directions of $G$ are shared between the $\dlat$ data directions and the residual, so there is no room for $\dlat+\nsamp$ non-zero modes.
```

### `prop:fourbulk` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: p >= n > d_lat > d_int; d_int, t fixed; proportional limit with gamma_perp, gamma_p fixed; Assumption ass:fourbulk-ge (a) proved by import, (b),(c) conjectured; synthetic data model: k centres of norm s in a d_int subspace, anisotropic Gaussian noise, random rotation; q(d_lat) formula uses this; xi-averaged matrix (the paper's object); single-copy version in rem:fourbulk-singlecopy; block (iii) and (iv) statements beyond mass/mean are characterisations (heterogeneous residual not solved in closed form)

```latex
Let $\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ be the $\xi$-averaged matrix of the notation section (estimated in the code with $K=50$ draws). Assume $\pwidth\ge\nsamp>\dlat>\dint$, $\dint$ and $t>0$ fixed, $\dlat,\nsamp,\pwidth\to\infty$ with $\gamma_\perp=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$, $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth$ fixed, and Assumption~\ref{ass:fourbulk-ge}. Let $q(\dlat)=e^{-2t}[(s^2+\dint\sigsig^2)/\dlat+\signoise^2(1-\dint/\dlat)]+\Delta_t=\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat$, $\sigma_r=a_*(q)^2/\nsamp$, $\varepsilon=\bar k\,\Delta_t/q$ with $\bar k=\sum_{k\ge3}k\,c_k(q)^2/\sum_{k\ge3}c_k(q)^2\in[3.2,3.9]$, and $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=\pwidth^{-1}(\sqrt{\nsamp-\dlat}+\sqrt{\pwidth-\dlat})^2$. The ordered spectrum of $\Umat$ consists of: (i) $\dint$ signal outliers $\lambda_j^{\mathrm{sig}}=\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}[e^{-2t}\lambda_j(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)+\Delta_t](1+O(\pwidth^{-1/2}))+\sigma_r(1+O(\nsamp/\pwidth))$, population mean $a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2/\dlat$; (ii) $\dlat-\dint$ noise-dimension eigenvalues equal to those of $\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}M_\perp^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)M_\perp^{1/2}$, $M_\perp=e^{-2t}\hat S_\perp+\Delta_t I$, i.e.\ the free multiplicative convolution $[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\mathrm{MP}(\gamma_\perp)+\Delta_t]\boxtimes\mathrm{MP}(\dlat/\pwidth)$ scaled by $a_1^2/\dlat$, whose mean is $a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ exactly and whose support is $\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2+\Delta_t]$ at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$, with relative edge corrections $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ and a $\pwidth$-independent median; (iii) $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample eigenvalues given by the Marchenko--Pastur--Silverstein transform of $\operatorname{spec}(P_\perp K_{\mathrm{res}}P_\perp)/\nsamp$ with $\pwidth-\dlat$ effective samples, mean $\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)(1-\varepsilon)$, top edge $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$ where in the homogeneous single-copy case $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}=\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}$ (plain MP of ratio $(\nsamp-\dlat)/(\pwidth-\dlat)$ and scale $\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)$) and in general $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}$ is set by the spread of $a_*(q_\mu)^2$ and the rank-$\lesssim k$ same-cluster component of $K_{\mathrm{res}}$; (iv) $\pwidth-\nsamp$ residual-floor eigenvalues of total mass $\simeq\varepsilon a_*(q)^2$ (exact zeros for a single diffused copy, Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-singlecopy}). Trace: $\dint\tfrac{a_1^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat}+(\dlat-\dint)\tfrac{a_1^2\beta_t^2}{\dlat}+\dlat\sigma_r(1+\tfrac{\nsamp-\dlat}{\pwidth})+(\nsamp-\dlat)\sigma_r(1-\tfrac{\dlat}{\pwidth})=a_1(q)^2q+a_*(q)^2=\E\tanh(\sqrt qz)^2$, and $\operatorname{tr}\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E\tanh(\sqrt{q_\mu}z)^2$ exactly, which is $\E\tanh(\sqrt qz)^2$ up to a Jensen correction $O(\operatorname{Var}_\mu q_\mu)$ (2--5\% over the sweep). All data-side eigenvalues scale as $a_1(q(\dlat))^2/\dlat$ and are $\pwidth$-independent at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$; the sample block scales as $a_*(q(\dlat))^2/\nsamp$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Sketch (full version in the appendix). Step 1 (Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-kernel}): $K=\nsamp D+K_{\mathrm{res}}$ exactly. Step 2 (Assumption~\ref{ass:fourbulk-ge}): $G$ is a sample covariance with population $K/\nsamp$ and $\pwidth$ samples, so its bulk is the MP--Silverstein transform of $\operatorname{spec}(K/\nsamp)$ at ratio $\gamma_p$. Step 3: the data part of $\Umat$ is exactly $a_1^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ up to the spread of $a_1(q_\mu)$, whose nonzero spectrum is that of $\hat M_t^{1/2}(W^\top W/\pwidth)\hat M_t^{1/2}a_1^2/\dlat$; after $\xi$-averaging $\hat M_t\to M_t$, block diagonal in the signal/null split up to $O(\nsamp^{-1/2})$ cross terms; the null block is $e^{-2t}\signoise^2\times$ a white Wishart of ratio $\gamma_\perp$ plus $\Delta_t I$, and $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$ is an independent white Wishart of ratio $(\dlat-\dint)/\pwidth$, giving (ii) by asymptotic freeness; the trace of a product of free elements is the product of traces, giving the exact mean. The signal block is a finite set of $\dint$ eigenvalues of the realized centre geometry (i). Counts by Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-counts}. Step 4: Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp} (spiked population covariance) gives the outlier shift and the sample-bulk mass $(\nsamp-\dlat)\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)$, and the trace identity closes exactly at the level of expectations. Step 5: the $\xi$-average replaces the residual of each point by a coherent part $a_*^2(1-\varepsilon)$ plus $K-1$ incoherent modes of weight $a_*^2\varepsilon/K$ each (appendix~\S\ref{app:fourbulk-xi}), giving the factor $(1-\varepsilon)$ in (iii) and the floor (iv). The heterogeneous residual is characterised, not solved: its MP--Silverstein image has width set by the spread of $a_*(q_\mu)^2$ (a factor $40$ at $\dlat=5$, $4$ at $\dlat=200$) and by the same-cluster component of size $\simeq(\nsamp/k)c_3(q)^2\rho_{\mathrm{same}}^3$, both of which shrink with $\dlat$; the measured $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}$ homogenises to $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}$ within the $\sim10\%$ inflation caused by the $K=50$ average.
```

### `lem:fourbulk-bbp` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: homogeneous residual (Sigma = D + sigma_r I); for the heterogeneous residual replace the MP law by the MP-Silverstein image of spec(P_perp K_res P_perp)/n; the mass statement (b) then holds with sigma_r replaced by the mean residual eigenvalue; Z independent of D (holds under ass:fourbulk-ge: residual features are independent of the data directions); (a) needs d_lat fixed; (b) is exact for any d_lat

```latex
Let $G=\Sigma^{1/2}(ZZ^\top/\pwidth)\Sigma^{1/2}$ with $Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$ i.i.d.\ $\mathcal N(0,1)$ and population $\Sigma=D+\sigma_rI$, $D\succeq0$ of rank $\dlat$ with eigenvalues $\theta_1\ge\dots\ge\theta_{\dlat}$ (spiked population covariance, $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth<1$). (a) [BBP] In the proportional limit with $\dlat$ fixed, the eigenvalue of $G$ associated with $\theta_j$ is $\lambda(\theta_j)=(\theta_j+\sigma_r)(1+\gamma_p\sigma_r/\theta_j)=\theta_j+\sigma_r(1+\gamma_p)+\gamma_p\sigma_r^2/\theta_j$ if $\theta_j>\theta_c=\sigma_r\sqrt{\gamma_p}$, and sticks to the edge $\sigma_r(1+\sqrt{\gamma_p})^2$ otherwise. (b) [Exact mass bookkeeping, any $\dlat<\min(\nsamp,\pwidth)$] Conditionally on the $\dlat$ rows of $Z$ in the spike eigenbasis, the restriction of $G$ to the orthogonal complement of the $\dlat$ spiked directions is $(\sigma_r/\pwidth)Z_2P^\perp Z_2^\top$ with $P^\perp$ the projector off the row space of $Z_1$: a white Wishart with $\pwidth-\dlat$ samples in $\nsamp-\dlat$ dimensions, hence a Marchenko--Pastur law of ratio $(\nsamp-\dlat)/(\pwidth-\dlat)$, scale $\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)$, expected mass $(\nsamp-\dlat)\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)$ and top edge $\sigma_r\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=\tfrac{\sigma_r}{\pwidth}(\sqrt{\nsamp-\dlat}+\sqrt{\pwidth-\dlat})^2$ (also valid for $\pwidth<\nsamp$, where the block has $\pwidth-\dlat$ modes). Since $\E\operatorname{tr}G=\operatorname{tr}\Sigma$, the $\dlat$ outliers carry expected total mass $\sum_j\theta_j+\dlat\sigma_r(1+\tfrac{\nsamp-\dlat}{\pwidth})$, i.e.\ a mean shift $\sigma_r(1+\tfrac{\nsamp-\dlat}{\pwidth})$ per outlier, consistent with (a) at leading order in $\dlat/\nsamp$. In code units $\theta_c\pwidth\in[0.03,0.1]$ while the smallest noise-dimension eigenvalue is $2$--$5$ at $\signoise=0.5$: all $\dlat$ data directions are outliers by $\ge20\times$, and the shift $\sigma_r$ is $0.15$--$0.25\%$ of $\lambda^{\mathrm{sig}}_{\min}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) is the spiked-sample-covariance theorem of \citet{baik2005phase} and \citet{baik2006eigenvalues} (real case), or the multiplicative case of \citet{benaychgeorges2011eigenvalues}: for a population eigenvalue $\ell=\theta+\sigma_r$ above the bulk level $\sigma_r$, the outlier is $\ell(1+\gamma_p\sigma_r/(\ell-\sigma_r))$ when $\ell>\sigma_r(1+\sqrt{\gamma_p})$. (b) Write $\Sigma^{1/2}Z=[\,(\Theta+\sigma_rI)^{1/2}Z_1;\ \sqrt{\sigma_r}Z_2\,]$ in the spike eigenbasis. The $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues of $G$ not associated with the spikes are, by the Schur complement / interlacing for the $\pwidth\times\pwidth$ dual $Z^\top\Sigma Z/\pwidth$, those of $\sigma_rZ_2P^\perp Z_2^\top/\pwidth$ up to $\dlat$ interlacing corrections; $Z_2P^\perp$ has, conditionally on $Z_1$, i.i.d.\ Gaussian rows in a $(\pwidth-\dlat)$-dimensional subspace, giving the Wishart statement. Expected mass is $\sigma_r(\nsamp-\dlat)(\pwidth-\dlat)/\pwidth$; subtracting from $\E\operatorname{tr}G=\operatorname{tr}\Sigma=\sum_j\theta_j+\nsamp\sigma_r$ gives the outlier mass. Numerical check (homogeneous $\nsamp=500$, $\dlat=40$, $\pwidth=2560$, $5$ reps): bulk mass$/(\sigma_r(\nsamp-\dlat))=0.9840$ vs $1-\dlat/\pwidth=0.9844$; top edge$/\sigma_r=1.987$ vs $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=2.005$ (the additive-model value $(1+\sqrt{\gamma_p})^2=2.079$ is excluded); the per-outlier shift is too noisy in a single run ($\theta_j$ fluctuates by $\theta_j\sqrt{2/\pwidth}$) to discriminate $\sigma_r$ from $1.18\sigma_r$, which is why (b) is stated in expectation.
```

### `cor:fourbulk-separation` (corollary, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Proposition prop:fourbulk; kappa_top taken as measured (or kappa_MP in the homogeneous single-copy limit); leading order in d_lat/p for the noise-dim edges

```latex
Under Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk}, at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$, the blocks are disjoint iff (signal $\succ$ noise-dim) $\lambda_{\min}(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)>\signoise^2(1+\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$, in which $a_1^2$, $1/\dlat$ and $\Delta_t$ cancel exactly; with population means as proxies this is $(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)/(\beta_t^2-\Delta_t)=(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)/\signoise^2>(1+\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$ (left side $11.2$ at $\signoise=0.5$; right side $\le2.7$ for $\dlat\le240$; the ratio is $\dlat/\nsamp$, not $\pwidth$); and (noise-dim $\succ$ sample) $\tfrac{\nsamp}{\dlat}\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{a_*(q)^2}[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1-\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2+\Delta_t]>\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}$, where the bracket carries an $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ correction from the $W$ factor (order one for $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$ at $\dlat=200$, where $\dlat/\pwidth=0.2$). The second condition has explicit $\dlat$ dependence through $\nsamp/\dlat$, the de-saturation ratio $a_1^2/a_*^2$ (which grows $2.3\to134$ over the sweep) and $(1-\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$; the measured gap $\lambda^{\mathrm{nd}}_{\min}/\lambda^{\mathrm{samp}}_{\max}$ is non-monotone in $\dlat$ on both width rules ($4.4,7.7,13,18,12$ at $\pwidth=64\dlat$; $5.1,5.9,8.2,11.4,6.1$ at $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$; $\dlat=10,\dots,200$), closing as $\dlat\to\nsamp$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Compare $\lambda^{\mathrm{sig}}_{\min}$ with the upper edge of block (ii) and the lower edge of block (ii) with $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$, using the edge formulas of Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk}; the common factor $a_1^2/\dlat$ and the additive $\Delta_t$ cancel in the first comparison, and $\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t=e^{-2t}(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)$, $\beta_t^2-\Delta_t=e^{-2t}\signoise^2$. Numbers from the released spectra (appendix Table).
```

### `rem:fourbulk-singlecopy` (remark, **proved**)

Hypotheses: p >= n

```latex
For the single-copy matrix $\Umat^{(1)}=\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ (one diffused copy per point) the statements of Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk} hold with: $\hat M_t$ in place of $M_t$, so that $\Delta_t$ enters block (ii) through the Wishart variance and the leading-order support is $\tfrac{a_1^2}{\dlat}\beta_t^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$ (lower edge $1.50$ vs $2.17$ code units at $\dlat=200$; measured $4.58$ vs formula $4.52$ at $\dlat=40$, $K=1$); $\varepsilon=0$; and exactly $\pwidth-\nsamp$ zero eigenvalues (numerically $\lambda_{\nsamp+1}/\lambda_\nsamp\sim3\times10^{-6}$ in the float32 code path, $10^{-16}$ in float64), i.e.\ a machine-precision cliff at index $\nsamp$. On the released $K=50$ spectra there is no visible drop at index $\nsamp$ for $\dlat\le20$ ($\lambda_\nsamp/\lambda_{\nsamp+1}=1.01$; $\gamma_p\ge0.4$ pushes the lower MP edge of the sample block to $\approx0$) and a drop by $1.32$, $1.68$, $1.73$ at $\dlat=40,100,200$; there is no drop at index $\dlat+\nsamp$ at any $\dlat$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Rank count of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-counts}; direct recomputation with the repository generator and \texttt{compute\_U} at $K=1$ and $K=50$.
```

### `rem:fourbulk-isotropic` (remark, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Proposition prop:fourbulk with sigma_perp = sigma_sig

```latex
For $\signoise=\sigsig$ the first separation condition fails once $\gamma_\perp\gtrsim0.1$ ($(s^2/\dint+1)/1=2.8$ vs $(1+\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$): blocks (i) and (ii) merge into the single population bulk $\rho_2$ of Bonnaire et al.\ with the $\dint$ signal directions as BBP outliers at its top, and (iii) is their $\rho_1$. Block (ii) does not disappear: at $\dlat=100$, $\signoise=1$ ($q=1.088$, $a_1^2=0.348$), its leading-order lower edge $a_1^2\pwidth[e^{-2t}(1-\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2+\Delta_t]/\dlat=7.4$ (measured $7.4$--$7.5$; the $W$-factor correction is $\approx-4\%$) sits $12\times$ above the sample top $0.61$, and the signal median is $61.8$ (predicted $61.6$). The buffer between $\rho_2$ and $\rho_1$ exists in the isotropic setting; anisotropy adds the split of $\rho_2$ into (i) and (ii), not the existence of (ii).
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Set $\signoise=\sigsig=1$ in Corollary~\ref{cor:fourbulk-separation} and evaluate; the numbers are from a direct simulation with the repository generator and estimator ($\dlat=100$, $\pwidth=6400$, $K=50$).
```

### `rem:fourbulk-sigperp0` (remark, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Proposition prop:fourbulk; heterogeneous sample bulk with kappa_top as measured

```latex
As $\signoise\to0$ the noise-dimension population eigenvalues collapse onto the diffusion floor $\theta_\perp=a_1^2\Delta_t/\dlat$, which is comparable to the top edge $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$ of the heterogeneous sample bulk ($\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\approx10$--$16$ at $\dlat\le40$); by Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp} such directions are not outliers far above the bulk but are repelled to just above its edge. Blocks (ii) and (iii) therefore merge, which is the smooth data/sample transition observed at $\signoise=0.01$: noise-dim min/median/max $=0.71/0.87/1.08$, $0.58/0.68/0.96$, $0.64/0.81/1.00$ (code units) at $\dlat=10,20,40$, versus floor $a_1^2\Delta_t\pwidth/\dlat=0.38,0.57,0.76$ and sample top $0.66,0.58,0.56$; gap ratio $1.08,1.01,1.14$. The $\signoise=0$ pencil of \citet{george2025dsm} is this merged limit: the $\dlat-\dint$ null directions are no longer separated from the sample block.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Evaluate block (ii) at $\signoise\to0$ and compare with $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$ from Table; numbers from \texttt{sigma\_noise\_0.01/exp2\_rfnn/raw\_data}.
```

## Predictions

- **Block (ii) is MP(gamma_perp) boxtimes MP(d_lat/p) scaled by a_1(q)^2/d_lat: its MEDIAN is p-independent (to ~1%) and given by the MP median at ratio gamma_perp; its EDGES move by O(sqrt(d_lat/p)) relative to a_1^2/d_lat [e^{-2t} sigma_perp^2 (1 +- sqrt(gamma_perp))^2 + Delta_t]; the block width grows with d_lat/n and its lower edge sinks toward a_1^2 Delta_t/d_lat as d_lat -> n.**  
  test: Existing files: theory-unit noise-dim medians on clean figures/p_n_plus_d_plus_r/exp2_main_gmm (p = d+n+300, K=500) vs p=64d released spectra; direct recomputation with the repo generator at d=40 for p = 16d, 64d, 256d (single copy). New cheap run: n-sweep at fixed d_lat=40 (n = 250, 500, 1000, 2000) checking the min edge follows (1-sqrt((d_lat-5)/n))^2.  
  support: p=64d (K=50): nd min meas/pred 4.10/3.92, 5.16/4.71, 4.90/4.81, 3.57/3.66, 2.12/2.17; median 5.22/4.75, 7.03/6.60, 8.36/8.19, 9.32/9.34, 9.22/9.31; max 6.44/5.69, 9.52/8.98, 13.8/13.0, 19.75/19.68, 27.2/26.7 at d=10..200. p=n+d+300 (theory units, meas/pred median) 0.0083/0.0074, 0.0051/0.0052, 0.0030/0.0033, 0.0014/0.0016, 0.0007/0.0008. Referee simulation d=40 single copy: min/max 1.05/4.07 vs 1.20/3.25 at p=16d (-13%/+25%), 4.63/13.47 vs 4.81/13.00 at p=64d, 18.96/54.9 vs 19.25/52.0 at p=256d; medians 2.07/8.11/33.5 vs 2.05/8.19/32.75 (p-independent).

- **The sample-bulk top edge homogenises to kappa_MP sigma_r with kappa_MP = (1/p)(sqrt(n-d)+sqrt(p-d))^2 (NOT (1+sqrt(n/p))^2) as d_lat grows, from above; the excess at small d_lat is set by the spread of a_*(q_mu)^2 over training points (range 0.0036-0.153 at d=5, 0.0024-0.0089 at d=200) plus a same-cluster component of size ~(n/k) c_3(q)^2 rho_same^3; the K-draw xi-average inflates the top by ~10%.**  
  test: Compute per-sample q_mu from the training set and a_*(q_mu)^2 by Gauss-Hermite; compare the sample-block eigenvalue distribution with the MP-Silverstein transform of {a_*(q_mu)^2} with p-d effective samples; regenerate data with k=1 at fixed norm to remove the cluster component; recompute kappa_top with K=1 to remove the xi-average inflation.  
  support: p=64d (K=50): kappa_top 19.0, 16.3, 11.1, 5.85, 2.41, 1.45 vs kappa_MP 5.00, 3.49, 2.57, 2.01, 1.54, 1.31 (d=5..200); at d=200, 1.45/1.11 = 1.31 once the measured ~11% K=50 inflation (0.371 -> 0.413 in referee simulation) is removed. p=n+d+300 (K=500): kappa_top 14.5, 12.1, 8.0, 3.10, 2.00 vs kappa_MP 3.14, 3.07, 2.94, 2.59, 2.08 (d=10..200): the corrected formula resolves the apparent 'below MP' value 2.00 vs old 2.91 at d=200. Kernel-limit simulation at p=40000 shows the width is intrinsic to K_res.

- **tau_mem(onset) = 1/(kappa_top sigma_r) in theory units; its growth over the sweep decomposes into the a_*(q(d_lat))^2 trajectory (16.7x, tanh de-saturation, closed form via eq. q(d_lat)) and the homogenisation of kappa_top (13.1x, characterised). Falsifiable content: a different sigma_perp changes q(d) and hence the predicted a_*^2 curve before any run.**  
  test: Regress log tau_mem(onset) on log a_*(q(d))^2 and log kappa_top across the sweep; predict a_*(q(d))^2 for sigma_noise = 0.1, 0.3 (clean figures/p64/exp2_robust_gaussian_sigma, Gaussian data) and 0.01 (sigma_noise_0.01/exp2_rfnn) and compare the sample-top eigenvalue without new runs.  
  support: Theory units (p/lambda_code) on the Table spectra: tau_mem(onset) 334, 688, 1918, 6843, 32249, 73857 (221x) = a_*^2 ratio 16.7 x kappa_top ratio 13.1; tau_mem/tau_gen 21.2, 34.3, 62.9, 143, 340, 426 (20.1x, monotone) = (a_1^2/a_*^2 ratio 58)(kappa_top ratio 13.1)/(n/d ratio 40) x realized lambda_min factor 1.05 = 20.

- **tau_gen = 1/lambda_min^sig = d_lat / (a_1(q(d_lat))^2 [e^{-2t} lambda_5(C_hat + S_hat_par) + Delta_t]) in theory units, with lambda_5 the fifth eigenvalue of the realized centre configuration; a_1^2 rising 0.18 -> 0.625 makes the growth ~11x over d_lat = 5..200 rather than 40x.**  
  test: Plot tau_gen(d_lat) a_1(q(d_lat))^2/d_lat; it should equal 1/(e^{-2t} lambda_5 + Delta_t) of the realized training set (computable from the generator with the run's seed). Repeat with several seeds of the centre geometry.  
  support: Released seed-42 spectra: tau_gen 15.8 -> 173 (10.96x) = 40 x (0.180/0.625) x (1.76/1.85), no residual; a_1(q)^2 (p/d) lambda_j(M_t^realized) reproduces all five signal eigenvalues to 2-5% at every d (fifth: 20.3/20.2, 31.9/31.5, 42.0/44.2, 53.6/55.9, 67.5/67.8, 73.9/75.1 meas/pred at d=5..200).

- **Memorization RETURNS as d_lat -> n because the noise-dim/sample gap closes: (1-sqrt((d_lat-d_int)/n))^2 -> 0 lowers the noise-dim floor onto a_1^2 Delta_t/d_lat while the sample block empties (n-d_lat -> 0); the gap ratio is non-monotone in d_lat with a peak near d_lat = 100.**  
  test: Spectral half: clean figures/p64/exp2_main_gmm already has d=500 and d=1000 (n=500): check nd-min and the drop at index n. Memorised-fraction half: train RFNN at d_lat = 300, 400, 480 (n=500) and check the fraction rises again. (Flag: config.json files in clean figures/p64 report d_latent=20 at every d; stale.)  
  support: Gap lambda_min^nd/lambda_max^samp = 4.4, 7.7, 13, 18, 12 (p=64d) and 5.1, 5.9, 8.2, 11.4, 6.1 (p=n+d+300) at d=10..200; at d=500 nd-min = 0.77 code units with a 3.9x drop at index n, at d=1000 the nd median collapses to 1.00 with a 2.9x drop; F13's hidden = 8 d_lat sweep shows memorization returning.

- **With one diffused copy per training point U has exactly p-n zero eigenvalues; with the K-draw xi-average the p-n block becomes a residual floor of total mass ~ kbar (Delta_t/q) a_*^2, kbar = 3.2-3.9, visible as a 1.3-1.8x drop at index n only when gamma_p <= 0.2 (d_lat >= 40 at p = 64 d_lat) and absent (ratio 1.00-1.01) for gamma_p >= 0.4 or for p = d+n+300.**  
  test: Recompute U with n_noise_samples in {1, 50} and t in {0.001, 0.01, 0.05}; measure lambda_n/lambda_{n+1} and the tail mass sum_{i>n} lambda_i/(p a_*^2), which should scale as Delta_t/q.  
  support: K=1: lambda_{n+1} ~ 3e-6 (float32) / 1e-16 (float64). K=50, p=64d: lambda_n/lambda_{n+1} = 1.01, 1.01, 1.32, 1.68, 1.73 and tail/a_*^2 = 0.001, 0.017, 0.065, 0.139, 0.196 at d=10..200 vs kbar Delta_t/q = 0.05, 0.08, 0.11, 0.16, 0.19 (matches at d >= 100; at small d the p-n dimensions are too few to host the incoherent modes, which stay inside the sample block). p=n+d+300, K=500: ratio 1.00-1.01 at every d.

- **Trace-level bookkeeping closes: tr U = (1/n) sum_mu E tanh^2(sqrt(q_mu) z) to 1.5%, and = a_1(q)^2 q + a_*(q)^2 to 2-5% (Jensen). Block-level bookkeeping does NOT yet close: the sample block carries only 0.57-0.81 of the predicted mass a_*^2 (1-d/n)(1-d/p)(1-epsilon), the deficit being largest at small d_lat where residual heterogeneity and the same-cluster component are strongest.**  
  test: Sum measured eigenvalues per block on any released spectrum; decompose K_res of the realized training set into diagonal, same-cluster and cross-cluster parts by Gauss-Hermite and check whether the same-cluster mass (which lives in the span of the cluster-indicator vectors and is absorbed by the signal outliers) accounts for the deficit.  
  support: tr U/p measured vs heterogeneous prediction: 0.545/0.548, 0.456/0.449, 0.363/0.361, 0.292/0.292, 0.233/0.233, 0.209/0.209 (homogeneous: 0.576, 0.470, 0.373, 0.297, 0.235, 0.209). Sample-block sum / a_*^2(1-d/n): 0.57, 0.65, 0.72, 0.77, 0.80, 0.81 at d=5..200; nd block sum at d=40: 0.118 vs 0.114.

- **As sigma_perp -> 0 blocks (ii) and (iii) merge: the noise-dim eigenvalues sit just above the top of the heterogeneous sample bulk, not at the floor a_1^2 Delta_t/d_lat; the merged transition is smooth and the buffer disappears in the spectral sense while the d_lat - d_int directions still exist.**  
  test: Sweep sigma_perp in {0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5} at d_lat = 40 and track lambda_min^nd/lambda_max^samp; it should cross 1 near the sigma_perp where a_1^2 e^{-2t} sigma_perp^2 (1-sqrt(gamma_perp))^2/d_lat ~ (kappa_top - 1) sigma_r.  
  support: sigma_noise_0.01 released spectra: nd min/med/max 0.71/0.87/1.08, 0.58/0.68/0.96, 0.64/0.81/1.00 vs floor 0.38, 0.57, 0.76 and sample top 0.66, 0.58, 0.56 (d=10,20,40); gap 1.08, 1.01, 1.14.

## Empirical anchor
Normalization: code eigenvalues = p x theory; theory-unit timescales = p/lambda_code. All numbers at sigma_perp=0.5, n=500, p=64 d_lat, t=0.01 (alpha_t^2=2.764, beta_t^2=0.265, Delta_t=0.0198), seed 42, K=50, from sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_exp3_dynamics/raw_data_exp2v3/di5_d{5,10,20,40}_n500_s42/eigenvalues_pre.npy and sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn/raw_data/di5_d{100,200}_n500_s42/eigenvalues_pre.npy. q(d)=2.764, 1.515, 0.890, 0.577, 0.390, 0.327; a_1^2=0.180, 0.281, 0.393, 0.494, 0.586, 0.625; a_*^2=0.0785, 0.0447, 0.0235, 0.0125, 0.0064, 0.0047; c_3^2=0.052, 0.033, 0.019, 0.011, 0.006, 0.004; kbar=3.9, 3.7, 3.5, 3.3, 3.2, 3.2. Signal eigenvalues measured vs realized-geometry prediction a_1^2 (p/d) lambda_j(e^{-2t} X^T X/n + Delta_t I): d=5: 46.6, 41.9, 29.7, 22.0, 20.3 vs 47.2, 39.5, 27.3, 24.3, 20.2; d=40: 124.3, 104.5, 72.8, 64.3, 53.6 vs 130.1, 109.1, 75.3, 67.3, 55.9; d=200: 167.8, 135.4, 99.8, 87.1, 73.9 vs 168.8, 141.4, 99.4, 88.0, 75.1 (mean-only a_1^2 alpha_t^2 psi_p: 31.9, 49.7, 69.5, 87.3, 103.6, 110.6 overshoots the median by 10-20%). Noise-dim median meas/MP-median pred: 5.22/4.75, 7.03/6.60, 8.36/8.19, 9.32/9.34, 9.22/9.31; min 4.10/3.92, 5.16/4.71, 4.90/4.81, 3.57/3.66, 2.12/2.17; max 6.44/5.69, 9.52/8.98, 13.8/13.0, 19.75/19.68, 27.2/26.7. kappa_top = lambda_max^samp/(a_*^2 p/n): 19.0, 16.3, 11.1, 5.85, 2.41, 1.45 vs kappa_MP=(1/p)(sqrt(n-d)+sqrt(p-d))^2 = 5.00, 3.49, 2.57, 2.01, 1.54, 1.31. tr U/p meas / het pred / hom pred: 0.545/0.548/0.576, 0.456/0.449/0.470, 0.363/0.361/0.373, 0.292/0.292/0.297, 0.233/0.233/0.235, 0.209/0.209/0.209. Sample-block sum / a_*^2(1-d/n): 0.57, 0.65, 0.72, 0.77, 0.80, 0.81. Tail (i>n) mass / a_*^2: 0.001, 0.017, 0.065, 0.139, 0.196 (d=10..200); lambda_n/lambda_{n+1} = 1.01, 1.01, 1.32, 1.68, 1.73. Gap lambda_min^nd/lambda_max^samp: 4.4, 7.7, 13, 18, 12. Timescales (theory units): tau_gen 15.8, 20.1, 30.5, 47.7, 94.8, 173 (10.96x = 40 x 0.180/0.625 x 1.76/1.85); tau_mem(onset) 334, 688, 1918, 6843, 32249, 73857 (221x); ratio 21.2, 34.3, 62.9, 143, 340, 426 (20.1x, monotone). p=n+d+300 set (clean figures/p_n_plus_d_plus_r/exp2_main_gmm, K=500, d=10..200): nd median theory units meas/pred 0.0083/0.0074, 0.0051/0.0052, 0.0030/0.0033, 0.0014/0.0016, 0.0007/0.0008; kappa_top 14.5, 12.1, 8.0, 3.10, 2.00 vs kappa_MP 3.14, 3.07, 2.94, 2.59, 2.08; gap 5.1, 5.9, 8.2, 11.4, 6.1; lambda_n/lambda_{n+1} = 1.00-1.01. sigma_noise_0.01 (exp2_rfnn/raw_data, d=10,20,40): nd min/med/max 0.71/0.87/1.08, 0.58/0.68/0.96, 0.64/0.81/1.00; floor 0.38, 0.57, 0.76; sample top 0.66, 0.58, 0.56; gap 1.08, 1.01, 1.14. Isotropic control (sigma_perp=1, d=100, p=6400, K=50, own simulation): nd-min 7.4-7.5 vs 7.40 predicted, nd-max 44.8 vs 45.5, signal median 61.8 vs 61.6, sample top 0.61, gap 12. Spiked-covariance simulation (homogeneous, n=500, d=40, p=2560): bulk mass/(sigma_r(n-d)) 0.9840 vs (1-d/p)=0.9844; top/sigma_r 1.987 vs kappa_MP 2.005 (additive-model 2.079 excluded). Fixed-mu_1 theory predicts flat 64.9/6.22 and is rejected.

## Open gaps
(1) F1 vs the released matrix (both referees; accepted): the proposition is now stated for the xi-averaged U (the paper's object), with the single-copy exact-rank statement moved to rem:fourbulk-singlecopy. Consequence for the draft that the orchestrator must apply: sec-rfnn.tex line 57 and clean figures/p64/README.md colour the sample block as indices d_lat+1..d_lat+n and rank-null as d_lat+n+1..p; both must become [d_lat+1, n] and [n+1, p], and the phrase 'cliff at index n (machine precision)' must be restricted to K=1. On the released K=50 spectra there is no drop at index n for d_lat<=20. F1's 'cliff at n' is a single-copy fact. (2) BBP model (both referees; accepted): the additive-spike lemma was the wrong model for G^GE; replaced by the spiked-population-covariance version with outlier shift sigma_r(1+gamma_p)+..., sample-bulk mass (n-d)sigma_r(1-d/p), and homogeneous top edge kappa_MP=(1/p)(sqrt(n-d)+sqrt(p-d))^2 (valid for p<n too). The mean-shift claim is proved only in expectation via the trace; my 5-rep simulation gives 0.97+-0.2 sigma_r, the referee's 1.41+-0.15 - neither discriminates from 1.18. The corrected kappa_MP also resolves the empirical referee's 'below MP' anomaly on the p=n+d+300 set (2.00 vs 2.08, not vs 2.91). (3) W-factor (math referee; accepted): block (ii) is MP(gamma_perp) boxtimes MP(d/p); edges corrected by O(sqrt(d/p)), medians p-independent. F2's '<0.5%' is a median statement; I have kept F2's leading-order edge formulas with the correction flagged. Whether this factor is what carries the psi_p dependence in Bonnaire Thm 3.1 has not been checked against their SM (also their zero-mass atom 1-n/p vs 1-(n+d)/p remains unchecked). (4) Block-level mass: the sample block carries only 0.57-0.81 of a_*^2(1-d/n)(1-d/p) on the released spectra while the trace closes to 1.5% with the heterogeneous prediction. Neither the (1-d/p) factor (1.6%) nor the tail (0-20%) nor trace heterogeneity of the split (<1%) explains the small-d deficit; my candidate explanation is the same-cluster component of K_res ((n/k)c_3^2 rho_same^3 ~ 10 a_*^2 per cluster mode at d=5), which lives in the span of the cluster-indicator vectors and is absorbed by the signal outliers. Not verified; prediction 7 is downgraded accordingly. (5) F5 numbers (empirical referee; accepted as a provenance issue, F5 kept qualitatively): tau_gen 788 -> 6413 (8.1x) and ratio 31 -> 576 are not reproducible from the Table's spectra in any unit I can identify (theory units give 15.8 -> 173, 11.0x; code units 3.64x; ratio 21 -> 426, 20.1x). The qualitative F5 content (tau_gen grows ~10x, tau_mem(onset) 221x, ratio monotone ~20x) is reproduced and is what the text now claims, with units and file paths. The 'only partly explained by seed dependence' sentence was wrong and is removed: the d/a_1^2 x realized-geometry prediction matches tau_gen exactly. (6) Gaussian equivalence: bulk part imported from Louart-Liao-Couillet 2018 / Liao-Couillet 2018 / Fan-Wang 2020 (I have not re-verified that LLC's hypotheses - Lipschitz sigma, bounded ||X|| - are met by the mixture at every d in the sweep beyond boundedness of q_mu, which holds); the d_int outliers and the xi-averaged matrix remain conjectural. (7) kappa_top is characterised, not derived: the '>= with iff' is removed; homogenisation to kappa_MP is an observation (1.45 vs 1.31 at d=200 with ~11% K=50 inflation). (8) kbar Delta_t/q for the floor mass is a heuristic (coherent/incoherent split of the same-point residual kernel); it matches at d>=100 and fails at d<=20 where p-n is too small to host the incoherent modes. (9) The proposition is a proportional-limit statement but the sweeps hold n=500 fixed (gamma_perp 0..0.47, gamma_p up to 0.6 for p=n+d+300); the d_lat=5 point has p<n (block (iv) empty, sample block has p-d modes, kappa_top reported in the same units for comparability only). (10) Referee points I did not adopt: none in substance. The math referee's list 'kappa_MP = 1.98, 1.85, 1.75, 1.55, 1.31 at d=20..200' is mislabelled (its own d=40 simulation gives 2.005); the correct values at p=64d are 3.49, 2.57, 2.01, 1.54, 1.31 for d=10..200 and are used here. (11) clean figures/p64/*/config.json all report d_latent=20 (stale); the repo owner should regenerate them before these directories are cited.

## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{Four spectral blocks of the feature correlation matrix}
\label{sec:theory-fourbulk}

Let $\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ be the $\xi$-averaged feature correlation matrix (estimated in the code with $K=50$ noise draws), and let $\Phi\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$ be the feature matrix of one diffused copy per point, so that $\Umat^{(1)}=\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ shares its nonzero spectrum with the Gram matrix $G=\Phi\Phi^\top/\nsamp$; in particular $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}\le\nsamp$. Write $q_\mu=\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ for the pre-activation variance of point $\mu$ and $q=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu q_\mu\simeq\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat$, which for the synthetic family is explicit,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-q}
  q(\dlat)=e^{-2t}\Bigl[\tfrac{s^2+\dint\sigsig^2}{\dlat}+\signoise^2\bigl(1-\tfrac{\dint}{\dlat}\bigr)\Bigr]+\Delta_t
  \;\xrightarrow{\;\dlat\to\infty\;}\; e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t ,
\end{equation}
and let $a_1(q)=\E[\tanh'(\sqrt{q}z)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\tanh(\sqrt{q}z)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$, $z\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$, $\sigma_r=a_*(q)^2/\nsamp$. Split $M_t=e^{-2t}(\hat C+\hat S)+\Delta_t I$ into its $\dint\times\dint$ signal block $e^{-2t}(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)+\Delta_t I$ and its null block $M_\perp=e^{-2t}\hat S_\perp+\Delta_t I$, where $\hat S_\perp$ is $\signoise^2$ times a white Wishart matrix of aspect ratio $\gamma_\perp=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$. Set $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=\pwidth^{-1}(\sqrt{\nsamp-\dlat}+\sqrt{\pwidth-\dlat})^2$.

\begin{proposition}[Four spectral blocks of $\Umat$; sketched]\label{prop:fourbulk}
Assume $\pwidth\ge\nsamp>\dlat>\dint$, $\dint$ and $t>0$ fixed, and take $\dlat,\nsamp,\pwidth\to\infty$ with $\gamma_\perp,\gamma_p$ fixed. Under the Gaussian-equivalence hypothesis of Appendix~\ref{app:fourbulk} (the feature Gram $G$ has the spectrum of a sample covariance with population $K/\nsamp$, $K$ the $\tanh$ kernel matrix of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-kernel}; proved for the bulk, open for the $\dint$ outliers and the $\xi$-average), the ordered spectrum of $\Umat$ consists of four index blocks:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] $\dint$ \emph{signal} outliers, $\lambda^{\mathrm{sig}}_j=\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\bigl[e^{-2t}\lambda_j(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)+\Delta_t\bigr]\bigl(1+O(\pwidth^{-1/2})\bigr)+\sigma_r\bigl(1+O(\nsamp/\pwidth)\bigr)$, population mean $a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2/\dlat$;
\item[(ii)] $\dlat-\dint$ \emph{noise-dimension} eigenvalues, those of $\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}M_\perp^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)M_\perp^{1/2}$, i.e.\ $\bigl[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\mathrm{MP}(\gamma_\perp)+\Delta_t\bigr]\boxtimes\mathrm{MP}(\dlat/\pwidth)$ scaled by $a_1^2/\dlat$: mean $a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ exactly, support $\tfrac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\bigl[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2+\Delta_t\bigr]$ at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$, edges corrected by $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$, median $\pwidth$-independent;
\item[(iii)] $\nsamp-\dlat$ \emph{sample} eigenvalues: the Marchenko--Pastur--Silverstein image, with $\pwidth-\dlat$ effective samples, of the residual kernel $P_\perp K_{\mathrm{res}}P_\perp/\nsamp$ (diagonal $a_*(q_\mu)^2$, $P_\perp$ off the data directions); mean $\sigma_r(1-\dlat/\pwidth)(1-\varepsilon)$ with $\varepsilon=\bar k\Delta_t/q$, $\bar k\in[3.2,3.9]$, top edge $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$, where $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}=\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}$ for a homogeneous residual and a single copy, and in general is set by the spread of $a_*(q_\mu)^2$ and the same-cluster part of $K_{\mathrm{res}}$;
\item[(iv)] $\pwidth-\nsamp$ \emph{residual-floor} eigenvalues of total mass $\simeq\varepsilon a_*(q)^2$; for a single diffused copy these are exact zeros (Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-singlecopy}).
\end{itemize}
The blocks satisfy the exact trace identity
\begin{align}\label{eq:fourbulk-trace}
  \underbrace{\dint\tfrac{a_1^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat}+(\dlat-\dint)\tfrac{a_1^2\beta_t^2}{\dlat}}_{=\,a_1(q)^2q}
  +\underbrace{\dlat\,\sigma_r\bigl(1+\tfrac{\nsamp-\dlat}{\pwidth}\bigr)}_{\text{outlier shifts}}+\underbrace{(\nsamp-\dlat)\,\sigma_r\bigl(1-\tfrac{\dlat}{\pwidth}\bigr)}_{\text{sample bulk}}
  =a_1(q)^2q+a_*(q)^2=\E\bigl[\tanh(\sqrt q z)^2\bigr],
\end{align}
while $\operatorname{tr}\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E\tanh(\sqrt{q_\mu}z)^2$ exactly, which differs from $\E\tanh(\sqrt qz)^2$ by a Jensen correction of $2$--$5\%$ over the sweep.
\end{proposition}
Every data-side eigenvalue scales as $a_1(q(\dlat))^2/\dlat$ and is $\pwidth$-independent at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$ (the code normalization $\Umat_{\mathrm{code}}=\pwidth\,\Umat$ reintroduces $\psi_p=\pwidth/\dlat$); the sample block scales as $a_*(q(\dlat))^2/\nsamp$. Because $q(\dlat)$ falls from $2.76$ to $0.33$ across the sweep (\eqref{eq:fourbulk-q}, $\signoise=0.5$), $\tanh$ de-saturates: $a_1^2$ rises $0.18\to0.63$ and $a_*^2$ falls $0.079\to0.0047$. The edges are therefore \emph{not} $\dlat$-independent, and Table~\ref{tab:fourbulk-numbers} shows that the drift is reproduced without a fitted parameter. The counts follow from rank alone: $G$ is $\nsamp\times\nsamp$ and equals a rank-$\dlat$ data term plus the residual kernel, so interlacing leaves $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues inside the residual bulk and $\dlat$ above it once the data directions are outliers (Appendix~\ref{app:fourbulk}); the last non-residual index is $\nsamp$, not $\dlat+\nsamp$, and the block colouring in Figure~\ref{fig:fourbulk-clean} uses these index ranges.

\begin{corollary}[Separation conditions; sketched]\label{cor:fourbulk-separation}
Under Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk}, at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$ the blocks are disjoint iff
\begin{align}
  \text{(signal $\succ$ noise-dim)}\qquad& \lambda_{\min}(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)\;>\;\signoise^2\bigl(1+\sqrt{\gamma_\perp}\bigr)^2,
  \label{eq:fourbulk-sep1}\\
  \text{(noise-dim $\succ$ sample)}\qquad& \frac{\nsamp}{\dlat}\,\frac{a_1(q)^2}{a_*(q)^2}\Bigl[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\bigl(1-\sqrt{\gamma_\perp}\bigr)^2+\Delta_t\Bigr]\;>\;\kappa_{\mathrm{top}} .
  \label{eq:fourbulk-sep2}
\end{align}
In \eqref{eq:fourbulk-sep1} the factors $a_1^2$, $1/\dlat$ and $\Delta_t$ cancel exactly; with population means as proxies it reads $(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)/(\beta_t^2-\Delta_t)=(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)/\signoise^2>(1+\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$ ($11.2$ versus $\le2.7$ at $\signoise=0.5$): signal/null separation is governed by the anisotropy against a Marchenko--Pastur spread of ratio $\dlat/\nsamp$, and $\pwidth$ never enters. In \eqref{eq:fourbulk-sep2} the left side carries the explicit factor $\nsamp/\dlat$, the de-saturation ratio $a_1^2/a_*^2$ (which grows from $2.3$ to $134$ over the sweep) and a bracket that closes as $\dlat\to\nsamp$ or $\signoise\to0$; the measured gap $\lambda^{\mathrm{nd}}_{\min}/\lambda^{\mathrm{samp}}_{\max}$ is non-monotone ($4.4,7.7,13,18,12$ at $\dlat=10,\dots,200$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$; $5.1,5.9,8.2,11.4,6.1$ at $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$).
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Isotropic limit]\label{rem:fourbulk-isotropic}
For $\signoise=\sigsig$ condition \eqref{eq:fourbulk-sep1} fails as soon as $\gamma_\perp\gtrsim0.1$ and blocks (i)--(ii) merge into a single population bulk with $\dint$ BBP-type outliers at its top: this is $\rho_2$ of \citet{bonnaire2025}, and (iii) is their $\rho_1$. The $\dlat-\dint$ noise-dimension modes therefore \emph{exist} in the isotropic case and remain separated from the sample bulk by \eqref{eq:fourbulk-sep2} (at $\dlat=100$: lower edge $7.4$ predicted, $7.4$--$7.5$ measured, $12\times$ above the sample top); anisotropy adds the split of $\rho_2$ into (i) and (ii), not the existence of (ii). Conversely, as $\signoise\to0$ the noise-dimension eigenvalues fall to the level of the sample-bulk edge and blocks (ii)--(iii) merge (Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-sigperp0}): this is the smooth data/sample transition seen at $\signoise=0.01$.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Relation to prior spectral results.}
Theorem~3.1 of \citet{bonnaire2025} gives the limiting density of $\Umat$ for an \emph{arbitrary} population spectral measure $\rho_\Sigma$; Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk} is that theorem evaluated at the two-atom measure $\rho_\Sigma=\tfrac{\dint}{\dlat}\delta_{\alpha_t^2}+(1-\tfrac{\dint}{\dlat})\delta_{\beta_t^2}$, with one caveat: at fixed $\dint$ the signal atom has vanishing mass in the proportional limit, so block (i) is a finite-rank spike set governed by spiked-covariance perturbation theory rather than a bulk. The bulk part of the Gaussian-equivalence hypothesis is a theorem for deterministic data \citep{louart2018random,liao2018spectrum,fanwang2020spectra}. \citet{george2025dsm} analyze a linear pencil with block dimensions $(\pwidth,\dlat-\dint,\dint,\nsamp)$ for data supported exactly on a $\dint$-dimensional subspace ($\signoise=0$), which is the merged limit of Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-sigperp0} in which the $\dlat-\dint$ null directions are no longer separated from the sample block. What is new here is strictly positive $\signoise$, which lifts (ii) off the sample bulk by $e^{-2t}\signoise^2$ and gives it a Marchenko--Pastur width of ratio $\gamma_\perp$, together with the gradient-flow reading of the blocks as learning-rate bands (Section~\ref{sec:rfnn}).
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Four-bulk structure: derivation and numerical checks}
\label{app:fourbulk}

Throughout, $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$ with $W_{ij}\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$ i.i.d.; the released code uses $\tanh(\tilde Wx)$ with $\tilde W=W/\sqrt{\dlat}$ and no $\pwidth^{-1/2}$, so $\Umat_{\mathrm{code}}=\pwidth\,\Umat$ and all eigenvalues quoted from the repository are $\pwidth$ times the theory values; timescales in theory units are $\pwidth/\lambda_{\mathrm{code}}$. $\Umat=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ is the $\xi$-averaged matrix ($K=50$ draws in \texttt{compute\_U}); $\Umat^{(1)}=\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ denotes the single-copy matrix with $x_t^\mu=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\xi^\mu$. We write $\sigma_r=a_*(q)^2/\nsamp$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=\pwidth^{-1}(\sqrt{\nsamp-\dlat}+\sqrt{\pwidth-\dlat})^2$.

\subsection{Step 1: the feature kernel is exactly a Hermite series}
\label{app:fourbulk-kernel}

\begin{lemma}[Kernel of the $\tanh$ features]\label{lem:fourbulk-kernel}
Fix the diffused training points $x_t^1,\dots,x_t^\nsamp$ and let $q_\mu=\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$, $\rho_{\mu\nu}=\langle x_t^\mu,x_t^\nu\rangle/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$. For $w\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat})$ the pre-activations $g_\mu=\langle w,x_t^\mu\rangle/\sqrt{\dlat}$ are jointly Gaussian with $\operatorname{Var}g_\mu=q_\mu$ and $\operatorname{Corr}(g_\mu,g_\nu)=\rho_{\mu\nu}$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-hermite}
  K_{\mu\nu}:=\E_w\bigl[\tanh(g_\mu)\tanh(g_\nu)\bigr]
  =\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}} c_k(q_\mu)c_k(q_\nu)\,\rho_{\mu\nu}^{\,k},\qquad
  c_k(q)=\E\bigl[\tanh(\sqrt q z)\,\mathrm{He}_k(z)\bigr]/\sqrt{k!},
\end{equation}
with only odd $k$ because $\tanh$ is odd. By Stein's identity $c_1(q)=\sqrt q\,\E[\tanh'(\sqrt q z)]=\sqrt q\,a_1(q)$, so the $k=1$ term equals $a_1(q_\mu)a_1(q_\nu)\langle x_t^\mu,x_t^\nu\rangle/\dlat$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-Ksplit}
  K = A_1\,\frac{X_tX_t^\top}{\dlat}\,A_1 + K_{\mathrm{res}},\qquad A_1=\diag\bigl(a_1(q_\mu)\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $(K_{\mathrm{res}})_{\mu\mu}=a_*(q_\mu)^2$ and $(K_{\mathrm{res}})_{\mu\nu}=\sum_{k\ge3\ \mathrm{odd}}c_k(q_\mu)c_k(q_\nu)\rho_{\mu\nu}^{\,k}$ for $\mu\ne\nu$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Joint Gaussianity is immediate since $g$ is linear in $w$. Expanding $\tanh(\sqrt{q}\,\cdot)$ in normalized Hermite polynomials and using Mehler's identity $\E[\mathrm{He}_k(g_\mu/\sqrt{q_\mu})\mathrm{He}_l(g_\nu/\sqrt{q_\nu})]=\delta_{kl}\,k!\,\rho_{\mu\nu}^k$ gives \eqref{eq:fourbulk-hermite}; oddness kills even $k$ (in particular $c_0=0$: the features are centred, so no rank-one $\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^\top$ term appears, unlike for ReLU). Stein's identity $\E[f(\sqrt q z)z]=\sqrt q\,\E[f'(\sqrt q z)]$ gives $c_1$. The diagonal of $K_{\mathrm{res}}$ is $\E\tanh(\sqrt{q_\mu}z)^2-a_1(q_\mu)^2q_\mu=a_*(q_\mu)^2$ by definition.
\end{proof}

For the synthetic family, $\rho_{\mu\nu}=O(\dlat^{-1/2})+O\bigl(s^2\langle m_{c(\mu)},m_{c(\nu)}\rangle/(\dlat q)\bigr)$ for points in different clusters (the generator uses $\dint$ orthogonal centres plus $k-\dint$ random unit centres, so cross-cluster centre overlaps are $O(s^2)$) and $\rho_{\mu\nu}\simeq s^2/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$ for the same cluster ($0.65$ at $\dlat=5$, $0.5$ at $\dlat=20$). The off-diagonal part of $K_{\mathrm{res}}$ is $O(\rho^3)$: $K_{\mathrm{res}}$ is diagonal-dominant with a same-cluster component of rank $\lesssim k$ and top eigenvalue $\simeq(\nsamp/k)\,c_3(q)^2\rho_{\mathrm{same}}^3$ ($c_3^2=0.052,0.033,0.019,0.011,0.006,0.004$ at $\dlat=5,\dots,200$; $\approx10\,a_*^2$ at $\dlat=5$, $\approx5\,a_*^2$ at $\dlat=20$). This is the precise form of the failure of the orthonormality ansatz $v_\mu^\top v_\nu=\delta_{\mu\nu}+o(1)$ for clustered data. The mixture is not Gaussian, but Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-kernel} needs only Gaussianity \emph{over $W$}, which is exact.

\subsection{Step 2: Gaussian equivalence and the spiked-covariance form}
\label{app:fourbulk-ge}

\begin{assumption}[Gaussian equivalence of the feature matrix]\label{ass:fourbulk-ge}
Let $G=\Phi\Phi^\top/\nsamp$. (a) [Bulk] In the limit $\pwidth,\nsamp,\dlat\to\infty$ with $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth$, $\gamma_\perp=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$ fixed and $\max_\mu q_\mu$ bounded, the empirical spectral distribution of $G$ coincides with that of
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-GE}
  G^{\mathrm{GE}}=\frac{1}{\nsamp}\,K^{1/2}\,\frac{ZZ^\top}{\pwidth}\,K^{1/2},\qquad Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}\ \text{i.i.d.}\ \mathcal N(0,1),
\end{equation}
a sample covariance with population $K/\nsamp$ and $\pwidth$ samples. (b) [Outliers] The $\dint$ largest eigenvalues (a finite-rank set) and the $\dlat-\dint$ noise-dimension eigenvalues of $G$ are located by the same replacement to relative accuracy $o(1)$. (c) [$\xi$-average] The same holds for $\Umat$ with the Gram of the $K\nsamp$ noise draws in place of $K$.
\end{assumption}
Part (a) is a theorem: the deterministic equivalent of \citet{louart2018random} for the resolvent of $\Phi^\top\Phi/\nsamp$ with Gaussian $W$, Lipschitz activation and arbitrary bounded-norm data is exactly the Silverstein equivalent of a sample covariance with population $K=\E_w[\sigma(w^\top X)^\top\sigma(w^\top X)]$; \citet{liao2018spectrum} treat Gaussian-mixture inputs with $O(1)$ same-cluster overlaps through the same Hermite coefficients, and \citet{fanwang2020spectra} give the Marchenko--Pastur--Silverstein form for the conjugate kernel; the replica counterpart is Theorem~3.1 of \citet{bonnaire2025}, and the Gaussian-equivalence principle for the learning problem is \citet{goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality,mei2022generalization}. Parts (b) and (c) are open: the signal directions are a finite-rank spike set at fixed $\dint$ (Liao--Couillet's isolated-eigenvalue results require growth conditions on the class means that the fixed $s=3$, $\dint=5$ regime does not meet), and the $K\nsamp$ rows of the averaged feature matrix are strongly dependent. This is why Proposition~\ref{prop:fourbulk} is labelled sketched.

\paragraph{Identity in distribution and the correct perturbation model.} In the homogeneous case $a_1(q_\mu)\equiv a_1$, let $L=[a_1X_t/\sqrt{\dlat},\;K_{\mathrm{res}}^{1/2}]\in\R^{\nsamp\times(\dlat+\nsamp)}$, so $LL^\top=K$. For $\tilde Z\in\R^{(\dlat+\nsamp)\times\pwidth}$ Gaussian, each column of $L\tilde Z$ is $\mathcal N(0,K)$, hence $L\tilde Z/\sqrt{\pwidth}\overset d=K^{1/2}Z/\sqrt{\pwidth}$. Writing $\tilde Z=[W^\top;\Theta]$, the $\pwidth\times\pwidth$ picture
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-Upicture}
  \Umat\simeq\frac{a_1^2}{\pwidth\,\dlat}\,W\hat M_tW^\top+\frac{1}{\pwidth\,\nsamp}\,\Theta^\top K_{\mathrm{res}}\Theta+\text{cross},\qquad \hat M_t=X_t^\top X_t/\nsamp,
\end{equation}
is therefore the same object as \eqref{eq:fourbulk-GE}, and the data directions are spikes of the \emph{population} covariance $K/\nsamp=D+R$ of a Wishart-type matrix (multiplicative model), not additive rank-$\dlat$ perturbations of a Wishart matrix; the cross terms in \eqref{eq:fourbulk-Upicture} are what the multiplicative model resolves. Two consequences that an additive model gets wrong: the data part $a_1^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ has the nonzero spectrum of $\hat M_t^{1/2}(W^\top W/\pwidth)\hat M_t^{1/2}\,a_1^2/\dlat$, so every data block is multiplied by a white Wishart of ratio $\dlat/\pwidth$ (Step~3); and the outlier shift and residual mass follow the spiked-covariance rules of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp} (Step~4).

\subsection{Step 3: spectrum of $K/\nsamp$, the data blocks and the four counts}
\label{app:fourbulk-counts}

\begin{lemma}[Counts by interlacing]\label{lem:fourbulk-counts}
Let $D=A_1X_tX_t^\top A_1/(\dlat\nsamp)$ (PSD, $\operatorname{rank}D\le\dlat$, $=\dlat$ a.s.\ for $\nsamp>\dlat$) and $R=K_{\mathrm{res}}/\nsamp$. Then at least $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues of $K/\nsamp=D+R$ lie in $[\lambda_{\min}(R),\lambda_{\max}(R)]$ and at most $\dlat$ lie above $\lambda_{\max}(R)$; exactly $\nsamp-\dlat$ lie inside iff every data direction is an outlier. $\Umat^{(1)}$ has exactly $\pwidth-\nsamp$ zero eigenvalues for $\pwidth\ge\nsamp$; $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\min(\pwidth,K\nsamp)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Cauchy interlacing for a PSD perturbation of rank $r\le\dlat$: $\lambda_{i}(R)\le\lambda_{i}(D+R)\le\lambda_{i-r}(R)$, so $\lambda_i(D+R)\in[\lambda_{\min}(R),\lambda_{\max}(R)]$ for all $i>r$, while the top $r$ may lie inside or above. The zeros follow from $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}=\operatorname{rank}\Phi\le\nsamp$; the averaged matrix is $\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}^\top\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}/(K\nsamp)$ with $\Phi_{\mathrm{all}}\in\R^{K\nsamp\times\pwidth}$.
\end{proof}
The outlier condition holds with a $\ge20\times$ margin at $\signoise=0.5$ (Step~4) and fails at $\signoise=0.01$ (\S\ref{app:fourbulk-sep}). The count $\nsamp-\dlat$ (not $\nsamp$) is forced: the $\nsamp$ non-trivial directions of $G$ are shared between the $\dlat$ data directions and the residual, and there is no room for $\dlat+\nsamp$ non-zero modes; the last non-residual index is $\nsamp$. The Marchenko--Pastur--Silverstein transform preserves this bookkeeping.

\paragraph{Data directions and the $W$ factor.} The nonzero eigenvalues of the data term are those of $\hat M_t^{1/2}(W^\top W/\pwidth)\hat M_t^{1/2}\,a_1^2/\dlat$ up to the spread of $a_1(q_\mu)$ across points. After $\xi$-averaging $\hat M_t\to M_t=e^{-2t}(\hat C+\hat S)+\Delta_t I$, which is block diagonal in the (rotated) signal/null split up to $O(\nsamp^{-1/2})$ empirical cross-covariances: the signal block $e^{-2t}(\hat C+\hat S_\parallel)+\Delta_t I_{\dint}$ has $\dint$ eigenvalues with mean $\alpha_t^2$, and the null block $M_\perp=e^{-2t}\hat S_\perp+\Delta_t I$ has $\dlat-\dint$ eigenvalues, $\hat S_\perp$ being $\signoise^2$ times a white Wishart with $\nsamp$ samples in $\dlat-\dint$ dimensions (Marchenko--Pastur, ratio $\gamma_\perp$, support $\signoise^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$). The factor $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$ is an independent white Wishart of ratio $(\dlat-\dint)/\pwidth$, asymptotically free of $M_\perp$, so block (ii) is $[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\mathrm{MP}(\gamma_\perp)+\Delta_t]\boxtimes\mathrm{MP}(\dlat/\pwidth)$ scaled by $a_1^2/\dlat$; the trace of a product of free elements is the product of traces, so the mean is $a_1^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ exactly, and the support is $\tfrac{a_1^2}{\dlat}[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2+\Delta_t]$ at leading order in $\dlat/\pwidth$ with relative edge corrections $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$. Direct check (repository generator, $\dlat=40$, single copy): noise-dim min/median/max $1.05/2.07/4.07$, $4.63/8.11/13.47$, $18.96/33.5/54.9$ at $\pwidth=16\dlat,64\dlat,256\dlat$ versus the leading-order formula $1.20/2.05/3.25$, $4.81/8.19/13.00$, $19.25/32.75/52.0$: the median is $\pwidth$-independent to $1\%$, the edges move by $-13\%/+25\%$ at $\dlat/\pwidth=1/16$ and by $\pm4\%$ at $1/64$. For the finite-rank signal spikes the $W$ factor contributes only $O(\pwidth^{-1/2})$ (signal-min$/\pwidth$: $0.0161,0.0156,0.0158$ across the three widths). The signal block at fixed $\dint=5$, $k=10$ is a finite set of eigenvalues of the realized centre geometry, not a self-averaging bulk: $a_1^2(\pwidth/\dlat)\lambda_j(M_t)$ computed from the seed-42 training set reproduces all five signal eigenvalues to $2$--$5\%$ at every $\dlat$ (e.g.\ $\dlat=200$: measured $167.8,135.4,99.8,87.1,73.9$ vs $168.8,141.4,99.4,88.0,75.1$; $\dlat=5$: $46.6,41.9,29.7,22.0,20.3$ vs $47.2,39.5,27.3,24.3,20.2$), whereas the mean-only value $a_1^2\alpha_t^2\psi_p$ overshoots the median by $10$--$20\%$. For the single-copy matrix $\Delta_t$ enters the Wishart variance instead and the leading-order support is $\tfrac{a_1^2}{\dlat}\beta_t^2(1\pm\sqrt{\gamma_\perp})^2$ (Remark~\ref{rem:fourbulk-singlecopy}).

\paragraph{Residual directions.} $R=K_{\mathrm{res}}/\nsamp$ has $\operatorname{tr}R=\tfrac1\nsamp\sum_\mu a_*(q_\mu)^2$, which equals $a_*(q)^2$ to $<1\%$ for $\dlat\ge20$ ($0.0733$ vs $0.0785$ at $\dlat=5$). If the residual is homogeneous ($R=\sigma_rI$) the sample block is the plain Marchenko--Pastur law of Lemma~\ref{lem:fourbulk-bbp}(b), with top edge $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}\sigma_r$. In general the block is the MP--Silverstein image of $\operatorname{spec}(P_\perp RP_\perp)$ with $\pwidth-\dlat$ effective samples, whose width is set by the spread of $a_*(q_\mu)^2$ (range $0.0036$--$0.153$ at $\dlat=5$, $0.0017$--$0.035$ at $\dlat=40$, $0.0024$--$0.0089$ at $\dlat=200$, because $q_\mu$ inherits the cross term $2\langle m_{c(\mu)},\xi^\mu\rangle/\dlat$ of the mixture) and by the same-cluster component of \S\ref{app:fourbulk-kernel}; both shrink with $\dlat$. We do not have a closed form for the resulting top edge $\kappa_{\mathrm{top}}\sigma_r$; it is characterised by this transform and measured in Table~\ref{tab:fourbulk-numbers}, where it decreases from $19.0$ to $1.45$ and reaches $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=1.31$ at $\dlat=200$ once the $\approx11\%$ inflation caused by the $K=50$ average (\S\ref{app:fourbulk-xi}) is removed. On the $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$ spectra ($K=500$) the measured values $14.5,12.1,8.0,3.10,2.00$ at $\dlat=10,\dots,200$ compare with $\kappa_{\mathrm{MP}}=3.14,3.07,2.94,2.59,2.08$; the additive-model value $(1+\sqrt{\gamma_p})^2=2.91$ at $\dlat=200$ would wrongly put the measured edge below the homogeneous one.

\subsection{Step 4: spiked-covariance outliers and the trace check}
\label{app:fourbulk-bbp}

\begin{lemma}[Outliers of the spiked population covariance]\label{lem:fourbulk-bbp}
Let $G=\Sigma^{1/2}(ZZ^\top/\pwidth)\Sigma^{1/2}$, $Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$ i.i.d.\ Gaussian, $\Sigma=D+\sigma_rI$ with $D\succeq0$ of rank $\dlat$ and eigenvalues $\theta_1\ge\dots\ge\theta_{\dlat}$. (a) For fixed $\dlat$ and $\gamma_p=\nsamp/\pwidth<1$, the eigenvalue of $G$ associated with $\theta_j$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fourbulk-bbp}
  \lambda(\theta_j)=(\theta_j+\sigma_r)\Bigl(1+\frac{\gamma_p\sigma_r}{\theta_j}\Bigr)=\theta_j+\sigma_r(1+\gamma_p)+\frac{\gamma_p\sigma_r^2}{\theta_j}
  \qquad\text{if}\qquad\theta_j>\theta_c=\sigma_r\sqrt{\gamma_p},
\end{equation}
and sticks to the edge $\sigma_r(1+\sqrt{\gamma_p})^2$ otherwise.
```
