## Empirical anchor
Two evaluation times, both from the code. MLP (t_eval = 0.1): Delta_t = 0.1813, e^{-2t} = 0.8187, gamma_t^2 = 1.000 exactly, isotropic alpha_t^2 = 2.474; actual seed-42 centers (five orthogonal + five random directions of norm 3) give eig C = {0.907, 1.033, 1.203, 2.491, 3.367} (tr 9), hence lambda^S = {1.742, 1.846, 1.985, 3.039, 3.756}, tr M_S^{-1} = 2.215, Monte-Carlo J_S = 3.582, R_irr = 1.367; beta_t^2 = 0.386 (sigma_perp=0.5; 1/beta^2 = 2.59, clock 0.648) and 0.1814 (sigma_perp=0.01; 5.51, 1.379). RFNN (t_fixed = 0.01, models.py default, run_experiment.py overrides t_eval): Delta_t = 0.0198, e^{-2t} = 0.9802, alpha_t^2 = 2.764, lambda^S = {1.889, 2.013, 2.179, 3.441, 4.300}, tr M_S^{-1} = 2.008, J_S = 3.686, R_irr = 1.678; beta_t^2 = 0.2649 (sigma_perp=0.5; 1/beta^2 = 3.78, clock 0.944) and 0.0199 (sigma_perp=0.01; 50.3, 12.56). The identification lambda_i(U) = a_1(q)^2 lambda_hat_i(hat M_t)/d_lat reproduces F2's edge formulas and the trace check (sum over data-side modes = a_1(q)^2 q). At t=0.01 the idealized q = 2.764 -> 0.327 and a_1(q)^2 = 0.180 -> 0.625 over d_lat = 5 -> 200 are exactly F3's values (the earlier attribution of the discrepancy to the empirical hat C was wrong; it was t). Finite-n null band of hat M_t (n=500, seed 42, sigma_perp=0.5): t=0.01: [0.195,0.361] (d=20), [0.156,0.410] (40), [0.098,0.516] (100), [0.054,0.658] (200), [0.045,0.707] (240) vs MP edges [0.187,0.357], [0.152,0.412], [0.098,0.525], [0.054,0.666], [0.044,0.716]; t=0.1: [0.295,0.507] (40), [0.247,0.596] (100), [0.210,0.714] (200). F3 check: 64 a_1^2 med(lambda_hat^N) = 4.77 (d=10), 9.33 (d=200) vs measured 5.22, 9.22 (population beta^2 gives 4.76, 10.60; the 13% shortfall is the MP median 0.233/0.265 = 0.88). Late-time population excess per dimension of -hat M_t^{-1} (full hat M_t): t=0.1: 0.066 (d=40), 0.167 (100), 0.365 (200); t=0.01: 0.323, 1.21, 4.81 (exceeds the zero-model 3.78 at d=200); leading-order F_t(c) = c (e^{-2t} sigma_perp^2)^2/beta_t^6 gives 0.051 vs exact 0.060 at t=0.1, d=40. At sigma_perp = 0.01 the null block of hat M_t is Delta_t I within 1% (no floor). RFNN median null clock: flow units 26.2 -> 302 (11.5x, t=0.01); steps 25/(a_1^2 beta^2) = 524 -> 151 (code/v3, T = 0.01 d_lat k) or 2620 -> 30190 (experiment_v2_rfnn.py, T = 0.01 k); stability 2 eta_eff lambda_max(U) ~ 0.05. Data-side/sample-bulk-mean ratio a_1^2 beta^2 n/(d_lat a_*^2) >= 70 across the sweep. Full-U check (compute_U, d=20, p=1280, t=0.01): null eigenvalues [3.96,5.43,7.63]e-3 vs predicted [3.82,5.13,7.10]e-3 (+4-7%), tr U = 0.357 vs 0.372. Predicted zero-init per-dim score errors: t=0.1: 0.716 (d=5), 2.357 (40), 2.544 (200); t=0.01: 0.737, 3.396, 3.700 (sigma_perp=0.5). The synthetic score_error curves themselves are not anchored yet (F14).

## Open gaps
(1) Referee-driven changes accepted in full: every RFNN-facing constant is now at t_fixed=0.01 (verified in code/v3/lib/models.py and run_experiment.py line 77), the null block is carried as the empirical hat M_t with its Marchenko-Pastur band (new prop:lin-emp), the step conversion carries the loss normalization c_loss for both scripts (eq:lin-steps), the 'empirical hat C' explanation of the F3 discrepancy is deleted (it was t), the P4 sign error is fixed, R_irr and lambda^S are the Monte-Carlo/actual-center values, 'affine unless all m_c=0' and 'iff p_t = N(0,M_t)' are corrected, enumerate uses \\item[(a)] so enumitem is not required, and the goldt/hu keys now match their years. (2) Where the two referees disagree I sided with the math referee on the late-time shape: it is U-shaped in d_lat (R_irr/d_lat still dominates at d_lat <= 20-40, the finite-n floor F_t(c) beyond), not 'monotone increasing at every T' as the empirical referee's fix suggested; both agree the original 'decreasing like R_irr/d_lat, never n-shaped' was wrong at the sweep's d_lat/n. (3) prop:lin-emp(b) drops the signal-null cross block of hat M_t; its effect is 1% on eigenvalues and 7-8% on the late floor at d_lat=200 (checked numerically), and the exact expression eq:lin-emp-excess needs no such approximation, but a clean analytic bound on the cross-block contribution (operator norm O(sqrt(c)), second-order effect on null eigenvalues through the O(1) signal-null gap) is not written out. Monotonicity of F_t(c) beyond leading order and the location of the U minimum are numerical. (4) The RFNN corollary (b) remains sketched: it needs (i) concentration of W^T W/p, (ii) control of the a_*(q) z cross terms on the data-side eigenvectors of the full U (the measured +4-7% shift is attributed, without proof, to row-wise q fluctuations and mixture pre-activations), (iii) a Gaussian-equivalence theorem with anisotropic covariance for the two-atom rho_Sigma (four-bulk piece, Bonnaire/Hu-Lu). (5) The linear model has no sample bulk and never reproduces training points; it explains the generalizing data-side modes, the score-error curve, and the finite-n null overfitting, not the NN-ratio fraction curve (F10). The bridge is cor:lin-rfnn(a): the sample modes obey the same clock law with weights lambda_i ||A_* u_i||^2 that this piece does not compute. (6) The MLP is trained with Adam, minibatches of 256 from the same n=500, t ~ U[0.01, 3], hidden=8 d_lat by default, non-zero default init; every MLP-facing prediction (monotone-then-U shape, affine law at small c, the sigma_perp control) is conjectural for it and rigorous only for gradient flow / small-step GD on a fixed-t linear or frozen-feature model. (7) The clock is 1/(4 lambda_i) for the risk and 1/(2 lambda_i) for the parameters; the draft's eq:rfnn-mode-decay uses a T twice ours and the parameter clock, so F5's absolute step counts (788 -> 6413) must be converted with eq:lin-steps (v2: T = 0.01 k) before comparison with any clock here; ratios are unaffected. (8) The E_eta-averaged U has no exact cliff at index n (eigenvalues 2.0e-6 and 1.8e-6 at indices n-1, n at d=20); F1's machine-precision cliff refers to a single-draw U, and the design section should say which object it reports. (9) The two-atom M_t and the exact linear null score (F12) are specific to cluster means with zero null component; for real VAE latents (continuous spectra, posterior collapse, F9) only the generic statements survive: mode clocks 1/(4 lambda_i), the MP broadening of under-sampled directions, and the finite-n floor.

## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{An exactly solvable linear score model}\label{sec:theory-linear}
Before the random-feature spectra we isolate the part of the mechanism that can be solved in closed form. Fix $t>0$ and write $x_t=(x_{t,\mathsf S},x_{t,\mathsf N})$ for the signal ($\dint$) and null ($\dlat-\dint$) coordinates in the rotated frame (App.~\ref{app:linear-model}, Def.~\ref{def:lin-setting}). The noised marginal is a Gaussian mixture with component covariance $\Sigma_t=\diag(\gamma_t^2I_{\dint},\beta_t^2I_{\dlat-\dint})$, $\gamma_t^2=e^{-2t}\sigsig^2+\Delta_t$, $\beta_t^2=e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t$, second moment $M_t=\E[x_tx_t^{\top}]=\diag(M_{t,\mathsf S},\beta_t^2I)$ with signal eigenvalues $\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i$, and empirical second moment $\widehat M_t=e^{-2t}\frac1n\sum_\mu x^\mu x^{\mu\top}+\Delta_tI$ on the $\nsamp=500$ training points. The code evaluates the MLP at $t=0.1$ and trains and evaluates the RFNN at $t=0.01$; constants for both are in Table~\ref{tab:lin-constants}.

\begin{lemma}[Lemma~\ref{lem:null-exact-lin}, compact]\label{lem:null-exact-lin-main}
The null block of the true score is exactly linear, $s^\star_{\mathsf N}(x_t)=-x_{t,\mathsf N}/\beta_t^2$, because the cluster means have no null component. Moreover $\E[s^\star x_t^{\top}]=-I$ (Stein), so the risk $L_t(B)=\E\|Bx_t-s^\star\|^2$ of a linear score $s_B(x)=Bx$ is the quadratic $\operatorname{tr}(BM_tB^{\top})+2\operatorname{tr}B+\mathrm{const}$ with unique minimizer $B^\star_t=-M_t^{-1}$: exact on the null block, and the best linear approximation on the signal block with irreducible residual $R_{\mathrm{irr}}=1.37$ ($t=0.1$), $1.68$ ($t=0.01$) for the actual centers.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[Propositions~\ref{prop:lin-gf-closed} and \ref{prop:lin-emp}, compact]\label{prop:lin-gf-closed-main}
Gradient flow from $B_0=0$ on the population risk gives $B(T)=-M_t^{-1}(I-e^{-2M_tT})$ with excess risk
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-excess-main}
\mathcal E(T)=\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat}\frac{e^{-4\lambda_i(M_t)T}}{\lambda_i(M_t)}
=\sum_{i\le\dint}\frac{e^{-4\lambda^{\mathsf S}_iT}}{\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i}+(\dlat-\dint)\frac{e^{-4\beta_t^2T}}{\beta_t^2}:
\end{equation}
each mode has clock $\tau_i=1/(4\lambda_i)$, its initial risk is its target energy $1/\lambda_i$, so the slow null modes are also the largest and dominate the late residual (their share grows as $\frac{\dlat-\dint}{\dint}e^{4(\lambda^{\mathsf S}_{\min}-\beta_t^2)T}$), and the per-dimension score error $[\mathcal E+R_{\mathrm{irr}}]/\dlat$ is affine in $\dlat$ after multiplication by $\dlat$ (Cor.~\ref{cor:lin-blocks}). On the \emph{empirical} risk the same flow converges to $-\widehat M_t^{-1}$, whose null block is not $\beta_t^2I$ but $e^{-2t}\signoise^2\,\mathcal W+\Delta_tI$ with $\mathcal W$ white Wishart: its eigenvalues fill the Marchenko--Pastur band $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1\pm\sqrt c)^2+\Delta_t$, $c=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$, so the null clock is a band whose slow edge grows with $\dlat/\nsamp$ ($[0.054,0.66]$ around $\beta_t^2=0.265$ at $\dlat=200$, $t=0.01$), and the population error of the trained model on the null block is exactly $\beta_t^2\sum_i\big(\beta_t^{-2}-(1-e^{-2\widehat\lambda_iT})/\widehat\lambda_i\big)^2$: modes with $\widehat\lambda_i<\beta_t^2$ overshoot, and the late-time error per dimension has a floor $F_t(c)=c(e^{-2t}\signoise^2)^2/\beta_t^6+O(c^2)$ that rises with $\dlat$ ($0.07\to0.37$ at $t=0.1$, $0.32\to4.8$ at $t=0.01$ over $\dlat=40\to200$). The score error is therefore predicted to increase with $\dlat$ early and to be U-shaped in $\dlat$ late, with an early-stopping optimum at large $\dlat$; at $\signoise=0.01$ the band collapses onto $\Delta_t$ and none of this occurs (Prop.~\ref{prop:lin-emp}, Rem.~\ref{rem:lin-iso}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[Corollary~\ref{cor:lin-rfnn}, compact]\label{cor:lin-rfnn-main}
For the random-feature model $s_A=A\phi$ with frozen $W$ and $A_0=0$, the same computation with $M_t\to\Umat$ gives $A(T)=A_\star(I-e^{-2\Umat T})$ and per-mode excess risk $\lambda_i(\Umat)e^{-4\lambda_i(\Umat)T}\|A_\star u_i\|^2$ (exact), so $\tau_i=1/(4\lambda_i(\Umat))$ for every bulk and modes in $\ker\Umat$ are frozen. Under Gaussian equivalence the $\dlat$ data-side eigenvalues are $\lambda_i(\Umat)=a_1(q)^2\widehat\lambda_i(\widehat M_t)/\dlat$, $q=\operatorname{tr}\widehat M_t/\dlat$ (sketched; this is the edge law of Sec.~\ref{sec:rfnn}, and with the MP median it reproduces the measured noise-dim medians, $9.33$ predicted vs $9.22$ at $\dlat=200$), and the finite-$n$ linear model applies with every clock multiplied by $\dlat/a_1(q)^2$. In gradient-flow units the median null clock $\dlat/(4a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2)$ grows $26\to302$ over $\dlat=5\to200$ ($t=0.01$), partly offset by the de-saturation $a_1(q)^2\uparrow$ as $q\downarrow$; in training steps the code's learning rate $0.01\,\dlat/\Delta_t$ cancels the factor $\dlat$, so the same clock is $25/(a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2)=524\to151$ steps (Rem.~\ref{rem:lin-gd}). The linear model has no sample bulk and never reproduces training points; it accounts for the generalizing data-side modes and for the finite-$n$ overfitting of the null block, and the slowest of its modes is the null band, not the signal block (Rems.~\ref{rem:lin-nomem}, \ref{rem:lin-taugen}).
\end{corollary}
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{The exactly solvable linear score model}\label{app:linear-model}

This appendix proves the statements of Sec.~\ref{sec:theory-linear}. Throughout, $t>0$ is fixed, $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$, and expectations are over the noised population distribution $p_t$ unless a subscript $n$ indicates the empirical one. Two values of $t$ occur: the MLP is evaluated at $t=0.1$ (\texttt{code/v3/run\_experiment.py}, \texttt{--t\_eval}), while the RFNN is trained \emph{and} evaluated at $t_{\rm fixed}=0.01$ (\texttt{RFNNScore} default in \texttt{code/v3/lib/models.py}; \texttt{run\_experiment.py} overrides \texttt{t\_eval} with \texttt{model.t\_fixed}; \texttt{experiment\_v2\_rfnn.py} uses the same value). Statements are basis-free; proofs are written in the rotated frame $Q^{\top}x$ where the signal subspace is coordinate-aligned.

\begin{definition}[Setting for the linear score model]\label{def:lin-setting}
Fix $t>0$ and write $x_t=(x_{t,\mathsf S},x_{t,\mathsf N})$ in the rotated frame $Q^{\top}x_t$, with $x_{t,\mathsf S}\in\R^{\dint}$ (signal block) and $x_{t,\mathsf N}\in\R^{\dlat-\dint}$ (null block). Under the data model $x=m_c+\xi$, $c\sim\mathrm{Unif}[k]$, $m_c\in\mathrm{span}(\mathsf S)$, $\xi\sim\mathcal N(0,\diag(\sigsig^2 I_{\dint},\signoise^2 I_{\dlat-\dint}))$, and $x_t=e^{-t}x+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\eta$, the noised marginal is the Gaussian mixture
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-pt}
p_t=\tfrac1k\textstyle\sum_{c}\mathcal N\big(e^{-t}m_c,\,\Sigma_t\big),\qquad \Sigma_t=\diag\big(\gamma_t^2 I_{\dint},\,\beta_t^2 I_{\dlat-\dint}\big),
\end{equation}
with $\gamma_t^2=e^{-2t}\sigsig^2+\Delta_t$ and $\beta_t^2=e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t$. Its \emph{uncentered} second moment is $M_t=\E[x_tx_t^{\top}]=\diag(M_{t,\mathsf S},\beta_t^2 I_{\dlat-\dint})$, $M_{t,\mathsf S}=e^{-2t}(C+\sigsig^2 I)+\Delta_t I$, $C=\tfrac1k\sum_c m_cm_c^{\top}$, with eigenvalues $\lambda^{\mathsf S}_1\ge\dots\ge\lambda^{\mathsf S}_{\dint}\ge\gamma_t^2$; they all equal $\alpha_t^2=e^{-2t}(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)+\Delta_t$ only for isotropic centers $C=(s^2/\dint)I$, and in general $\alpha_t^2$ is their trace average (exact because $\operatorname{tr}C=s^2$). $M_t$ is the second moment, not the covariance, of $x_t$; the two differ on the signal block by $e^{-2t}\bar m\bar m^{\top}$, $\bar m=\frac1k\sum_cm_c$ ($\|\bar m\|=1.15$ for the repo's centers), and it is $M_t$ that enters the dynamics. Given a training set $\{x^\mu\}_{\mu\le\nsamp}$, the \emph{empirical} second moment is $\widehat M_t=e^{-2t}\frac1n\sum_\mu x^\mu x^{\mu\top}+\Delta_tI$. The \emph{linear score model} is $s_B(x)=Bx$, $B\in\R^{\dlat\times\dlat}$, trained by gradient flow $\dot B=-\nabla_BL$ from $B(0)=B_0$ on either the population score-matching risk $L_t(B)=\E_{p_t}\|Bx_t-s^\star(x_t)\|^2$, $s^\star=\nabla\log p_t$, or the empirical denoising risk $L_{t,n}(B)=\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta\|Bx^\mu_t+\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t}\|^2$, $x_t^\mu=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$. The population denoising objective equals $L_t$ up to a $B$-independent constant \citep{vincent2011connection}, so it generates the same flow; $L_{t,n}$ with $\E_\eta$ is the mean path of full-batch SGD with a fresh noise draw per step, which is what the code runs (momentum $0$).
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[The null block of the score is exactly linear; the best linear score is $-M_t^{-1}$]\label{lem:null-exact-lin}
Under Definition~\ref{def:lin-setting}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] (\emph{exact null block}) For every $x_t$ and every $t>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-null-score}
s^\star_{\mathsf N}(x_t)=-\,x_{t,\mathsf N}/\beta_t^2 ,
\end{equation}
while $s^\star_{\mathsf S}(x_t)=-\gamma_t^{-2}\big(x_{t,\mathsf S}-e^{-t}\sum_c w_c(x_{t,\mathsf S})\,m_c\big)$ with softmax weights $w_c\propto\exp(-\|x_{t,\mathsf S}-e^{-t}m_c\|^2/2\gamma_t^2)$; this is affine if all centers coincide and linear only if all $m_c=0$. The weights depend on $x_t$ only through $x_{t,\mathsf S}$.
\item[(b)] (\emph{Stein identity}) $\E_{p_t}[s^\star(x_t)\,x_t^{\top}]=-I_{\dlat}$.
\item[(c)] (\emph{best linear score}) $L_t$ is a strictly convex quadratic with unique minimizer $B^\star_t=-M_t^{-1}$, which is block diagonal; on the null block $B^\star_{t,\mathsf N}=-\beta_t^{-2}I$ reproduces \eqref{eq:lin-null-score} exactly. The irreducible risk is supported on the signal block and equals
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-irr}
R_{\mathrm{irr}}(t):=\min_B L_t(B)=\E\|s^\star_{\mathsf S}\|^2-\operatorname{tr}M_{t,\mathsf S}^{-1}\;\in\;\Big[0,\ \frac{\dint}{\gamma_t^2}-\sum_{i=1}^{\dint}\frac1{\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i}\Big],
\end{equation}
with $R_{\mathrm{irr}}=0$ iff $p_t=\mathcal N(0,M_t)$ (a centered Gaussian). For the repo's centers (Table~\ref{tab:lin-constants}) a Monte-Carlo evaluation gives $R_{\mathrm{irr}}=1.37$ at $t=0.1$ and $1.68$ at $t=0.01$, independent of $\dlat$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) Because $m_{c,\mathsf N}=0$ and $\Sigma_t$ is block diagonal, each mixture component factorizes, $\mathcal N(x;e^{-t}m_c,\Sigma_t)=\mathcal N(x_{\mathsf S};e^{-t}m_c,\gamma_t^2 I)\,\mathcal N(x_{\mathsf N};0,\beta_t^2 I)$, and the second factor is common to all $c$. Hence $p_t(x)=p_{t,\mathsf S}(x_{\mathsf S})\,\mathcal N(x_{\mathsf N};0,\beta_t^2I)$ and $\nabla_{\mathsf N}\log p_t=-x_{\mathsf N}/\beta_t^2$. The signal-block formula is the gradient of $\log$ of the $\dint$-dimensional mixture $p_{t,\mathsf S}$; with a single center $m$ it is $-\gamma_t^{-2}(x_{\mathsf S}-e^{-t}m)$, affine with nonzero intercept unless $m=0$.

(b) $\E[s^\star x^{\top}]=\int\nabla p_t(x)\,x^{\top}dx=-\int p_t(x)\,\nabla(x^{\top})\,dx=-I$, integrating by parts coordinate-wise; the boundary term $p_t(x)x^{\top}$ vanishes at infinity because $p_t$ has Gaussian tails.

(c) Expanding and using (b),
\[
L_t(B)=\operatorname{tr}(BM_tB^{\top})-2\operatorname{tr}\big(B\,\E[x_ts^{\star\top}]\big)+\E\|s^\star\|^2=\operatorname{tr}(BM_tB^{\top})+2\operatorname{tr}B+\mathcal J_t,
\]
where $\mathcal J_t:=\E\|s^\star\|^2=\operatorname{tr}J(p_t)$ is the Fisher information of $p_t$. Since $M_t\succeq\Delta_tI\succ0$, $L_t$ is strictly convex with $\nabla_BL_t=2BM_t+2I$, so $B^\star_t=-M_t^{-1}$ and $L_t(B^\star_t)=\mathcal J_t-\operatorname{tr}M_t^{-1}$. Independence of $x_{t,\mathsf S}$ and $x_{t,\mathsf N}$ together with $\E x_{t,\mathsf N}=0$ makes $M_t$ block diagonal, hence so is $B^\star_t$; on the null block $B^\star_{t,\mathsf N}x_{t,\mathsf N}=-x_{t,\mathsf N}/\beta_t^2=s^\star_{\mathsf N}$ by (a), so the null block contributes $0$ and $\mathcal J_t-\operatorname{tr}M_t^{-1}=\E\|s^\star_{\mathsf S}\|^2-\operatorname{tr}M_{t,\mathsf S}^{-1}$. Nonnegativity is $L_t\ge0$, with equality iff $s^\star(x)=-M_t^{-1}x$ a.e.\ iff $p_t=\mathcal N(0,M_t)$. For the upper bound, write $p_{t,\mathsf S}=\sum_cw_cq_c$ with $q_c=\mathcal N(e^{-t}m_c,\gamma_t^2I)$; by joint convexity of $(a,b)\mapsto\|a\|^2/b$ on $\R^{\dint}\times\R_{>0}$, $\|\nabla p_{t,\mathsf S}\|^2/p_{t,\mathsf S}\le\sum_cw_c\|\nabla q_c\|^2/q_c$; integrating, $\E\|s^\star_{\mathsf S}\|^2\le\sum_cw_c\operatorname{tr}J(q_c)=\dint/\gamma_t^2$. The Monte-Carlo values use $4\times10^5$ samples of $p_{t,\mathsf S}$ with the seed-42 centers and the exact softmax score: $\mathcal J_{t,\mathsf S}=3.582$, $\operatorname{tr}M_{t,\mathsf S}^{-1}=2.215$ at $t=0.1$; $3.686$ and $2.008$ at $t=0.01$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Closed-form gradient flow and per-mode risk]\label{prop:lin-gf-closed}
Let $M_t=\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat}\lambda_iv_iv_i^{\top}$ be the spectral decomposition. Under Definition~\ref{def:lin-setting}, for every $B_0$ and $T\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-closed-form}
B(T)=-M_t^{-1}+\big(B_0+M_t^{-1}\big)\,e^{-2M_tT},
\end{equation}
and for every $B$, $L_t(B)=L_t(B^\star_t)+\operatorname{tr}\big((B-B^\star_t)M_t(B-B^\star_t)^{\top}\big)$, so the excess risk $\mathcal E(T):=L_t(B(T))-L_t(B^\star_t)$ decomposes over the eigenmodes of $M_t$ as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-excess}
\mathcal E(T)=\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat}\lambda_i\,e^{-4\lambda_iT}\,\big\|(B_0+M_t^{-1})v_i\big\|^2\quad\xrightarrow{\;B_0=0\;}\quad \mathcal E(T)=\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat}\frac{e^{-4\lambda_iT}}{\lambda_i}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for $B_0=0$: (i) the risk carried by mode $i$ decays with the \emph{mode clock} $\tau_i=1/(4\lambda_i)$ (the parameter error on that mode decays with $1/(2\lambda_i)$; all ratios of clocks are the same); (ii) the risk initially carried by mode $i$ is its target energy $\E\langle v_i,B^\star_tx_t\rangle^2=1/\lambda_i$, so the slowest modes are also the largest; (iii) if $B_0$ is block diagonal (in particular $B_0=0$) then so is $B(T)$ for all $T$: the signal and null blocks never interact during population training.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
From the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:null-exact-lin}(c), $\dot B=-2(BM_t+I)$. Set $E(T)=B(T)+M_t^{-1}$; then $\dot E=-2EM_t$, with unique solution $E(T)=E(0)e^{-2M_tT}$, which is \eqref{eq:lin-closed-form}. Substituting $B=B^\star_t+E$ into $L_t(B)=\operatorname{tr}(BM_tB^{\top})+2\operatorname{tr}B+\mathcal J_t$ gives $L_t(B)=L_t(B^\star_t)+2\operatorname{tr}(EM_tB^{\star\top}_t)+2\operatorname{tr}E+\operatorname{tr}(EM_tE^{\top})=L_t(B^\star_t)+\operatorname{tr}(EM_tE^{\top})$, since $B^\star_t=-M_t^{-1}$ makes $2\operatorname{tr}(EM_tB^{\star\top}_t)=-2\operatorname{tr}E$. Hence $\mathcal E(T)=\operatorname{tr}\big(E(0)e^{-2M_tT}M_te^{-2M_tT}E(0)^{\top}\big)=\sum_i\lambda_ie^{-4\lambda_iT}\|E(0)v_i\|^2$. With $B_0=0$, $E(0)v_i=v_i/\lambda_i$, giving \eqref{eq:lin-excess}. (i) is read off from $e^{-4\lambda_iT}$ versus $E(T)v_i=e^{-2\lambda_iT}E(0)v_i$. (ii): $\E\langle v_i,B^\star_tx_t\rangle^2=\lambda_i^{-2}v_i^{\top}M_tv_i=1/\lambda_i$. (iii): $M_t$ and $M_t^{-1}$ are block diagonal, so \eqref{eq:lin-closed-form} preserves block diagonality. (Checked against an Euler integration at $d=6$: $\max|B_{\rm Euler}-B_{\rm closed}|=3\times10^{-4}$ at $dt=10^{-3}$.)
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Discrete steps, loss normalization, and the code's clocks]\label{rem:lin-gd}
Gradient descent with step $\eta$ on $c_{\rm loss}\cdot L$ (the code multiplies the risk by a normalization $c_{\rm loss}$) gives $B_k+M_t^{-1}=(B_0+M_t^{-1})(I-2\eta_{\rm eff}M_t)^k$ with $\eta_{\rm eff}=\eta c_{\rm loss}$, i.e.\ \eqref{eq:lin-excess} with $e^{-4\lambda_iT}\to(1-2\eta_{\rm eff}\lambda_i)^{2k}$; it is stable iff $\eta_{\rm eff}<1/\lambda_{\max}$ and reduces to the flow with $T=\eta_{\rm eff}k$ when $2\eta_{\rm eff}\lambda_{\max}\ll1$, so that $\tau_i=1/(4\eta_{\rm eff}\lambda_i)$ steps. The repo's two RFNN scripts share $\eta=0.01\,\dlat/\Delta_t$ but differ in $c_{\rm loss}$: \texttt{code/v3} sums the residual $\|\sqrt{\Delta_t}s+\eta\|^2$ over dimensions and averages over the batch, so $c_{\rm loss}=\Delta_t$, whereas \texttt{experiment\_v2\_rfnn.py} (the source of the saved sweeps) divides additionally by $\dlat$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-steps}
T=0.01\,\dlat\,k\ \ (\texttt{code/v3}),\qquad T=0.01\,k\ \ (\texttt{experiment\_v2\_rfnn.py}).
\end{equation}
Stability holds in both: $2\eta_{\rm eff}\lambda_{\max}(\Umat)\approx0.02\,a_1(q)^2\lambda^{\mathsf S}_{\max}\approx0.05$ (v3) and $\le0.01$ (v2). Both use momentum $0$ and full batch with fresh $\eta$ each step, so the realized trajectory is this ODE plus zero-mean fluctuations. The draft's Eq.~\eqref{eq:rfnn-gradflow} writes $\dot A=-(A-A_\star)\Umat$ with per-mode factor $e^{-\lambda_iT}$: its $T$ is twice ours and its factor is the \emph{parameter} clock, so the risk clock $1/(4\lambda_i)$ here is $1/(2\lambda_i)$ in the draft's units. Adaptive optimizers (the MLP's Adam) are not covered.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Population risk versus $(\dlat,T)$ in the block-anisotropic model]\label{cor:lin-blocks}
With $B_0=0$ and population training, the excess risk of Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-gf-closed} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-total}
\mathcal E(T;\dlat)=\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{\dint}\frac{e^{-4\lambda^{\mathsf S}_iT}}{\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i}}_{\text{signal block}}\;+\;\underbrace{(\dlat-\dint)\,\frac{e^{-4\beta_t^2T}}{\beta_t^2}}_{\text{null block}},
\end{equation}
and the per-dimension score error is $\mathrm{err}_\infty(T;\dlat)=\big[\mathcal E(T;\dlat)+R_{\mathrm{irr}}\big]/\dlat$. Writing $a(T)=\sum_i e^{-4\lambda_i^{\mathsf S}T}/\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i+R_{\mathrm{irr}}$ and $b(T)=e^{-4\beta_t^2T}/\beta_t^2$, both independent of $\dlat$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] (\emph{affine law}) $\dlat\cdot\mathrm{err}_\infty(T;\dlat)=a(T)+(\dlat-\dint)\,b(T)$ is affine in $\dlat$ at every $T$, with slope $b(T)$ decaying at the single rate $4\beta_t^2$.
\item[(b)] (\emph{monotone in $\dlat$}) $\mathrm{err}_\infty(T;\dlat)=b(T)+\big(a(T)-\dint b(T)\big)/\dlat$ is monotone in $\dlat$ at every $T$: increasing toward the plateau $b(T)$ while $a(T)<\dint b(T)$, which holds at $T=0$ whenever $\signoise<\sigsig$ since $a(0)\le\dint/\gamma_t^2<\dint b(0)$, and decreasing like $R_{\mathrm{irr}}/\dlat$ for $T$ beyond the $\dlat$-independent crossover $T_\times$ solving $a(T_\times)=\dint b(T_\times)$. The late regime and $T_\times$ are $n=\infty$ statements; at the $\dlat/\nsamp$ of the sweeps they are replaced by Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-emp}(d).
\item[(c)] (\emph{null dominance}) For $\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i\equiv\alpha_t^2$, $\mathcal E_{\mathsf N}/\mathcal E_{\mathsf S}=\frac{\dlat-\dint}{\dint}\cdot\frac{\alpha_t^2}{\beta_t^2}\cdot e^{4(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)T}$, increasing in both $\dlat$ and $T$; with the actual centers replace $\alpha_t^2$ by the slowest signal eigenvalue $\lambda^{\mathsf S}_{\min}$ for the late-time rate.
\item[(d)] (\emph{$\varepsilon$-time}) The null block's per-dimension excess risk falls below $\varepsilon$ at $T_\varepsilon=(4\beta_t^2)^{-1}\log\big(1/(\beta_t^2\varepsilon)\big)$, independent of $\dlat$ at $n=\infty$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Insert the block spectrum of $M_t$ into \eqref{eq:lin-excess} and add the irreducible term of \eqref{eq:lin-irr}; (a)--(d) are algebraic rearrangements. In (b), $\partial_{\dlat}\mathrm{err}_\infty=-(a-\dint b)/\dlat^2$ has the sign of $\dint b-a$, independent of $\dlat$; $a(0)=\mathcal J_{t,\mathsf S}\le\dint/\gamma_t^2$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:null-exact-lin}(c). In (c) divide the two terms of \eqref{eq:lin-total}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Isotropic control and the diffusion floor]\label{rem:lin-iso}
If $\signoise=\sigsig$ then $\beta_t^2=\gamma_t^2$ ($=1$ for all $t$ when $\sigsig=1$): the null block still exists, still carries energy $(\dlat-\dint)/\gamma_t^2$ and still has its own clock $1/(4\gamma_t^2)$; what disappears is only the \emph{separation} $\lambda^{\mathsf S}_i/\beta_t^2$ of its clock from the signal clocks. If instead $\signoise\to0$ then $\beta_t^2\to\Delta_t$: the null block sits on the diffusion floor, its target energy per dimension $1/\Delta_t$ is set by the noising process alone and its clock $1/(4\Delta_t)$ is the slowest attainable at that $t$. In this limit the empirical $\widehat M_t$ has null block $\Delta_tI$ up to $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\sqrt c)^2\le2.5\times10^{-4}$ (relative $1\%$ at $\signoise=0.01$), so the finite-$n$ effects of Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-emp} are absent: the $\signoise=0.01$ sweep is the control in which this corollary applies verbatim.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Zero-initialization score error.} With $B_0=0$ the predicted per-dimension score error at the first evaluation is $[\mathcal J_{t,\mathsf S}+(\dlat-\dint)/\beta_t^2]/\dlat$ with the Monte-Carlo $\mathcal J_{t,\mathsf S}$ of Table~\ref{tab:lin-constants} (a point prediction, no fitted parameters). At $t=0.1$, $\signoise=0.5$: $0.716$ ($\dlat=5$), $1.654$ ($10$), $2.122$ ($20$), $2.357$ ($40$), $2.497$ ($100$), $2.544$ ($200$), limit $2.59$; at $\signoise=0.01$: $0.716$, $3.115$, $4.315$, $4.914$, $5.274$, $5.394$, limit $5.51$. At the RFNN's $t=0.01$, $\signoise=0.5$: $0.737$, $2.256$, $3.016$, $3.396$, $3.624$, $3.700$, limit $3.78$; at $\signoise=0.01$: $0.737$, $25.5$, $37.9$, $44.1$, $47.8$, $49.0$, limit $50.3$. The code's first logged evaluation is step $1$, which moves the top signal mode by a factor $(1-2\eta_{\rm eff}\lambda_{\max})^2\approx0.9$ and the null modes by less than $1\%$.

\begin{proposition}[Empirical-risk flow: finite-$n$ null band, overshoot, and late-time floor]\label{prop:lin-emp}
Let $\widehat B(T)$ be gradient flow on $L_{t,n}$ from $B_0=0$ and let $M_t=\diag(M_{t,\mathsf S},\beta_t^2I)$ be the population second moment.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] (\emph{exact}) $\nabla_BL_{t,n}=2(B\widehat M_t+I)$, hence $\widehat B(T)=-\widehat M_t^{-1}\big(I-e^{-2\widehat M_tT}\big)\to-\widehat M_t^{-1}$, and the population excess risk of the trained model is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-emp-excess}
\mathcal E_n(T)=\operatorname{tr}\big(E(T)M_tE(T)^{\top}\big),\qquad E(T)=M_t^{-1}-\widehat M_t^{-1}\big(I-e^{-2\widehat M_tT}\big).
\end{equation}
\item[(b)] (\emph{null block}) Let $\widehat M_{t,\mathsf N}$ be the null--null principal block of $\widehat M_t$, with eigenvalues $\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i$. Dropping the signal--null cross block of $\widehat M_t$, the null contribution to \eqref{eq:lin-emp-excess} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-emp-null}
\mathcal E_{n,\mathsf N}(T)=\beta_t^2\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat-\dint}\Big(\frac1{\beta_t^2}-\frac{1-e^{-2\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_iT}}{\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i}\Big)^2 .
\end{equation}
A mode with $\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i\ge\beta_t^2$ decays monotonically to its floor $\beta_t^2(1/\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i-1/\beta_t^2)^2$; a mode with $\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i<\beta_t^2$ first reaches \emph{zero} population error at $T^0_i=(2\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i)^{-1}\log\big[\beta_t^2/(\beta_t^2-\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_i)\big]$ and then rises to the same floor: the empirical fit overshoots the population score on under-sampled null directions.
\item[(c)] (\emph{Marchenko--Pastur band}) $\widehat M_{t,\mathsf N}=e^{-2t}\signoise^2\,\mathcal W+\Delta_tI$ with $\mathcal W=\frac1n X_{\mathsf N}^{\top}X_{\mathsf N}$ a white Wishart matrix of aspect ratio $c=(\dlat-\dint)/\nsamp$. As $n\to\infty$ with $c$ fixed its spectrum follows the shifted, scaled Marchenko--Pastur law \citep{marchenko1967distribution,couillet2011rmt}, with edges
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-mp-edges}
\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_\pm=e^{-2t}\signoise^2\big(1\pm\sqrt{c}\big)^2+\Delta_t,\qquad c=\frac{\dlat-\dint}{\nsamp}.
\end{equation}
The null clock is therefore a band $\big[1/(4\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_+),\,1/(4\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_-)\big]$ whose slow edge grows with $\dlat/\nsamp$ and diverges as $c\to1$ (the regime where the sample bulk $\nsamp-\dlat$ vanishes). By Cauchy interlacing the $\dlat-\dint$ smallest eigenvalues of the full $\widehat M_t$ obey the same bracket up to an index shift of $\dint$.
\item[(d)] (\emph{late-time floor}) As $T\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lin-floor}
\mathrm{err}_n(\infty;\dlat)=\frac{R_{\mathrm{irr}}+\mathcal E^{\rm est}_{\mathsf S}}{\dlat}+\frac{\dlat-\dint}{\dlat}\,F_t(c),\qquad F_t(c)=\beta_t^2\!\int\!\Big(\frac1{\widehat\lambda}-\frac1{\beta_t^2}\Big)^2d\mu^{\rm MP}_{t,c}(\widehat\lambda)=c\,\frac{(e^{-2t}\signoise^2)^2}{\beta_t^6}+O(c^2),
\end{equation}
with $\mathcal E^{\rm est}_{\mathsf S}=O(\dint/\nsamp)$ the signal-block estimation error. $F_t$ vanishes at $c=0$ and at $\signoise\to0$ and increases with $c$; hence at late times $\mathrm{err}_n$ is \emph{U-shaped} in $\dlat$ (the $R_{\mathrm{irr}}/\dlat$ decrease at small $\dlat$, the $F_t(c)$ increase at large $\dlat$), and at large $\dlat/\nsamp$ the trained model is worse on the null block than the zero model (per null dimension $F_t>1/\beta_t^2$ at $\dlat\ge200$, $t=0.01$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) $\nabla_BL_{t,n}=\frac2n\sum_\mu\E_\eta[(Bx_t^\mu+\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t})x_t^{\mu\top}]=2B\widehat M_t+\frac{2}{\sqrt{\Delta_t}}\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta[\eta x_t^{\mu\top}]=2(B\widehat M_t+I)$, using $\E_\eta[\eta x_t^{\mu\top}]=\sqrt{\Delta_t}I$ and $\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta[x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}]=\widehat M_t$. The ODE $\dot B=-2(B\widehat M_t+I)$ is solved as in Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-gf-closed} with $M_t\to\widehat M_t$. The population excess of any $B$ is $\operatorname{tr}((B+M_t^{-1})M_t(B+M_t^{-1})^{\top})$ by the exact expansion in that proposition, and $\widehat B(T)+M_t^{-1}=E(T)$.

(b) With the cross block dropped, $\widehat M_t$ and $E(T)$ are block diagonal; on the null block $M_t=\beta_t^2I$ and $E(T)$ is diagonal in the eigenbasis of $\widehat M_{t,\mathsf N}$ with entries $1/\beta_t^2-g_i(T)$, $g_i(T)=(1-e^{-2\widehat\lambda_iT})/\widehat\lambda_i$, giving \eqref{eq:lin-emp-null}. $g_i$ increases from $0$ to $1/\widehat\lambda_i$; if $\widehat\lambda_i<\beta_t^2$ then $1/\widehat\lambda_i>1/\beta_t^2$ and $g_i(T^0_i)=1/\beta_t^2$ at the stated $T^0_i$, after which $(1/\beta_t^2-g_i)^2$ increases to $(1/\beta_t^2-1/\widehat\lambda_i)^2$. Numerically (seed 42, $n=500$, $\signoise=0.5$, $t=0.01$, $\dlat=200$) the full-$\widehat M_t$ evaluation of \eqref{eq:lin-emp-excess} gives a late floor of $4.81$ per dimension versus $4.50$ with the cross block dropped, and the null eigenvalues move from $[0.0554,0.661]$ to $[0.0544,0.658]$.

(c) In the rotated frame the null coordinates of the training points are i.i.d.\ $\mathcal N(0,\signoise^2I_{\dlat-\dint})$, independent of the signal coordinates and of the labels, so $\frac1nX_{\mathsf N}^{\top}X_{\mathsf N}=\signoise^2\mathcal W$ with $\mathcal W$ white Wishart; the MP law with variance parameter $1$ has support $[(1-\sqrt c)^2,(1+\sqrt c)^2]$, mean $1$ and variance $c$. Interlacing: for a principal submatrix, $\lambda_{k+\dint}(\widehat M_t)\le\lambda_k(\widehat M_{t,\mathsf N})\le\lambda_k(\widehat M_t)$. At $n=500$ the measured null band and the MP edges \eqref{eq:lin-mp-edges} agree to $1$--$3\%$ (Table~\ref{tab:lin-band}).

(d) Let $T\to\infty$ in (b) and average over the MP law. Expanding $1/\widehat\lambda-1/\beta_t^2=-(\widehat\lambda-\beta_t^2)/\beta_t^4+O((\widehat\lambda-\beta_t^2)^2)$ and using $\E(\widehat\lambda-\beta_t^2)^2=(e^{-2t}\signoise^2)^2\operatorname{Var}_{\rm MP}=(e^{-2t}\signoise^2)^2c$ gives the leading term (at $t=0.1$, $\dlat=40$: $0.051$ versus the exact $0.060$). On the signal block $\widehat B_{\mathsf S}(\infty)=-\widehat M_{t,\mathsf S}^{-1}$ with $\widehat M_{t,\mathsf S}-M_{t,\mathsf S}=O_P(n^{-1/2})$ of fixed rank $\dint$, so its excess is $O(\dint/\nsamp)$. Monotonicity of $F_t$ in $c$ and the U-shape are verified numerically across the sweep (Table~\ref{tab:lin-band}): the finite-$n$ term overtakes $R_{\mathrm{irr}}/\dlat$ at $\dlat\approx40$ ($t=0.1$) and $\dlat\approx20$ ($t=0.01$).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Random-feature readout: substitute $M_t\to\Umat$]\label{cor:lin-rfnn}
Let $L_n(A)=\frac1n\sum_{\mu}\E_\eta\|A\phi(x_t^\mu)+\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t}\|^2$ and $V:=-\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta[\eta\,\phi(x_t^\mu)^{\top}]/\sqrt{\Delta_t}\in\R^{\dlat\times\pwidth}$. Let $\Umat=\sum_{i}\lambda_i(\Umat)u_iu_i^{\top}$ and $A_\star:=V\Umat^{+}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] (\emph{exact}) Gradient flow on $L_n$ from $A_0=0$ gives $A(T)=A_\star\big(I-e^{-2\Umat T}\big)$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rfnn-excess}
L_n(A(T))-\min_AL_n=\sum_{i:\lambda_i(\Umat)>0}\lambda_i(\Umat)\,e^{-4\lambda_i(\Umat)T}\,\|A_\star u_i\|^2 ,
\end{equation}
so every mode of $\Umat$ has clock $\tau_i=1/(4\lambda_i(\Umat))$ and modes in $\ker\Umat$ never move. For a single fixed noise draw $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$, so at least $\pwidth-\nsamp$ such modes exist (the cliff at index $\nsamp$); for the $\E_\eta$-averaged $\Umat$ the eigenvalues beyond index $\nsamp$ are not exactly zero but $O(10^{-6})$, with clocks far beyond any training horizon. This is Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-gf-closed} with $M_t\to\Umat$ and $M_t^{-1}\to A_\star$.
\item[(b)] (\emph{data-side identification, sketched}) Under the Gaussian-equivalence decomposition $\phi(x)=Gx+a_*(q)z(x)$, $G=a_1(q)W/\sqrt{\pwidth\dlat}$, restricted to the linear part $\phi_{\rm lin}=Gx$ and with $W^{\top}W=\pwidth I$, the $\dlat$ nonzero eigenvalues of $\Umat_{\rm lin}=G\widehat M_tG^{\top}$ are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rfnn-eig-id}
\lambda_i(\Umat)=\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\,\widehat\lambda_i(\widehat M_t),\qquad q=\frac{\operatorname{tr}\widehat M_t}{\dlat}\simeq\beta_t^2+\frac{\dint(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)}{\dlat},
\end{equation}
and the population excess risk carried by these modes is that of the empirical-risk linear model, Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-emp}, with every clock multiplied by $\dlat/a_1(q)^2$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rfnn-risk-id}
\mathcal E^{\rm RF}(T;\dlat)=\mathcal E_n\big(a_1(q)^2T/\dlat\big),
\end{equation}
so that the null clock is the band
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rfnn-null-clock}
\tau_{\mathsf N}^{\rm RF}(\dlat)\in\frac{\dlat}{4\,a_1(q)^2}\Big[\frac1{\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_+},\frac1{\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_-}\Big],\qquad \text{median}\approx\frac{\dlat}{4\,a_1(q)^2\,\beta_t^2}\ (\text{MP median}/\beta_t^2=0.88\text{ at }\dlat=200),
\end{equation}
with $\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_\pm$ from \eqref{eq:lin-mp-edges}. In gradient-flow units of $L_n$ the median clock grows essentially linearly in $\dlat$, partly offset by the de-saturation of $\tanh$ ($q\downarrow$, $a_1(q)^2\uparrow$): at $t=0.01$, $\signoise=0.5$ it is $26.2\to302$ over $\dlat=5\to200$ ($11.5\times$), and its slow edge is a further $4.8\times$ longer at $\dlat=200$. In \emph{training steps} the code's learning rate $0.01\,\dlat/\Delta_t$ removes the factor $\dlat$ (\eqref{eq:lin-steps}): $\tau^{\rm RF}_{\mathsf N}=25/(a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2)$ steps in \texttt{code/v3}, \emph{decreasing} $524\to151$ across the sweep, and $25\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2)=2620\to30\,190$ steps for the saved v2 sweeps. The trace of \eqref{eq:rfnn-eig-id} over the data-side modes is $a_1(q)^2q$, the linear part of $\E\tanh^2(\sqrt q\,z)=a_1(q)^2q+a_*(q)^2$; the remaining mass $a_*(q)^2$ lives on the $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample modes, which are invisible to this corollary.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(a) $L_n$ is a quadratic in $A$ with $\nabla_AL_n=2(A\Umat-V)$, so $\dot A=-2(A\Umat-V)$. The rows of $V$ lie in the row space of $\Umat$: if $u\in\ker\Umat$ then $\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta(\phi(x_t^\mu)^{\top}u)^2=0$, so $\phi(x_t^\mu)^{\top}u=0$ a.s.\ and $Vu=0$. Hence $V=A_\star\Umat$ with $A_\star=V\Umat^{+}$; $E:=A-A_\star$ obeys $\dot E=-2E\Umat$ and $E(T)=-A_\star e^{-2\Umat T}$. Expanding $L_n(A_\star+E)=L_n(A_\star)+\operatorname{tr}(E\Umat E^{\top})$ (the cross term vanishes because $A_\star\Umat=V$) gives \eqref{eq:rfnn-excess}; modes with $\lambda_i(\Umat)=0$ contribute nothing and $A(T)u_i=0$ for them. The rank statement for a single noise draw is $\Umat=\frac1n\Phi^{\top}\Phi$ with $\Phi\in\R^{\nsamp\times\pwidth}$; for the $\E_\eta$ average, the repo's \texttt{compute\_U} at $\dlat=20$, $\pwidth=1280$, $t=0.01$ gives eigenvalues $2.0\times10^{-6}$ and $1.8\times10^{-6}$ at indices $\nsamp-1$ and $\nsamp$: no exact cliff, but clocks of order $10^5$ flow units.

(b) Empirical version, linear part. The empirical fixed point solves $A_\star G\widehat M_tG^{\top}=-G^{\top}$ (the empirical Stein identity $\frac1n\sum_\mu\E_\eta[(\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t})x_t^{\mu\top}]=I$ replaces Lemma~\ref{lem:null-exact-lin}(b)). With $G^{\top}G=cI_{\dlat}$, $c=a_1(q)^2W^{\top}W/(\pwidth\dlat)=a_1(q)^2/\dlat$, right-multiplying by $G$ gives $A_\star G=-\widehat M_t^{-1}$. Also $\Umat_{\rm lin}G=G\widehat M_tG^{\top}G=cG\widehat M_t$, hence $e^{-2\Umat_{\rm lin}T}G=Ge^{-2c\widehat M_tT}$ and the nonzero spectrum of $\Umat_{\rm lin}$ is $c\,\widehat\lambda_i(\widehat M_t)$ with eigenvectors $G\widehat v_i/\|G\widehat v_i\|$, which is \eqref{eq:rfnn-eig-id}. The population excess risk of $s_{A(T)}$ on these modes is $\operatorname{tr}((A(T)G+M_t^{-1})M_t(A(T)G+M_t^{-1})^{\top})$ with $A(T)G+M_t^{-1}=M_t^{-1}-\widehat M_t^{-1}(I-e^{-2c\widehat M_tT})=E(cT)$ in the notation of \eqref{eq:lin-emp-excess}, which is \eqref{eq:rfnn-risk-id}; the band \eqref{eq:rfnn-null-clock} is Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-emp}(c) rescaled. The step conversions follow from Remark~\ref{rem:lin-gd}. Consistency with the measured spectra: at $t=0.01$, $\signoise=0.5$, the idealized $q=\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat$ runs $2.764\to0.327$ and $a_1(q)^2=0.180\to0.625$ over $\dlat=5\to200$, exactly the values reported in Sec.~\ref{sec:rfnn}; the code-normalized noise-dim medians predicted by \eqref{eq:rfnn-eig-id} with the MP median of $\widehat M_{t,\mathsf N}$ are $64\,a_1(q)^2\,\mathrm{med}(\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N})=4.77$ ($\dlat=10$) and $9.33$ ($\dlat=200$) against measured $5.22$ and $9.22$; using the population $\beta_t^2$ instead gives $4.76$ and $10.60$, so the $13\%$ shortfall at $\dlat=200$ is the MP median ($0.233/0.265=0.88$), not a finite-$\pwidth$ effect. What is only sketched: (i) $W^{\top}W=\pwidth I$ holds in expectation with relative fluctuations $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$, which perturb the data-side eigenvalues by the same relative amount (the measured $\pwidth$-independence of the edges is consistent with this); (ii) the residual features $a_*(q)z$ are uncorrelated with $x$ under Gaussian equivalence \citep{pennington2017nonlinear,benigni2021eigenvalue,goldt2022gaussian,hu2023universality,mei2022generalization} but not orthogonal to $Gx$ sample by sample, so the data-side eigenvectors of the full $\Umat$ are those of $\Umat_{\rm lin}$ only to leading order; the data-side eigenvalues of the full $\Umat$ measured with \texttt{compute\_U} ($\dlat=20$, $\pwidth=1280$, $t=0.01$) exceed \eqref{eq:rfnn-eig-id} by $4$--$7\%$ (null block $[3.96,5.43,7.63]\times10^{-3}$ versus $[3.82,5.13,7.10]\times10^{-3}$) and $\operatorname{tr}\Umat=0.357$ versus $a_1^2q+a_*^2=0.372$; this few-percent correction is consistent with row-wise fluctuations of the pre-activation variance $q_j=w_j^{\top}\widehat M_tw_j/\dlat$ (Jensen) and with the non-Gaussian mixture pre-activations $w_j^{\top}m_c/\sqrt{\dlat}$, both decreasing with $\dlat$, and is much larger than the sample-bulk mean $a_*(q)^2/\nsamp\approx4\times10^{-5}$; (iii) Gaussian equivalence itself is proved for Gaussian or subspace data with isotropic-plus-spike covariance; a rigorous version of (b) would follow from a Gaussian-equivalence theorem for the conjugate kernel with anisotropic input covariance \citep{hu2023universality,bonnaire2025} evaluated at the two-atom spectral measure, and for data exactly on a subspace ($\signoise=0$) the corresponding block structure appears in \citet{george2025dsm}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the linear model cannot and can see]\label{rem:lin-nomem}
Trained on the empirical denoising risk, the linear model converges to $-\widehat M_t^{-1}$, the score of the centered Gaussian with the empirical second moment; it never reproduces training points, because it has no sample bulk. Memorization in the NN-ratio sense requires the residual features $a_*(q)z$, whose second moment is the $\nsamp-\dlat$-mode sample bulk of $\Umat$; Corollary~\ref{cor:lin-rfnn}(a) gives those modes the same clock law $1/(4\lambda_i(\Umat))$ but with weights $\lambda_i\|A_\star u_i\|^2$ that are not $1/\lambda_i$, and they are treated separately. What the linear model \emph{does} see is the finite-sample overfitting of the null block (Proposition~\ref{prop:lin-emp}): the null directions are estimated from $\nsamp$ points, the empirical eigenvalues spread over the MP band, and the fitted inverse overshoots on the under-sampled directions with a late-time population penalty $F_t(\dlat/\nsamp)$ that grows with $\dlat$. This is the linear-model form of the empirical observation that memorization returns as $\dlat\to\nsamp$, and it lives entirely in the $\dlat$ data-side modes.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Which block sets the generalization clock]\label{rem:lin-taugen}
Since $\beta_t^2<\lambda^{\mathsf S}_{\min}$ whenever $\signoise<\sigsig$ ($0.265$ versus $1.89$ at $t=0.01$), the slowest \emph{data-side} modes are the null band, not the signal block: the population score error is learned on the clock band $[1/(4\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_+),1/(4\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf N}_-)]$ (linear model) or $\dlat/(4a_1(q)^2)$ times that (RFNN), ordered between the signal clocks and the sample-bulk onset. If $\taugen$ is defined from the signal edge, the ordering $\taugen<\tau_{\mathsf N}<\taumem$ holds; if it is defined from the score error, it is the slow edge of $\tau_{\mathsf N}$ itself. Like with like, at $t=0.01$: the factor $\dlat/a_1(q)^2$ common to all data-side clocks grows $11.5\times$ over $\dlat=5\to200$ in flow units, while the measured signal-edge $\taugen$ of Sec.~\ref{sec:rfnn} grows $8.1\times$ with the actual (non-isotropic, empirical) signal spectrum; the two are the same quantity only up to the spread of $\widehat\lambda^{\mathsf S}$ and the step conversion \eqref{eq:lin-steps}, which must be applied before any absolute comparison of step counts.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{What this predicts for the corrected synthetic score error.} Because the previously reported synthetic score-error curves were computed with a transposed whitening matrix, no saved value constrains this appendix. For the rerun, the linear model predicts: (P1) the zero-initialization values listed above, at $t=0.1$ for a zero-readout MLP and at $t=0.01$ for the RFNN; (P2) $\dlat\cdot\mathrm{err}$ affine in $\dlat$ with the single slope $b(T)$ only where the null band is narrow, i.e.\ $\dlat\le40$ at $\signoise=0.5$ and the whole sweep at $\signoise=0.01$, with the slope decaying per step at the rates of \eqref{eq:lin-steps}; (P3) $\mathrm{err}$ increasing in $\dlat$ at early steps and U-shaped at late steps with the floors of Table~\ref{tab:lin-band}, never n-shaped, and non-monotone in steps at $\dlat\ge100$ (early-stopping minimum $\approx0.36$ per dimension at $\dlat=200$, $t=0.01$, versus a floor of $4.8$); (P4) at $\signoise=0.01$ the null block is $2.1\times$ ($t=0.1$) or $13\times$ ($t=0.01$) larger and slower than at $\signoise=0.5$, the slope ratio is $b_{0.01}/b_{0.5}=(\beta^2_{0.5}/\beta^2_{0.01})e^{+4(\beta^2_{0.5}-\beta^2_{0.01})T}$, and there is no finite-$n$ floor, so the late $\dlat$-dependence has opposite sign in the two sweeps.
```



---

# Piece 5: Bridge lemma: from residual sample-bulk error to near-duplicate generation (memorization fraction)

## Statements

### `def:bridge-objects` (definition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Training set fixed; sampler is the reverse VP-SDE run from t_max to t_min>0 (Euler--Maruyama, no terminal denoising step, as in the released code).

```latex
\begin{definition}[Empirical score, residual error, NN-ratio]\label{def:bridge-objects}
Let $\{x^\mu\}_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\subset\R^{\dlat}$ be the training set and $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$. The \emph{exact empirical score} is the score of the noised empirical measure $\rho^{\rm emp}_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathcal N(e^{-t}x^\mu,\Delta_t I)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:semp}
 s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp} w_\mu(x,t)\,\frac{e^{-t}x^\mu-x}{\Delta_t},\qquad w_\mu(x,t)=\frac{\exp\!\big(-\|x-e^{-t}x^\mu\|^2/2\Delta_t\big)}{\sum_\nu \exp\!\big(-\|x-e^{-t}x^\nu\|^2/2\Delta_t\big)} .
\end{equation}
For a score model $s_T$ at training time $T$ we write $\zeta_T(x,t):=s_T(x,t)-s_{\rm emp}(x,t)$ and define the \emph{residual error at diffusion time $t$}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ET}
 \mathcal E_t(T):=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu}\E_{\eta}\big\|\zeta_T(e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta,\,t)\big\|^2,\qquad \eta\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat}),
\end{equation}
together with its per-sample version $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)$ (drop the average over $\mu$). For the RFNN of Section~\ref{sec:rfnn} at its training time $t$, $\mathcal E_t(T)=\sum_i\lambda_i\|a_i(0)-a_i^\star\|^2e^{-2\lambda_iT}+\mathcal E_t(\infty)$, where $\mathcal E_t(\infty)$ is the projection error of $s_{\rm emp}$ onto the span of the $\pwidth$ features; we split $\mathcal E_t=\mathcal E^{\rm sig}_t+\mathcal E^{\rm null}_t+\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t+\mathcal E_t(\infty)$ by bulk. Let $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)=\min_{\nu\neq\mu}\|x^\mu-x^\nu\|$, let $G_{\rm NN}$ be its empirical CDF over $\mu$, and let $d_{\rm NN}^{(r)}$ be its $r$-quantile. For a generated point $\hat x$ with nearest and second-nearest training points $\mathrm{NN}_1,\mathrm{NN}_2$, the \citet{somepalli2023diffusion} statistic is $\varrho(\hat x)=\|\hat x-\mathrm{NN}_1\|/\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|$, and $\hat x$ is counted as memorized iff $\varrho(\hat x)<1/3$; the reported memorization fraction is $F_{\rm mem}(T)=\Pr[\varrho(\hat x)<1/3]$ over the sampler's randomness.
\end{definition}
```

### `lem:condensation` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Gaussian forward noise; no assumption on the training set beyond d_NN(mu)>0.

```latex
\begin{lemma}[Condensation of the empirical softmax; \citealp{biroli2024dynamical}]\label{lem:condensation}
Fix $t$, a training index $\mu$, and $\delta\in(0,1)$. Let $x=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\eta$ with $\eta\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat})$. If
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cond-rigorous}
 e^{2t}\Delta_t\;\le\;\frac{d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2}{32\,\log(\nsamp/\delta)},
\end{equation}
then with probability at least $1-\delta$ over $\eta$, $\;1-w_\mu(x,t)\le \delta^{8}\nsamp^{-7}$, and on that event $s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=(e^{-t}x^\mu-x)/\Delta_t+r$ with $\|r\|\le \delta^{8}\nsamp^{-7}\max_\nu\|e^{-t}(x^\mu-x^\nu)\|/\Delta_t$. Evaluating the log-weight ratio at $\eta=0$ instead of with high probability gives the sufficient condition $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$, which for $t\ll1$ is the condensation threshold $\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log \nsamp)$ of \citet{biroli2024dynamical}. Since $e^{2t}\Delta_t=e^{2t}-1$ is increasing in $t$, condensation at $t_c$ implies condensation at every $t\le t_c$.
\end{lemma}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
For $\nu\neq\mu$ write $D_\nu=\|x^\mu-x^\nu\|\ge d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$. From \eqref{eq:semp}, $\log\frac{w_\nu}{w_\mu}=-\frac{\|x-e^{-t}x^\nu\|^2-\|x-e^{-t}x^\mu\|^2}{2\Delta_t}$. Substituting $x-e^{-t}x^\nu=e^{-t}(x^\mu-x^\nu)+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$ and $x-e^{-t}x^\mu=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$ gives
\[\log\frac{w_\nu}{w_\mu}=-\frac{e^{-2t}D_\nu^2}{2\Delta_t}-\frac{e^{-t}\langle\eta,x^\mu-x^\nu\rangle}{\sqrt{\Delta_t}} .\]
The second term is Gaussian with standard deviation $e^{-t}D_\nu/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$; by a union bound over the $\nsamp-1$ indices, with probability $\ge1-\delta$ simultaneously $|\langle\eta,x^\mu-x^\nu\rangle|\le D_\nu\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}$ for all $\nu$. On this event
\[\log\frac{w_\nu}{w_\mu}\le-\frac{e^{-t}D_\nu}{2\sqrt{\Delta_t}}\Big(\frac{e^{-t}D_\nu}{\sqrt{\Delta_t}}-2\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}\Big).\]
Condition \eqref{eq:cond-rigorous} says $e^{-t}d_{\rm NN}/\sqrt{\Delta_t}\ge4\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}$, so the bracket is at least $\tfrac12e^{-t}D_\nu/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$ and $\log(w_\nu/w_\mu)\le-e^{-2t}D_\nu^2/(4\Delta_t)\le-8\log(\nsamp/\delta)$. Summing, $\sum_{\nu\ne\mu}w_\nu/w_\mu\le\nsamp(\delta/\nsamp)^8$, hence $1-w_\mu\le\delta^8\nsamp^{-7}$. The bound on $r=\sum_{\nu\neq\mu}w_\nu\,(e^{-t}(x^\nu-x^\mu))/\Delta_t$ follows from $\sum_{\nu\ne\mu}w_\nu=1-w_\mu$. At $\eta=0$ the cross term vanishes and $\sum_{\nu\neq\mu}w_\nu/w_\mu\le\nsamp e^{-e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/2\Delta_t}<1$ iff $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)$. Monotonicity of $e^{2t}-1$ is immediate.
```

### `lem:endpoint` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Condensation at t_c for the point mu; residual r of Lemma condensation neglected; sampler stops at t_min>0 without a terminal denoising step.

```latex
\begin{lemma}[Endpoint law of the exact empirical sampler after condensation]\label{lem:endpoint}
Suppose condensation \eqref{eq:cond-rigorous} holds for $\mu$ at $t=t_c$ (hence on $[t_{\min},t_c]$), and that the reverse VP-SDE $dx=(x+2s_{\rm emp})\,d\tau+\sqrt2\,dW$ ($\tau=-t$) is started at $x_{t_c}\sim\mathcal N(e^{-t_c}x^\mu,\Delta_{t_c}I)$ and run with the residual $r$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:condensation} neglected. Then $x_{t_{\min}}\sim\mathcal N(e^{-t_{\min}}x^\mu,\Delta_{t_{\min}}I)$. For the probability-flow ODE with the same score, the offset $y_t=x_t-e^{-t}x^\mu$ satisfies $y_{t_{\min}}=\sqrt{\Delta_{t_{\min}}/\Delta_{t_c}}\,y_{t_c}$. In either case the endpoint is $x^\mu$ up to a displacement whose per-coordinate variance is at most $\Delta_{t_{\min}}$ (plus the $O(t_{\min}\|x^\mu\|)$ mean shrinkage). Consequently, for the exact empirical score, $\varrho(x_{t_{\min}})\approx\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}/d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ and the reported memorization fraction cannot exceed the \emph{metric ceiling}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ceiling}
 F^{\max}_{\rm mem}=1-G_{\rm NN}\big(3\sqrt{\dlat\,\Delta_{t_{\min}}}\big)\qquad(\text{SDE sampler stopped at }t_{\min},\ \dlat\gg1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Under condensation $s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=-(x-e^{-t}x^\mu)/\Delta_t$ is the score of $\mathcal N(e^{-t}x^\mu,\Delta_tI)$, which is exactly the time-$t$ marginal of the forward VP process started at $\delta_{x^\mu}$. The reverse SDE with the exact score of a forward process transports its time-$t_c$ marginal to its time-$t_{\min}$ marginal \citep{song2021scoresde}, giving the first claim. For the ODE, $\frac{d x}{d\tau}=x+s_{\rm emp}$ with $\frac{d}{d\tau}(e^{-t}x^\mu)=e^{-t}x^\mu$ gives $\frac{dy}{d\tau}=y-\frac{y}{\Delta_t}=-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\Delta_t}y$, and $\int_{t_{\min}}^{t_c}\frac{e^{-2t}}{1-e^{-2t}}dt=\tfrac12\log(\Delta_{t_c}/\Delta_{t_{\min}})$, so $\|y_{t_{\min}}\|=\sqrt{\Delta_{t_{\min}}/\Delta_{t_c}}\|y_{t_c}\|$. For the ceiling: $\|x_{t_{\min}}-x^\mu\|^2=\Delta_{t_{\min}}\|\eta\|^2+O(t_{\min}\|x^\mu\|)$ concentrates at $\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$; when this is below $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/9$ we have $\mathrm{NN}_1=x^\mu$ and $\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|=d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$, so $\varrho<1/3$ iff $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)>3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}$; averaging the indicator over $\mu$ (the condensed sampler lands on each training point with the weight $w_\mu(x_{t_c})$, which is $1/\nsamp$ on average) gives \eqref{eq:ceiling}. Euler--Maruyama adds a discretization variance of order $dt$ per coordinate, which only lowers the ceiling.
```

### `prop:bridge` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: (B1) condensation of s_emp at the attachment time for the relevant training points; (B2) residual isotropic Gaussian with variance E_{t,mu}(T)/d_lat, independent of the forward noise; (B3) endpoint = Tweedie denoiser of the learned score at the attachment time + terminal sampler noise (one-attachment reduction); d_lat large enough for chi-square concentration; ||delta|| < d_NN/2 so that NN_1 is the attachment point

```latex
\begin{proposition}[Bridge: residual error acts as an effective terminal noise level]\label{prop:bridge}
Fix a diffusion time $t\in[t_{\min},t_c]$ at which the sampler makes its final attachment to a training point, and a training time $T$. Assume
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(B1)] (\emph{condensation}) $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$ for the training points $\mu$ under consideration;
\item[(B2)] (\emph{isotropic residual}) conditionally on $x_t=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$, the residual $\zeta_T(x_t,t)$ is approximately $\mathcal N\big(0,(\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)/\dlat)\,I_{\dlat}\big)$ and independent of $\eta$;
\item[(B3)] (\emph{one-attachment reduction}) the endpoint of the learned-score sampler equals the Tweedie denoiser of the learned score applied at the attachment time $t$, plus the sampler's own terminal noise of Lemma~\ref{lem:endpoint}.
\end{enumerate}
Then the generated point is $\hat x=x^\mu+\delta$ with $\delta\sim\mathcal N(0,\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)\,I_{\dlat})$, where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Deff}
 \Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)=\Delta_{t_{\min}}+c_t\,\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T),\qquad c_t=\frac{e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}}{\dlat},
\end{equation}
so the endpoint has the law of the \emph{exact} empirical sampler stopped at the noise level $\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:endpoint}). Since $\|\delta\|^2=\dlat\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$, the sample attached to $\mu$ is counted as memorized iff
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mem-criterion}
 \dlat\,\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)<\tfrac19\,d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)<\frac{d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{9\,e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}} ,
\end{equation}
and the memorization fraction is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Fmem}
 F_{\rm mem}(T)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\mathbf 1\Big[\,d_{\rm NN}(\mu)>3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}\,\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)}\,\Big]\;\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal E_{t,\mu}\equiv\mathcal E_t\ }\;1-G_{\rm NN}\Big(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}\,\mathcal E_t(T)}\Big).
\end{equation}
A necessary companion condition is condensation at the effective level, $e^{2t}\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$; it is implied by \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} whenever $9\dlat>2e^{2t}\log\nsamp$, i.e.\ for every $\dlat\ge2$ in our sweeps ($\nsamp=500$, $t\le0.1$). The metric threshold, not condensation, is therefore the binding one.
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
\emph{Sketch.} By (B1) and Lemma~\ref{lem:condensation}, $s_{\rm emp}(x_t,t)=-(x_t-e^{-t}x^\mu)/\Delta_t$ up to the negligible residual $r$. Tweedie's identity for the learned score gives the denoiser output $\hat x=e^{t}\big(x_t+\Delta_t s_T(x_t,t)\big)=e^{t}\big(x_t+\Delta_ts_{\rm emp}(x_t,t)\big)+e^{t}\Delta_t\zeta_T(x_t,t)=x^\mu+e^{t}\Delta_t\,\zeta_T(x_t,t)$. By (B2) the displacement $e^{t}\Delta_t\zeta_T$ is $\mathcal N(0,e^{2t}\Delta_t^2\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)/\dlat\,I)$; by (B3) it adds independently to the sampler's terminal Gaussian of Lemma~\ref{lem:endpoint}, giving \eqref{eq:Deff}. Two independent isotropic Gaussians centred at $x^\mu$ sum to one with variance $\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}$, which is the endpoint law of the exact empirical sampler stopped at that noise level---this is the precise sense of ``behaves like $s_{\rm emp}$ at $\Delta_{\rm eff}$''. Concentration of $\|\delta\|^2$ (a scaled $\chi^2_{\dlat}$) and the identification $\mathrm{NN}_1=x^\mu$, $\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|=d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ valid when $\|\delta\|<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)/3$ (indeed $<d_{\rm NN}/2$ suffices) give \eqref{eq:mem-criterion}; averaging over the attachment index $\mu$, which the condensed sampler visits with frequency $1/\nsamp$ on average, gives \eqref{eq:Fmem}. For the comparison of thresholds: \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} reads $\Delta_{\rm eff}<d_{\rm NN}^2/(9\dlat)$, condensation reads $\Delta_{\rm eff}<e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)$; the former implies the latter iff $9\dlat>2e^{2t}\log\nsamp$.

\emph{When (B2) is justified.} At $T\gg\taugen$ the signal and noise-dim modes are absorbed and $\zeta_T$ is carried by the sample bulk, $\zeta_T(x)=-(A^\star-A(T))\phi(x)$ restricted to sample modes. The sample-bulk eigen-directions are the feature-space images of the per-sample deviations $\xi^\mu$ after the linear (population) part is removed; their output directions in $\R^{\dlat}$ are, after the random rotation $Q$, exchangeable across coordinates, and the residual at a given $x_t$ is a sum over the $N_{\rm res}(T)=\#\{i\in\text{samp}:\lambda_iT\lesssim1\}$ unabsorbed modes. When $N_{\rm res}(T)\gg1$ and $\dlat\gg1$ a CLT over these generic directions makes $\zeta_T$ approximately isotropic Gaussian with the stated variance. The approximation fails (i) early, when the residual is dominated by the $\dint$ signal modes (it is then rank-$\dint$ and not isotropic; but then $\mathcal E_t$ is far above the threshold in \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} and $F_{\rm mem}=0$ regardless), and (ii) at the very end, when only a few sample modes remain: the residual is then low-rank and localized on a few training points, and \eqref{eq:Fmem} should be read with the per-sample $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}$. It also requires $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2$ not to be negligible against $\dint\sigsig^2$, otherwise an $O(1)$ fraction of $\|\zeta_T\|^2$ is carried by $\dint$ coordinates and the $\chi^2_{\dlat}$ concentration is replaced by a $\chi^2_{\dint}$ one (wider, but the threshold \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} is unchanged in mean).
```

### `cor:taumem-fraction` (corollary, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Proposition bridge hypotheses; residual mu-independent and monotone in T; q below the metric ceiling.

```latex
\begin{corollary}[Fraction curve and quantile-indexed memorization time]\label{cor:taumem-fraction}
Under Proposition~\ref{prop:bridge} with a $\mu$-independent residual $\mathcal E_t(T)$ decreasing in $T$, the memorization fraction first reaches level $q\in(0,F^{\max}_{\rm mem})$ at
\begin{equation}\label{eq:taumem-q}
 \taumem(q)=\inf\Big\{T:\ \mathcal E_t(T)\le \frac{\big(d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q)}\big)^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{9\,e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}}\Big\},
\end{equation}
where $d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q)}$ is the $(1-q)$-quantile of the training-set NN distance: the first-memorized training points are the most isolated ones. The condensation-form threshold, $\taumem^{\rm cond}=\inf\{T:\mathcal E_t(T)\le[e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)-\Delta_{t_{\min}}]/c_t\}$, is weaker for all $\dlat\ge2$ and is not what the fraction curve reports. If the sample-bulk residual is a single exponential, $\mathcal E_t(T)=\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t(0)e^{-2\lambda_{\rm samp}T}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:taumem-spread}
 \taumem(q_2)-\taumem(q_1)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\rm samp}}\log\frac{d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q_1)}}{d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q_2)}}+O\!\Big(\frac{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{\lambda_{\rm samp}(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2}\Big),
\end{equation}
so the width of the fraction curve in $\log T$ is set by the spread of $d_{\rm NN}$ (data level) and, through the within-bulk spread of $\lambda_i$ that replaces the single $\lambda_{\rm samp}$, by the sample bulk's width (model level). A single sample-bulk edge with a degenerate $G_{\rm NN}$ would give a step function; both spreads are needed to produce the measured smooth curves.
\end{corollary}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Immediate from \eqref{eq:Fmem}: $1-G_{\rm NN}(u)\ge q$ iff $u\le d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q)}$; substitute $u=3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^2\mathcal E_t(T)}$ and solve for $T$ using monotonicity. For \eqref{eq:taumem-spread} take logarithms of $3e^{t}\Delta_t\sqrt{\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t(0)}\,e^{-\lambda_{\rm samp}T}=\sqrt{(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}$ and expand the square root in $9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}/(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2$.
```

### `cor:dnn-scaling` (corollary, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Synthetic Gaussian-mixture data of the paper; n/k points per cluster large; d_lat - d_int large for the null-part concentration.

```latex
\begin{corollary}[What $\taumem$ depends on through $d_{\rm NN}$]\label{cor:dnn-scaling}
For the synthetic data of Section~\ref{sec:design} ($k$ clusters of $\nsamp/k$ points each, within-cluster spread $\sigsig$ in a $\dint$-dimensional subspace, null spread $\signoise$ in the remaining $\dlat-\dint$ coordinates),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dnn-scaling}
 d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\;=\;\underbrace{C_{\dint}\,\sigsig^2\,(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}\,\kappa_\mu}_{\text{signal part}}\;+\;\underbrace{2\signoise^2(\dlat-\dint)\big(1+O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/(\dlat-\dint)})\big)}_{\text{null part}},
\end{equation}
where $C_{\dint}$ depends only on $\dint$ and $\kappa_\mu$ is an $O(1)$ random factor with a fixed law (density-dependent). Hence: (i) in the regime $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2\ll\sigsig^2(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}$ (e.g.\ $\signoise=0.01$), the threshold in \eqref{eq:taumem-q} scales as $\nsamp^{-2/\dint}$ and is \emph{independent of $\dlat$}: all $\dlat$-dependence of $\taumem$ then enters through $\mathcal E_t(T)$, i.e.\ through the sample-bulk eigenvalues, and $\partial\log\taumem/\partial\log\nsamp$ acquires the additive term $-\frac{2}{\dint}\cdot\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\rm samp}\taumem}$ on top of the spectral $\nsamp$-dependence---a $\dint$-, not $\dlat$-, exponent; (ii) in the regime $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2\gg\sigsig^2(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}$ (e.g.\ $\signoise=0.5$, $\dlat\gtrsim20$), $d_{\rm NN}^2\simeq2\signoise^2\dlat$, the criterion \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} becomes the per-coordinate condition $\Delta_{\rm eff}<2\signoise^2/9$, the spread $d^{(0.9)}_{\rm NN}/d^{(0.1)}_{\rm NN}\to1$ (distance concentration), and the same metric that reports the fraction also drives the mean NN-ratio of non-memorized samples toward $1$; (iii) the metric ceiling \eqref{eq:ceiling} is $\dlat$-dependent in opposite directions in the two regimes: it collapses like $1-G_{\rm NN}(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}})$ with $G_{\rm NN}$ fixed in regime (i), and tends to $1$ in regime (ii).
\end{corollary}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Within a cluster the signal coordinates of the $\nsamp/k$ points are i.i.d.\ $\mathcal N(0,\sigsig^2I_{\dint})$; the nearest-neighbour distance of $m$ i.i.d.\ points from a smooth density in $\dint$ dimensions scales as $m^{-1/\dint}$ with a limiting law for the rescaled distance (Poissonization of the $m$-point process; see e.g.\ the two-NN estimator literature, \citealp{facco2017twonn,levina2004maximum}), which gives the first term with $\kappa_\mu$ distributed as the rescaled NN distance at the local density. The null coordinates of any two distinct points are independent $\mathcal N(0,\signoise^2I_{\dlat-\dint})$, so their squared distance is $2\signoise^2\chi^2_{\dlat-\dint}$, and the minimum over $\nsamp-1$ such variables is $2\signoise^2(\dlat-\dint)(1-O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/(\dlat-\dint)}))$ by Gaussian concentration and a union bound; the two parts add because signal and null coordinates are independent (up to the rotation $Q$, which preserves norms). The three consequences follow by inserting \eqref{eq:dnn-scaling} into \eqref{eq:taumem-q}, \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} and \eqref{eq:ceiling}.
```

### `rem:pixel` (remark, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: Decoder Lipschitz and co-Lipschitz on the training latents; NN-ratio computed in pixel space after decoding.

```latex
\begin{remark}[Real data: latent versus pixel space]\label{rem:pixel}
For VAE latents the sampler runs in $\R^{\dlat}$ but $\varrho$ is computed in pixel space after decoding with $D:\R^{\dlat}\to\R^{P}$ (Section~\ref{sec:real-data}). If $D$ is $L$-Lipschitz on the relevant region and co-Lipschitz on the training latents, $\ell\,\|z-z'\|\le\|D(z)-D(z')\|\le L\|z-z'\|$, then $\varrho_{\rm pix}\le(L/\ell)\,\varrho_{\rm lat}$ and \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} holds in pixel space with $d_{\rm NN}$ replaced by $d^{\rm pix}_{\rm NN}/L$ in the numerator and $\ell$-rescaled spacings in the denominator, i.e.\ the effective threshold is $\tfrac13(\ell/L)$ rather than $\tfrac13$. Two consequences: (a) the pixel-space fraction is a \emph{lower} bound on the latent-space fraction whenever $L/\ell>1$, and $L/\ell$ is not controlled across the $\dlat$ sweep (a different VAE per $\dlat$); (b) posterior-collapsed coordinates (F9: $\lambda_{\min}=\Delta_t$ exactly for $\dlat\ge140$ on CelebA) contribute to $\dlat\Delta_{\rm eff}$ in latent space but are annihilated by $D$, so in regime (ii) of Corollary~\ref{cor:dnn-scaling} the relevant dimension is the decoder-visible one, closer to the effective rank $\approx0.5\dlat$ than to $\dlat$. We do not attempt to estimate $L/\ell$ here.
\end{remark}
```

## Predictions

- **Zero-parameter fraction prediction (synthetic): F_mem(T) = 1 - G_NN(3 sqrt(d_lat Delta_{t_min} + e^{2t}Delta_t^2 E_t(T))) with E_t(T) measured against the closed-form empirical score s_emp at t = t_min on noised training points, and G_NN computed from the training set.**  
  test: For each d_lat in the 5M-step sweep, at every eval checkpoint compute E_{t_min}(T) = mean_mu E_eta ||s_theta(e^{-t}x^mu + sqrt(Delta_t)eta, t) - s_emp(.)||^2 (closed form, eq. semp), compute G_NN from the training set, plot the predicted curve against memorization_fraction(T). A per-sample refinement uses E_{t,mu}(T) and the indicator form of eq. Fmem. Success = curves for different d_lat collapse without fitting; failure mode = systematic overprediction (would indicate (B2)/(B3) fail and the blend-model correction is needed).  
  support: Not yet run. Indirect support: all measured final fractions (0.29 at d=5 down to 0.0018 at d=40, seed 42) lie below the predicted metric ceilings (0.676 at d=5, 0.79 at d=8, 0.87 at d=10, 0.98 at d=20, ~1 at d>=25).

- **Metric ceiling at sigma_perp = 0.01: the reported fraction must vanish for d_lat >= 20 regardless of training time, because the sampler's terminal noise sqrt(d_lat Delta_{t_min}) exceeds a third of the NN spacing (ceiling = 0.668, 0.364, 0.250, 0.165, 0.100, 0.034, 0.015, 0.008, 0.002 at d_lat = 5, 8, 10, 12, 15, 20, 25, 30, 40).**  
  test: (a) Train the sigma_perp = 0.01 sweep to 5M steps with hidden 256 and compare peak fractions to the ceilings; (b) sharper: add a final Tweedie denoising step (or lower t_min to 1e-3) to the sampler, which removes the terminal noise; the theory predicts the sigma_perp = 0.01 fractions then jump for d_lat in 8..30 while the sigma_perp = 0.5 fractions barely change.  
  support: Old exp2_mlp sigma_perp=0.01 sweep (300k steps, hidden 8 d_lat): memorization fraction 0.0062 (d=5), 0.0010 (8), 0.0014 (10), 0.0002 (15), 0.0000 for 20 <= d <= 200 (except the unexplained d=150/200 reappearance in the sigma_perp=0.5 run), consistent with but not a decisive test of the ceiling because those runs are undertrained.

- **d_int-not-d_lat scaling of the data-side threshold: the NN distance obeys d_NN * n^{1/d_int} = const in the signal-dominated regime, so at sigma_perp -> 0 the threshold in eq. taumem-q scales as n^{-2/d_int} and is d_lat-independent; a sweep in n at fixed d_lat, d_int should shift log tau_mem(q) by (2/d_int)(log n)/(2 lambda_samp) beyond the spectral n-dependence, and a sweep in d_int at fixed n should change the exponent.**  
  test: Simulate training sets with n in {100, 500, 1000, 5000} at d_int in {3, 5, 8} and sigma_perp = 0.01; verify d_NN median * n^{1/d_int} is flat; then run the MLP at these n and check that the ordering of tau_mem(1%) across n has the extra n^{-2/d_int} term after dividing out lambda_samp measured from the RFNN spectrum.  
  support: Simulation in this piece: median d_NN * n^{1/5} = 3.66, 3.72, 3.77, 3.76 for n = 100, 500, 1000, 5000 (flat within 3%).

- **Fraction-curve width shrinks with d_lat at sigma_perp = 0.5 (data-level contribution): the spread d_NN^{(0.9)}/d_NN^{(0.1)} falls 2.28 (d=5) -> 1.72 (10) -> 1.41 (20) -> 1.25 (40) -> 1.09 (200), so by eq. taumem-spread the log-T width of the fraction curve, in units of 1/lambda_samp, should fall from log 2.28 = 0.82 toward log 1.09 = 0.09; at sigma_perp = 0.01 it stays at log 2.3.**  
  test: Extract tau_mem(q) for q in {1%, 2%, 5%} from the 5M-step sweep, normalize by the RFNN sample-bulk 1/lambda (median) for the same d_lat, and compare the residual spread to log(d_NN^{(0.99)}/d_NN^{(0.95)}); the excess is attributable to the within-bulk eigenvalue spread.  
  support: Measured crossing-time ratios T(5%)/T(1%) = 2.33, 2.2, 2.33, 2.5, 3.5 for d_lat = 5, 8, 10, 12, 15 (seed 42) -- grows with d_lat, which the data-level term alone does not produce; this indicates the model-level (eigenvalue-spread) term dominates the width and grows with d_lat, consistent with F5's growing tau_mem(onset)/tau_gen.

- **The exact empirical score condenses earlier in the reverse process at larger d_lat when sigma_perp = 0.5 (t_c = 0.046, 0.105, 0.21, 0.39, 0.72, 1.02 for d_lat = 5, 10, 20, 40, 100, 200) and at a d_lat-independent t_c = 0.045 when sigma_perp = 0.01; therefore the observed delay of memorization with d_lat is not a property of the empirical measure and must come from the learned score's residual E(T), i.e. from the sample-bulk spectrum.**  
  test: Run the closed-form s_emp sampler (no learning) for each d_lat and record the fraction: the theory predicts fraction = metric ceiling at every d_lat (i.e. ~1 at sigma_perp = 0.5, d_lat >= 25), the opposite of the learned-score trend.  
  support: Lemma condensation applied to simulated training sets (numbers above); no sampler run yet.

## Empirical anchor
Anchors computed from the repo's own data-generating process (n=500, k=10, d_int=5, s=3, sigma_sig=1; 5 replicates) and from multiseed_runs/exp2_mlp_dlat_sn05_5m_bonnaire_hidden256 (seed 42, 5M steps, hidden 256, t_min=0.01, 500 E-M steps, no terminal denoising):
(1) Metric ceiling 1-G_NN(3 sqrt(d_lat Delta_{t_min})), Delta_{0.01}=0.0198: sigma_perp=0.5: 0.676 (d=5), 0.790 (8), 0.870 (10), 0.924 (12), 0.950 (15), 0.979 (20), 0.998 (25), ~1 (>=30). Measured final fractions: 0.290, 0.224, 0.168, 0.127, 0.079, 0.044, 0.017, 0.006, 0.0038, 0.0018 (d=5..40) -- all below the ceiling, as required; at d=5 the run is within a factor 2.3 of the ceiling. sigma_perp=0.01 ceilings: 0.668, 0.364, 0.250, 0.165, 0.100, 0.034, 0.015, 0.008, 0.002 (d=5..40); old sigma_perp=0.01 runs show 0.006, 0.001, 0.0014, 0.0002, then exactly 0 for d>=20.
(2) d_NN quantiles (q10/q50/q90) sigma_perp=0.5: 0.72/1.10/1.63 (d=5), 1.28/1.71/2.21 (10), 2.13/2.56/3.00 (20), 3.41/3.81/4.25 (40), 8.75/9.16/9.56 (200); sigma_perp=0.01: 0.70/1.07/1.60 flat in d_lat. Spread q90/q10: 2.28 -> 1.09 (0.5), 2.3 flat (0.01).
(3) n-scaling: median d_NN * n^{1/5} = 3.66, 3.72, 3.77, 3.76 for n=100, 500, 1000, 5000.
(4) Condensation times of the exact empirical score (Lemma, eta=0 form): t_c = 0.046 -> 1.02 over d_lat 5->200 at sigma_perp=0.5; 0.045 flat at 0.01.
(5) mean_nn_ratio of generated samples 0.524 (d=5) -> 0.868 (d=40) matches the training-set self-ratio d_NN1/d_NN2 trend 0.83 -> 0.96 in direction (distance concentration, regime (ii)).
(6) Crossing times T(1%)/T(5%)/T(10%) in steps: 150k/350k/550k (d=5), 250k/550k/1.0M (8), 300k/700k/1.7M (10), 400k/1.0M/2.65M (12), 600k/2.1M/-- (15), 1.05M/--/-- (20), 2.55M (25).

## Open gaps
What is missing for a full proof, in decreasing order of importance.
(G1) Additive-isotropic residual vs. structured residual. The gradient-flow residual is s_T - s_emp = -(1-m(T))(s_emp - s_pop) + (absorbed-bulk remainders), i.e. a *blend* of the empirical and population denoisers, hat x_T = (1-m) hat x_pop + m hat x_emp, not an isotropic jitter. A one-step evaluation of the blend at the condensation time gives ||hat x_pop - x^mu||^2 ~ d_lat g_c sigma^2/(sigma^2 + g_c) with g_c = e^{2t_c}-1 = d_NN^2/(2 log n), and the memorization criterion (1-m)^2 < (2 c_d/9)(1 + log n/(c_d d_lat)) with c_d = d_NN^2/(2 d sigma^2); this yields an O(1) absorption threshold m_* in (0.4, 0.6) that is only weakly d_lat-dependent, so the two models agree on the qualitative conclusion (tau_mem is set by the sample-bulk eigenvalues) but disagree on the constant c_t and on the sqrt(E) vs E functional form. Deciding between them requires the zero-parameter test in the predictions list. (B2) is stated with its justification and failure windows, but not proved.
(G2) One-attachment reduction (B3). The real sampler integrates the score over [t_min, t_c]; the offset ODE dy/dtau = -(e^{-2t}/Delta_t) y + zeta_t gives y(t_min) = int_{t_min}^{t_c} sqrt(Delta_{t_min}/Delta_s) zeta_s ds, so c_t is only known up to how zeta_s scales with s and its correlation along the trajectory. With the natural scaling E_s propto 1/Delta_s and a persistent (bias-like) error, Delta_eff = Delta_{t_min}[1 + r^2 (1/4) log^2(t_c/t_min)] where r^2 = E_s Delta_s/d_lat is the relative error; with decorrelated error the log^2 becomes a log. Both forms keep Delta_eff = Delta_{t_min} + c E with a different c; the proposition fixes c by the Tweedie step at a single attachment time t and should be read as accurate to a logarithmic factor in t_c/t_min (about 1.5-4.6 across the sweep).
(G3) Which t. The RFNN spectrum is at t=0.01=t_min, but the memorization decision is made near t_c (0.05 at d=5, up to 1.0 at d=200 for sigma_perp=0.5). The proposition uses E_t at one t; relating E_{t_c}(T) to the fixed-t RFNN spectrum is not done here (the MLP is trained over all t).
(G4) Identification of NN_1 with the attachment point: exact only when ||delta|| < d_NN/2; for the non-memorized tail the NN-ratio is not modelled (only its concentration toward 1 in regime (ii) is noted).
(G5) Rank-null floor E_t(infinity): s_emp is generically not in the span of the p random features, so E_t(T) -> E_t(infinity) > 0. If E_t(infinity) exceeds the threshold in eq. mem-criterion for some mu, those points are never memorized at finite p; at p = 64 d_lat this floor is small but not computed. This is also the natural place where the empirical 'memorization returns as d_lat -> n' effect (F13) must enter, since the sample bulk n - d_lat empties; the bridge does not explain that regime.
(G6) Per-sample residual E_{t,mu}(T): eq. Fmem in indicator form is stated but the map from sample-bulk eigen-modes to per-training-point residuals is not derived; the isolated points (large d_NN) are also the ones whose feature directions are least supported and presumably carry the smallest lambda_i, which would partially cancel the data-level ordering 'most isolated memorized first'. This cancellation is a concrete, testable ambiguity.
(G7) Real data: L/ell of the decoder is unknown and uncontrolled across the VAE sweep; the pixel-space fraction is a lower bound on the latent one only under the stated Lipschitz hypotheses.
(G8) Lemma condensation gives the constant 32 log(n/delta) rigorously versus Biroli's 2 log n heuristic; the numerical t_c values quoted use the 2 log n form.
