## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{From residual error to near-duplicate generation}\label{sec:theory-bridge}
The spectral clocks above fix \emph{when} the sample bulk is absorbed; they say nothing about \emph{why} absorbing it produces near-duplicates. The bridge is the closed-form score of the noised empirical measure \citep{scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization}, $s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=\sum_\mu w_\mu(x,t)(e^{-t}x^\mu-x)/\Delta_t$ with softmax weights $w_\mu$ (Definition~\ref{def:bridge-objects}). Write $\mathcal E_t(T)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E\|s_T-s_{\rm emp}\|^2$ for the residual of the partially trained score on noised training points; for the RFNN it equals $\sum_i\lambda_i\|a_i(0)-a_i^\star\|^2e^{-2\lambda_iT}$ plus a rank-null floor, and for $T\gg\taugen$ it is carried by the sample bulk. Let $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ be the training-set nearest-neighbour distance and $G_{\rm NN}$ its empirical CDF.

Two elementary facts (Lemmas~\ref{lem:condensation}--\ref{lem:endpoint}, Appendix~\ref{app:bridge}): the weights $w_\mu$ concentrate on a single training point whenever $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$ \citep{biroli2024dynamical}, and once they have, the exact empirical sampler stopped at $t_{\min}$ lands at $x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_{t_{\min}}}\,\eta$. Hence even a perfect memorizer is reported as memorized by the $1/3$ nearest-neighbour-ratio test \citep{somepalli2023diffusion} only if $3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$: the test has a \emph{metric ceiling} $1-G_{\rm NN}(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}})$, equal to $0.68$ at $\dlat=5$ and $\to1$ for $\dlat\ge25$ at $\signoise=0.5$, but collapsing to $0.03$ by $\dlat=20$ at $\signoise=0.01$.

\begin{proposition}[Bridge, sketched]\label{prop:bridge}
Assume condensation at the attachment time $t$, that the residual $s_T-s_{\rm emp}$ at a noised training point is approximately isotropic Gaussian with total variance $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)$ (justified when many sample modes are still unabsorbed and $\dlat\gg1$), and that the endpoint is the Tweedie denoiser of the learned score at $t$ plus the sampler's terminal noise. Then the generated point is $x^\mu+\delta$ with $\delta\sim\mathcal N(0,\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)I)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Deff-main}
\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)=\Delta_{t_{\min}}+\frac{e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}}{\dlat}\,\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T),
\end{equation}
i.e.\ the law of the \emph{exact} empirical sampler stopped at noise level $\Delta_{\rm eff}$, and the reported fraction is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Fmem-main}
F_{\rm mem}(T)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathbf 1\!\Big[d_{\rm NN}(\mu)>3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)}\Big]\;\approx\;1-G_{\rm NN}\!\Big(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}\mathcal E_t(T)}\Big).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
Three consequences. (a) A single sample-bulk edge would make $F_{\rm mem}$ a step in $T$; the measured smooth curves come from the spread of $d_{\rm NN}$ (data level: $d^{(0.9)}_{\rm NN}/d^{(0.1)}_{\rm NN}=2.3$ at $\dlat=5$) and from the within-bulk spread of $\lambda_i$ (model level). The level-$q$ time is $\taumem(q)=\inf\{T:\mathcal E_t(T)\le[(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}]/(9e^{2t}\Delta_t^2)\}$: the most isolated training points are memorized first. The condensation form $\Delta_{\rm eff}<d^2_{\rm NN}/(2\log\nsamp)$ is implied by the metric form for all $\dlat\ge2$, so it is the metric, not condensation, that binds. (b) $d_{\rm NN}^2=C\sigsig^2(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}\kappa_\mu+2\signoise^2(\dlat-\dint)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:dnn-scaling}); when the null term is negligible the data-side threshold scales as $\nsamp^{-2/\dint}$ and is $\dlat$-independent---a $\dint$, not $\dlat$, exponent, and every $\dlat$-dependence of $\taumem$ then comes from the spectrum. When it dominates ($\signoise=0.5$, $\dlat\gtrsim20$) the criterion becomes the per-coordinate condition $\Delta_{\rm eff}<2\signoise^2/9$ and the same distance concentration pushes the NN-ratio of non-memorized samples toward $1$ (measured mean ratio $0.52\to0.87$). (c) For VAE latents the test is applied after decoding; with decoder Lipschitz constants $\ell\le\|D(z)-D(z')\|/\|z-z'\|\le L$ the pixel-space threshold is effectively $\tfrac13\ell/L$, so the pixel-space fraction lower-bounds the latent one (Remark~\ref{rem:pixel}). What is not proved is the isotropic-residual step: the true residual is a blend $(1-m)\hat x_{\rm pop}+m\,\hat x_{\rm emp}$ of the two denoisers, and a one-step evaluation of the blend gives an $O(1)$ absorption threshold $m_\star\approx0.5$ that agrees with \eqref{eq:Deff-main} on the ordering of $\taumem$ with $\dlat$ but not on its constant; Appendix~\ref{app:bridge} lists this and the multi-step accumulation as the open gaps and states the zero-parameter test that decides between them.
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Bridge from residual error to memorization}\label{app:bridge}
This appendix proves the two elementary lemmas used in Section~\ref{sec:theory-bridge}, gives the sketch of Proposition~\ref{prop:bridge}, derives its corollaries, and records the empirical anchors on the paper's own synthetic data. Throughout, the sampler is the reverse VP-SDE $dx=(x+2s_T)\,d\tau+\sqrt2\,dW$ ($\tau=-t$) run from $t_{\max}=3$ to $t_{\min}=0.01$ by Euler--Maruyama with $500$ steps and no terminal denoising step, as in the released code; $\Delta_{t_{\min}}=1-e^{-0.02}=0.0198$.

\begin{definition}[Empirical score, residual error, NN-ratio]\label{def:bridge-objects}
Let $\{x^\mu\}_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\subset\R^{\dlat}$ be the training set and $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$. The \emph{exact empirical score} is the score of the noised empirical measure $\rho^{\rm emp}_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathcal N(e^{-t}x^\mu,\Delta_t I)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:semp}
 s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp} w_\mu(x,t)\,\frac{e^{-t}x^\mu-x}{\Delta_t},\qquad w_\mu(x,t)=\frac{\exp\!\big(-\|x-e^{-t}x^\mu\|^2/2\Delta_t\big)}{\sum_\nu \exp\!\big(-\|x-e^{-t}x^\nu\|^2/2\Delta_t\big)}
\end{equation}
\citep{scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization}. For a score model $s_T$ at training time $T$ write $\zeta_T(x,t):=s_T(x,t)-s_{\rm emp}(x,t)$ and define the \emph{residual error at diffusion time $t$}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ET}
 \mathcal E_t(T):=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu}\E_{\eta}\big\|\zeta_T(e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta,\,t)\big\|^2,\qquad \eta\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat}),
\end{equation}
and its per-sample version $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)$. For the RFNN of Section~\ref{sec:rfnn} at its training time $t$, $\mathcal E_t(T)=\sum_i\lambda_i\|a_i(0)-a_i^\star\|^2e^{-2\lambda_iT}+\mathcal E_t(\infty)$, where $\mathcal E_t(\infty)$ is the projection error of $s_{\rm emp}$ onto the span of the $\pwidth$ features (the gradient-flow fixed point is that projection); we split $\mathcal E_t=\mathcal E^{\rm sig}_t+\mathcal E^{\rm null}_t+\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t+\mathcal E_t(\infty)$ by bulk. Let $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)=\min_{\nu\neq\mu}\|x^\mu-x^\nu\|$, $G_{\rm NN}$ its empirical CDF over $\mu$, and $d_{\rm NN}^{(r)}$ its $r$-quantile. For a generated point $\hat x$ with nearest and second-nearest training points $\mathrm{NN}_1,\mathrm{NN}_2$, the \citet{somepalli2023diffusion} statistic is $\varrho(\hat x)=\|\hat x-\mathrm{NN}_1\|/\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|$; $\hat x$ is memorized iff $\varrho<1/3$, and $F_{\rm mem}(T)=\Pr[\varrho(\hat x)<1/3]$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Condensation of the empirical softmax; \citealp{biroli2024dynamical}]\label{lem:condensation}
Fix $t$, a training index $\mu$, and $\delta\in(0,1)$. Let $x=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\eta$ with $\eta\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{\dlat})$. If
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cond-rigorous}
 e^{2t}\Delta_t\;\le\;\frac{d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2}{32\,\log(\nsamp/\delta)},
\end{equation}
then with probability at least $1-\delta$ over $\eta$, $1-w_\mu(x,t)\le \delta^{8}\nsamp^{-7}$, and on that event $s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=(e^{-t}x^\mu-x)/\Delta_t+r$ with $\|r\|\le \delta^{8}\nsamp^{-7}\max_\nu\|e^{-t}(x^\mu-x^\nu)\|/\Delta_t$. Evaluating the log-weight ratio at $\eta=0$ gives the sufficient condition $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$, which for $t\ll1$ is the condensation threshold $\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log \nsamp)$ of \citet{biroli2024dynamical}. Since $e^{2t}\Delta_t=e^{2t}-1$ is increasing in $t$, condensation at $t_c$ implies condensation at every $t\le t_c$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $\nu\neq\mu$ write $D_\nu=\|x^\mu-x^\nu\|\ge d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$. From \eqref{eq:semp},
\[\log\frac{w_\nu}{w_\mu}=-\frac{\|x-e^{-t}x^\nu\|^2-\|x-e^{-t}x^\mu\|^2}{2\Delta_t}=-\frac{e^{-2t}D_\nu^2}{2\Delta_t}-\frac{e^{-t}\langle\eta,x^\mu-x^\nu\rangle}{\sqrt{\Delta_t}},\]
using $x-e^{-t}x^\nu=e^{-t}(x^\mu-x^\nu)+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$ and $x-e^{-t}x^\mu=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$. The second term is Gaussian with standard deviation $e^{-t}D_\nu/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$; by a union bound, with probability $\ge1-\delta$ simultaneously $|\langle\eta,x^\mu-x^\nu\rangle|\le D_\nu\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}$ for all $\nu$. On this event
\[\log\frac{w_\nu}{w_\mu}\le-\frac{e^{-t}D_\nu}{2\sqrt{\Delta_t}}\Big(\frac{e^{-t}D_\nu}{\sqrt{\Delta_t}}-2\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}\Big).\]
Condition \eqref{eq:cond-rigorous} says $e^{-t}d_{\rm NN}/\sqrt{\Delta_t}\ge4\sqrt{2\log(\nsamp/\delta)}$, so the bracket is at least $\tfrac12e^{-t}D_\nu/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$ and $\log(w_\nu/w_\mu)\le-e^{-2t}D_\nu^2/(4\Delta_t)\le-8\log(\nsamp/\delta)$. Summing, $\sum_{\nu\ne\mu}w_\nu/w_\mu\le\nsamp(\delta/\nsamp)^8$, hence $1-w_\mu\le\delta^8\nsamp^{-7}$. The bound on $r=\sum_{\nu\neq\mu}w_\nu\,e^{-t}(x^\nu-x^\mu)/\Delta_t$ follows from $\sum_{\nu\ne\mu}w_\nu=1-w_\mu$. At $\eta=0$ the cross term vanishes and $\sum_{\nu\neq\mu}w_\nu/w_\mu\le\nsamp e^{-e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/2\Delta_t}<1$ iff $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Endpoint law of the exact empirical sampler after condensation]\label{lem:endpoint}
Suppose condensation \eqref{eq:cond-rigorous} holds for $\mu$ at $t=t_c$ (hence on $[t_{\min},t_c]$), and the reverse VP-SDE with score $s_{\rm emp}$ is started at $x_{t_c}\sim\mathcal N(e^{-t_c}x^\mu,\Delta_{t_c}I)$ and run with the residual $r$ neglected. Then $x_{t_{\min}}\sim\mathcal N(e^{-t_{\min}}x^\mu,\Delta_{t_{\min}}I)$. For the probability-flow ODE with the same score, the offset $y_t=x_t-e^{-t}x^\mu$ satisfies $y_{t_{\min}}=\sqrt{\Delta_{t_{\min}}/\Delta_{t_c}}\,y_{t_c}$. In either case the endpoint is $x^\mu$ up to a displacement of per-coordinate variance at most $\Delta_{t_{\min}}$ (plus the $O(t_{\min}\|x^\mu\|)$ mean shrinkage). Consequently $\varrho(x_{t_{\min}})\approx\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}/d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ and the reported fraction cannot exceed the \emph{metric ceiling}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ceiling}
 F^{\max}_{\rm mem}=1-G_{\rm NN}\big(3\sqrt{\dlat\,\Delta_{t_{\min}}}\big)\qquad(\text{SDE sampler stopped at }t_{\min},\ \dlat\gg1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Under condensation $s_{\rm emp}(x,t)=-(x-e^{-t}x^\mu)/\Delta_t$ is the score of $\mathcal N(e^{-t}x^\mu,\Delta_tI)$, the time-$t$ marginal of the forward VP process started at $\delta_{x^\mu}$; the reverse SDE with the exact score transports the time-$t_c$ marginal to the time-$t_{\min}$ marginal \citep{song2021scoresde}. For the ODE, $\frac{dx}{d\tau}=x+s_{\rm emp}$ and $\frac{d}{d\tau}(e^{-t}x^\mu)=e^{-t}x^\mu$ give $\frac{dy}{d\tau}=-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\Delta_t}y$, and $\int_{t_{\min}}^{t_c}\frac{e^{-2t}}{1-e^{-2t}}dt=\tfrac12\log(\Delta_{t_c}/\Delta_{t_{\min}})$. For \eqref{eq:ceiling}: $\|x_{t_{\min}}-x^\mu\|^2=\Delta_{t_{\min}}\|\eta\|^2+O(t_{\min}\|x^\mu\|)$ concentrates at $\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$; when this is below $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/9$ we have $\mathrm{NN}_1=x^\mu$ and $\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|=d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$, so $\varrho<1/3$ iff $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)>3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}$. Averaging over $\mu$ (the condensed sampler lands on training points with frequency $1/\nsamp$ on average) gives \eqref{eq:ceiling}. Euler--Maruyama adds $O(dt)$ variance per coordinate, which only lowers the ceiling.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Bridge: residual error acts as an effective terminal noise level]\label{prop:bridge}
Fix a diffusion time $t\in[t_{\min},t_c]$ at which the sampler makes its final attachment to a training point, and a training time $T$. Assume
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(B1)] (\emph{condensation}) $e^{2t}\Delta_t<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$ for the training points $\mu$ under consideration;
\item[(B2)] (\emph{isotropic residual}) conditionally on $x_t=e^{-t}x^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$, $\zeta_T(x_t,t)\approx\mathcal N\big(0,(\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)/\dlat)\,I_{\dlat}\big)$, independent of $\eta$;
\item[(B3)] (\emph{one-attachment reduction}) the endpoint of the learned-score sampler equals the Tweedie denoiser of the learned score applied at the attachment time $t$, plus the sampler's terminal noise of Lemma~\ref{lem:endpoint}.
\end{enumerate}
Then the generated point is $\hat x=x^\mu+\delta$ with $\delta\sim\mathcal N(0,\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)\,I_{\dlat})$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Deff}
 \Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)=\Delta_{t_{\min}}+c_t\,\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T),\qquad c_t=\frac{e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}}{\dlat},
\end{equation}
so the endpoint has the law of the \emph{exact} empirical sampler stopped at noise level $\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)$. Since $\|\delta\|^2=\dlat\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$, the sample attached to $\mu$ is memorized iff
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mem-criterion}
 \dlat\,\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)<\tfrac19\,d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)<\frac{d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{9\,e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}} ,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Fmem}
 F_{\rm mem}(T)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\mathbf 1\Big[\,d_{\rm NN}(\mu)>3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}\,\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)}\,\Big]\;\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal E_{t,\mu}\equiv\mathcal E_t\ }\;1-G_{\rm NN}\Big(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}\,\mathcal E_t(T)}\Big).
\end{equation}
The companion condition ``condensation at the effective level'', $e^{2t}\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}(T)<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2/(2\log\nsamp)$, is implied by \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} whenever $9\dlat>2e^{2t}\log\nsamp$, i.e.\ for every $\dlat\ge2$ in our sweeps ($\nsamp=500$, $t\le0.1$): the metric threshold, not condensation, binds.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Sketch]
By (B1) and Lemma~\ref{lem:condensation}, $s_{\rm emp}(x_t,t)=-(x_t-e^{-t}x^\mu)/\Delta_t$ up to the negligible $r$. Tweedie's identity \citep{efron2011tweedie} for the learned score gives $\hat x=e^{t}\big(x_t+\Delta_t s_T(x_t,t)\big)=x^\mu+e^{t}\Delta_t\,\zeta_T(x_t,t)$. By (B2) the displacement is $\mathcal N(0,e^{2t}\Delta_t^2\mathcal E_{t,\mu}(T)/\dlat\,I)$; by (B3) it adds independently to the terminal Gaussian of Lemma~\ref{lem:endpoint}, giving \eqref{eq:Deff}. Two independent isotropic Gaussians centred at $x^\mu$ sum to one of variance $\Delta_{\rm eff,\mu}$, which is the endpoint law of the exact empirical sampler stopped at that level---the precise sense of ``behaves like $s_{\rm emp}$ at $\Delta_{\rm eff}$''. $\chi^2_{\dlat}$ concentration of $\|\delta\|^2$ and the identification $\mathrm{NN}_1=x^\mu$, $\|\mathrm{NN}_1-\mathrm{NN}_2\|=d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ (valid when $\|\delta\|<d_{\rm NN}(\mu)/2$) give \eqref{eq:mem-criterion}; averaging over the attachment index gives \eqref{eq:Fmem}. Comparison of thresholds: \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} reads $\Delta_{\rm eff}<d_{\rm NN}^2/(9\dlat)$, condensation reads $\Delta_{\rm eff}<e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)$.

\emph{When (B2) is justified.} At $T\gg\taugen$ the signal and noise-dim modes are absorbed and $\zeta_T(x)=-(A^\star-A(T))\phi(x)$ restricted to sample modes. The sample-bulk eigendirections are the feature-space images of the per-sample deviations $\xi^\mu$ with the linear (population) part removed; after the random rotation $Q$ their output directions in $\R^{\dlat}$ are exchangeable across coordinates, and the residual at $x_t$ is a sum over the $N_{\rm res}(T)=\#\{i\in\text{samp}:\lambda_iT\lesssim1\}$ unabsorbed modes. When $N_{\rm res}(T)\gg1$ and $\dlat\gg1$ a CLT over these generic directions makes $\zeta_T$ approximately isotropic Gaussian with the stated variance. The approximation fails (i) early, when the residual is dominated by the $\dint$ signal modes (then $\mathcal E_t$ is far above the threshold and $F_{\rm mem}=0$ regardless), and (ii) at the very end, when only a few sample modes remain: the residual is then low-rank and localized on a few training points, and \eqref{eq:Fmem} must be read with the per-sample $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}$. It also requires $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2$ not to be negligible against $\dint\sigsig^2$; otherwise an $O(1)$ fraction of $\|\zeta_T\|^2$ sits in $\dint$ coordinates and the $\chi^2_{\dlat}$ concentration is replaced by a wider $\chi^2_{\dint}$ one (the threshold in mean is unchanged).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Fraction curve and quantile-indexed memorization time]\label{cor:taumem-fraction}
Under Proposition~\ref{prop:bridge} with a $\mu$-independent residual $\mathcal E_t(T)$ decreasing in $T$, the fraction first reaches level $q\in(0,F^{\max}_{\rm mem})$ at
\begin{equation}\label{eq:taumem-q}
 \taumem(q)=\inf\Big\{T:\ \mathcal E_t(T)\le \frac{\big(d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q)}\big)^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{9\,e^{2t}\Delta_t^{2}}\Big\}:
\end{equation}
the most isolated training points are memorized first. The condensation-form threshold, $\taumem^{\rm cond}=\inf\{T:\mathcal E_t(T)\le[e^{-2t}d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\log\nsamp)-\Delta_{t_{\min}}]/c_t\}$, is weaker for all $\dlat\ge2$ and is not what the fraction curve reports. If $\mathcal E_t(T)=\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t(0)e^{-2\lambda_{\rm samp}T}$ then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:taumem-spread}
 \taumem(q_2)-\taumem(q_1)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\rm samp}}\log\frac{d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q_1)}}{d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q_2)}}+O\!\Big(\frac{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}{\lambda_{\rm samp}(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2}\Big),
\end{equation}
so the width of the fraction curve in $\log T$ is set by the spread of $d_{\rm NN}$ (data level) and, through the within-bulk spread of $\lambda_i$ replacing the single $\lambda_{\rm samp}$, by the sample bulk's width (model level). A single edge with a degenerate $G_{\rm NN}$ would give a step function.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$1-G_{\rm NN}(u)\ge q$ iff $u\le d_{\rm NN}^{(1-q)}$; substitute $u=3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^2\mathcal E_t(T)}$ and use monotonicity. For \eqref{eq:taumem-spread} take logarithms of $3e^{t}\Delta_t\sqrt{\mathcal E^{\rm samp}_t(0)}\,e^{-\lambda_{\rm samp}T}=\sqrt{(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2-9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}}$ and expand in $9\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}/(d^{(1-q)}_{\rm NN})^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[What $\taumem$ depends on through $d_{\rm NN}$]\label{cor:dnn-scaling}
For the synthetic data of Section~\ref{sec:design},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dnn-scaling}
 d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\;=\;\underbrace{C_{\dint}\,\sigsig^2\,(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}\,\kappa_\mu}_{\text{signal part}}\;+\;\underbrace{2\signoise^2(\dlat-\dint)\big(1+O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/(\dlat-\dint)})\big)}_{\text{null part}},
\end{equation}
with $C_{\dint}$ depending only on $\dint$ and $\kappa_\mu$ an $O(1)$ random factor with a fixed law. Hence: (i) if $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2\ll\sigsig^2(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}$ (e.g.\ $\signoise=0.01$) the threshold in \eqref{eq:taumem-q} scales as $\nsamp^{-2/\dint}$ and is independent of $\dlat$: all $\dlat$-dependence of $\taumem$ enters through $\mathcal E_t(T)$, and $\partial\log\taumem/\partial\log\nsamp$ acquires the additive term $-\frac{1}{\dint\lambda_{\rm samp}\taumem}$ beyond the spectral $\nsamp$-dependence---a $\dint$, not $\dlat$, exponent; (ii) if $(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2\gg\sigsig^2(\nsamp/k)^{-2/\dint}$ (e.g.\ $\signoise=0.5$, $\dlat\gtrsim20$), $d_{\rm NN}^2\simeq2\signoise^2\dlat$, \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} becomes the per-coordinate condition $\Delta_{\rm eff}<2\signoise^2/9$, the spread $d^{(0.9)}_{\rm NN}/d^{(0.1)}_{\rm NN}\to1$, and the same metric drives the NN-ratio of non-memorized samples toward $1$; (iii) the ceiling \eqref{eq:ceiling} collapses with $\dlat$ in regime (i) and tends to $1$ in regime (ii).
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Within a cluster the signal coordinates of the $\nsamp/k$ points are i.i.d.\ $\mathcal N(0,\sigsig^2I_{\dint})$; the NN distance of $m$ i.i.d.\ points from a smooth density in $\dint$ dimensions scales as $m^{-1/\dint}$ with a limiting law for the rescaled distance \citep{levina2004maximum,facco2017twonn}. The null coordinates of two distinct points are independent $\mathcal N(0,\signoise^2I_{\dlat-\dint})$, so their squared distance is $2\signoise^2\chi^2_{\dlat-\dint}$; the minimum over $\nsamp-1$ such variables is $2\signoise^2(\dlat-\dint)(1-O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/(\dlat-\dint)}))$ by Gaussian concentration and a union bound. The parts add because signal and null coordinates are independent and $Q$ preserves norms.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Real data: latent versus pixel space]\label{rem:pixel}
For VAE latents the sampler runs in $\R^{\dlat}$ but $\varrho$ is computed in pixel space after decoding with $D:\R^{\dlat}\to\R^{P}$. If $\ell\|z-z'\|\le\|D(z)-D(z')\|\le L\|z-z'\|$ on the relevant region, then $\varrho_{\rm pix}\le(L/\ell)\varrho_{\rm lat}$ and \eqref{eq:mem-criterion} holds in pixel space with threshold $\tfrac13\ell/L$ instead of $\tfrac13$. Hence (a) the pixel-space fraction lower-bounds the latent-space fraction when $L/\ell>1$, and $L/\ell$ is not controlled across the sweep (one VAE per $\dlat$); (b) posterior-collapsed coordinates ($\lambda_{\min}=\Delta_t$ exactly for $\dlat\ge140$ on CelebA, Section~\ref{sec:real-data}) contribute to $\dlat\Delta_{\rm eff}$ in latent space but are annihilated by $D$, so in regime (ii) the relevant dimension is the decoder-visible one ($\approx0.5\dlat$, the measured effective rank), not $\dlat$.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Empirical anchors (synthetic data, $\nsamp=500$, $k=10$, $\dint=5$, $s=3$, $\sigsig=1$; five replicate training sets).}
(1) Metric ceiling \eqref{eq:ceiling} at $\signoise=0.5$: $0.676,\ 0.790,\ 0.870,\ 0.924,\ 0.950,\ 0.979,\ 0.998$ for $\dlat=5,8,10,12,15,20,25$ and $\approx1$ beyond; measured final fractions of the 5M-step MLP sweep (seed 42): $0.290,0.224,0.168,0.127,0.079,0.044,0.017,0.006,0.004,0.002$ for $\dlat=5,\dots,40$---all below the ceiling. At $\signoise=0.01$ the ceiling is $0.668,0.364,0.250,0.165,0.100,0.034,0.015,0.008,0.002$ for the same $\dlat$; the available $\signoise=0.01$ runs report fractions $\le0.006$ and exactly $0$ for $\dlat\ge20$. (2) $d_{\rm NN}$ deciles $(q_{10}/q_{50}/q_{90})$ at $\signoise=0.5$: $0.72/1.10/1.63$ ($\dlat=5$), $2.13/2.56/3.00$ ($20$), $8.75/9.16/9.56$ ($200$); at $\signoise=0.01$: $0.70/1.07/1.60$, flat in $\dlat$. (3) $\mathrm{median}(d_{\rm NN})\cdot\nsamp^{1/5}=3.66,3.72,3.77,3.76$ for $\nsamp=100,500,1000,5000$ at $\signoise=0.01$. (4) Condensation times of the \emph{exact} empirical score ($\eta=0$ form of Lemma~\ref{lem:condensation}, median $d_{\rm NN}$): $t_c=0.046,0.105,0.21,0.39,0.72,1.02$ for $\dlat=5,10,20,40,100,200$ at $\signoise=0.5$, and $0.045$ flat at $\signoise=0.01$. The exact empirical measure therefore memorizes \emph{earlier} in the reverse process as $\dlat$ grows; the observed delay is a property of the learned residual $\mathcal E_t(T)$, not of the data.

\paragraph{What is not proved.}
(G1) \emph{Isotropic residual.} The gradient-flow residual is a blend of denoisers, $\hat x_T=(1-m)\hat x_{\rm pop}+m\,\hat x_{\rm emp}$ with $m(T)$ the absorbed fraction of the sample bulk, not an isotropic jitter. A one-step evaluation of the blend at $t_c$ gives $\|\hat x_{\rm pop}-x^\mu\|^2\simeq\dlat\,g_c\sigma^2/(\sigma^2+g_c)$ with $g_c=e^{2t_c}-1$, and the memorization criterion $(1-m)^2<\tfrac{2c_d}{9}\big(1+\tfrac{\log\nsamp}{c_d\dlat}\big)$, $c_d=d_{\rm NN}^2/(2\dlat\sigma^2)$: an $O(1)$ absorption threshold $m_\star\in(0.4,0.6)$ across the sweep. Both models place $\taumem$ on the sample-bulk clock and agree on its ordering in $\dlat$; they differ in the constant $c_t$ and in the $\sqrt{\mathcal E}$ versus $\mathcal E$ form. The zero-parameter test---compute $\mathcal E_{t_{\min}}(T)$ against the closed form \eqref{eq:semp} at every checkpoint and compare $1-G_{\rm NN}(3\sqrt{\dlat\Delta_{t_{\min}}+e^{2t}\Delta_t^2\mathcal E_t(T)})$ with the measured fraction---decides between them. (G2) \emph{Multi-step accumulation.} With the offset ODE $\frac{dy}{d\tau}=-\frac{e^{-2t}}{\Delta_t}y+\zeta_t$ one gets $y_{t_{\min}}=\int_{t_{\min}}^{t_c}\sqrt{\Delta_{t_{\min}}/\Delta_s}\,\zeta_s\,ds$; under the scaling $\mathcal E_s\propto1/\Delta_s$ and a persistent error this gives $\Delta_{\rm eff}=\Delta_{t_{\min}}[1+\tfrac14 r^2\log^2(t_c/t_{\min})]$, $r^2=\mathcal E_s\Delta_s/\dlat$, i.e.\ the same additive form with $c_t$ known only up to a logarithm in $t_c/t_{\min}$ ($1.5$--$4.6$ across the sweep). (G3) The RFNN spectrum is at $t=0.01$ while attachment occurs near $t_c$; relating $\mathcal E_{t_c}(T)$ to the fixed-$t$ spectrum is not done. (G4) $\mathrm{NN}_1$ equals the attachment point only for $\|\delta\|<d_{\rm NN}/2$; the non-memorized tail of $\varrho$ is not modelled. (G5) The rank-null floor $\mathcal E_t(\infty)>0$ is not computed; it is also where the empirical return of memorization as $\dlat\to\nsamp$ must enter, which the bridge does not explain. (G6) The per-sample residual $\mathcal E_{t,\mu}$ is not derived from the sample-bulk modes; isolated points (large $d_{\rm NN}$) may carry the least-supported, smallest-$\lambda_i$ modes, partially cancelling the ordering ``most isolated first''. (G7) $L/\ell$ of the decoders is unknown. (G8) Lemma~\ref{lem:condensation} is rigorous with $32\log(\nsamp/\delta)$; the quoted $t_c$ values use the $2\log\nsamp$ form.
```



---

# Piece 6: From the frozen-latent spectrum to a memorisation-fraction predictor: noise-averaged sample modes, the missing sample-bulk term, and per-sample resolution

## Statements

### `lem:ge-coeff-pred` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: sigma = tanh (any odd, bounded, smooth activation works for (i)-(iii),(v); for a non-odd activation such as GELU the k=0 and k=2 terms must be kept); z, z' standard Gaussian, correlation rho in [0,1]

```latex
Let $\sigma=\tanh$, $z\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$, $q>0$, $a_1(q)=\E[\sigma'(\sqrt q z)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\sigma(\sqrt q z)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$, and let $\sigma(\sqrt q z)=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}}c_k(q)\,\mathrm{He}_k(z)/k!$ be the Hermite expansion. Then (i) $\E[\sigma(\sqrt q z)z]=\sqrt q\,a_1(q)$; (ii) $a_*(q)^2=\E[(\sigma(\sqrt q z)-a_1(q)\sqrt q z)^2]\ge0$ is the mean square of the best linear residual of $\sigma$ at pre-activation variance $q$; (iii) $a_1(q)^2q=c_1(q)^2$ and $a_*(q)^2=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}c_k(q)^2/k!$; (iv) $a_*(q)^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$ and $a_*^2$ is increasing on $[0.05,3.5]$; 120-node Gauss--Hermite values: $a_*^2=0.0047,\,0.0274,\,0.0359,\,0.0544,\,0.0784$ and $a_1^2=0.623,\,0.367,\,0.320,\,0.246,\,0.180$ at $q=0.33,\,1,\,1.247,\,1.834,\,2.76$; (v) (\emph{noise-averaged residual coefficient}) for jointly Gaussian $(z,z')$ with correlation $\varrho\in[0,1]$,
\[ g(q,\varrho):=\E[\sigma(\sqrt qz)\sigma(\sqrt qz')]-a_1(q)^2q\varrho=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}\frac{c_k(q)^2}{k!}\varrho^k, \]
so $0\le g(q,\varrho)\le\varrho^3a_*(q)^2$, $g(q,1)=a_*(q)^2$, and $g$ is increasing in $\varrho$. Define $r_t(q):=g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)/a_*(q)^2\in(0,1]$. Numerically, at $t=0.1$: $r_t=0.086,\,0.505,\,0.538,\,0.574,\,0.668,\,0.675,\,0.755$ at $q=0.33,\,1,\,1.108,\,1.247,\,1.783,\,1.834,\,2.76$; at $t=0.01$: $r_t=0.822,\,0.943,\,0.969$ at $q=0.33,\,1.247,\,2.76$. The product $r_t(q)a_*(q)^2=g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)$ is increasing in $q$ on the same range for $t\in\{0.01,0.1\}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(i) Stein's lemma $\E[f(z)z]=\E[f'(z)]$ with $f(z)=\sigma(\sqrt qz)$. (ii) Expand the square and use (i). (iii) $\sigma$ is odd, so only odd Hermite coefficients are nonzero; orthogonality with $\E\mathrm{He}_k^2=k!$ gives $\E\sigma^2=\sum_kc_k^2/k!$, and $c_1=\E[\sigma(\sqrt qz)\mathrm{He}_1(z)]=\sqrt qa_1$ by (i). (iv) $\tanh x=x-x^3/3+2x^5/15+O(x^7)$ gives $\E\sigma^2=q-2q^2+\tfrac{17}{3}q^3+O(q^4)$ and $a_1=1-q+2q^2+O(q^3)$, hence $a_1^2q=q-2q^2+5q^3+O(q^4)$ and the difference is $\tfrac23q^3$ (numerically $a_*^2/q^3=0.62$ at $q=0.01$). Monotonicity and the table are 120-node Gauss--Hermite quadrature (the integrand is smooth; 80 nodes agree to four digits). (v) Mehler's formula $\E[\mathrm{He}_k(z)\mathrm{He}_l(z')]=\delta_{kl}k!\varrho^k$ for correlated standard Gaussians; the subtraction removes the $k=1$ term and oddness the even ones. Each term is nonnegative and increasing in $\varrho$; the bound $g\le\varrho^3a_*^2$ is $\varrho^k\le\varrho^3$ for $k\ge3$, $\varrho\le1$. The $r_t$ values were computed both by two-dimensional Gauss--Hermite quadrature and by the Hermite series truncated at $k=39$; they agree to four digits.
```

### `prop:mode-clocks-pred` (proposition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: quadratic loss in A (fixed features); gradient flow from A(0)=0; lambda_i>0 for the modes considered (modes with lambda_i=0 never move); for the 'meaning' clause: eigenvector localised on one training point and sample-specific target approximately constant over the noise draw (leading order in the corrections of prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred)

```latex
Fix $t>0$ and a feature map $\phi:\R^{\dlat}\to\R^{\pwidth}$. Let $L(A)=\frac1{2\nsamp}\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\E_\xi\|A\phi(x_t^\mu)-y_\mu(x_t^\mu)\|^2$ for square-integrable targets $y_\mu$, $\Umat=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]=\sum_i\lambda_iu_iu_i^\top$, $V=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[y_\mu\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$, and let $\dot A=-\nabla L(A)$ from $A(0)=0$ with $T=\eta s$ ($\eta$ the learning rate, $s$ the step). With $a_i(T)=A(T)u_i$ and $a_i^*=Vu_i/\lambda_i$ ($\lambda_i>0$),
\[ a_i(T)=a_i^*(1-e^{-\lambda_iT}),\qquad L(T)-L(\infty)=\tfrac12\sum_i\lambda_i\|a_i^*\|^2e^{-2\lambda_iT}. \]
Mode $i$ thus has absorbed \emph{amplitude} $1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is}$, absorbed \emph{loss} $1-e^{-2\eta\lambda_is}$ and learned-\emph{energy} fraction $\|a_i(T)\|^2/\|a_i^*\|^2=(1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is})^2$, ordered $(1-e^{-x})^2\le1-e^{-x}\le1-e^{-2x}$. The released clock $P_d(s)=\mathrm{mean}_i(1-e^{-\kappa\lambda_is})^2$ is the third with $\kappa\lambda_i$ standing in for $\eta\lambda_i(\Umat)$. \emph{Units.} The exponent $\eta\lambda_is$ is invariant under rescaling of the features: for the repository's normalisation $\phi_{\rm code}=\sqrt{\pwidth}\,\phi$ one has $\Umat_{\rm code}=\pwidth\Umat$ and $\eta_{\rm code}\lambda_{\rm code}=\eta_{\rm code}\pwidth\lambda$, so $T=\eta_{\rm code}\pwidth s$ in theory units. \emph{Meaning of the amplitude fraction.} If $u$ is a unit eigenvector localised on training point $\mu$, $u\propto\bar\rho_\mu:=\E_\xi\rho(x_t^\mu)$ with eigenvalue $\Lambda_\mu=\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2/\nsamp$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}), and $\bar y_\mu$ is the sample-specific target component, then to leading order $Vu=\bar y_\mu\|\bar\rho_\mu\|/\nsamp$, $a^*=\bar y_\mu/\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$, and the model output at $x_t^\mu$ along this mode is $A(T)uu^\top\bar\phi_\mu=\bar y_\mu(1-e^{-\Lambda_\mu T})$: the amplitude fraction is the fraction of the sample-specific score \emph{output} at the training point that the model produces, an absolute output-space quantity.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
$\nabla_AL=A\Umat-V$; projecting on $u_i$ gives $\dot a_i=-\lambda_i(a_i-a_i^*)$, integrated from $a_i(0)=0$. The loss identity is the quadratic expansion of $L$ about $A^*=V\Umat^+$ ($Vu_i=0$ whenever $\lambda_i=0$, so $A^*$ is a minimiser). The inequalities are elementary for $x\ge0$. Units: substitute $A_{\rm code}=A/\sqrt{\pwidth}$, $\Umat_{\rm code}=\pwidth\Umat$, $V_{\rm code}=\sqrt{\pwidth}V$ into the same flow. Meaning: with $u=\bar\rho_\mu/\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$, only the $\nu=\mu$ term of $V$ correlates with $u$ at leading order (the cross terms are the off-diagonal Gram entries of Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}(ii)); if the sample-specific part of $y_\mu$ is approximately constant over $\xi$, $\E_\xi[y_\mu\phi_\mu^\top]u=\bar y_\mu\bar\rho_\mu^\top\bar\rho_\mu/\|\bar\rho_\mu\|=\bar y_\mu\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$, and $a^*=Vu/\Lambda_\mu=\bar y_\mu/\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$; the output along $u$ at $\bar\phi_\mu$ is $a(T)\,u^\top\bar\phi_\mu=a(T)\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$. For the RFNN, $\phi=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$ and $L$ is the score-matching loss with fresh noise at every step, which is exactly the $\E_\xi$-averaged loss above; full-batch gradient descent agrees with the flow to $O(\eta\lambda_{\max})$ per step.
```

### `rem:square-pred` (remark, **proved**)

```latex
\emph{The square in the released clock is not derived.} For a single mode all three fractions of Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred} are monotone reparametrisations of $\eta\lambda_is$, so the choice is absorbed into the threshold $\theta$; the square matters only through the \emph{average over modes}, where it suppresses slow modes quadratically at early times ($(1-e^{-x})^2\sim x^2$ vs.\ $1-e^{-2x}\sim2x$). Justifying it would require showing that the NN-ratio memorisation statistic is, at small absorbed amplitude, a quadratic functional of the learned sample-specific displacement. We do not have such a derivation; the amplitude clock has the direct output-space meaning given in Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred} and is the one used below.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Immediate from Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred}.
```

### `prop:data-side-rates-pred` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: p/d_lat -> infinity (p-independence of edges is only approximate at finite p); Gaussian equivalence of the tanh random-feature kernel for the data distribution at hand (proved for Gaussian/sub-Gaussian inputs; assumed for the clustered synthetic data and for VAE latents); first-layer weights iid N(0, sigma_w^2), gain gamma = d_lat sigma_w^2

```latex
(Data-side rates; restates the Gaussian-equivalence step of Piece~4.) Let $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$ with $W_{ij}\sim\mathcal N(0,\sigma_w^2)$ i.i.d., define the pre-activation gain $\gamma:=\dlat\sigma_w^2$ ($\gamma=1$ for the RFNN), $M_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E[x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}]=\sum_{i\le\dlat}\lambda_i(M_t)v_iv_i^\top$, $q_d=\mathrm{tr}(M_t)/\dlat$, and let $\pwidth/\dlat\to\infty$. Then the $\dlat$ data-side eigenvalues of $\Umat$ are, to leading order,
\[ \lambda_i(\Umat)=\frac{\gamma\,a_1(\gamma q_d)^2}{\dlat}\,\lambda_i(M_t)\,(1+o(1)),\qquad i=1,\dots,\dlat, \]
with trace $a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\gamma q_d$. The data-side amplitude clock at step $s$ is $1-\exp[-\eta\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)s/\dlat]$: the released $\kappa$ is not a $\dlat$-independent constant but $\kappa_d=\eta\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2/\dlat$ in theory units. In the repository's code units ($\phi_{\rm code}=\tanh(x_tW^\top)$, $W\sim\mathcal N(0,1/\dlat)$, $\lambda_{\rm code}=\pwidth\lambda$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$) the same rate reads $\eta_{\rm code}\,64\,a_1(q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)$: the explicit $1/\dlat$ cancels against $\pwidth$ and the $\dlat$-dependence is carried by $a_1(q_d)^2$ alone ($0.180\to0.625$ over $\dlat=5\to200$, F3).
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Write $\tanh(w^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat})=a_1(q_x)w^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat}+r_w(x)$ with $q_x=\gamma\|x\|^2/\dlat$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(i) the residual is uncorrelated (over $w$) with the linear part, so the linear--residual cross block of $\Umat$ vanishes at leading order. The linear block is $\frac{\sigma_w^2}{\pwidth}W'\tilde M_tW'^\top/\dlat$ with $W'$ standard Gaussian and $\tilde M_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu a_1(q_{x_t^\mu})^2\E x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}$; replacing $a_1(q_{x_t^\mu})$ by $a_1(\gamma q_d)$ costs a relative error of order $\mathrm{cv}(q_\mu)\cdot|a_1'|q/a_1$ (the heterogeneity treated in Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}). The nonzero eigenvalues of $W'\tilde M_tW'^\top/\pwidth$ equal those of $\tilde M_t(W'^\top W'/\pwidth)$, and $W'^\top W'/\pwidth\to I_{\dlat}$ as $\pwidth/\dlat\to\infty$, giving $\gamma a_1^2\lambda_i(M_t)/\dlat$. This is the Gaussian-equivalence decomposition of the conjugate kernel \citep{pennington2017nonlinear,benigni2021eigenvalue,hu2022universality,goldt2022gaussian}; \citet{bonnaire2025} state the resulting spectral law for arbitrary $\rho_\Sigma$ and \citet{george2025dsm} the block structure $(\pwidth,\dlat-\dint,\dint,\nsamp)$ at $\signoise=0$. Finite-$\pwidth$ corrections are the $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ Marchenko--Pastur broadening of $W'^\top W'/\pwidth$ \citep{marchenko1967}, consistent with the $<0.5\%$ edge motion under a $5.8\times$ change of $\pwidth$ (F2). Trace: $\gamma a_1^2\mathrm{tr}M_t/\dlat=a_1^2\gamma q_d$, matching F2. Code units: multiply by $\pwidth=64\dlat$.
```

### `prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: same as prop:data-side-rates-pred with gamma=1; p > n (in fact p >> n so that sqrt(n/p) << 1); fails on the p=64 d_lat sweep for d_lat <= 7; n << d_lat^3 r_t(q_d)^2 a_*(q_d)^4 for per-eigenvalue attachment (semicircle scaling); fails for the synthetic data at all d_lat <= 200 and holds marginally for the real latents at d_lat >= 200 -- otherwise the attachment holds only in the quantile sense; no near-duplicate pairs (|rho_{mu nu}| = O(1)); clustered data violates this at small d_lat, see rem:collective-pred; the noise-averaged statements refer to the E_xi object (training loss, compute_U with many draws); F1's rank-n cliff refers to the single-draw object

```latex
(Per-sample resolution of the sample bulk; single-draw versus noise-averaged objects.) In the setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:data-side-rates-pred} with $\gamma=1$, assume in addition $\pwidth>\nsamp$. Write $\phi=\phi_{\rm lin}+\rho$ with $\rho(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}[\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})-a_1(q_x)Wx/\sqrt{\dlat}]$, and for each training point set
\[ q_\mu:=\E_\xi\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t,\qquad \varrho_\mu:=1-\Delta_t/q_\mu,\qquad \bar\rho_\mu:=\E_\xi\rho(x_t^\mu). \]
Distinguish the \emph{single-draw} matrix $\Umat^{(1)}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top$ (one noise draw per point; the object described by F1--F2) from the \emph{noise-averaged} $\Umat=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi\phi^\top]$ (the training loss with fresh noise; the $n_\xi\to\infty$ limit of the repository's \texttt{compute\_U}, which averages $n_\xi=50$ draws). Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\E_W\|\rho(x)\|^2=a_*(q_x)^2$ exactly, with relative concentration $\pwidth^{-1/2}$ over the rows of $W$, and $\E_{W,\xi}\|\rho(x_t^\mu)\|^2=a_*(q_\mu)^2(1+O(\dlat^{-1}))$.
\item For inputs $x,y$ with normalised overlap $\varrho_{xy}=x^\top y/(\dlat\sqrt{q_xq_y})$: $\E_W[\rho(x)^\top\rho(y)]=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}c_k(q_x)c_k(q_y)\varrho_{xy}^k/k!=O(\varrho_{xy}^3)$. In particular, two independent noise draws of the \emph{same} training point have overlap $\varrho_\mu$ and $\E_W\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2=g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))=r_t(q_\mu)\,a_*(q_\mu)^2(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$.
\item (\emph{Noise-averaged decomposition.}) $\Umat_*=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\rho\rho^\top]=\Umat_{\rm loc}+\Umat_{\rm cloud}$ with $\Umat_{\rm loc}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\bar\rho_\mu\bar\rho_\mu^\top$ and $\Umat_{\rm cloud}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathrm{Cov}_\xi(\rho(x_t^\mu))\succeq0$. To leading order in $\max_{\mu\ne\nu}|\varrho_{\mu\nu}|^3$ and $\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$, $\Umat_{\rm loc}$ has one eigenvalue per training point, with eigenvector $\propto\bar\rho_\mu$,
\[ \Lambda_\mu=\frac{r_t(q_\mu)\,a_*(q_\mu)^2}{\nsamp}, \]
while $\Umat_{\rm cloud}$ has trace $\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu(1-r_t(q_\mu))a_*(q_\mu)^2\approx(1-r_t(q_d))a_*(q_d)^2$ spread over up to $\pwidth$ directions (at finite $n_\xi$, over at most $\min\{\pwidth,(n_\xi-1)\nsamp\}$), i.e.\ per-direction mass $O((1-r_t)a_*^2/\pwidth)\ll\Lambda_\mu$ when $\pwidth\gg\nsamp$. For the single-draw matrix the same argument with $\varrho_\mu$ replaced by $1$ gives $\Lambda^{(1)}_\mu=a_*(\hat q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$ ($\hat q_\mu$ the realised norm), no cloud, and $\mathrm{rank}\,\Umat^{(1)}\le\nsamp$. Trace check (both objects): $\mathrm{tr}\,\Umat_{\rm lin}+\mathrm{tr}\,\Umat_{\rm loc}+\mathrm{tr}\,\Umat_{\rm cloud}=a_1^2q_d+r_ta_*^2+(1-r_t)a_*^2=\E\tanh^2(\sqrt{q_d}z)=O(1)$.
\item (\emph{Corrections and separation.}) At fixed $W$ the $\pwidth^{-1/2}$ fluctuations of the Gram entries give a Marchenko--Pastur spread of the $\Lambda_\mu$ with relative standard deviation $\varsigma=\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$ and edges $(1\pm\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$. The off-diagonal entries $O(\varrho_{\mu\nu}^3)$ with $\varrho_{\mu\nu}=O(\dlat^{-1/2})$ of random sign have operator norm $O(\sqrt\nsamp\,\dlat^{-3/2})$ (semicircle), so per-eigenvalue attachment $\Lambda_\mu\leftrightarrow\mu$ requires $\nsamp\ll\dlat^3r_t^2a_*^4$; otherwise the attachment holds only in the quantile sense of Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}. The $\dlat$ data-side modes sit above the sample modes because $\gamma a_1^2\lambda_i(M_t)/\dlat\gg r_ta_*^2/\nsamp$ (synthetic $\dlat=200$, $t=0.01$: noise-dim $8\times10^{-4}$ vs.\ $7.7\times10^{-6}$), so for the single-draw object the $\nsamp$ nonzero directions split into $\dlat$ data-side and $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample modes (F1); for the noise-averaged object the sample modes are the $\nsamp-\dlat$ localised ones, followed by the cloud without a rank cliff.
\end{enumerate}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(1) Condition on $x$; row $j$ of $W$ contributes $\pwidth^{-1}[\tanh(\sqrt{q_x}z_j)-a_1(q_x)\sqrt{q_x}z_j]^2$ with $z_j=w_j^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat q_x}\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$ i.i.d.\ over $j$; apply Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(ii) and the LLN over $\pwidth$ rows. Over $\xi$: $\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t\|\eta\|^2/\dlat+2e^{-t}\sqrt{\Delta_t}x^{\mu\top}\eta/\dlat$ concentrates at $q_\mu$ with $O(\dlat^{-1/2})$ fluctuations and $a_*^2$ is smooth. (2) Mehler's formula as in Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(v) applied to $(z,z')=(w^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat q_x},w^\top y/\sqrt{\dlat q_y})$. For two draws $\xi,\xi'$ of the same point, $x_t(\xi)^\top x_t(\xi')/\dlat=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+O(\dlat^{-1/2})$, so $\varrho=(q_\mu-\Delta_t)/q_\mu=\varrho_\mu$ up to $O(\dlat^{-1/2})$; then $\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2=\E_{\xi,\xi'}\rho(x_t(\xi))^\top\rho(x_t(\xi'))$ and take $\E_W$. (3) The decomposition $\E[\rho\rho^\top]=\bar\rho\bar\rho^\top+\mathrm{Cov}(\rho)$ is the variance identity. The nonzero eigenvalues of $\Umat_{\rm loc}$ are those of the $\nsamp\times\nsamp$ Gram $\bar G/\nsamp$, $\bar G_{\mu\nu}=\bar\rho_\mu^\top\bar\rho_\nu$; by (2), $\bar G=\mathrm{diag}(r_t(q_\mu)a_*(q_\mu)^2)+E$ with $|E_{\mu\nu}|=O(|\varrho_{\mu\nu}|^3)$ where now $\varrho_{\mu\nu}=e^{-2t}x^{\mu\top}x^\nu/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$ is the noise-averaged overlap. The cloud trace is $\E_\xi\|\rho_\mu\|^2-\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2$ by (1)--(2); its rank bound follows from $\mathrm{Cov}_\xi$ estimated from $n_\xi$ draws having rank $\le n_\xi-1$ per point. Single draw: $\Umat^{(1)}_*=\Phi_*\Phi_*^\top/\nsamp$ with $\Phi_*\in\R^{\pwidth\times\nsamp}$, so the Gram argument applies with $\varrho_\mu=1$. (4) Fixed-$W$ entry fluctuations of $\bar G$ are $O(\pwidth^{-1/2})$ and approximately independent, which is the Marchenko--Pastur setting with aspect ratio $\nsamp/\pwidth$ \citep{marchenko1967}; the semicircle bound is the standard operator-norm estimate for a symmetric matrix with independent mean-zero entries of size $\dlat^{-3/2}$; the separation numbers are $a_1(q_d)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ versus $r_ta_*^2/\nsamp$ with F3's coefficients. \emph{Why sketched.} The attachment is a first-order perturbation claim: on the synthetic data at $\dlat=200$ ($a_*^2=0.0047$) one has $\dlat^3a_*^4=173<\nsamp=500$, so per-eigenvalue attachment fails there and only the quantile statement survives; on real latents ($\dlat=200$, $a_*^2=0.036$, $r_t=0.57$) $\dlat^3r_t^2a_*^4\approx3.4\times10^3>\nsamp=10^3$, marginally sufficient. Same-cluster pairs with $|\varrho_{\mu\nu}|=O(1)$ violate the hypothesis (Remark~\ref{rem:collective-pred}), and for $\pwidth<\nsamp$ (the $\pwidth=64\dlat$ sweep at $\dlat\le7$) the Gram is rank-deficient, there are only $\pwidth-\dlat$ sample modes with mean $a_*^2/\pwidth$, and no rank-null block. \emph{Numerical check} (repository spectra, $\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $t=0.01$, $n_\xi=50$, seed 42, code units $=\pwidth\times$theory): at $\dlat=200$ the sample-bulk median is $0.093$ against $\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\nsamp=0.098$ (the single-draw formula gives $0.119$); the sample-bulk mass (indices $\dlat..\nsamp-1$) is $28.9$ against $(\nsamp-\dlat)\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\nsamp=29.4$; the mass beyond index $\nsamp$ is $11.7$ against the cloud prediction $(1-r_t)\pwidth a_*^2=10.8$; and $\lambda_{\nsamp-1}/\lambda_{\nsamp}=1.7$ (a shoulder, not a cliff). At $\dlat=150,100$ the medians are $0.077,0.059$ against $0.084,0.070$; below $\dlat\approx60$ the per-sample prediction fails progressively ($0.032$ vs $0.057$ at $\dlat=40$; $0.0018$ vs $0.076$ at $\dlat=5$) because the mass migrates into a few collective modes (Remark~\ref{rem:collective-pred}).
```

### `rem:single-vs-averaged-pred` (remark, **proved**)

```latex
(Which object each statement is about.) F1's machine-precision rank cliff at index $\nsamp$ is a property of the single-draw matrix $\Umat^{(1)}$ ($\mathrm{rank}\le\nsamp$). The repository's saved spectra are computed with $n_\xi=50$ noise draws (\texttt{compute\_U}, \texttt{n\_noise\_samples=50}), for which $\mathrm{rank}\le\min\{\pwidth,n_\xi\nsamp\}=\pwidth$: all $\pwidth$ eigenvalues exceed $10^{-8}$ at every $\dlat$, and the ratio $\lambda_{\nsamp-1}/\lambda_{\nsamp}$ is $1.01$ at $\dlat=10,20$ and $1.7$ at $\dlat=100,200$, i.e.\ a soft shoulder whose position and size are those of the cloud of Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}(3). The training loss (fresh noise every step) is the noise-averaged object, so the eigenvalue that sets a training point's clock is $r_t(q_\mu)a_*(q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$, not $a_*(q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$; the two differ by $r_t=0.82$--$0.97$ on the synthetic sweep ($t=0.01$) and by $r_t=0.09$--$0.76$ at $t=0.1$, the diffusion time of the real-data spectra. The cloud modes carry the part of the residual feature that is not reproducible across noise draws; they are essentially never absorbed within the training horizon and contribute to the irreducible loss.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Rank counts are immediate; the numbers are read from \texttt{sigma\_noise\_0.5/exp2\_rfnn/raw\_data/di5\_d*\_n500\_s42/eigenvalues\_pre.npy}.
```

### `def:two-source-predictor-pred` (definition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Gaussian-equivalence structure of the first layer (prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred) and p > n; memorised generated samples are distributed uniformly over the memorisable training points, so a fraction of generated samples equals a fraction of training points; condensation time t_c supplied by Piece 5; the decoder approximately preserves nearest-neighbour ordering: D_{t_c} is defined with latent-space d_NN whereas the memorisation statistic is computed in pixel space after decoding (F10); odd first-layer activation (else keep k=0,2 Hermite terms in g); lazy regime: the first layer stays at its initialisation statistics

```latex
(Two-source memorised-fraction predictor.) Given frozen-encoder latents $\{z^\mu\}_{\mu\le\nsamp}\subset\R^{\dlat}$, diffusion time $t$, a first layer with odd activation $\sigma$ and pre-activation gain $\gamma=\dlat\sigma_w^2$ read from the initialisation, define $q_\mu=\gamma(e^{-2t}\|z^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t)$, $\varrho_\mu=1-\gamma\Delta_t/q_\mu$, $\Lambda_\mu=g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)/\nsamp=r_t(q_\mu)a_*(q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$ with $g,a_*$ computed for $\sigma$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(v), and $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)=\min_{\nu\ne\mu}\|z^\mu-z^\nu\|$. Let $\mathcal D_{t_c}=\{\mu:\ d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\ge2\Delta_{t_c}\log\nsamp\}$ be the set of training points whose empirical-score weight condenses at the sampler's memorisation time $t_c$ (Piece~5; \citealp{biroli2024dynamical}). The predicted memorised fraction at step $s$ is
\[ \widehat F(s)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu=1}^{\nsamp}\mathbf 1[\mu\in\mathcal D_{t_c}]\;\mathbf 1\!\big[\Lambda_\mu s\ge C\big],\qquad C:=\frac{\log(1/(1-\theta))}{\eta}, \]
optionally smoothed by replacing the second indicator with $\Phi\big(\log(\Lambda_\mu s/C)/\varsigma\big)$, $\varsigma=\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$ (the Marchenko--Pastur relative standard deviation; not fitted). $\theta$ is the absorbed-amplitude threshold, i.e.\ by Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred} the fraction of the sample-specific score output at $z^\mu$ that must be produced for the reverse dynamics to return $z^\mu$; $\eta$ is the learning rate; only the ratio $C$ enters. If the first layer also receives a fixed-norm time embedding $e(t)\in\R^{m_e}$ with fan-in-scaled init $\sigma_w^2=c/(\dlat+m_e)$, the pre-activation variance is instead $q_\mu=c\,(e^{-2t}\|z^\mu\|^2+\dlat\Delta_t+\|e(t)\|^2)/(\dlat+m_e)$ and $\varrho_\mu=(q_\mu-c\Delta_t\dlat/(\dlat+m_e))/q_\mu$ (the embedding is reproducible across noise draws); the real-data numbers quoted in this piece use the \emph{surrogate} $\sigma=\tanh$, $\gamma=1$, $m_e=0$, for which $q_\mu$ is the per-sample analogue of the released $\lambda_{\rm mean}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Definition; the form of $\Lambda_\mu$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}(3), the clock is the amplitude fraction of Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred}, and $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ is the condensation criterion of \citet{biroli2024dynamical} applied per training point. The two sources are combined as an intersection because a training point is returned by the sampler only if the model has learned its sample-specific component (learning side) \emph{and} that component wins the softmax competition along the reverse trajectory (dynamics side, F15); if the two conditions are approximately independent across $\mu$, $\widehat F(s)\approx(|\mathcal D_{t_c}|/\nsamp)\cdot\frac1\nsamp\#\{\mu:\Lambda_\mu s\ge C\}$. The time-embedding formula is the pre-activation variance of $w^\top[x_t;e(t)]$ with i.i.d.\ weights.
```

### `cor:shape-from-qmu-pred` (corollary, **proved**)

Hypotheses: g(q, 1 - Delta_t/q) monotone in q on the range of q_mu (verified numerically for t in {0.01, 0.1}, q in [0.3, 3]); collapsed coordinates carry exactly zero data variance, so ||z||^2 is unchanged when they are dropped

```latex
Under Definition~\ref{def:two-source-predictor-pred} with $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ fixed: (i) the training points in $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ are memorised in decreasing order of $q_\mu$, i.e.\ of latent norm $\|z^\mu\|$; (ii) for $f\le|\mathcal D_{t_c}|/\nsamp$ the step at which $\widehat F$ reaches level $f$ is $s_f=C\nsamp/g(q_{(f)},\varrho_{(f)})$ where $q_{(f)}$ is the upper $(f\nsamp/|\mathcal D_{t_c}|)$-quantile of $\{q_\mu:\mu\in\mathcal D_{t_c}\}$, so the shape of the fraction curve in $\log s$ is the empirical distribution of $\log g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)$ over memorisable points and needs no fitted response function; (iii) the sample-bulk clock depends on $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ only through the pre-activation variance $q_\mu$ (hence through $q_d=\mathrm{tr}M_t/\dlat$ and the per-sample norms), not through individual eigenvalues. In particular coordinates with zero data variance ($\lambda_i(M_t)=\Delta_t$; posterior collapse, F9) enter the clock \emph{only} through $q_\mu$, via their share of $\Delta_t$ and via the $1/\dlat$ fan-in normalisation of the first layer---the same $q$-dilution channel by which the synthetic $\signoise\to0$ excess coordinates delay memorisation. Quantitatively, on CelebA at $\dlat=200$ ($q_d=1.247$, effective rank $\approx88$), dropping the collapsed coordinates at fixed gain raises $q_d$ to $2.60$ and multiplies $g$ by $2.7$ ($a_*^2$ alone: $2.1$), i.e.\ predicts memorisation $\approx2.7\times$ \emph{earlier}; dropping them while rescaling the gain so that $q_\mu$ is unchanged leaves the predicted curve unchanged.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(i)--(ii): $g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)$ is increasing in $q$ on the relevant range (Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(v)), so $\Lambda_\mu s\ge C$ is a threshold on $q_\mu$; invert within $\mathcal D_{t_c}$. (iii): $\Lambda_\mu$ is a function of $q_\mu$ alone; the numbers use $q'=e^{-2t}\,\overline{\|z\|^2}/d_{\rm eff}+\Delta_t$ with $\overline{\|z\|^2}/\dlat=1.302$ from the released CSV and $d_{\rm eff}=88$.
```

### `rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred` (remark, **proved**)

```latex
(What a clock built from $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ measures.) By Propositions~\ref{prop:data-side-rates-pred}--\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred} the $\dlat$ eigenvalues of $M_t$ label the \emph{data-side} modes of $\Umat$ (signal and noise-dimension bulks), whose targets are population score components---the generalising part of the model; the $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample modes that carry the sample-specific components and hence memorisation (F1, F5) are absent from $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$. The released $P_d(s)$ therefore measures the absorption of the generalising modes and tracks memorisation only insofar as their clock co-varies with the sample-mode clock, which by Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}(iii) happens through $q_d$. The two clocks couple to posterior collapse through different channels: the sample-mode clock through $q$-dilution (the channel of the paper's synthetic $\signoise\to0$ buffer), the released clock through the collapsed eigenvalues $\lambda_i=\Delta_t=0.181$ themselves ($\dlat\ge140$ CelebA, $\ge160$ CIFAR-10, excess $<2\times10^{-3}$), which are its slowest modes and dominate the late-time growth of $P_d(s)$ although their score target is the trivial $-x_i/\Delta_t$. CelebA's effective rank saturates ($89.5,\,90.3,\,87.9$ at $\dlat=160,180,200$) while memorisation keeps falling; a clock reading $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ reproduces this only through the growing number of collapsed coordinates, i.e.\ through a channel that carries no data-side information, whereas the sample-mode clock reproduces it through $q_d$ ($1.83\to1.25$), which the collapsed coordinates dilute.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Immediate from the two propositions, Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}(iii) and F9.
```

### `rem:param-count-pred` (remark, **proved**)

```latex
(Honest parameter count and fit sets.) Released predictor (F11; \texttt{calibrate()} in \texttt{make\_*\_spectral\_mem\_predictor\_figures.py}): one global $\kappa$ on a 121-point $\log_{10}$ grid over $[-12,-4]$ (grid step $10^{1/15}=1.17\times$) plus six monotone (PAVA) threshold levels $\theta_q$, $q\in\{1,5,10,25,50,75\}\%$, i.e.\ 7 fitted numbers per dataset, fitted on \emph{all uncensored} $(\dlat,q)$ rows including pre-threshold dims ($\dlat=20,30$ CelebA; $40$ CIFAR-10) with no hold-out; the main text's 3-parameter $(\kappa,a,b)$ sigmoid is not what the code fits. Derived predictor (Definition~\ref{def:two-source-predictor-pred}): $\gamma$ from the initialisation (0 fitted), $\varsigma=\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$ (0), $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ from Piece~5 (0 if $t_c$ is the sampler's condensation time, else 1), and one scalar $C$ for timing (1 fitted; $\theta$ and $\eta$ are degenerate for timing and $\eta$ is known, so the single unknown is $\theta$). Total $1(+1)$ versus 7. All one-constant fits in this piece use $C=\exp\{\mathrm{median}_{\dlat}\log(\Lambda_{(f)}\hat s_f)\}$ on declared dim sets: CelebA $\dlat\ge50$ (11 dims) or $\ge70$ (the paper's $d_{\rm ID}(0.95)$; 9 dims) or all uncensored $\ge20$ (14); CIFAR-10 $\dlat\ge60$ (11), $\ge100$ (9) or $\ge140$ ($d_{\rm ID}$; 7 at $5\%$, 4 at $25\%$). The released residuals quoted alongside are recomputed on the same sets.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Count; code inspection.
```

### `rem:collective-pred` (remark, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: clustered data with k centres of norm s in a d_int-dimensional subspace; d_lat small enough that e^{-2t} s^2/(d_lat q_d) = O(1); p = 64 d_lat sweep: p < n at d_lat <= 7, so the d_lat = 5 point is outside prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred for two reasons

```latex
(Conjecture, numerically supported: the top of the sample bulk at small $\dlat$ is cluster-collective, not sample-specific.) All numbers are code units ($\pwidth\times$theory), $\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $t=0.01$, seed 42. \emph{What the per-sample picture predicts.} The sample-bulk median is $\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\min\{\nsamp,\pwidth\}$ and the top edge $\pwidth r_ta_*^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2/\nsamp$ for $\pwidth>\nsamp$: the median should rise $0.054\to0.098$ ($1.8\times$) and the top edge fall $0.193\to0.141$ ($1.4\times$) over $\dlat=10\to200$ ($\dlat=5$ has $\pwidth=320<\nsamp$ and is outside Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}). \emph{What is observed.} The median rises $0.0039\to0.093$ ($24\times$; $0.0018$ at $\dlat=5$) and the largest sample-bulk eigenvalue falls $0.930\to0.173$ ($5.4\times$; $0.957$ at $\dlat=5$); the top ten sample-bulk modes carry $47\%,\,35\%,\,22\%,\,6.3\%,\,5.4\%$ of the sample-bulk mass at $\dlat=5,10,20,100,200$ (flat reference $10/300=3.3\%$), and $\lambda_{\rm top}/\lambda_{\rm median}=532,\,236,\,42,\,3.4,\,1.9$. So at $\dlat\ge100$ the per-sample+MP picture is quantitatively right, while at small $\dlat$ a number of modes of order $k=10$ absorbs half the sample-bulk mass. The ratio $\lambda^{\rm signal}_{\min}/\lambda^{\rm sample}_{\rm top}$ grows $21\to427$ ($20\times$; F5 reports $31\to576$ on its onset statistic). \emph{Conjectured mechanism.} Same-cluster points have noise-averaged overlap $\varrho_c=e^{-2t}s^2/(\dlat q_d)$ ($0.64,\,0.58,\,0.50,\,0.23,\,0.135$ at $\dlat=5,10,20,100,200$), so the residual Gram $\bar G$ acquires, per cluster, a block of $\nsamp/k$ entries $g(q_d,\varrho_c)$; first-order perturbation gives $k$ collective eigenvalues $\approx\pwidth[r_ta_*^2+(\nsamp/k-1)g(q_d,\varrho_c)]/\nsamp=0.53,\,0.50,\,0.36,\,0.11,\,0.11$, which reproduces the observed $5.4\times$ trend from $\dlat=10$ within a uniform factor $1.6$--$1.9$ (cross-cluster overlaps, also $O(1)$ at $\dlat=5$ where $|m_c^\top m_{c'}|\sim s^2/\sqrt{\dint}$, and the MP edge are omitted). These modes learn the nonlinear shape of a cluster, a \emph{generalising} quantity; if confirmed, the onset statistic of F5 at small $\dlat$ is not a memorisation clock and the buffer claim must be re-based on the localised part of the sample bulk. \emph{Test.} Inverse participation ratio of the top $20$ sample-bulk eigenvectors of $\Umat$ in the training-point basis ($\mathrm{IPR}\approx1$: sample-specific; $\approx k/\nsamp$: collective); requires re-running the RFNN sweep with eigenvectors (or $W$) saved.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Not proved. The block estimate is first-order perturbation of Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}(3) in the regime where same-cluster overlaps are $O(1)$, i.e.\ exactly where that proposition's hypotheses fail; the observed mass concentration in $\approx k$ modes is consistent with it but the IPR test has not been run.
```

## Predictions

- **Training points are memorised in decreasing order of latent norm: at any fixed step the memorised set is enriched in large-||z^mu|| points, ranked by g(q_mu, rho_mu) = r_t(q_mu) a_*(q_mu)^2 (Corollary cor:shape-from-qmu-pred(i)).**  
  test: Requires a new run: log nn1_idx (returned by remote_metrics.nn_ratio_memorization but not currently written). For each seed and checkpoint, compare ||z^mu||^2/d of the NN1 training latents of memorised samples with the full training distribution (AUC of a norm-ranked ROC). Prediction: AUC > 0.5 early in training, shrinking toward 0.5 as the fraction saturates.  
  support: Nothing in the repo yet; the CSV columns z_normsq_over_d_mean/std (cv 0.22-0.48) show the required spread exists.

- **Collapsed latent coordinates (lambda_i(M_t) = Delta_t within 2e-3, d >= 140 CelebA / >= 160 CIFAR-10) enter the memorisation clock only through the first-layer pre-activation variance q_mu. Dropping them at FIXED input gain raises q_d from 1.25 to 2.60 at CelebA d=200 and should make memorisation ~2.7x faster (factor g(q')/g(q) = 2.7; a_*^2 alone 2.1x); dropping them while rescaling the gain so that q_mu is unchanged should leave the fraction curve unchanged. The released P_d(s) moves in both cases because it loses its slowest modes.**  
  test: Requires re-encoding and new training runs: train the diffusion model on latents with collapsed coordinates removed, (a) with default init and (b) with first-layer weight variance rescaled by d_eff/d; compare fraction curves at matched steps. Recompute the released P_d(s) with and without those eigenvalues.  
  support: F9 and the released CSVs: lambda_min = Delta_t for the stated dims; CelebA effective rank saturates (89.5, 90.3, 87.9 at d=160,180,200) while memorisation keeps falling and q_d keeps falling (1.83 -> 1.25).

- **The single-constant sample-mode clock tau_f(d) = C n / g(q_d, 1 - Delta_t/q_d) with q_d = lambda_mean(d) from the released spectral-feature CSVs reproduces the observed hitting times on the declared dim sets with leave-one-d-out log-error comparable to the released 7-parameter in-sample fit, but under-predicts the d-slope: it accounts for 1.78x of the observed 3.9x (CelebA d=50->200, 5%), 1.74x of 2.9x (d=100->200) and 1.85x of 2.1x (CIFAR d=100->240; d=260 has sd 1.4M steps and is not used as an anchor).**  
  test: Testable now from the tau tables: fit C on all but one d, predict its 5% and 25% hitting times; report mean |log residual|. Then add the detection-drift correction (mean NN-ratio vs d, F10) and the deployed-network q_mu (time embedding, GELU) and check whether the residual slope disappears.  
  support: Computed here from the repo's CSVs (median-C rule). CelebA 5%, d>=50: g-clock 0.309 in-sample / 0.407 LOO, a_*^2-clock 0.347/0.449, tau ∝ d 0.125/0.125, released 0.339 (7 params, no hold-out); CelebA 25%, d>=50: 0.207/0.262, 0.246/0.308, 0.083/0.095, released 0.169; CelebA 5%, d>=70: 0.277/0.384 vs released 0.311; CelebA 5% all uncensored d>=20: 0.346/0.417 vs released 0.365 and tau ∝ d 0.407/0.477 (worst model there). CIFAR 5%, d>=60: 0.130/0.151 vs released 0.146 (tau ∝ d 0.155/0.198); d>=100: 0.120/0.145 vs 0.116; d>=140: 0.112/0.136 vs 0.106; CIFAR 25%, d>=60: 0.168/0.171 vs 0.143.

- **The width of the fraction curve in log(step) set by the learning side alone is too narrow: at CelebA d=200 the spread of g(q_mu) over training points predicts a span of 1.46x in steps between the levels that correspond to 5% and 25% absolute memorisation (11% and 56% of the 0.448 asymptote), against the observed tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14; a second source (the d_NN quantile, Piece 5) is required.**  
  test: Requires the per-sample latents (not in the repo; only mean/std columns are): compute the empirical per-d quantile ratios of g(q_mu) and compare with the observed ratios of iso-memorisation times at levels matched to the asymptote; attribute the residual width to the spread of d_NN(mu)^2/(2 log n).  
  support: Gaussian-moment estimate from the CSV mean 1.302 and sd 0.2905 of ||z||^2/d at CelebA d=200: 10-90 quantile ratio of a_*^2 = 1.81 (of g: 2.36); matched-level (11%-56%) ratio 1.31 (a_*^2) / 1.46 (g); observed tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14 (CelebA d=200), 3.0 (CIFAR d=200).

- **Data-side rate check (not a memorisation prediction): on the synthetic RFNN the data-side eigenvalues of U scale as gamma a_1(q_d)^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d_lat, i.e. 64 a_1(q_d)^2 lambda_i(M_t) in code units on the p = 64 d_lat sweep, so a per-d refit of the released kappa on an RFNN would scale as a_1(q_d)^2 (not as a constant, and not as 1/d_lat in code units).**  
  test: Compare the measured signal and noise-dim bulk medians of the saved RFNN spectra with 64 a_1(q_d)^2 alpha_t^2 and 64 a_1(q_d)^2 beta_t^2 across d_lat; regress log(median) on log a_1(q_d)^2.  
  support: F3: a_1(q)^2 rises 0.180 -> 0.625 over d_lat 5 -> 200 and the measured signal/noise-dim medians (29.7 -> 99.8; 5.22 -> 9.22) follow a_1(q)^2 alpha_t^2 psi_p / a_1^2 beta_t^2 psi_p (31.9 -> 110.6; 4.76 -> 10.60) to within 10-15%.

- **The top sample-bulk eigenvectors of U at small d_lat on the synthetic data are delocalised over same-cluster training points (collective), becoming localised on single points as d_lat grows; the number of collective modes is ~k = 10 and their eigenvalue is ~p[r_t a_*^2 + (n/k - 1) g(q_d, rho_c)]/n within a factor 2 (Remark rem:collective-pred).**  
  test: Requires re-running experiment_v2_rfnn.py with U eigenvectors (or W) saved: compute the training-point-basis IPR of the top 20 sample-bulk eigenvectors at d_lat in {5,10,20,50,100,200}.  
  support: From the saved spectra (code units, t=0.01, seed 42): the top 10 sample-bulk modes carry 47% / 35% / 22% / 6.3% / 5.4% of the sample-bulk mass at d=5/10/20/100/200 (flat reference 3.3%); top/median = 532 / 236 / 42 / 3.4 / 1.9; the block estimate 0.50 -> 0.11 (d=10 -> 200) reproduces the observed 0.93 -> 0.17 trend within a uniform factor 1.6-1.9, whereas the per-sample+MP top edge predicts 0.19 -> 0.14.

- **Cross-dataset transfer at the one-constant level currently FAILS: with identical architecture and optimiser settings on both datasets (bigmlp, SGD lr 1e-3, momentum 0.8 per the run READMEs), so that no eta/width rescaling applies, the fitted C differs by 2.0x (g-clock, 5%: CelebA 14.5 vs CIFAR-10 29.1; a_*^2-clock 21.5 vs 49.9, 2.3x) and 1.6x at 25% (42.5 vs 69.7). An unmodelled dataset-dependent factor of order 2 (candidates: |D_{t_c}|/n, decoder distortion of d_NN, the deployed first layer's q_mu) is required.**  
  test: Fit C on CelebA, predict CIFAR-10 hitting times, report the systematic log offset (log 2.0 = 0.69); then test whether |D_{t_c}|/n computed from the two latent sets (Piece 5) accounts for it.  
  support: The fitted C values above, from the released tau tables and spectral-feature CSVs. The released kappa values (1.36e-6 vs 1.0e-6) are not evidence of transfer: kappa sits on a grid of step 1.17x and is jointly fit with six theta levels.

- **Recomputing the synthetic U with a single noise draw (n_noise_samples = 1) restores a machine-precision rank cliff at index n and raises the sample-bulk median by 1/r_t(q_d) relative to the 50-draw spectra (1.22x at d=200, 1.08x at d=20, t=0.01); increasing n_noise_samples beyond 50 leaves the localised modes unchanged and only fills in the cloud beyond index n toward total mass (1 - r_t) p a_*^2.**  
  test: Cheap: call compute_U with n_noise_samples in {1, 50, 500} on the saved synthetic data at d_lat in {20, 200}; compare the index-n cliff, the sample-bulk median and the mass beyond index n with p r_t a_*^2 / n and (1 - r_t) p a_*^2.  
  support: 50-draw spectra at d=200: median 0.093 vs p r_t a_*^2/n = 0.098 (single-draw formula 0.119); mass beyond index n 11.7 vs (1 - r_t) p a_*^2 = 10.8; lambda_{n-1}/lambda_n = 1.7 (no cliff). The math referee's single-draw simulation (d=50, n=200, p=3200): cliff at n, median 5.1e-5 vs a_*^2/n = 6.2e-5.

## Empirical anchor
All numbers are from the repository (clean figures/{celeba,cifar10}_spectral_mem_predictor/*_spectral_features_primary_t.csv and *_spectral_tau_table.csv at t=0.1, n=1000; sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn/raw_data/di5_d*_n500_s42/eigenvalues_pre.npy with config t_fixed=0.01, p_ratio=64, n_noise_samples=50, lr auto = 0.01 d/Delta_t) or from the established facts. (1) Gaussian-equivalence coefficients (tanh, 120-node Gauss-Hermite): a_1^2 = 0.623/0.367/0.320/0.246/0.180 and a_*^2 = 0.0047/0.0274/0.0359/0.0544/0.0784 at q = 0.33/1/1.247/1.834/2.76 (matches F3's 0.180 -> 0.625). Noise-averaged fraction r_t(q) = g(q, 1 - Delta_t/q)/a_*^2 at t=0.1: 0.086/0.505/0.538/0.574/0.668/0.675/0.755 at q = 0.33/1/1.108/1.247/1.783/1.834/2.76; at t=0.01 (the synthetic RFNN sweep): 0.969 (q=2.764, d=5) -> 0.821 (q=0.327, d=200). (2) Real latents: q_d = lambda_mean = e^{-2t} z_normsq_over_d_mean + Delta_t exactly: CelebA 2.575 (d=10), 1.834 (50), 1.808 (100), 1.247 (200); CIFAR 2.271 (20), 1.783 (100), 1.192 (240), 1.108 (260). cv of ||z||^2/d: CelebA 0.48 -> 0.22, CIFAR 0.35 -> 0.17. lambda_min = Delta_t = 0.1813 within 2e-4 for CelebA d>=140 (0.18145) and within 2e-3 for CIFAR d>=160 (0.18327). CelebA effective rank 89.5/90.3/87.9 at d=160/180/200. (3) Real-data slopes: g-clock ratio 1.78x (CelebA 50->200; a_*^2 alone 1.51x) vs observed tau_5 300k -> 1160k = 3.87x; 1.74x (100->200) vs 2.9x; CIFAR 1.85x (100->240) vs 720k -> 1500k = 2.08x; CIFAR 100->260: 2.11x vs 3.5x but tau_5(260) = 2520k has sd 1392k. (4) One-constant fits (median-C rule; mean|log residual| in-sample / LOO): CelebA 5% d>=50 (11 dims): g-clock 0.309/0.407, a_*^2 0.347/0.449, tau ∝ d 0.125/0.125, released 0.339; CelebA 25% d>=50: 0.207/0.262, 0.246/0.308, 0.083/0.095, released 0.169; CelebA 5% d>=70 (9): 0.277/0.384, 0.322/0.437, 0.132/0.165, released 0.311; CelebA 5% d>=20 (14): 0.346/0.417, 0.379/0.461, 0.407/0.477, released 0.365. CIFAR 5% d>=60 (11): 0.130/0.151, 0.161/0.176, 0.155/0.198, released 0.146; CIFAR 25% d>=60 (8): 0.168/0.171, 0.158/0.165, 0.272/0.274, released 0.143; CIFAR 5% d>=100 (9): 0.120/0.145, 0.122/0.132, 0.138/0.167, released 0.116; d>=140 (7): 0.112/0.136, 0.113/0.127, 0.123/0.133, released 0.106. Fitted C (g-clock, 5%): CelebA 14.5 vs CIFAR 29.1 (2.0x); 25%: 42.5 vs 69.7 (1.6x). (5) Width at CelebA d=200 (Gaussian-moment estimate from mean 1.302, sd 0.2905): 10-90 ratio a_*^2 1.81 / g 2.36; matched to the 0.448 asymptote (11%/56%): 1.31 / 1.46; observed tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14. (6) Collapsed-coordinate test: CelebA d=200 q_d 1.247 -> 2.60 at d_eff = 88: g ratio 2.70 (a_*^2 2.08). (7) Deployed first layer (nn.Linear(d+64, 1024) + GELU on [x_t; 32 sin + 32 cos], PyTorch default init var 1/(3 fan_in)): q_mu ≈ (e^{-2t}||z||^2 + d Delta_t + 32)/(3(d+64)) = 0.36 (CelebA d=50), 0.43 (100), 0.36 (200); 0.43 (CIFAR 100), 0.33 (260) -- nearly d-independent; all real-data numbers in this piece use the tanh / gamma=1 / no-embedding surrogate. (8) Synthetic spectra, code units: sample-bulk median 0.0018/0.0039/0.0157/0.032/0.043/0.052/0.059/0.077/0.093 at d = 5/10/20/40/60/80/100/150/200 vs p r_t a_*^2/min(n,p) = 0.076/0.054/0.056/0.057/0.060/0.065/0.070/0.084/0.098; largest sample-bulk eigenvalue 0.957/0.930/0.667/0.374/0.287/0.225/0.198/0.169/0.173; top-10 mass share 47%/35%/22%/-/-/-/6.3%/-/5.4%; lambda_min^signal 20.3 (d=5) -> 73.9 (d=200); lambda_{n-1}/lambda_n = 1.01 (d=10,20), 1.7 (d=100,200); at d=200 sample-bulk mass 28.9 vs 29.4 predicted, mass beyond n 11.7 vs 10.8, all p eigenvalues > 1e-8 at every d. Semicircle condition d^3 a_*^4: synthetic d=200 173 (< n=500, fails); CelebA d=200 1.0e4 (r_t^2-weighted 3.4e3 > 1000); CIFAR d=260 1.7e4. MP numbers for p = 64 d, n=500: sqrt(n/p) = 1.25 (d=5, p<n), 0.198 (d=200); (1+sqrt(n/p))^2 = 1.43 (d=200).

## Open gaps
(1) [Referee correction adopted, with one amendment] The noise-averaged eigenvalue r_t(q_mu) a_*(q_mu)^2/n replaces a_*(q_mu)^2/n throughout (math referee, major). The referee's 'corrected synthetic slope 145x' is however wrong for the repository's own sweep: the synthetic RFNN spectra are computed at t_fixed = 0.01 (config.json), not t = 0.1, and at t = 0.01 r_t only ranges 0.97 -> 0.82, so the theory-unit slope moves from 16.5x to 19.5x; the 145x figure applies only at t = 0.1. In code units the relevant comparison is the one in rem:collective-pred (median 1.8x predicted vs 24x observed from d=10; top edge 1.4x predicted vs 5.4x observed), where the per-sample clock explains essentially none of the small-d behaviour and the collective block carries the whole trend. (2) F5's step counts (788 -> 6413, 221x) could not be reproduced from eigenvalues_pre.npy: with the auto learning rate lr = 0.01 d/Delta_t both 1/(lr lambda_min^signal) and 1/(lr lambda_top^sample) FALL with d on the saved spectra; only the unit-free ratio lambda_min^signal/lambda_top^sample (21 -> 427, 20x; F5: 31 -> 576, 18.6x) is reproduced. The piece therefore uses eigenvalue ratios only; F5's step numbers need their convention documented. (3) F1's machine-precision cliff at index n is a single-draw statement; the saved spectra (50 draws) have full rank p with a soft shoulder at n (rem:single-vs-averaged-pred). Whichever object F1 used should be stated in Section 3. (4) Per-eigenvalue attachment needs n << d^3 r_t^2 a_*^4, which fails for the synthetic data at every d <= 200 and holds only marginally for the real latents at d >= 200; the quantile-level statement of cor:shape-from-qmu-pred is what the predictor uses and is not proved. (5) The learning-side clock explains 1.7-1.9x of the 2.1-3.9x observed real-data slowdown (was 1.5-1.7x before the r_t correction); candidate sources of the rest: the NN-ratio detection drift with d (F10), the d-dependence of D_{t_c} (Piece 5), and a d-proportional factor (a tau ∝ d baseline fits CelebA d>=50 best, 0.125, but is the worst model on all uncensored dims, 0.407). (6) The deployed real-data first layer (GELU, fan-in d+64 with a 64-dimensional fixed-norm time embedding, PyTorch default init) has q_mu ≈ 0.36 nearly independent of d, and GELU is not odd (k=0,2 Hermite terms, rank-one spike). Applying the recipe literally to the deployed network would therefore predict almost no d-dependence from the first layer; all real-data numbers here are for the tanh / gamma=1 / m_e=0 surrogate. Either the surrogate stands in for feature learning in deeper layers (outside the lazy analysis; Ba et al. 2022) or the real-data d-slope comes mainly from the dynamics side and detection drift. This is the largest open issue for the real-data reading. (7) The threshold theta now has an output-space meaning (fraction of the sample-specific score output produced), but relating it to the softmax condensation basin of the empirical score (F15) is not done. (8) The intersection of the two sources assumes approximate independence of q_mu and d_NN(mu); on real latents large-norm points may be isolated, making the sources positively correlated. Also D_{t_c} uses latent-space d_NN while memorisation is measured in pixel space (F10); 'decoder preserves NN ordering' is now an explicit hypothesis. (9) Cross-dataset transfer fails at the one-constant level (C differs by 2.0x with identical optimiser settings); the missing dataset-dependent factor is unidentified. (10) Predictions 1, 2, 4 and 6 need new runs (nn1_idx logging; re-encoded latents; per-sample latents; saved eigenvectors); predictions 3, 5, 7, 8 are testable from existing files, and 3, 7, 8 have been evaluated here. (11) The regime d -> n where the sample bulk vanishes and memorisation returns (F13), and p < n (d <= 7 on the p = 64 d sweep), are outside the predictor's validity. (12) Referee points not adopted: none of substance. The empirical referee's request to state the 'corrected median comparison 1.55x vs 1.04x' was superseded by reading the medians directly from the spectra (24x observed rise from d=10, matching the per-sample prediction only for d >= 100).
