## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{From the frozen spectrum to a memorisation-fraction predictor}
\label{sec:theory-predictor}

\paragraph{Mode clocks.} For any fixed feature map the score-matching loss is quadratic in the readout $A$, so gradient flow from $A(0)=0$ decouples in the eigenbasis of $\Umat=\sum_i\lambda_iu_iu_i^\top$: $a_i(T)=a_i^*(1-e^{-\lambda_iT})$ with $T=\eta s$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred}). Mode $i$ has an absorbed \emph{amplitude} $1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is}$, an absorbed \emph{loss} $1-e^{-2\eta\lambda_is}$ and a learned-\emph{energy} fraction $(1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is})^2$; the clock of Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}, $P_d(s)=\mathrm{mean}_i(1-e^{-\kappa\lambda_i(M_t)s})^2$, is the third with $\kappa\lambda_i(M_t)$ standing in for $\eta\lambda_i(\Umat)$. The square is not derived (Remark~\ref{rem:square-pred}), and by Proposition~\ref{prop:data-side-rates-pred} the data-side rate is $\eta\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)/\dlat$ with $q_d=\mathrm{tr}M_t/\dlat$ and $\gamma$ the first-layer gain, so $\kappa$ is not $\dlat$-independent. For a mode localised on one training point the amplitude fraction is the fraction of that point's sample-specific score \emph{output} the model produces, which gives the threshold below an absolute meaning.

\paragraph{The missing term.} $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ labels only the $\dlat$ \emph{data-side} modes of $\Umat$; the $\nsamp-\dlat$ \emph{sample} modes that carry sample-specific information are absent from it (Remark~\ref{rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred}). Gaussian equivalence resolves them per training point for the noise-averaged $\Umat$ that the training loss actually uses (Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}): with $q_\mu=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t$ and $\varrho_\mu=1-\Delta_t/q_\mu$ the overlap of two noise draws of the same point,
\begin{equation}
\Lambda_\mu=\frac{g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)}{\nsamp},\qquad g(q,\varrho)=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}\frac{c_k(q)^2}{k!}\varrho^k=r_t(q)\,a_*(q)^2,
\label{eq:Lambda-mu-pred}
\end{equation}
up to a Marchenko--Pastur spread of relative width $\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$; the remaining residual mass $(1-r_t)a_*^2$ is a diffuse cloud over up to $\pwidth$ directions that is never absorbed, and $a_1^2q_d+a_*^2=\E\tanh^2(\sqrt{q_d}z)$. On the repository's spectra at $\dlat=200$ the predicted sample-bulk median, mass and cloud mass are within $5\%$, $2\%$ and $8\%$ of the measured values. Because $g$ is increasing in $q$ and $q_d$ falls with $\dlat$ (CelebA: $1.83\to1.25$ over $\dlat=50\to200$; synthetic: $2.76\to0.33$), excess latent dimensions de-saturate the nonlinearity and shrink the reproducible sample-specific feature mass---the learning-side mechanism of delay. It depends on $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ only through $q$, which is also the channel through which collapsed VAE coordinates act, exactly as the $\signoise\to0$ buffer of Section~\ref{sec:rfnn} (Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}(iii)).

\paragraph{Two-source fraction predictor.} A training point is returned when its sample mode is absorbed ($\Lambda_\mu s\ge C$, $C=\log\frac1{1-\theta}/\eta$) \emph{and} its empirical-score weight condenses ($d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\ge2\Delta_{t_c}\log\nsamp$, Section~\ref{sec:theory-mem}):
\begin{equation}
\widehat F(s)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_{\mu}\mathbf 1\big[d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\ge2\Delta_{t_c}\log\nsamp\big]\;\mathbf 1\big[g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)\,s\ge C\nsamp\big].
\label{eq:two-source-pred}
\end{equation}
Points are memorised in decreasing order of latent norm, the curve's shape in $\log s$ is the distribution of $\log g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)$ over memorisable points, and one scalar $C$ fixes the timing of all curves (Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}), versus $1+6$ fitted numbers in Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}. On the released tables, $\tau\propto\nsamp/g(q_d,\varrho_d)$ with one constant reaches leave-one-$\dlat$-out log-errors of $0.41$ (CelebA, $\dlat\ge50$) and $0.15$ (CIFAR-10, $\dlat\ge60$) at the $5\%$ level against $0.34$ and $0.15$ for the 7-parameter in-sample fit, but explains only $1.8\times$ of the $2.1$--$3.9\times$ observed slowdown; the same constant differs by $2\times$ between datasets. Appendix~\ref{app:theory-predictor} gives the recipe, the validation protocol, the status of the surrogate first layer, and the cluster-collective modes that dominate the small-$\dlat$ synthetic sample bulk.
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Derivation of the spectral predictor and its sample-bulk term}
\label{app:theory-predictor}

Throughout, $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$ with $W_{ij}\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$, $x_t=e^{-t}x+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\eta$, $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$, $M_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E[x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}]$, $q_d=\mathrm{tr}(M_t)/\dlat$, and $\Umat=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$. The repository's code uses $\phi_{\rm code}=\tanh(x_tW^\top)$ with $W\sim\mathcal N(0,1/\dlat)$, i.e.\ $\Umat_{\rm code}=\pwidth\,\Umat$; ``code units'' below means $\pwidth\times$ theory units.

\subsection{Gaussian-equivalence coefficients}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:ge-coeff-pred}
Let $\sigma=\tanh$, $z\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$, $q>0$, $a_1(q)=\E[\sigma'(\sqrt q z)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\sigma(\sqrt q z)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$, and $\sigma(\sqrt qz)=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}}c_k(q)\mathrm{He}_k(z)/k!$. Then (i) $\E[\sigma(\sqrt qz)z]=\sqrt q\,a_1(q)$; (ii) $a_*(q)^2=\E[(\sigma(\sqrt qz)-a_1(q)\sqrt qz)^2]\ge0$; (iii) $a_1^2q=c_1^2$ and $a_*^2=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}c_k^2/k!$; (iv) $a_*^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$, increasing on $[0.05,3.5]$, with $a_*^2=0.0047,\,0.0274,\,0.0359,\,0.0544,\,0.0784$ and $a_1^2=0.623,\,0.367,\,0.320,\,0.246,\,0.180$ at $q=0.33,\,1,\,1.247,\,1.834,\,2.76$; (v) for jointly Gaussian $(z,z')$ with correlation $\varrho\in[0,1]$,
\[ g(q,\varrho):=\E[\sigma(\sqrt qz)\sigma(\sqrt qz')]-a_1(q)^2q\varrho=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}\frac{c_k(q)^2}{k!}\varrho^k\in[0,\varrho^3a_*(q)^2], \]
increasing in $\varrho$ with $g(q,1)=a_*^2$. With $r_t(q):=g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)/a_*(q)^2$: at $t=0.1$, $r_t=0.086,\,0.505,\,0.574,\,0.675,\,0.755$ at $q=0.33,\,1,\,1.247,\,1.834,\,2.76$; at $t=0.01$, $r_t=0.822,\,0.943,\,0.969$ at $q=0.33,\,1.247,\,2.76$. $g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)$ is increasing in $q$ on this range for both $t$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(i) Stein's lemma. (ii) Expand and use (i). (iii) $\tanh$ is odd; orthogonality with $\E\mathrm{He}_k^2=k!$. (iv) $\E\sigma^2=q-2q^2+\tfrac{17}3q^3+O(q^4)$ and $a_1^2q=q-2q^2+5q^3+O(q^4)$; the table is 120-node Gauss--Hermite quadrature and reproduces the $0.180\to0.625$ range of $a_1^2$ measured in Section~\ref{sec:rfnn}. (v) Mehler's formula $\E[\mathrm{He}_k(z)\mathrm{He}_l(z')]=\delta_{kl}k!\varrho^k$; the subtraction removes $k=1$, oddness the even terms; each term is nonnegative and increasing in $\varrho$, and $\varrho^k\le\varrho^3$. The $r_t$ values agree between two-dimensional quadrature and the Hermite series truncated at $k=39$ to four digits.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Mode clocks under gradient flow}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:mode-clocks-pred}
Let $L(A)=\frac1{2\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\xi\|A\phi(x_t^\mu)-y_\mu(x_t^\mu)\|^2$ for square-integrable targets $y_\mu$, $\Umat=\sum_i\lambda_iu_iu_i^\top$, $V=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[y_\mu\phi^\top]$, and let $\dot A=-\nabla L(A)$, $A(0)=0$, $T=\eta s$. With $a_i(T)=A(T)u_i$ and $a_i^*=Vu_i/\lambda_i$ ($\lambda_i>0$),
\[ a_i(T)=a_i^*\,(1-e^{-\lambda_iT}),\qquad L(T)-L(\infty)=\tfrac12\sum_i\lambda_i\|a_i^*\|^2e^{-2\lambda_iT}. \]
Mode $i$ thus has absorbed amplitude $1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is}$, absorbed loss $1-e^{-2\eta\lambda_is}$ and learned-energy fraction $(1-e^{-\eta\lambda_is})^2$, ordered $(1-e^{-x})^2\le1-e^{-x}\le1-e^{-2x}$. The exponent is normalisation-invariant: in code units $T=\eta_{\rm code}\pwidth s$ and $\eta\lambda s=\eta_{\rm code}\lambda_{\rm code}s$. If $u$ is a unit eigenvector localised on training point $\mu$, $u\propto\bar\rho_\mu=\E_\xi\rho(x_t^\mu)$ with eigenvalue $\Lambda_\mu=\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2/\nsamp$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}), and $\bar y_\mu$ is the sample-specific target, then at leading order $a^*=\bar y_\mu/\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$ and the model output at $x_t^\mu$ along $u$ is $\bar y_\mu(1-e^{-\Lambda_\mu T})$: the amplitude fraction is the fraction of the sample-specific score output produced at the training point.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$\nabla_AL=A\Umat-V$; project on $u_i$ and integrate. The loss identity is the quadratic expansion about $A^*=V\Umat^+$. Units: substitute $A_{\rm code}=A/\sqrt{\pwidth}$, $\Umat_{\rm code}=\pwidth\Umat$. Meaning: only the $\nu=\mu$ term of $V$ correlates with $u$ at leading order, so $Vu=\bar y_\mu\bar\rho_\mu^\top\bar\rho_\mu/(\nsamp\|\bar\rho_\mu\|)=\bar y_\mu\|\bar\rho_\mu\|/\nsamp$ and $a(T)\,u^\top\bar\phi_\mu=a^*(1-e^{-\Lambda_\mu T})\|\bar\rho_\mu\|$. For the RFNN, $L$ with fresh noise at every step is exactly the $\E_\xi$-averaged loss; full-batch gradient descent agrees with the flow to $O(\eta\lambda_{\max})$ per step.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The square is not derived]\label{rem:square-pred}
The released clock $P_d(s)=\mathrm{mean}_i(1-e^{-\kappa\lambda_i(M_t)s})^2$ is the learned-energy fraction averaged over the eigenvalues of $M_t$ rather than of $\Umat$. For a single mode the three fractions are monotone reparametrisations of $\eta\lambda_is$, so the exponent is absorbed into the threshold; it matters only through the average over modes, where the square suppresses slow modes at early times quadratically. A derivation would have to show that the NN-ratio memorisation statistic is a quadratic functional of the learned sample-specific displacement at small absorbed amplitude; we do not have one, and use the amplitude clock, which has the output-space meaning above.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Data-side rates: what $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ labels}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:data-side-rates-pred}
Let $W_{ij}\sim\mathcal N(0,\sigma_w^2)$, $\gamma:=\dlat\sigma_w^2$ ($\gamma=1$ for the RFNN), $\pwidth/\dlat\to\infty$, and assume Gaussian equivalence of the tanh random-feature kernel for the input distribution \citep{pennington2017nonlinear,benigni2021eigenvalue,hu2022universality,goldt2022gaussian}. Then the $\dlat$ data-side eigenvalues of $\Umat$ are $\lambda_i(\Umat)=\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)/\dlat\,(1+o(1))$, $i\le\dlat$, with trace $a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\gamma q_d$. The data-side amplitude clock is $1-\exp[-\eta\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)s/\dlat]$, so the released $\kappa$ equals $\eta\gamma a_1(\gamma q_d)^2/\dlat$ in theory units; in code units on the $\pwidth=64\dlat$ sweep the rate is $64\,\eta_{\rm code}a_1(q_d)^2\lambda_i(M_t)$, and the $\dlat$-dependence is carried by $a_1(q_d)^2$ alone ($0.180\to0.625$, F3).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Decompose $\tanh(w^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat})=a_1(q_x)w^\top x/\sqrt{\dlat}+r_w(x)$, $q_x=\gamma\|x\|^2/\dlat$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(i) the residual is uncorrelated with the linear part over $w$, so the linear--residual cross block vanishes at leading order. The linear block is $\sigma_w^2W'\tilde M_tW'^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ with $\tilde M_t=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu a_1(q_{x_t^\mu})^2\E x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}$; replacing $a_1(q_{x_t^\mu})$ by $a_1(\gamma q_d)$ is a heterogeneity correction of relative size $\mathrm{cv}(q_\mu)|a_1'|q/a_1$, and the nonzero spectrum of $W'\tilde M_tW'^\top/\pwidth$ is that of $\tilde M_t(W'^\top W'/\pwidth)\to\tilde M_t$. This is \citet{bonnaire2025}'s spectral law evaluated on the data-side block, and the block structure $(\pwidth,\dlat-\dint,\dint,\nsamp)$ of \citet{george2025dsm} with $\signoise>0$. Finite-$\pwidth$ corrections are the $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ Marchenko--Pastur broadening of $W'^\top W'/\pwidth$ \citep{marchenko1967}, consistent with the $<0.5\%$ edge motion under a $5.8\times$ change of $\pwidth$. Trace: $\gamma a_1^2\mathrm{tr}M_t/\dlat=a_1^2\gamma q_d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What a clock built from $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ measures]\label{rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred}
The $\dlat$ eigenvalues of $M_t$ label the signal and noise-dimension bulks of $\Umat$, whose targets are population-score components. The $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample modes, which carry the sample-specific components and hence memorisation, are absent from $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$. The released $P_d(s)$ therefore measures absorption of the generalising modes and tracks memorisation only insofar as their clock co-varies with the sample-mode clock, which happens through $q_d$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}(iii)). The two clocks couple to posterior collapse through different channels: the sample-mode clock through $q$-dilution---the channel of the synthetic $\signoise\to0$ buffer---and the released clock through the collapsed eigenvalues $\lambda_i=\Delta_t=0.181$ themselves ($\dlat\ge140$ CelebA, $\ge160$ CIFAR-10; excess $<2\times10^{-3}$), its slowest modes, which dominate the late-time growth of $P_d(s)$ although their score target is $-x_i/\Delta_t$. CelebA's effective rank saturates ($89.5,\,90.3,\,87.9$ at $\dlat=160,180,200$) while memorisation keeps falling; a clock reading $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ reproduces this only through the growing number of collapsed coordinates, a channel carrying no data-side information, whereas the sample-mode clock reproduces it through $q_d=1.83\to1.25$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Per-sample resolution of the sample bulk}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred}
In the setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:data-side-rates-pred} with $\gamma=1$ and $\pwidth>\nsamp$, let $\rho(x)=\phi(x)-\pwidth^{-1/2}a_1(q_x)Wx/\sqrt{\dlat}$, $q_\mu=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t$, $\varrho_\mu=1-\Delta_t/q_\mu$, $\bar\rho_\mu=\E_\xi\rho(x_t^\mu)$. Distinguish the single-draw $\Umat^{(1)}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top$ (F1--F2) from the noise-averaged $\Umat$ (the training loss; the $n_\xi\to\infty$ limit of \texttt{compute\_U}, which averages $n_\xi=50$ draws). Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\E_W\|\rho(x)\|^2=a_*(q_x)^2$ exactly, with relative concentration $\pwidth^{-1/2}$, and $\E_{W,\xi}\|\rho(x_t^\mu)\|^2=a_*(q_\mu)^2(1+O(\dlat^{-1}))$.
\item For $\varrho_{xy}=x^\top y/(\dlat\sqrt{q_xq_y})$: $\E_W[\rho(x)^\top\rho(y)]=\sum_{k\ \mathrm{odd}\ge3}c_k(q_x)c_k(q_y)\varrho_{xy}^k/k!=O(\varrho_{xy}^3)$; two noise draws of the same point have overlap $\varrho_\mu$, so $\E_W\|\bar\rho_\mu\|^2=g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))=r_t(q_\mu)a_*(q_\mu)^2(1+O(\dlat^{-1/2}))$.
\item $\Umat_*=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\xi[\rho\rho^\top]=\Umat_{\rm loc}+\Umat_{\rm cloud}$, $\Umat_{\rm loc}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\bar\rho_\mu\bar\rho_\mu^\top$, $\Umat_{\rm cloud}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathrm{Cov}_\xi(\rho_\mu)\succeq0$. To leading order in $\max_{\mu\ne\nu}|\varrho_{\mu\nu}|^3$ and $\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$, $\Umat_{\rm loc}$ has one eigenvalue per training point, eigenvector $\propto\bar\rho_\mu$,
\[ \Lambda_\mu=\frac{r_t(q_\mu)\,a_*(q_\mu)^2}{\nsamp}, \]
and $\Umat_{\rm cloud}$ has trace $\approx(1-r_t(q_d))a_*(q_d)^2$ over up to $\pwidth$ directions (at finite $n_\xi$, at most $\min\{\pwidth,(n_\xi-1)\nsamp\}$), i.e.\ per-direction mass $O((1-r_t)a_*^2/\pwidth)\ll\Lambda_\mu$ for $\pwidth\gg\nsamp$. For $\Umat^{(1)}$ the same argument with $\varrho_\mu\to1$ gives $\Lambda^{(1)}_\mu=a_*(\hat q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$, no cloud and $\mathrm{rank}\le\nsamp$. Trace check for both objects: $a_1^2q_d+r_ta_*^2+(1-r_t)a_*^2=\E\tanh^2(\sqrt{q_d}z)$.
\item Corrections: Marchenko--Pastur spread of the $\Lambda_\mu$ with relative standard deviation $\varsigma=\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$ and edges $(1\pm\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ ($\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}=0.198$ at $\dlat=200$; at $\dlat\le7$ on the $\pwidth=64\dlat$ sweep $\pwidth<\nsamp$ and the proposition does not apply: there are $\pwidth-\dlat$ sample modes with mean $a_*^2/\pwidth$ and no rank-null block). Off-diagonal entries $O(\varrho_{\mu\nu}^3)$, $\varrho_{\mu\nu}=O(\dlat^{-1/2})$ of random sign, have operator norm $O(\sqrt\nsamp\,\dlat^{-3/2})$, so per-eigenvalue attachment requires $\nsamp\ll\dlat^3r_t^2a_*^4$ (synthetic $\dlat=200$: $\dlat^3a_*^4=173<500$, fails; CelebA $\dlat=200$: $3.4\times10^3>10^3$, marginal); otherwise the attachment holds only in the quantile sense of Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}. The $\dlat$ data-side modes lie above the sample modes because $a_1^2\lambda_i(M_t)/\dlat\gg r_ta_*^2/\nsamp$ (synthetic $\dlat=200$: $8\times10^{-4}$ vs.\ $7.7\times10^{-6}$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(1) Condition on $x$; row $j$ contributes $\pwidth^{-1}[\tanh(\sqrt{q_x}z_j)-a_1\sqrt{q_x}z_j]^2$, $z_j$ i.i.d.\ standard Gaussian; Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(ii) and the LLN over rows. Over $\xi$, $\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ concentrates at $q_\mu$ with $O(\dlat^{-1/2})$ fluctuations. (2) Mehler, Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(v); for two draws of one point $x_t(\xi)^\top x_t(\xi')/\dlat=e^{-2t}\|x^\mu\|^2/\dlat+O(\dlat^{-1/2})$, so $\varrho=\varrho_\mu$. (3) Variance identity $\E[\rho\rho^\top]=\bar\rho\bar\rho^\top+\mathrm{Cov}(\rho)$; the nonzero eigenvalues of $\Umat_{\rm loc}$ are those of the Gram $\bar G/\nsamp$, $\bar G_{\mu\nu}=\bar\rho_\mu^\top\bar\rho_\nu=\mathrm{diag}(r_ta_*^2)+E$ with $|E_{\mu\nu}|=O(|\varrho_{\mu\nu}|^3)$, $\varrho_{\mu\nu}=e^{-2t}x^{\mu\top}x^\nu/(\dlat\sqrt{q_\mu q_\nu})$. (4) Fixed-$W$ entry fluctuations of $\bar G$ are $O(\pwidth^{-1/2})$, the Marchenko--Pastur setting with ratio $\nsamp/\pwidth$ \citep{marchenko1967}; semicircle bound for $E$; separation numbers from F3.

\emph{Numerical check} (repository spectra, $\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $t=0.01$, $n_\xi=50$, seed 42, code units). At $\dlat=200$: sample-bulk median $0.093$ vs.\ $\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\nsamp=0.098$ (single-draw formula: $0.119$); sample-bulk mass $28.9$ vs.\ $(\nsamp-\dlat)\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\nsamp=29.4$; mass beyond index $\nsamp$ $11.7$ vs.\ cloud $(1-r_t)\pwidth a_*^2=10.8$; $\lambda_{\nsamp-1}/\lambda_{\nsamp}=1.7$. At $\dlat=150,100$: medians $0.077,0.059$ vs.\ $0.084,0.070$. Below $\dlat\approx60$ the per-sample prediction fails progressively ($0.032$ vs.\ $0.057$ at $\dlat=40$; $0.0018$ vs.\ $0.076$ at $\dlat=5$): the mass migrates into a few collective modes (Remark~\ref{rem:collective-pred}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Single-draw versus noise-averaged objects]\label{rem:single-vs-averaged-pred}
F1's machine-precision rank cliff at index $\nsamp$ is a property of $\Umat^{(1)}$. The saved spectra use $n_\xi=50$ draws, so $\mathrm{rank}\le\min\{\pwidth,n_\xi\nsamp\}=\pwidth$: all $\pwidth$ eigenvalues exceed $10^{-8}$ at every $\dlat$ and $\lambda_{\nsamp-1}/\lambda_{\nsamp}$ is $1.01$ at $\dlat=10,20$ and $1.7$ at $\dlat=100,200$---a shoulder whose position and mass are those of the cloud. The training loss is the noise-averaged object, so the clock of a training point is $r_t(q_\mu)a_*(q_\mu)^2/\nsamp$; $r_t$ is $0.82$--$0.97$ on the synthetic sweep ($t=0.01$) and $0.09$--$0.76$ at $t=0.1$, the diffusion time of the real-data spectra. Cloud modes carry the part of the residual feature not reproducible across noise draws; they are essentially never absorbed within the training horizon.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The two-source fraction predictor}

\begin{definition}\label{def:two-source-predictor-pred}
Given latents $\{z^\mu\}_{\mu\le\nsamp}$, diffusion time $t$, an odd first-layer activation $\sigma$ and gain $\gamma=\dlat\sigma_w^2$ read from the initialisation, set $q_\mu=\gamma(e^{-2t}\|z^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t)$, $\varrho_\mu=1-\gamma\Delta_t/q_\mu$, $\Lambda_\mu=g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)/\nsamp$ (with $g$ for $\sigma$), $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)=\min_{\nu\ne\mu}\|z^\mu-z^\nu\|$ and $\mathcal D_{t_c}=\{\mu:d_{\rm NN}(\mu)^2\ge2\Delta_{t_c}\log\nsamp\}$ \citep{biroli2024dynamical}. The predicted memorised fraction at step $s$ is
\[ \widehat F(s)=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\mathbf 1[\mu\in\mathcal D_{t_c}]\;\mathbf 1[\Lambda_\mu s\ge C],\qquad C=\log\tfrac1{1-\theta}/\eta, \]
optionally with the second indicator replaced by $\Phi(\log(\Lambda_\mu s/C)/\varsigma)$, $\varsigma=\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}$ (not fitted). $\theta$ is the fraction of the sample-specific score output that must be produced for the sampler to return $z^\mu$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:mode-clocks-pred}). If the first layer also receives a fixed-norm time embedding $e(t)\in\R^{m_e}$ with fan-in init $\sigma_w^2=c/(\dlat+m_e)$, then $q_\mu=c(e^{-2t}\|z^\mu\|^2+\dlat\Delta_t+\|e(t)\|^2)/(\dlat+m_e)$ and $\varrho_\mu=1-c\Delta_t\dlat/((\dlat+m_e)q_\mu)$. The intersection encodes that a point is returned only if its sample-specific component is learned and wins the softmax competition of the empirical score (F15); under approximate independence $\widehat F(s)\approx(|\mathcal D_{t_c}|/\nsamp)\cdot\frac1\nsamp\#\{\mu:\Lambda_\mu s\ge C\}$. Hypotheses: $\pwidth>\nsamp$; lazy first layer; the decoder approximately preserves nearest-neighbour ordering ($\mathcal D_{t_c}$ is latent-space, the statistic is pixel-space, F10).
\end{definition}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}
With $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ fixed: (i) memorisable points are memorised in decreasing order of $q_\mu$, i.e.\ of latent norm; (ii) for $f\le|\mathcal D_{t_c}|/\nsamp$, $s_f=C\nsamp/g(q_{(f)},\varrho_{(f)})$ with $q_{(f)}$ the upper $(f\nsamp/|\mathcal D_{t_c}|)$-quantile of $\{q_\mu:\mu\in\mathcal D_{t_c}\}$, so the shape of the curve in $\log s$ is the empirical distribution of $\log g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)$; (iii) the sample-mode clock depends on $\mathrm{spec}(M_t)$ only through $q_\mu$. Collapsed coordinates ($\lambda_i(M_t)=\Delta_t$) therefore enter only through $q$-dilution---their share of $\Delta_t$ and the $1/\dlat$ fan-in normalisation---the same channel as the synthetic $\signoise\to0$ buffer. At CelebA $\dlat=200$ ($q_d=1.247$, $d_{\rm eff}\approx88$), dropping them at fixed gain raises $q_d$ to $2.60$ and $g$ by $2.7\times$ (memorisation earlier); dropping them with the gain rescaled to keep $q_\mu$ fixed leaves the curve unchanged.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$g(q,1-\Delta_t/q)$ is increasing in $q$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:ge-coeff-pred}(v)); invert the threshold within $\mathcal D_{t_c}$. (iii) $\Lambda_\mu$ is a function of $q_\mu$ alone; $q'=e^{-2t}\overline{\|z\|^2}/d_{\rm eff}+\Delta_t$ with $\overline{\|z\|^2}/\dlat=1.302$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Parameter count and fit sets]\label{rem:param-count-pred}
Released: one global $\kappa$ (121-point $\log_{10}$ grid on $[-12,-4]$, step $1.17\times$) plus six PAVA threshold levels, 7 fitted numbers per dataset, fitted on \emph{all uncensored} rows including pre-threshold dims ($\dlat=20,30$ CelebA; $40$ CIFAR-10) with no hold-out; the main text's $(\kappa,a,b)$ sigmoid is not what the code fits. Derived: $\gamma$ (0 fitted), $\varsigma$ (0), $\mathcal D_{t_c}$ (0 or 1), one scalar $C$ (the single unknown $\theta$). All one-constant fits below use $C=\exp\{\mathrm{median}_{\dlat}\log(\Lambda_{(f)}\hat s_f)\}$ on declared dim sets.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Practitioner recipe}

Inputs: frozen encoder, $\nsamp\approx10^3$ training latents $Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\dlat}$, diffusion time $t$, the diffusion network's first-layer activation, fan-in and initialisation variance (giving $\gamma$, $c$, $m_e$), learning rate $\eta$, sampler condensation time $t_c$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item $q_\mu$ and $\varrho_\mu$ from Definition~\ref{def:two-source-predictor-pred} (surrogate: $q_\mu=e^{-2t}\|z^\mu\|^2/\dlat+\Delta_t$, which is \texttt{z\_normsq\_over\_d} times $e^{-2t}$ plus $\Delta_t$).
\item $g(q,\varrho)$ by two-dimensional Gauss--Hermite quadrature ($40$--$80$ nodes per axis): $\sum_{jk}w_jw_k\sigma(\sqrt q x_j)\sigma(\sqrt q(\varrho x_j+\sqrt{1-\varrho^2}x_k))-a_1(q)^2q\varrho$; $\Lambda_\mu=g(q_\mu,\varrho_\mu)/\nsamp$.
\item $d_{\rm NN}(\mu)$ in latent space; $\mathcal D_{t_c}=\{d_{\rm NN}^2\ge2\Delta_{t_c}\log\nsamp\}$.
\item Calibrate $C$ once as the median over training dims of $\log(\Lambda_{(5\%)}\hat s_{5\%})$; report $\hat s_f$ for all levels and all $\dlat$ from the same $C$.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Validation protocol and current status}

\emph{Leave-one-$\dlat$-out.} Fit $C$ on all dims of a declared set but one; predict the held-out hitting times; score by $|\log\hat s_f/s_f|$. \emph{Cross-dataset.} Fit $C$ on CelebA, predict CIFAR-10. \emph{Collapsed-coordinate test.} Retrain on latents with collapsed coordinates removed, with and without gain rescaling (Corollary~\ref{cor:shape-from-qmu-pred}(iii)); requires new runs. \emph{Ordering test.} Memorised training points should have larger $\|z^\mu\|$ early in training; requires logging \texttt{nn1\_idx}.

\emph{Status on the released tables} (mean $|\log$ residual$|$, in-sample / leave-one-out; released 7-parameter in-sample on the same set). CelebA $5\%$, $\dlat\ge50$ (11 dims): $g$-clock $0.309/0.407$; $a_*^2$-clock $0.347/0.449$; $\tau\propto\dlat$ $0.125/0.125$; released $0.339$. CelebA $25\%$, $\dlat\ge50$: $0.207/0.262$; $0.246/0.308$; $0.083/0.095$; released $0.169$. CelebA $5\%$, $\dlat\ge70$ ($d_{\rm ID}$, 9 dims): $0.277/0.384$ vs.\ released $0.311$. CelebA $5\%$, all uncensored $\dlat\ge20$ (14): $0.346/0.417$ vs.\ released $0.365$, with $\tau\propto\dlat$ the worst model ($0.407/0.477$). CIFAR-10 $5\%$, $\dlat\ge60$ (11): $0.130/0.151$ vs.\ released $0.146$ ($\tau\propto\dlat$ $0.155/0.198$); $\dlat\ge100$: $0.120/0.145$ vs.\ $0.116$; $\dlat\ge140$ (7): $0.112/0.136$ vs.\ $0.106$. CIFAR-10 $25\%$, $\dlat\ge60$ (8): $0.168/0.171$ vs.\ $0.143$. The $g$-clock is thus comparable to the released fit on CIFAR-10 and somewhat worse on CelebA, with no fitted response function and one constant. It accounts for $1.78\times$ of the observed $3.9\times$ (CelebA $\dlat=50\to200$), $1.74\times$ of $2.9\times$ ($100\to200$) and $1.85\times$ of $2.1\times$ (CIFAR-10 $100\to240$; $\dlat=260$ has $\tau_5=2520$k with s.d.\ $1392$k and is not used). The fitted $C$ differs between datasets by $2.0\times$ ($5\%$: $14.5$ vs.\ $29.1$) and $1.6\times$ ($25\%$) with identical optimiser settings, so one-constant transfer fails and a dataset-dependent factor ($|\mathcal D_{t_c}|/\nsamp$, decoder distortion of $d_{\rm NN}$) is missing. The width from the learning side alone is too narrow: at CelebA $\dlat=200$ the Gaussian-moment estimate (mean $1.302$, s.d.\ $0.2905$ of $\|z\|^2/\dlat$; per-sample latents are not in the repository) gives a $1.46\times$ span between the levels matching $5\%$ and $25\%$ absolute memorisation ($11\%$ and $56\%$ of the $0.448$ asymptote) against the observed $\tau_{25}/\tau_5=2.14$.

\emph{Surrogate caveat.} All real-data numbers use $\sigma=\tanh$, $\gamma=1$, $m_e=0$. The deployed network's first layer is $\mathrm{Linear}(\dlat+64,1024)$ with GELU on $[x_t;\,32\ \sin,\,32\ \cos]$ and PyTorch default init ($\sigma_w^2=1/(3\,\mathrm{fan\_in})$), for which $q_\mu\approx(e^{-2t}\|z\|^2+\dlat\Delta_t+32)/(3(\dlat+64))=0.36,\,0.43,\,0.36$ at CelebA $\dlat=50,100,200$ and $0.43,\,0.33$ at CIFAR-10 $\dlat=100,260$---nearly $\dlat$-independent---and GELU is not odd (its $k=0,2$ Hermite terms add a rank-one spike and $O(\varrho^2)$ off-diagonals). The derived predictor has therefore not been evaluated with the deployed network's own first-layer statistics; either the surrogate stands in for feature learning in deeper layers \citep{ba2022highdim}, or the real-data $\dlat$-slope arises mainly on the dynamics side and from the NN-ratio detection drift (F10).

\begin{remark}[Conjecture, numerically supported: cluster-collective modes at small $\dlat$]\label{rem:collective-pred}
Code units, $\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $t=0.01$, seed 42. The per-sample picture predicts a sample-bulk median $\pwidth r_ta_*^2/\min\{\nsamp,\pwidth\}$ rising $0.054\to0.098$ ($1.8\times$) and a top edge $\pwidth r_ta_*^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2/\nsamp$ falling $0.193\to0.141$ ($1.4\times$) over $\dlat=10\to200$ ($\dlat=5$ has $\pwidth<\nsamp$). Observed: median $0.0039\to0.093$ ($24\times$), largest sample-bulk eigenvalue $0.930\to0.173$ ($5.4\times$; $0.957$ at $\dlat=5$); the top ten sample-bulk modes carry $47\%,\,35\%,\,22\%,\,6.3\%,\,5.4\%$ of the sample-bulk mass at $\dlat=5,10,20,100,200$ (flat reference $3.3\%$), with $\lambda_{\rm top}/\lambda_{\rm median}=532,\,236,\,42,\,3.4,\,1.9$. At $\dlat\ge100$ the per-sample+MP picture is quantitatively right; at small $\dlat$ about $k=10$ modes absorb half the mass. The ratio $\lambda^{\rm signal}_{\min}/\lambda^{\rm sample}_{\rm top}$ grows $21\to427$ ($20\times$; F5 reports $31\to576$). Conjectured mechanism: same-cluster points have noise-averaged overlap $\varrho_c=e^{-2t}s^2/(\dlat q_d)=0.64,\,0.58,\,0.50,\,0.23,\,0.135$, so $\bar G$ acquires per cluster a block of $\nsamp/k$ entries $g(q_d,\varrho_c)$ and $k$ collective eigenvalues $\approx\pwidth[r_ta_*^2+(\nsamp/k-1)g(q_d,\varrho_c)]/\nsamp=0.53,\,0.50,\,0.36,\,0.11,\,0.11$, reproducing the observed trend from $\dlat=10$ within a uniform factor $1.6$--$1.9$ (cross-cluster overlaps, also $O(1)$ at $\dlat=5$, and the MP edge are omitted). Such modes learn the nonlinear shape of a cluster and are generalising; if confirmed, the small-$\dlat$ onset statistic of F5 is not a memorisation clock and the buffer claim must be re-based on the localised part of the sample bulk. Test: training-point-basis IPR of the top 20 sample-bulk eigenvectors ($\approx1$ localised, $\approx k/\nsamp$ collective); requires re-running the sweep with eigenvectors saved.
\end{remark}
```

# Piece 7: Beyond two atoms: general latent spectra, effective dimension, and posterior collapse

## Statements

### `def:deff-real` (definition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Z in R^{n x d_lat} holds encoder means; Sigma_0 = Z^T Z / n with eigenvalues s_1 >= ... >= s_dlat >= 0; M_t = e^{-2t} Sigma_0 + Delta_t I, lambda_i(M_t) = e^{-2t} s_i + Delta_t; S_t = n^{-1} sum_mu x_t^mu x_t^mu^T is the one-copy sample second moment with E_eta S_t = M_t

```latex
Let $\rho_{\dlat}:=\dlat^{-1}\sum_i\delta_{s_i}$ be the empirical spectral measure of $\Sigma_0$, $q[\rho_{\dlat}]:=\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat=e^{-2t}\!\int s\,\mathrm d\rho_{\dlat}+\Delta_t$, and $\hat q:=\operatorname{tr}S_t/\dlat=\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ ($\E\hat q=q$). For $\delta>0$ define the floor count $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(\delta):=\#\{i:\lambda_i(M_t)\le(1+\delta)\Delta_t\}$, the effective dimension $d_{\mathrm{eff}}:=\big(\sum_i(\lambda_i(M_t)-\Delta_t)\big)^2/\sum_i(\lambda_i(M_t)-\Delta_t)^2=(\sum_i s_i)^2/\sum_i s_i^2$ (participation ratio of $\Sigma_0$; floor-free since $\Delta_t$ is known), and the excess $d_{\mathrm{exc}}:=\dlat-d_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Definitions. Two-atom check: $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=(\dint S+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2)^2/(\dint S^2+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^4)\to\dint$ as $\signoise\to0$ but $=76.6$ at $(\dlat,\signoise)=(200,0.5)$. The released estimator (\texttt{effective\_rank\_excess}) is the participation ratio of $(\lambda_i-\hat\beta)_+$ with $\hat\beta$ the median of the lowest $30\%$ of eigenvalues; on the \emph{population} two-atom spectrum $\hat\beta=\beta_t^2$ and it returns $\dint$ (on a one-copy finite-$\nsamp$ spectrum the noise block is MP-spread, Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}, and it does not). On CelebA $\hat\beta$ jumps from $0.72$ ($\dlat=140$) to $0.189,0.182,0.181$ ($\dlat=160,180,200$; $\Delta_t=0.1813$), so the estimator coincides with $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ only where $\hat\beta=\Delta_t$, and its values across that boundary are not comparable.
```

### `lem:sampling-real` (lemma, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: x_t^mu = e^{-t} z^mu + sqrt(Delta_t) eta^mu, one copy per training point, eta^mu iid N(0, I); F a set of d_F coordinates with z_i^mu = 0 for all i in F and all mu; L the complement, d_L = d_lat - d_F; (a),(b) proved; (c) sketched (replacement of P_L(lambda) by P_L(0))

```latex
Write $H_F\in\R^{\nsamp\times d_F}$ for the noise on the collapsed coordinates and $X_L\in\R^{\nsamp\times d_L}$ for the diffused live coordinates. (a) $(S_t)_{FF}=\Delta_tH_F^\top H_F/\nsamp$ exactly, with $H_F$ iid $\mathcal N(0,1)$ independent of $X_L$; for $u>0$, with probability $\ge1-2e^{-u^2/2}$, $\operatorname{spec}(S_t)_{FF}\subset\Delta_t\big[(1-\sqrt{d_F/\nsamp}-u/\sqrt{\nsamp})^2,(1+\sqrt{d_F/\nsamp}+u/\sqrt{\nsamp})^2\big]$. (b) $\lambda_{d_L+k}(S_t)\le\lambda_k((S_t)_{FF})$ for $k\le d_F$: the $d_F$ smallest eigenvalues of $S_t$ lie below the upper MP edge of (a). (c) The floor eigenvalues $\lambda$ of $S_t$ solve $\det\big(\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top(I-P_L(\lambda))H_F-\lambda\big)=0$, $P_L(\lambda):=X_L(X_L^\top X_L-\nsamp\lambda)^{-1}X_L^\top$; replacing $P_L(\lambda)$ by the orthogonal projector $P_L(0)$ onto $\operatorname{col}(X_L)$ (relative error $\le\lambda/(\lambda_{\min}((S_t)_{LL})-\lambda)$), $\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top(I-P_L)H_F$ is $\Delta_t\frac{\nsamp-d_L}{\nsamp}$ times a normalized Wishart$(d_F,\nsamp-d_L)$, so to leading order the floor block of $S_t$ is a Marchenko--Pastur bulk on $\Delta_t(1-d_L/\nsamp)\big(1\pm\sqrt{d_F/(\nsamp-d_L)}\big)^2$. The same holds with $\Delta_t\to\beta_t^2$ for the null block of the two-atom model (whose null coordinates are iid $\mathcal N(0,\beta_t^2)$).
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) $x^\mu_{t,i}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta^\mu_i$ for $i\in F$ and $\eta_F$ is independent of $(z^\mu,\eta_L)$; the singular-value bound is \citet[Cor.~7.3.3]{vershynin2018hdp} applied to $H_F$. (b) Cauchy interlacing for the principal submatrix $(S_t)_{FF}$. (c) $\det(S_t-\lambda)=\det((S_t)_{LL}-\lambda)\det\big((S_t)_{FF}-\lambda-(S_t)_{FL}((S_t)_{LL}-\lambda)^{-1}(S_t)_{LF}\big)$ with $(S_t)_{LF}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}X_L^\top H_F/\nsamp$, so the correction is $\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top X_L(X_L^\top X_L-\nsamp\lambda)^{-1}X_L^\top H_F=\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top P_L(\lambda)H_F$. $I-P_L(0)$ is a rank-$(\nsamp-d_L)$ orthogonal projector independent of $H_F$, so $(I-P_L)H_F$ is an $(\nsamp-d_L)\times d_F$ Gaussian matrix in a rotated basis; its normalized Gram matrix has MP edges $(1\pm\sqrt{d_F/(\nsamp-d_L)})^2$. Numbers: CelebA $\dlat=200$, $d_F\ge30$, $\nsamp=1000$: block alone $[0.68,1.38]\Delta_t$, with the projection factor $0.83$ and aspect $30/830$: $[0.54,1.18]\Delta_t$. Synthetic null block ($d_F=195$, $d_L=5$, $\nsamp=500$): $[0.14,2.62]\beta_t^2$. Both dwarf the width-$\varepsilon$ ($=0.27$ at $\pwidth=64\dlat$) term of Proposition~\ref{prop:pushforward-real}; the signal block ($\dint=5$, aspect $0.01$) has sampling width of order $\sqrt{\dint/\nsamp}=0.1$, comparable to $\varepsilon$.
```

### `assump:gep-real` (assumption, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: RFNN features phi(x)=p^{-1/2}tanh(Wx/sqrt(d_lat)), W iid N(0,1); U = n^{-1} sum_mu phi(x_t^mu) phi(x_t^mu)^T from one diffused copy per training point, rank U <= n (the matrix the code diagonalises, F1); p > n > d_lat, proportional asymptotics, epsilon_0 < 1

```latex
$\Umat=\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}+\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$ with $\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\pwidth\dlat}WS_tW^\top$ ($S_t$ the one-copy sample second moment), $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\succeq0$, $\operatorname{tr}\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}=a_\ast(q)^2(1+o(1))$, $\|\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\|=c_+a_\ast(q)^2/\nsamp$ with $c_+=O(1)$, where $q=q[\rho_{\dlat}]$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Not proved here. Gaussian-equivalence decomposition \citep{bonnaire2025,goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality,mei2022generalization} asserted for diffused latent inputs. Obstacles: real latents are non-Gaussian; the pre-diffusion $\|z^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ has coefficient of variation $0.22$--$0.23$ across $\mu$ (CelebA $\dlat\ge160$), hence $\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ about $0.20$, so $q$ is the average of per-sample $q_\mu$ ($\hat q=q$ to relative $0.2/\sqrt{\nsamp}$). With one copy per point the residual $r_\mu=\phi(x_t^\mu)-a_1Wx_t^\mu/\sqrt{\pwidth\dlat}$ is correlated with $Wx_t^\mu$; indeed $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$, $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\dlat$ and $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\succeq0$ force $\operatorname{range}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}\subset\operatorname{range}\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$, so $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$ is not an isotropic Wishart independent of $W$ and the MP value $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ is not the right reference for $c_+$. $c_+$ is a fitted $O(1)$ constant; on the synthetic sweep it is $\dlat$-dependent (Definition~\ref{def:ratio-real}).
```

### `prop:pushforward-real` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Assumption assump:gep-real; W in R^{p x d_lat} iid N(0,1); epsilon_0 = sqrt(d_lat/p) + u/sqrt(p) < 1

```latex
With probability $\ge1-2e^{-u^2/2}$ over $W$: (i) $\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})=\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\lambda_i(S_t)(1+\epsilon_i)$, $|\epsilon_i|\le\varepsilon:=2\varepsilon_0+\varepsilon_0^2$, $i\le\dlat$; (ii) $\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})\le\lambda_i(\Umat)\le\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})+c_+a_\ast(q)^2/\nsamp$ for all $i$. Hence the $\dlat$ largest eigenvalues of $\Umat$ are the pushforward $(\lambda\mapsto a_1(q)^2\lambda/\dlat)_\#$ of the spectrum of $S_t$, which is the spectrum of $M_t$ broadened by one-copy sampling (Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}: an eigenvalue of $M_t$ of multiplicity $m$ carrying no data variance becomes an MP-type bulk of aspect $m/\nsamp$), up to relative error $\varepsilon+c_+(a_\ast^2/a_1^2)(\dlat/\nsamp)/\lambda_i(S_t)$. The next $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues form the residual bulk with mass $a_\ast(q)^2-\operatorname{tr}(P\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}P)\in[a_\ast^2(1-c_+\dlat/\nsamp),a_\ast^2]$ ($P$ the top-$\dlat$ projector), mean in $[a_\ast^2(1-c_+\dlat/\nsamp),a_\ast^2]/(\nsamp-\dlat)$ (the $\dlat/\nsamp\to0$ limit is $a_\ast^2/\nsamp$), and upper edge $c_+a_\ast(q)^2/\nsamp$; the remaining $\pwidth-\nsamp$ vanish; $\sum_i\lambda_i(\Umat)=a_1(q)^2\hat q+a_\ast(q)^2+o(1)=\E[\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)]$. A two-atom $\rho$ gives the four bulks with sampling widths $(1\pm\sqrt{\dint/\nsamp})^2$ and $(1\pm\sqrt{(\dlat-\dint)/(\nsamp-\dint)})^2$; a general $\rho$ gives a data-side continuum on $[a_1^2(e^{-2t}s_{\dlat}+\Delta_t)/\dlat,\,a_1^2(e^{-2t}s_1+\Delta_t)/\dlat]$ plus a floor bulk of $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(0)$ eigenvalues with median $\approx a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$ and relative half-width $\approx2\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{floor}}/\nsamp}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(i) The nonzero spectrum of $WS_tW^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ equals that of $\dlat^{-1}S_t^{1/2}GS_t^{1/2}$, $G=W^\top W/\pwidth$. By \citet[Cor.~7.3.3]{vershynin2018hdp}, $\sqrt{\pwidth}-\sqrt{\dlat}-u\le s_{\min}(W)\le s_{\max}(W)\le\sqrt{\pwidth}+\sqrt{\dlat}+u$ w.p. $\ge1-2e^{-u^2/2}$, so $\operatorname{spec}G\subset[(1-\varepsilon_0)^2,(1+\varepsilon_0)^2]$ and $\|G-I\|\le\varepsilon$ (needs $\varepsilon_0<1$ for $G\succ0$). Ostrowski's theorem for the congruence gives $\lambda_i(S_t^{1/2}GS_t^{1/2})=\theta_i\lambda_i(S_t)$, $\theta_i\in[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]$. (ii) Weyl for a PSD perturbation. Counts: $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\dlat$, $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$. Mass: Weyl bounds the residual trace absorbed by the top $\dlat$ eigenvalues by $\dlat c_+a_\ast^2/\nsamp$. Trace: $\operatorname{tr}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=a_1^2\operatorname{tr}(S_tG)/\dlat=a_1^2\hat q(1+O(\varepsilon))$. Sketched: identifying the intermediate $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues with the residual bulk and its edge with $c_+a_\ast^2/\nsamp$ rests on the assumption; the sampling widths come from Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}(c). Weyl term relative to the floor median: CelebA $\dlat=200$, $\nsamp=1000$: $0.12c_+$; synthetic noise-dimension edge at the $t$ where $q=0.33$ ($\beta_t^2=0.268$, $\dlat=200$, $\nsamp=500$): $0.011c_+$.
```

### `def:ratio-real` (definition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Per-mode gradient-flow decay a_i(T)-a_i^* = (a_i(0)-a_i^*)e^{-lambda_i(U) T}; Assumption assump:gep-real

```latex
For $\theta\in(0,1]$ let $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ be the largest level such that $\sum_{i:\lambda_i(M_t)\ge\lambda_{(\theta)}}s_i\ge\theta\sum_is_i$. Define $\taugen^{(\theta)}:=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\lambda_{(\theta)})$, $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}:=1/\lambda_+^{\mathrm{samp}}=\nsamp/(c_+a_\ast(q)^2)$, $R^{(\theta)}[\rho_{\dlat}]:=\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}/\taugen^{(\theta)}=\frac{\nsamp}{c_+\dlat}\frac{a_1(q)^2}{a_\ast(q)^2}\lambda_{(\theta)}$. No count of coordinates appears: $\dlat$ enters through the fan-in $1/\dlat$, through $q[\rho_{\dlat}]$, and through $c_+$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Definition. Two-atom model: $\lambda_{(\theta)}=\alpha_t^2$ only for $\theta$ below the signal variance share $\dint S/(\dint S+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2)$, which is $0.22$ at $(\dlat,\signoise)=(200,0.5)$ and $0.05$ at $\signoise=1$; for larger $\theta$, $\lambda_{(\theta)}=\beta_t^2$ and $\taugen^{(\theta)}$ is the noise-dimension timescale. Disclosure on $c_+$: with the MP guess $c_+=(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ and $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $q\,2.76\to0.33$ over $\dlat=5\to200$ gives $a_\ast^2$ ratio $16.5$ and $c_+$ ratio $3.5$, i.e.\ predicted onset growth $58\times$, $R$ growth $5\times$, $\taugen$ growth $11.6\times$, against measured (F5) $221\times$, $19\times$, $8.1\times$. So $c_+$ on clustered data carries a further $\approx4\times$ $\dlat$-dependence over the sweep; the synthetic monotone growth of $R$ supports the sign, not the magnitude, and every real-data ratio below is taken under the explicit hypothesis that $c_+$ is constant across the compared widths.
```

### `lem:dead-real` (lemma, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Coordinate i of the encoder means vanishes on the training set: z_i^mu = 0 for all mu; (a),(b),(c),(e) proved unconditionally; (d) under Assumption assump:gep-real and Lemma lem:sampling-real(c)

```latex
(a) $\Sigma_0e_i=0$, $M_te_i=\Delta_te_i$: $\lambda_i(M_t)=\Delta_t$ exactly, the minimum possible. (b) $x^\mu_{t,i}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta_i$ and the exact score of the diffused empirical measure \citep{scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization,biroli2024dynamical} has $s^\ast_i(x_t)=-x_{t,i}/\Delta_t$, linear in $x_{t,i}$ alone. (c) The coordinate contributes exactly $\Delta_t/\dlat$ to $q$. (d) In $\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}$ the $d_F$ collapsed coordinates contribute the floor bulk $a_1(q)^2\lambda(1+\epsilon)/\dlat$, $\lambda\in\Delta_t(1-d_L/\nsamp)(1\pm\sqrt{d_F/(\nsamp-d_L)})^2$, the slowest data-side modes; median timescale $\tau_{\mathrm{floor}}=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\Delta_t)$. (e) For a linear score $s(x)=Bx$ under gradient flow on $\E\|Bx_t-s^\ast\|^2$, column $i$ decouples: $B_{ii}(T)=-\Delta_t^{-1}+(B_{ii}(0)+\Delta_t^{-1})e^{-2\Delta_tT}$, $B_{ji}(T)=B_{ji}(0)e^{-2\Delta_tT}$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) $(\Sigma_0)_{ij}=\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu z_i^\mu z_j^\mu=0$. (b) The diffused empirical measure is $\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu\mathcal N(e^{-t}z^\mu,\Delta_tI)$ with all centres having zero $i$-th component, so the softmax weights do not depend on $x_{t,i}$, the $i$-th marginal is $\mathcal N(0,\Delta_t)$ independent of the rest, and $s^\ast_i=\sum_\mu w_\mu(x)(0-x_{t,i})/\Delta_t=-x_{t,i}/\Delta_t$. (c) $\operatorname{tr}M_t$ gains $\Delta_t$. (d) Prop.~\ref{prop:pushforward-real}(i) with Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}(c). (e) $\nabla_{Be_i}=2(BM_te_i-\E[s^\ast x_{t,i}])$; $M_te_i=\Delta_te_i$; $\E[s^\ast_jx_{t,i}]=0$ for $j\ne i$ by independence, $\E[s^\ast_ix_{t,i}]=-1$ (Stein). Hence $\frac{d}{dT}Be_i=-2\Delta_t(Be_i+e_i/\Delta_t)$.
```

### `prop:deadblock-real` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: d_0 live coordinates with fixed spectrum rho^live and q_0 = q[rho^live]; n_dead collapsed coordinates appended, d_lat = d_0 + n_dead; Assumption assump:gep-real; c_+ constant in n_dead; a_*(q)^2 increasing in q on [Delta_t, 50] (verified by 200-node Gauss-Hermite on a 5000-point grid, min increment 3.3e-5; not proved); all sweeps have q <= 2.6

```latex
For any $\theta$: (i) $q(\dlat)=\Delta_t+\frac{d_0}{\dlat}(q_0-\Delta_t)$, strictly decreasing in $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ with limit $\Delta_t$; $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ unchanged; $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(0)$ grows by $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$. (ii) $a_1(q)$ is strictly decreasing in $q$, so $a_1(q(\dlat))^2\uparrow a_1(\Delta_t)^2=0.746$ ($t=0.1$); $a_\ast(q(\dlat))^2\downarrow a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2=1.39\times10^{-3}$. (iii) Every live data-side eigenvalue is rescaled by $\frac{a_1(q(\dlat))^2}{a_1(q_0)^2}\frac{d_0}{\dlat}$; $\taugen^{(\theta)}(\dlat)\ge\dlat/(a_1(\Delta_t)^2\lambda_{(\theta)})$ grows at least linearly in $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$; the floor bulk's lower edge falls by the further factor $(1-d_0/\nsamp)(1-\sqrt{n_{\mathrm{dead}}/(\nsamp-d_0)})^2$. (iv) $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ increases with $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ but $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}(\dlat)/\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}(d_0)\le a_\ast(q_0)^2/a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$. (v) $R^{(\theta)}(\dlat)/R^{(\theta)}(d_0)=\frac{d_0}{\dlat}\frac{a_1(q)^2}{a_1(q_0)^2}\frac{a_\ast(q_0)^2}{a_\ast(q)^2}$, independent of $\theta,\nsamp$ (and of $c_+$ under the constancy hypothesis), and for $q_0\le50$, $R^{(\theta)}(\dlat)\le536\,\nsamp\lambda_{(\theta)}/(c_+\dlat)\to0$. Collapsed coordinates change the buffer ratio only through $q$, by a bounded factor, and eventually erode it.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(i) Lemma~\ref{lem:dead-real}(c) applied $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ times. (ii) $a_1(q)=\E[\operatorname{sech}^2(\sqrt qz)]$ is strictly decreasing since $\operatorname{sech}^2$ is on $(0,\infty)$. Monotonicity of $a_\ast^2$ on $[\Delta_t,50]$ by quadrature (values $0.0014,0.031,0.036,0.043,0.048,0.054,0.066,0.074$ at $q=0.181,1.11,1.25,1.47,1.62,1.83,2.27,2.58$; $a_\ast^2\to1-2/\pi$ as $q\to\infty$). (iii)--(v) substitution into Prop.~\ref{prop:pushforward-real}, Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}(c) and Def.~\ref{def:ratio-real}; bounds use $a_1^2\le a_1(\Delta_t)^2$, $a_\ast^2\ge a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$ for $q\in[\Delta_t,50]$, and $a_1(\Delta_t)^2/a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2=536$.
```

### `cor:flatdeff-real` (corollary, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: Prop. prop:deadblock-real, treating the RFNN surrogate at t=0.1 with unit-variance first layer as a proxy for the depth-5, width-1024 MLP trained across t (Remark rem:blunt-real); (H_c) c_+ constant across the compared widths; Measured CelebA/CIFAR-10 spectra at t=0.1, n=1000 (repo CSVs); d_eff values are the released estimator's, equal to Def. def:deff-real's only where beta_hat = Delta_t; Memorization curve depends on training only through the sample bulk, rescaled uniformly by a_*(q)^2 (surrogate hypothesis)

```latex
CelebA, $\dlat=160,180,200$: $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0=252.5,255.2,260.4$ (increments $0.14$--$0.35$ per added coordinate against a live mean $2.8$--$3.0$), estimator $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=89.5,90.3,87.9$ ($\hat\beta=0.189,0.182,0.181$), $\lambda_{\min}-\Delta_t\le6\times10^{-5}$, $q=1.473,1.342,1.247$, $a_1^2=0.286,0.305,0.320$, $a_\ast^2=0.0434,0.0391,0.0359$. Relative to $\dlat=160$ the mechanism predicts $\taugen\times1.06,1.12$; $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}\times1.11,1.21$; $R\times1.05,1.08$; every iso-memorization hitting time $\times1.11,1.21$; final fraction $F_d(T_{\max})=F_{160}(T_{\max}a_\ast(q_d)^2/a_\ast(q_{160})^2)=0.595,0.559$. Observed: hitting-time ratios $160\to180$: $1.17$--$1.33$; $160\to200$: $1.26,1.33,1.37,1.41$ (thresholds $0.05,0.01,0.10,0.25$); final fractions $0.608,0.509,0.448$. CIFAR-10 ($\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0=281$--$299$ for $\dlat=160$--$260$; $q\;1.621\to1.108$): predicted $\taumem\times1.54$, $\taugen\times1.26$, $R\times1.22$ ($160\to260$); final fractions $0.557,0.552,0.517,0.505,0.472$ at $\dlat=180,\dots,260$; observed $0.456,0.346,0.213,0.183,0.125$; hitting times $\times1.6$ (thr $0.01$), $\times2.4$ (thr $0.05$, $95\%$ CI $\pm1.7$M at $\dlat=260$), $\times2.7$ (thr $0.10$, one of five seeds censored).
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Substitution of the CSV values into Prop.~\ref{prop:deadblock-real}(iii)--(v); hitting-time and final-fraction predictions follow from the rescaling hypothesis by reading the seed-mean $\dlat=160$ curve at the rescaled step ($4.51$M, $4.14$M for CelebA). Sensitivity: with PyTorch fan-in initialisation ($q\to q/3$) the CelebA $160\to200$ factors become $\taumem\times1.33$, $R\times1.14$, and CIFAR $160\to260$ $\taumem\times1.93$; the sign is scale-free, the magnitude is not. The proposition's hypotheses ($\pwidth>\nsamp>\dlat$, $\varepsilon_0<1$) hold only marginally for the real model ($\pwidth=1024$, $\nsamp=1000$, $\dlat\le260$), and its first-layer features are learned; hence 'conjectured'.
```

### `rem:blunt-real` (remark, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Cor. cor:flatdeff-real

```latex
When $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is flat, added coordinates change $q$ and the floor bulk, not any buffer count; $d_{\mathrm{exc}}=71,90,112$ enters no timescale. The mechanism accounts for the sign, for $0.55$--$0.82$ of the log-slowdown of CelebA hitting times ($\log1.21/\log\{1.41,1.34,1.26\}$), for $0.28$ of the log-decline of the CelebA final fraction ($\log(0.559/0.608)/\log(0.448/0.608)$), and on CIFAR-10 for $0.46$ of the hitting-time log-slowdown ($\log1.54/\log2.55$) and $0.15$ of the final-fraction log-decline. The real score model is a depth-5, width-1024 MLP ($\pwidth=1024>\nsamp=1000$, so an $\nsamp-\dlat$ residual bulk is only marginally accommodated) with sinusoidal time features trained by SGD across $t\in[0.01,3]$; its first-layer features are learned, so RFNN quantities are a surrogate. Over $\dlat=20$--$120$ (CelebA) $q$ is flat ($1.71$--$1.84$): the surrogate predicts no change in $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ there; the observed rise is the decoder identity-threshold regime, outside the theory. Memorization is not a function of $q$ alone across datasets (CelebA $0.45$ vs CIFAR-10 $0.21$ at $q\approx1.25$--$1.29$); the pixel-space NN-ratio has no ambient-dimension drift (fixed decoded dimension), but a decoder-dependent drift of its baseline has not been checked.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Arithmetic on Cor.~\ref{cor:flatdeff-real}; architecture from the run scripts (\texttt{--hidden 1024 --depth 5 --optimizer sgd --lr 0.001 --momentum 0.80 --n\_train 1000}).
```

### `rem:samples-real` (remark, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: Diffusion model trained on posterior samples z ~ N(mu(x), diag sigma^2(x)) instead of encoder means; Collapsed coordinate has mu_i = 0, sigma_i = 1

```latex
With posterior samples a collapsed coordinate has $s_i=1$ and $\lambda_i(M_t)=e^{-2t}+\Delta_t=1$: it becomes an isotropic noise-dimension coordinate with $\signoise=1$ rather than a floor coordinate, and the floor bulk moves up by $1/\Delta_t\approx5.5$. Then $q=\Delta_t+[d_0(q_0-\Delta_t)+n_{\mathrm{dead}}e^{-2t}]/\dlat$ exceeds the mean-latent value, so $a_\ast(q)^2$ is larger and $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ shorter, while $a_1(q)^2$ is smaller and $\taugen$ longer; $R$ shrinks by both factors. The theory predicts more memorization (of the onset kind) for sampled than for mean latents at large $\dlat$, with the caveat that the training latents are then a stochastic set, so the empirical-score target of Lemma~\ref{lem:dead-real}(b) and the memorization criterion itself change, not only $q$.
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Substitution into Def.~\ref{def:deff-real} and Prop.~\ref{prop:deadblock-real}; untested.
```

## Predictions

- **Pure q-rescaling: for CelebA d_lat >= 160 every iso-memorization hitting time scales by a_*(q_160)^2/a_*(q_d)^2 = 1.11 (d=180), 1.21 (d=200), independent of threshold, and the memorization-fraction curves collapse when step is multiplied by a_*(q_d)^2/a_*(q_160)^2.**

  support: Partially: observed 160->200 ratios at thresholds 0.05/0.01/0.1/0.25 are 1.26/1.33/1.37/1.41 (roughly threshold-independent, consistent with a time rescaling) but 5-20% larger than 1.21; 160->180 ratios 1.17-1.33 vs 1.11; predicted final fractions 0.595/0.559 vs observed 0.509/0.448.

- **The coordinates added beyond d_lat ~ 140 (CelebA) / ~ 200 (CIFAR-10) are posterior-collapsed: d_floor(2e-3) >= 0.15 d_lat for CelebA d >= 180 and CIFAR d >= 220, and tr Sigma_0 is flat.**

Also check the predicted MP width of the floor block: the d_floor smallest eigenvalues of the one-copy S_t should occupy ~Delta_t (1-d_L/n)(1 +- sqrt(d_floor/(n-d_L)))^2.  
  support: beta_floor - Delta_t = 2.4e-4 (CelebA 180), 9e-5 (200), 1.27e-3 (CIFAR 220), 1.1e-4 (240), 5.3e-5 (260), implying >= 15% of coordinates within 2e-3 of the floor; lambda_min - Delta_t = 1.3e-5..6e-5; tr Sigma_0 = 252.5/255.2/260.4 (CelebA 160-200), 281-299 (CIFAR 160-260), i.e. 0.14-0.35 variance per added coordinate against a live mean of 2.8-3.0.

- **Training the latent diffusion model on posterior samples instead of encoder means at large d_lat (e.g. CelebA d=200) INCREASES onset memorization relative to means, because collapsed coordinates move from the floor (lambda = Delta_t) to lambda = 1, raising q and a_*(q)^2 (while also lowering a_1(q)^2, so tau_gen lengthens).**  
  test: REQUIRES NEW TRAINING on ML6: re-encode the 1k subset with z ~ q(z|x) (fixed sample), retrain the d=200 MLP diffusion with identical budget, compare final memorization fraction and hitting times with the mean-latent run.  
  support: Nothing yet.

- **Projecting out the floor coordinates (training on the d_live-dimensional live subspace of the d=200 CelebA latents) restores memorization toward the d ~ 120-140 level, with tau_mem shortened by a_*(q_live)^2/a_*(q_200)^2 (~0.7-0.8 for n_dead = 30-60).**  
  test: REQUIRES NEW TRAINING: diagonalize Sigma_0 at d=200, drop eigen-directions with lambda_i(M_t) <= (1+2e-3)Delta_t, train the score model on the projected latents, decode by zero-padding; compare memorization curves.  
  support: Nothing yet; the direct converse of the corollary.

- **At fixed nominal d_lat, changing the VAE KL capacity C (hence n_dead) reorders memorization by q, not by d_lat: runs with the same d_lat but more collapsed coordinates memorize later.**

  support: Nothing yet.

- **Memorization is NOT a function of q alone across datasets: at matched q ~ 1.25-1.29 the final fractions differ (CelebA 0.448 at q=1.247 vs CIFAR-10 0.213 at q=1.292), so the q-dilution mechanism is incomplete.**

## Open gaps
(1) Assumption assump:gep-real (Gaussian equivalence on real, non-Gaussian latents; per-sample CV of ||x_t||^2/d about 0.20) is not proved; with one copy per point U^res is correlated with W, so c_+ is a fitted O(1) constant, not an MP value (F8). On the repo's own synthetic spectra (F5) the q- and MP-c_+ dependence predict 58x onset growth against the measured 221x, so c_+ carries an additional ~4x d_lat-dependence on clustered data; all real-data ratios are therefore conditional on the explicit hypothesis (H_c) that c_+ is constant across compared widths, and are sign-and-order-of-magnitude statements. (2) ARCHITECTURE CORRECTION FOR THE PARENT: the real-data score model is a depth-5, width-1024 MLP (run scripts: --hidden 1024 --depth 5 --optimizer sgd --lr 0.001 --momentum 0.80), not depth-3/width-256; sec-real-data.tex line 40 must be fixed to match. Hence p = 1024 > n = 1000 and the former 'p < n, no room for a sample bulk' argument is deleted; the hypotheses p > n > d_lat and epsilon_0 < 1 hold only marginally (epsilon_0 ~ 0.5 at d = 260), the features are learned, and the RFNN quantities at t = 0.1 are a surrogate whose absolute q depends on the first-layer scale (q -> q/3 turns the CelebA tau_mem factor 1.21 into 1.33). (3) The pure time-rescaling hypothesis behind the final-fraction predictions is a modelling choice; it accounts for 0.28 of the CelebA final-fraction log-decline, 0.55-0.82 of the CelebA hitting-time log-slowdown, 0.46 / 0.15 of the CIFAR-10 hitting-time / final-fraction log-changes; the remainder is not the buffer/q mechanism. Candidates: feature learning, non-uniform sample bulk of clustered real features, decoder sensitivity to imperfectly learned floor coordinates (FID worsens 38.9 -> 47-50 at d >= 160). (4) The d_eff values quoted are the released estimator's (participation ratio of (lambda - beta_hat)_+) and equal Def. def:deff-real's only where beta_hat = Delta_t (CelebA d >= 180 to 2e-4; at d = 160 beta_hat - Delta_t = 8e-3); the CIFAR d = 180, 200 estimator values (93, 106) are not Def. d_eff. spectra.npz, VAE checkpoints and diagnostics/ are absent from the local repo, so d_floor(delta) and the floor-free d_eff can only be bounded (>= 15% of coordinates on the floor for CelebA d >= 180 and CIFAR d >= 220 at delta = 2e-3), not computed; predictions 2-5 require ML6 assets or new training, predictions 1, 6, 7 are executable from local files. (5) The theta-quantile tau_gen is a convention; in the two-atom model it is the signal timescale only for theta below the signal variance share (0.22 at (200, 0.5); 0.05 at sigma_perp = 1); stated predictions are theta-independent because the live spectrum is flat over the confronted range. (6) Monotonicity of a_*(q)^2 in q is verified numerically on [Delta_t, 50] (min increment 3.3e-5), not proved; all sweeps have q <= 2.6. (7) Lemma lem:sampling-real(c): the location of the floor bulk under one-copy sampling, Delta_t(1 - d_L/n)(1 +- sqrt(d_F/(n-d_L)))^2, is exact for the Schur complement at lambda = 0 and approximate for the eigenvalues (relative error lambda/lambda_min((S_t)_LL) ~ Delta_t/lambda_live); the CelebA numbers ([0.54,1.18] Delta_t) assume d_F ~ 30; the exact d_F needs spectra.npz. (8) The cross-dataset comparison (CelebA 0.45 vs CIFAR 0.21 at matched q) falsifies a q-only reading; a per-dataset live-spectrum or decoder term is needed and not supplied. (9) Referee disagreements: the empirical referee's synthetic Weyl aside value 0.0079 c_+ does not reproduce; with beta_t^2 = 0.268 at the t where q = 0.33 the term is 0.011 c_+ (math referee's value), which is what the text now states. The math referee's suggestion to restrict (v) to q <= 3 was superseded by extending the quadrature check to q <= 50. burgess2018understanding was dropped from citations_needed (never cited); posterior collapse is cited to lucas2019dontblame only.

## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\subsection{Beyond two atoms: real latent spectra, effective dimension, posterior collapse}
\label{sec:theory-real}

Real VAE latents are not two-atom. With $\Sigma_0=Z^\top Z/\nsamp$ the second moment of the encoder means and $M_t=e^{-2t}\Sigma_0+\Delta_t I$ (the matrix of Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}), the eigenvalues $\lambda_i(M_t)=e^{-2t}s_i+\Delta_t$ form a continuous spectrum with an exact atom at the diffusion floor $\Delta_t$. Nothing in the random-feature analysis requires two atoms: \citet{bonnaire2025} state their Theorem~3.1 for an arbitrary spectral measure, and the four-bulk picture of Section~\ref{sec:theory} is that theorem evaluated at a two-atom measure (\citealp{george2025dsm} treat $\signoise=0$). Appendix~\ref{app:theory-real} records what the same analysis says for general spectra, with the one-copy feature matrix $\Umat$ ($\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$) built from the sample second moment $S_t=\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top}$, $\E S_t=M_t$.

\begin{definition}[Effective dimensions]\label{def:deff-real-main}
Let $\rho_{\dlat}=\dlat^{-1}\sum_i\delta_{s_i}$ and $q[\rho_{\dlat}]=\operatorname{tr}M_t/\dlat$. Define the floor count $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(\delta)=\#\{i:\lambda_i(M_t)\le(1+\delta)\Delta_t\}$, the effective dimension $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=(\sum_i s_i)^2/\sum_i s_i^2$ (participation ratio of $\Sigma_0$; no estimated floor), and $d_{\mathrm{exc}}=\dlat-d_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Data-side spectrum is a pushforward; proved conditional on Assumption~\ref{assump:gep-real}]\label{prop:pushforward-real-main}
Under the Gaussian-equivalence decomposition $\Umat=\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}+\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$ with $\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=a_1(q)^2WS_tW^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$, the $\dlat$ largest eigenvalues of $\Umat$ are $a_1(q)^2\lambda_i(S_t)/\dlat$ up to a multiplicative $1\pm O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ (Wishart bound plus Ostrowski) and an additive $\le\|\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\|=c_+a_\ast(q)^2/\nsamp$ (Weyl). The spectrum of $S_t$ is that of $M_t$ broadened by sampling: a block of $m$ coordinates carrying only noise of variance $v$ becomes a Marchenko--Pastur bulk on $v(1-d_L/\nsamp)(1\pm\sqrt{m/(\nsamp-d_L)})^2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}), which for the synthetic null block ($m=195$, $\nsamp=500$) is $[0.14,2.6]\beta_t^2$ and dwarfs the $O(\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth})$ term. The remaining $\nsamp-\dlat$ nonzero eigenvalues form the residual sample bulk of mass $a_\ast(q)^2(1-O(c_+\dlat/\nsamp))$; the total trace is $a_1(q)^2q+a_\ast(q)^2=\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)$. A two-atom $\rho$ gives the four bulks; a general $\rho$ gives a data-side continuum plus a floor bulk of $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(0)$ eigenvalues with median $\approx a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$, which replaces the noise-dimension bulk.
\end{proposition}

With $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ the $\theta$-variance quantile of the live spectrum, $\taugen^{(\theta)}=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\lambda_{(\theta)})$, $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}=\nsamp/(c_+a_\ast(q)^2)$ and the buffer ratio is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ratio-real-main}
R^{(\theta)}[\rho_{\dlat}]=\frac{\nsamp}{c_+\dlat}\,\frac{a_1(q)^2}{a_\ast(q)^2}\,\lambda_{(\theta)},\qquad q=q[\rho_{\dlat}] ,
\end{equation}
in which no count of coordinates appears: $\dlat$ acts through the fan-in $1/\dlat$, through $q$, and through $c_+$. On the synthetic sweep the $q$-dependence with an MP $c_+$ predicts onset growth $58\times$ and $R$ growth $5\times$ against the measured $221\times$ and $19\times$, so $c_+$ itself grows with $\dlat$ on clustered data; the real-data ratios below assume $c_+$ constant across the compared widths and are sign-and-order-of-magnitude statements.

\begin{lemma}[Collapsed coordinates; (a)--(c),(e) proved, block consequences under Assumption~\ref{assump:gep-real}]\label{lem:dead-real-main}
If $z^\mu_i=0$ for all $\mu$, then $\lambda_i(M_t)=\Delta_t$ exactly, the true score is exactly $s^\ast_i=-x_{t,i}/\Delta_t$, the coordinate adds exactly $\Delta_t/\dlat$ to $q$, and the collapsed block contributes the slowest data-side modes (floor bulk, median $a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$). Appending $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ such coordinates to $d_0$ live ones gives $q=\Delta_t+\tfrac{d_0}{\dlat}(q_0-\Delta_t)$, so $a_1(q)^2\uparrow a_1(\Delta_t)^2=0.746$, $a_\ast(q)^2\downarrow a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2=1.4\times10^{-3}$ ($t=0.1$; monotonicity of $a_\ast^2$ verified numerically on $[\Delta_t,50]$): $\taugen$ grows at least linearly in $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$, $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ grows but by at most $a_\ast(q_0)^2/a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$, and $R\le536\,\nsamp\lambda_{(\theta)}/(c_+\dlat)\to0$ (Prop.~\ref{prop:deadblock-real}).
\end{lemma}

\paragraph{Confronting the measured spectra.} On CelebA, $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0$ grows by $\approx2$ per coordinate up to $\dlat\approx140$ and then saturates ($252.5,255.2,260.4$ at $\dlat=160,180,200$; $0.14$--$0.35$ per added coordinate against a live mean of $2.8$--$3.0$), the estimator's $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ plateaus at $89.5,90.3,87.9$, and $\lambda_{\min}-\Delta_t\le6\times10^{-5}$ with at least $15\%$ of coordinates on the floor for $\dlat\ge180$: the added coordinates are posterior-collapsed \citep{lucas2019dontblame}. Only $q$ changes ($1.473\to1.247$). Treating the RFNN surrogate as a proxy for the depth-5, width-1024 MLP actually trained ($\pwidth=1024>\nsamp=1000$, learned features), Corollary~\ref{cor:flatdeff-real} predicts, relative to $\dlat=160$, $\taugen\times1.12$, $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}\times1.21$, $R\times1.08$ at $\dlat=200$, hence hitting times $\times1.21$ and a final fraction $0.56$ under pure time-rescaling; observed: hitting times $\times1.26$--$1.41$, final fraction $0.45$ (from $0.61$). On CIFAR-10 ($\dlat=160\to260$) the prediction is $\taumem\times1.54$ and a final fraction $0.47$ against observed $\times1.6$--$2.7$ and $0.125$. Bluntly: when $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is flat, added coordinates change $q$ and the floor bulk, not a buffer count; $d_{\mathrm{exc}}=71,90,112$ enters no timescale. The mechanism accounts for the sign, for $0.55$--$0.82$ of the log-slowdown of CelebA hitting times, for $0.28$ of the log-decline of the CelebA final fraction, and for $0.46$ / $0.15$ of the CIFAR-10 hitting-time / final-fraction log-changes; the rest lies outside the surrogate (Remark~\ref{rem:blunt-real}). Memorization is also not a function of $q$ alone across datasets (CelebA $0.45$ vs CIFAR-10 $0.21$ at $q\approx1.25$--$1.29$). We therefore report $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$, $d_{\mathrm{floor}}$ and $q$ alongside $\dlat$ for every real-data run (Remark~\ref{rem:xaxis-real}).
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Theory for general latent spectra: effective dimension and posterior collapse}
\label{app:theory-real}

This appendix drops the two-atom assumption of Section~\ref{sec:theory}. Nothing in the random-feature analysis requires it: \citet{bonnaire2025} state their Theorem~3.1 for an arbitrary spectral measure of the input covariance (their supplement derives the Gaussian-equivalent problem under arbitrary input covariance), and the four-bulk picture is that theorem evaluated at a two-atom measure; \citet{george2025dsm} treat data on a $\dint$-dimensional subspace with $\signoise=0$. Here we record what the same analysis gives for the continuous, partially collapsed spectra of real VAE latents, and we confront it with the spectra measured in Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}.

\paragraph{Setting.}
Let $Z\in\R^{\nsamp\times\dlat}$ hold the encoder means $z^\mu$ of the $\nsamp$ training images, $\Sigma_0:=Z^\top Z/\nsamp=\sum_{i=1}^{\dlat}s_iv_iv_i^\top$ with $s_1\ge\dots\ge s_{\dlat}\ge0$, $x_t^\mu=e^{-t}z^\mu+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta^\mu$ with one diffused copy per training point, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Mt-real}
M_t:=e^{-2t}\Sigma_0+\Delta_tI_{\dlat},\qquad S_t:=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu x_t^\mu x_t^{\mu\top},\qquad \E_\eta S_t=M_t,\qquad \lambda_i(M_t)=e^{-2t}s_i+\Delta_t .
\end{equation}
$M_t$ is the matrix used by the predictor of Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor}; its eigenvectors are those of $\Sigma_0$, and $\Delta_t$ is the smallest value any of its eigenvalues can take. The feature second-moment matrix $\Umat=\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top$ is formed from the same single copies (this is the matrix diagonalised in Section~\ref{sec:theory}; hence $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$), so the data-side spectrum of $\Umat$ is controlled by $S_t$, not by $M_t$; the difference is a sampling broadening quantified in Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}. The coefficients $a_1(q)=\E[\tanh'(\sqrt qz)]$, $a_\ast(q)^2=\E[\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)]-a_1(q)^2q$ are as in Section~\ref{sec:theory}. At $t=0.1$, $\Delta_t=0.1813$, $a_1(\Delta_t)^2=0.746$, $a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2=1.39\times10^{-3}$.

\begin{definition}[Latent spectral measure and effective dimensions]\label{def:deff-real}
Let $\rho_{\dlat}:=\dlat^{-1}\sum_i\delta_{s_i}$ be the empirical spectral measure of $\Sigma_0$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:q-real}
q[\rho_{\dlat}]:=\frac{\operatorname{tr}M_t}{\dlat}=e^{-2t}\!\int\! s\,\mathrm d\rho_{\dlat}(s)+\Delta_t,\qquad \hat q:=\frac{\operatorname{tr}S_t}{\dlat}=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\frac{\|x_t^\mu\|^2}{\dlat},\qquad \E\hat q=q .
\end{equation}
For $\delta>0$ define the \emph{floor count}, the \emph{effective dimension}, and the \emph{excess}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:deff-real}
d_{\mathrm{floor}}(\delta):=\#\{i:\lambda_i(M_t)\le(1+\delta)\Delta_t\},\qquad
d_{\mathrm{eff}}:=\frac{\big(\sum_i(\lambda_i(M_t)-\Delta_t)\big)^2}{\sum_i(\lambda_i(M_t)-\Delta_t)^2}=\frac{(\sum_is_i)^2}{\sum_is_i^2},\qquad
d_{\mathrm{exc}}:=\dlat-d_{\mathrm{eff}} .
\end{equation}
\end{definition}
$d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is the participation ratio of $\Sigma_0$; because $\Delta_t$ is known exactly, it involves no estimated floor. In the two-atom model, $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=(\dint S+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2)^2/(\dint S^2+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^4)$ with $S=s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2$; this tends to $\dint$ as $\signoise\to0$ but equals $76.6$ at $(\dlat,\signoise)=(200,0.5)$: a participation ratio counts a heavy excess block partially, which is why it is the wrong quantity to substitute for $\dint$ when $\signoise$ is not small. The released estimator of Section~\ref{sec:spectral-predictor} (\texttt{effective\_rank\_excess}) takes the participation ratio of $(\lambda_i-\hat\beta)_+$ with $\hat\beta$ the median of the lowest $30\%$ of eigenvalues; on the \emph{population} two-atom spectrum $\hat\beta=\beta_t^2$ and it returns $\dint$ exactly (on a one-copy finite-$\nsamp$ spectrum the noise block is MP-spread, Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}, and it does not). On the real spectra $\hat\beta$ switches from $0.72$ (CelebA, $\dlat=140$) to $0.189,0.182,0.181$ ($\dlat=160,180,200$), so the estimator coincides with $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ of \eqref{eq:deff-real} only where $\hat\beta=\Delta_t$, and its values on the two sides of that boundary measure different things. We recommend reporting $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $d_{\mathrm{floor}}$ separately (Remark~\ref{rem:xaxis-real}).

\begin{lemma}[Sampling broadening of a collapsed block]\label{lem:sampling-real}
Let $F$ be a set of $d_F$ coordinates with $z^\mu_i=0$ for all $i\in F$ and all $\mu$, $L$ its complement ($d_L=\dlat-d_F$), $H_F\in\R^{\nsamp\times d_F}$ the matrix of noises $\eta^\mu_F$ and $X_L\in\R^{\nsamp\times d_L}$ the matrix of diffused live coordinates.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $(S_t)_{FF}=\Delta_tH_F^\top H_F/\nsamp$ exactly, with $H_F$ iid $\mathcal N(0,1)$ independent of $X_L$. For $u>0$, with probability $\ge1-2e^{-u^2/2}$,
$\operatorname{spec}(S_t)_{FF}\subset\Delta_t\big[(1-\sqrt{d_F/\nsamp}-u/\sqrt{\nsamp})^2,\,(1+\sqrt{d_F/\nsamp}+u/\sqrt{\nsamp})^2\big]$.
\item[(b)] (Interlacing) $\lambda_{d_L+k}(S_t)\le\lambda_k((S_t)_{FF})$ for $k\le d_F$; the $d_F$ smallest eigenvalues of $S_t$ lie below the upper edge in (a).
\item[(c)] (Schur complement; sketched) The floor eigenvalues $\lambda$ of $S_t$ solve $\det\big(\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top(I-P_L(\lambda))H_F-\lambda\big)=0$ with $P_L(\lambda):=X_L(X_L^\top X_L-\nsamp\lambda)^{-1}X_L^\top$. Replacing $P_L(\lambda)$ by the orthogonal projector $P_L:=P_L(0)$ onto $\operatorname{col}(X_L)$ (relative error $\le\lambda/(\lambda_{\min}((S_t)_{LL})-\lambda)$), the matrix $\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top(I-P_L)H_F$ is $\Delta_t\frac{\nsamp-d_L}{\nsamp}$ times a normalized Wishart$(d_F,\nsamp-d_L)$; hence, to leading order, the floor block of $S_t$ is a Marchenko--Pastur bulk on
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mp-floor-real}
\Delta_t\Big(1-\frac{d_L}{\nsamp}\Big)\Big(1\pm\sqrt{\frac{d_F}{\nsamp-d_L}}\Big)^2 .
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
The same statements hold with $\Delta_t\to\beta_t^2$ for the null block of the two-atom model, whose null coordinates are iid $\mathcal N(0,\beta_t^2)$ independent of the signal block.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(a) $x^\mu_{t,i}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta^\mu_i$ for $i\in F$, and $\eta_F$ is independent of $(z^\mu,\eta_L)$; the singular-value bound is \citet[Cor.~7.3.3]{vershynin2018hdp} applied to $H_F$. (b) Cauchy interlacing for the principal submatrix. (c) $\det(S_t-\lambda)=\det((S_t)_{LL}-\lambda)\det\big((S_t)_{FF}-\lambda-(S_t)_{FL}((S_t)_{LL}-\lambda)^{-1}(S_t)_{LF}\big)$ and $(S_t)_{LF}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}X_L^\top H_F/\nsamp$, so the correction equals $\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top X_L(X_L^\top X_L-\nsamp\lambda)^{-1}X_L^\top H_F=\Delta_t\nsamp^{-1}H_F^\top P_L(\lambda)H_F$. $I-P_L$ is a rank-$(\nsamp-d_L)$ orthogonal projector independent of $H_F$, so $(I-P_L)H_F$ is an $(\nsamp-d_L)\times d_F$ Gaussian matrix in a rotated basis, whose normalized Gram matrix has MP edges $(1\pm\sqrt{d_F/(\nsamp-d_L)})^2$.
\end{proof}
\emph{Numbers.} CelebA $\dlat=200$, $\nsamp=1000$, $d_F\ge30$: the block alone gives $[0.68,1.38]\Delta_t$; with the projection factor $1-170/1000=0.83$ and aspect $30/830$, \eqref{eq:mp-floor-real} gives $[0.54,1.18]\Delta_t$. Synthetic null block ($d_F=195$, $d_L=5$, $\nsamp=500$): $[0.14,2.62]\beta_t^2$. Both dwarf the width-$\varepsilon$ term of Proposition~\ref{prop:pushforward-real} ($\varepsilon=0.27$ at $\pwidth=64\dlat$). The signal block ($\dint=5$, aspect $0.01$) has a sampling width of order $\sqrt{\dint/\nsamp}=0.1$, of the same order as $\varepsilon$, which is why the signal edge statistic of Section~\ref{sec:theory} is sharp while the noise-dimension block is broad.

\begin{assumption}[Gaussian equivalence on latents]\label{assump:gep-real}
As $\dlat,\nsamp,\pwidth\to\infty$ with $\pwidth>\nsamp>\dlat$ and $\varepsilon_0:=\sqrt{\dlat/\pwidth}+u/\sqrt{\pwidth}<1$, the feature second-moment matrix admits the decomposition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Usplit-real}
\Umat=\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}+\Umat^{\mathrm{res}},\qquad
\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\pwidth\,\dlat}\,WS_tW^\top,\qquad \Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\succeq0,\qquad
\operatorname{tr}\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}=a_\ast(q)^2(1+o(1)),\qquad \|\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\|=:c_+\frac{a_\ast(q)^2}{\nsamp},
\end{equation}
with $q=q[\rho_{\dlat}]$ and $c_+=O(1)$.
\end{assumption}
This is the Gaussian-equivalence decomposition used in Section~\ref{sec:theory} for the two-atom case \citep{bonnaire2025,goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality,mei2022generalization}, now asserted for diffused latent inputs and written, as the one-copy $\Umat$ requires, with the sample second moment $S_t$ (the $\eta$-averaged version with $M_t$ would have $\operatorname{rank}\Umat$ unconstrained by $\nsamp$ and no $\nsamp-\dlat$ sample count). Three things make it an assumption rather than a theorem. Real latents are not Gaussian. The pre-diffusion $\|z^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ has coefficient of variation $0.22$--$0.23$ across $\mu$ on CelebA for $\dlat\ge160$ (hence $\|x_t^\mu\|^2/\dlat$ about $0.20$), so $q$ is the average of per-sample values $q_\mu$ and every edge below is correspondingly broadened ($\hat q=q$ to relative $0.2/\sqrt{\nsamp}$). And with one copy per point the residual $r_\mu=\phi(x_t^\mu)-a_1Wx_t^\mu/\sqrt{\pwidth\dlat}$ is correlated with $Wx_t^\mu$: since $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$, $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\dlat$ and $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}\succeq0$, one has $\operatorname{range}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}\subset\operatorname{range}\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$, so $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$ is not an isotropic Wishart independent of $W$, and the Marchenko--Pastur value $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ is not the right reference for $c_+$ (Section~\ref{sec:theory}, F8). We carry $c_+$ as a fitted $O(1)$ constant; on the synthetic sweep it is $\dlat$-dependent (Definition~\ref{def:ratio-real}).

\begin{proposition}[The data-side spectrum of $\Umat$ is a pushforward of the spectrum of $S_t$]\label{prop:pushforward-real}
Under Assumption~\ref{assump:gep-real}, with probability at least $1-2e^{-u^2/2}$ over $W$,
\begin{align}
\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})&=\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\,\lambda_i(S_t)\,(1+\epsilon_i),\qquad |\epsilon_i|\le\varepsilon:=2\varepsilon_0+\varepsilon_0^2,\qquad i\le\dlat,\label{eq:push-real}\\
\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})&\le\lambda_i(\Umat)\le\lambda_i(\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}})+c_+\frac{a_\ast(q)^2}{\nsamp},\qquad i\le\pwidth .\label{eq:weyl-real}
\end{align}
Consequently the $\dlat$ largest eigenvalues of $\Umat$ are, to relative accuracy $\varepsilon+c_+(a_\ast^2/a_1^2)(\dlat/\nsamp)/\lambda_i(S_t)$, the pushforward $\big(\lambda\mapsto a_1(q)^2\lambda/\dlat\big)_\#$ of the spectrum of $S_t$, which is the spectrum of $M_t$ broadened by one-copy sampling (Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}). The next $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues form the sample bulk of $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$, with mass $a_\ast(q)^2-\operatorname{tr}(P\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}P)\in[a_\ast^2(1-c_+\dlat/\nsamp),a_\ast^2]$ ($P$ the projector on the top $\dlat$ eigenvectors), mean in $[a_\ast^2(1-c_+\dlat/\nsamp),a_\ast^2]/(\nsamp-\dlat)$ (whose $\dlat/\nsamp\to0$ limit is the $a_\ast^2/\nsamp$ of Section~\ref{sec:theory}), and upper edge $c_+a_\ast(q)^2/\nsamp$; the remaining $\pwidth-\nsamp$ are zero. The trace identity $\sum_i\lambda_i(\Umat)=a_1(q)^2\hat q+a_\ast(q)^2=\E[\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)]$ holds to $o(1)$.
\end{proposition}
\emph{Special cases.} (a) Two-atom $\rho_{\dlat}$ with atoms $\alpha_t^2$ ($\dint$ of them) and $\beta_t^2$ ($\dlat-\dint$): \eqref{eq:push-real} with Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real} gives the signal block at $a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2/\dlat$ with sampling width of order $\sqrt{\dint/\nsamp}$ and the noise-dimension bulk on $a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2(1-\dint/\nsamp)(1\pm\sqrt{(\dlat-\dint)/(\nsamp-\dint)})^2/\dlat$, median $\approx a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$; this is the four-bulk statement of Section~\ref{sec:theory}, whose noise-dimension medians ($5.22\to9.22$ in code units) the formula reproduces (F3). (b) A general $\rho_{\dlat}$ gives a data-side \emph{continuum} on $[a_1^2(e^{-2t}s_{\dlat}+\Delta_t)/\dlat,\;a_1^2(e^{-2t}s_1+\Delta_t)/\dlat]$ together with a \emph{floor bulk} of $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(0)$ eigenvalues with median $\approx a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$ and relative half-width $\approx2\sqrt{d_{\mathrm{floor}}/\nsamp}$ by \eqref{eq:mp-floor-real}; the noise-dimension bulk is replaced by the part of the spectrum near the floor. (c) For CelebA at $\dlat=200$, $\nsamp=1000$, the additive term in \eqref{eq:weyl-real} is $0.12\,c_+$ of the floor median; for the synthetic noise-dimension edge at the $t$ where $q=0.33$ ($\beta_t^2=0.268$, $\dlat=200$, $\nsamp=500$) it is $0.011\,c_+$.

\begin{proof}
(i) The nonzero eigenvalues of $WS_tW^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ equal those of $\dlat^{-1}S_t^{1/2}GS_t^{1/2}$ with $G:=W^\top W/\pwidth$, since $AB$ and $BA$ share their nonzero spectrum. By \citet[Cor.~7.3.3]{vershynin2018hdp}, $\sqrt{\pwidth}-\sqrt{\dlat}-u\le s_{\min}(W)\le s_{\max}(W)\le\sqrt{\pwidth}+\sqrt{\dlat}+u$ with probability $\ge1-2e^{-u^2/2}$, hence $\operatorname{spec}G\subset[(1-\varepsilon_0)^2,(1+\varepsilon_0)^2]$ and $\|G-I_{\dlat}\|\le\varepsilon$; $\varepsilon_0<1$ makes $G\succ0$. Ostrowski's theorem for the congruence $S_t^{1/2}GS_t^{1/2}$ gives $\lambda_i(S_t^{1/2}GS_t^{1/2})=\theta_i\lambda_i(S_t)$ with $\theta_i\in[\lambda_{\min}(G),\lambda_{\max}(G)]\subset[1-\varepsilon,1+\varepsilon]$. (ii) is Weyl's inequality for the positive-semidefinite perturbation $\Umat^{\mathrm{res}}$. The counts follow from $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=\dlat$ and $\operatorname{rank}\Umat\le\nsamp$. Mass: by \eqref{eq:weyl-real} the residual trace absorbed by the top $\dlat$ eigenvalues is at most $\dlat c_+a_\ast^2/\nsamp$. Trace: $\operatorname{tr}\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}=a_1^2\operatorname{tr}(S_tG)/\dlat=a_1^2\hat q(1+O(\varepsilon))$ and \eqref{eq:Usplit-real}. The identification of the intermediate $\nsamp-\dlat$ eigenvalues with the residual bulk and of its edge with $c_+a_\ast^2/\nsamp$ rests on the assumption and is the sketched part.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Timescales and buffer ratio for a general spectrum]\label{def:ratio-real}
Per-mode gradient-flow timescales are $\tau_i=1/\lambda_i(\Umat)$ (Section~\ref{sec:theory}). Fix $\theta\in(0,1]$ and let $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ be the largest level such that the eigendirections of $M_t$ at or above it carry a fraction $\theta$ of the latent variance, $\sum_{i:\lambda_i(M_t)\ge\lambda_{(\theta)}}s_i\ge\theta\sum_is_i$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:taus-real}
\taugen^{(\theta)}:=\frac{\dlat}{a_1(q)^2\,\lambda_{(\theta)}},\qquad
\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}:=\frac1{\lambda_+^{\mathrm{samp}}}=\frac{\nsamp}{c_+\,a_\ast(q)^2},\qquad
R^{(\theta)}[\rho_{\dlat}]:=\frac{\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}}{\taugen^{(\theta)}}=\frac{\nsamp}{c_+\dlat}\;\frac{a_1(q)^2}{a_\ast(q)^2}\;\lambda_{(\theta)} .
\end{equation}
\end{definition}
```
