```latex
For the two-atom model, $\lambda_{(\theta)}=\alpha_t^2$ and $\taugen^{(\theta)}=1/\lambda^{\mathrm{signal}}_{\min}$ (the statistic of Section~\ref{sec:theory}) only for $\theta$ below the signal variance share $\dint S/(\dint S+(\dlat-\dint)\signoise^2)$, which is $0.22$ at $(\dlat,\signoise)=(200,0.5)$ and $0.05$ at $\signoise=1$; for larger $\theta$, $\lambda_{(\theta)}=\beta_t^2$ and $\taugen^{(\theta)}$ is the noise-dimension timescale. $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ is the top of the sample bulk, the onset statistic on which the buffer ratio grows monotonically with $\dlat$ in the synthetic sweep. On a continuous spectrum there is no canonical $\theta$; the results below are stated so that $\theta$ drops out. No count of coordinates appears in \eqref{eq:taus-real}: $\dlat$ enters through the fan-in $1/\dlat$, through $q[\rho_{\dlat}]$, and through $c_+$. This is the general-spectrum form of the observation in Section~\ref{sec:theory} that modes relax in parallel under a diagonal flow, so that the number of modes between two eigenvalues does not lengthen any traversal.

\emph{What the synthetic sweep says about $c_+$.} With the Marchenko--Pastur guess $c_+=(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ and $\pwidth=64\dlat$, the sweep $\dlat=5\to200$ ($q\,2.76\to0.33$) gives an $a_\ast^2$ ratio of $16.5$ and a $c_+$ ratio of $3.5$, i.e.\ a predicted onset growth of $58\times$, $R$ growth of $5\times$, and $\taugen$ growth of $(200/5)(0.180/0.623)=11.6\times$, against the measured $221\times$, $19\times$, and $8.1\times$ (Section~\ref{sec:theory}). Thus $c_+$ on clustered data carries a further $\approx4\times$ $\dlat$-dependence over the sweep. The synthetic monotone growth of $R$ therefore supports the sign of the $q$-mechanism, not its magnitude, and every real-data ratio below is taken under the explicit hypothesis
\begin{equation}\label{eq:Hc-real}
(\mathrm H_c)\qquad c_+\ \text{is constant across the compared widths,}
\end{equation}
which is what ``$c_+$ cancels'' means.

\begin{lemma}[A collapsed coordinate sits exactly on the floor]\label{lem:dead-real}
Suppose coordinate $i$ of the encoder means vanishes on the training set, $z^\mu_i=0$ for all $\mu$ (posterior collapse, \citealp{lucas2019dontblame}). Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $\Sigma_0e_i=0$ and $M_te_i=\Delta_te_i$: $\lambda=\Delta_t$ exactly, the minimum possible eigenvalue of $M_t$, with eigenvector $e_i$.
\item[(b)] $x^\mu_{t,i}=\sqrt{\Delta_t}\,\eta_i$, and the exact score of the diffused empirical measure \citep{scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization,biroli2024dynamical} has $s^\ast_i(x_t)=-x_{t,i}/\Delta_t$, a linear function of $x_{t,i}$ alone.
\item[(c)] The coordinate contributes exactly $\Delta_t/\dlat$ to $q$.
\item[(d)] Under Assumption~\ref{assump:gep-real}, the $d_F$ collapsed coordinates contribute to $\Umat^{\mathrm{lin}}$ the floor bulk $a_1(q)^2\lambda(1+\epsilon)/\dlat$ with $\lambda$ in \eqref{eq:mp-floor-real}, the slowest data-side modes; the median timescale is $\tau_{\mathrm{floor}}=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\Delta_t)$ and the slowest floor mode is slower by the further factor $\big[(1-d_L/\nsamp)(1-\sqrt{d_F/(\nsamp-d_L)})^2\big]^{-1}$.
\item[(e)] For a linear score model $s(x)=Bx$ trained by gradient flow on $\E\|Bx_t-s^\ast(x_t)\|^2$, column $i$ of $B$ evolves independently of all other entries: $B_{ii}(T)=-\Delta_t^{-1}+(B_{ii}(0)+\Delta_t^{-1})e^{-2\Delta_tT}$ and $B_{ji}(T)=B_{ji}(0)e^{-2\Delta_tT}$ for $j\ne i$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(a) $(\Sigma_0)_{ij}=\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu z^\mu_iz^\mu_j=0$ for every $j$. (b) The diffused empirical measure is $\nsamp^{-1}\sum_\mu\mathcal N(e^{-t}z^\mu,\Delta_tI)$ and every centre has zero $i$-th component, so the softmax weights $w_\mu(x)\propto\exp(-\|x-e^{-t}z^\mu\|^2/2\Delta_t)$ do not depend on $x_{t,i}$ (the $x_{t,i}^2$ term is common to all $\mu$), the $i$-th marginal is $\mathcal N(0,\Delta_t)$ independent of the other coordinates, and $s^\ast_i=\sum_\mu w_\mu(x)(0-x_{t,i})/\Delta_t=-x_{t,i}/\Delta_t$. (c) is \eqref{eq:q-real}. (d) is \eqref{eq:push-real} with Lemma~\ref{lem:sampling-real}(c). (e) The gradient with respect to column $i$ is $2\E[(Bx_t-s^\ast)x_{t,i}]=2\big(BM_te_i-\E[s^\ast x_{t,i}]\big)$. Here $M_te_i=\Delta_te_i$; for $j\ne i$, $s^\ast_j$ depends only on $x_{t,-i}$, so $\E[s^\ast_jx_{t,i}]=\E[s^\ast_j]\E[x_{t,i}]=0$; and $\E[s^\ast_ix_{t,i}]=-\E[x_{t,i}^2]/\Delta_t=-1$. Hence $\frac{d}{dT}(Be_i)=-2\Delta_t\big(Be_i+e_i/\Delta_t\big)$, whose solution is as stated.
\end{proof}
The contrast between (d) and (e) is a normalization: the RFNN rate carries the fan-in factor $1/\dlat$ of $\phi(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$, the linear model does not. Ratios of timescales are normalization-free. Note that (a) and (c) are exact statements about $M_t$; the one-copy eigenvalue of a collapsed coordinate is not exactly $\Delta_t$ but MP-spread around it, (d).

\begin{proposition}[Effect of a block of $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ collapsed coordinates]\label{prop:deadblock-real}
Let a latent representation have $d_0$ live coordinates with spectrum $\rho^{\mathrm{live}}$ and $q_0=q[\rho^{\mathrm{live}}]\le50$, and append $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ collapsed coordinates, $\dlat=d_0+n_{\mathrm{dead}}$. Then, under Assumption~\ref{assump:gep-real}, \eqref{eq:Hc-real}, and for any $\theta$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $q(\dlat)=\Delta_t+\dfrac{d_0}{\dlat}\,(q_0-\Delta_t)$, strictly decreasing in $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ with limit $\Delta_t$; $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ are unchanged; $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(0)$ increases by $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$.
\item[(ii)] $a_1(q)$ is strictly decreasing in $q$, so $a_1(q(\dlat))^2$ increases with $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ toward $a_1(\Delta_t)^2$; $a_\ast(q)^2$ is increasing in $q$ on $[\Delta_t,50]$ (verified numerically), so $a_\ast(q(\dlat))^2$ decreases toward $a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$.
\item[(iii)] Every live data-side eigenvalue is rescaled by $\dfrac{a_1(q(\dlat))^2}{a_1(q_0)^2}\dfrac{d_0}{\dlat}$, and $\taugen^{(\theta)}(\dlat)=\dfrac{\dlat}{a_1(q(\dlat))^2\lambda_{(\theta)}}\ge\dfrac{\dlat}{a_1(\Delta_t)^2\lambda_{(\theta)}}$ grows at least linearly in $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$; the floor bulk has median timescale $\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\Delta_t)$, slower than $\taugen^{(\theta)}$ by $\lambda_{(\theta)}/\Delta_t$, and its lower edge falls by the further factor $(1-d_0/\nsamp)(1-\sqrt{n_{\mathrm{dead}}/(\nsamp-d_0)})^2$.
\item[(iv)] $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}(\dlat)=\nsamp/(c_+a_\ast(q(\dlat))^2)$ increases with $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ but is bounded: $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}(\dlat)/\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}(d_0)\le a_\ast(q_0)^2/a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$.
\item[(v)] $\dfrac{R^{(\theta)}(\dlat)}{R^{(\theta)}(d_0)}=\dfrac{d_0}{\dlat}\,\dfrac{a_1(q)^2}{a_1(q_0)^2}\,\dfrac{a_\ast(q_0)^2}{a_\ast(q)^2}$, independent of $\theta$ and $\nsamp$ (and of $c_+$ by \eqref{eq:Hc-real}); and $R^{(\theta)}(\dlat)\le\dfrac{\nsamp\lambda_{(\theta)}}{c_+\dlat}\,\dfrac{a_1(\Delta_t)^2}{a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2}=536\,\dfrac{\nsamp\lambda_{(\theta)}}{c_+\dlat}\to0$ as $n_{\mathrm{dead}}\to\infty$.
\end{enumerate}
Collapsed coordinates therefore change the buffer ratio only through $q$, by a bounded factor, and eventually erode it. The count $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ never appears except through $q$ and the MP width of the floor bulk.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) Lemma~\ref{lem:dead-real}(c) applied $n_{\mathrm{dead}}$ times; $\lambda_{(\theta)}$ depends only on the live $s_i$. (ii) $a_1(q)=\E[\operatorname{sech}^2(\sqrt qz)]$; for $q'>q$ and $z\ne0$, $\operatorname{sech}^2(\sqrt{q'}|z|)<\operatorname{sech}^2(\sqrt q|z|)$ because $\operatorname{sech}^2$ is strictly decreasing on $(0,\infty)$; integrate. Monotonicity of $a_\ast^2$ on $[\Delta_t,50]$ is checked by $200$-node Gauss--Hermite quadrature on a $5000$-point grid (minimum increment $3.3\times10^{-5}$; sample values $0.0014,0.031,0.036,0.043,0.048,0.054,0.066,0.074$ at $q=0.181,1.11,1.25,1.47,1.62,1.83,2.27,2.58$; $a_\ast^2\to1-2/\pi$ as $q\to\infty$); all sweeps in this paper have $q\le2.6$. The limits are quadrature values at $q=\Delta_t$. (iii)--(v) are substitutions into \eqref{eq:push-real}, \eqref{eq:mp-floor-real} and \eqref{eq:taus-real}; the bounds use $a_1(q)^2\le a_1(\Delta_t)^2$ and $a_\ast(q)^2\ge a_\ast(\Delta_t)^2$ for $q\in[\Delta_t,50]$, both from (ii).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Flat effective dimension: what the mechanism predicts; conjectural]\label{cor:flatdeff-real}
Treat the RFNN surrogate at $t=0.1$ with unit-variance first-layer weights as a proxy for the depth-5, width-1024 MLP actually trained (Remark~\ref{rem:blunt-real}), and assume \eqref{eq:Hc-real}. On CelebA ($t=0.1$, $\nsamp=1000$) the measured spectra give $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0=252.5,255.2,260.4$ at $\dlat=160,180,200$ (increments $0.35,0.14,0.26$ per added coordinate against a live mean $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0/d_{\mathrm{eff}}=2.8$--$3.0$), estimator $d_{\mathrm{eff}}=89.5,90.3,87.9$ (with $\hat\beta=0.189,0.182,0.181$; these equal \eqref{eq:deff-real} up to the $8\times10^{-3}$ floor offset at $\dlat=160$), $\lambda_{\min}-\Delta_t=6\times10^{-5},2\times10^{-5},1\times10^{-5}$, and the median of the lowest $30\%$ of eigenvalues within $2.4\times10^{-4}$ of $\Delta_t$ for $\dlat\ge180$, so at least $15\%$ of coordinates are on the floor. The live spectrum is flat: the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:deadblock-real} hold to this accuracy. With $q=1.473,1.342,1.247$, $a_1(q)^2=0.286,0.305,0.320$, $a_\ast(q)^2=0.0434,0.0391,0.0359$, the proposition predicts, relative to $\dlat=160$,
\[
\taugen\times1.06,\;1.12;\qquad \taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}\times1.11,\;1.21;\qquad R\times1.05,\;1.08\qquad(\dlat=180,\,200).
\]
If the memorization-fraction curve depends on training only through the sample bulk, uniformly rescaled by $a_\ast(q)^2$, then every iso-memorization hitting time scales by $a_\ast(q_{160})^2/a_\ast(q_d)^2$ and the final fraction at budget $T_{\max}$ is $F_d(T_{\max})=F_{160}\big(T_{\max}\,a_\ast(q_d)^2/a_\ast(q_{160})^2\big)$, i.e.\ the $\dlat=160$ curve read at $4.51$M and $4.14$M steps: $0.595$ and $0.559$. Observed: hitting-time ratios $160\to180$ are $1.17$--$1.33$ (thresholds $0.01$--$0.5$) against the predicted $1.11$; $160\to200$ they are $1.33,1.26,1.37,1.41$ for thresholds $0.01,0.05,0.10,0.25$ against $1.21$; final fractions are $0.608,0.509,0.448$. On CIFAR-10 ($\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0=281$--$299$ for $\dlat=160$--$260$; floor reached to $1.3\times10^{-3}$ at $\dlat=220$ and to $10^{-4}$ for $\dlat\ge240$; $q\;1.621\to1.108$, $a_\ast^2\;0.0480\to0.0312$) the same construction predicts $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}\times1.54$, $\taugen\times1.26$, $R\times1.22$ over $160\to260$, and final fractions $0.557,0.552,0.517,0.505,0.472$ at $\dlat=180,\dots,260$ against observed $0.456,0.346,0.213,0.183,0.125$; observed hitting times $160\to260$ are $\times1.6$ (threshold $0.01$), $\times2.4$ (threshold $0.05$; $95\%$ CI $\pm1.7$M steps at $\dlat=260$), $\times2.7$ (threshold $0.10$; one of five seeds censored).
\end{corollary}
The absolute $q$ depends on the first-layer weight scale of the surrogate (unit variance here; PyTorch fan-in initialization gives $q/3$). With $q\to q/3$ the CelebA $160\to200$ factors become $\taumem\times1.33$, $R\times1.14$ and the CIFAR-10 $160\to260$ factor $\taumem\times1.93$: the sign of every prediction is scale-free, the magnitude is not. The proposition's hypotheses hold only marginally for the real model ($\pwidth=1024$, $\nsamp=1000$, $\dlat\le260$, $\varepsilon_0\approx0.4$--$0.5$), and its features are learned; hence the corollary is a conjectural prediction, not a consequence.

\begin{remark}[What the mechanism can and cannot account for]\label{rem:blunt-real}
When $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ is flat, added coordinates change $q$ and the floor bulk, not any buffer count. The released $\texttt{buffer\_proxy}=\dlat-\mathrm{round}(d_{\mathrm{eff}})=71,90,112$ enters no timescale. What the $q$-mechanism does deliver is the sign: hitting times lengthen and the final fraction falls as coordinates collapse. Quantitatively it accounts for $\log1.21/\log\{1.41,1.34,1.26\}=0.55$--$0.82$ of the log-slowdown of CelebA hitting times, for $\log(0.559/0.608)/\log(0.448/0.608)=0.28$ of the log-decline of the CelebA final fraction, and on CIFAR-10 for $\log1.54/\log2.55=0.46$ of the hitting-time log-slowdown and $\log(0.472/0.600)/\log(0.125/0.600)=0.15$ of the final-fraction log-decline. The remainder is outside the surrogate. The real-data score model is a depth-5, width-1024 MLP with sinusoidal time features, trained by SGD (learning rate $10^{-3}$, momentum $0.8$) across $t\in[0.01,3]$ on $\nsamp=1000$ encoder means; $\pwidth=1024>\nsamp=1000$, so an $\nsamp-\dlat$ residual bulk is only marginally accommodated, and its first-layer features are learned, so the RFNN quantities are a surrogate evaluated at one $t$. The residual bulk of clustered real features is not Marchenko--Pastur (and $c_+$ is $\dlat$-dependent even on the synthetic sweep); and the decoder's response to imperfectly learned floor coordinates (the slowest data-side modes) is not modelled, although the FID worsening from $38.9$ ($\dlat=140$) to $47$--$50$ ($\dlat=160$--$200$) suggests it is not inert. Over $\dlat=20$--$120$ on CelebA, $q$ is flat ($1.71$--$1.84$, $a_\ast^2=0.051$--$0.055$) because $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0$ grows by $\approx2$ per coordinate; there the surrogate predicts no change in $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ and $\taugen\propto\dlat$, and the observed rise of memorization belongs to the decoder identity-threshold regime of Section~\ref{sec:real-data}. Finally, memorization is not a function of $q$ alone across datasets: at $q\approx1.25$--$1.29$ the final fractions are $0.45$ (CelebA) and $0.21$ (CIFAR-10). The pixel-space NN-ratio has no ambient-dimension drift across the $\dlat$ sweep (decoded images have fixed dimension), but a decoder-dependent drift of its baseline has not been checked.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Encoder means versus posterior samples]\label{rem:samples-real}
The latents here are encoder means, for which a collapsed coordinate is identically zero. Had the diffusion model been trained on posterior samples $z\sim\mathcal N(\mu(x),\operatorname{diag}\sigma^2(x))$, a collapsed coordinate ($\mu_i\equiv0$, $\sigma_i\equiv1$) would have $s_i=1$ and $\lambda_i(M_t)=e^{-2t}+\Delta_t=1$: it would be an isotropic noise-dimension coordinate with $\signoise=1$, the isotropic control of Section~\ref{sec:theory}, rather than a floor coordinate, and the floor bulk would move up by $1/\Delta_t\approx5.5$ at $t=0.1$. Then $q=\Delta_t+[d_0(q_0-\Delta_t)+n_{\mathrm{dead}}e^{-2t}]/\dlat$ exceeds the mean-latent value, so $a_\ast(q)^2$ is larger and $\taumem^{\mathrm{onset}}$ shorter, while $a_1(q)^2$ is smaller and $\taugen$ longer; $R$ shrinks by both factors. The theory therefore predicts earlier onset memorization for sampled than for mean latents at large $\dlat$, with the caveat that the training latents are then a stochastic set, so the empirical-score target of Lemma~\ref{lem:dead-real}(b) and the memorization criterion itself change, not only $q$. Untested.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Reporting]\label{rem:xaxis-real}
For every real-data run we recommend reporting, alongside $\dlat$: $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (participation ratio of $\Sigma_0$, floor at the known $\Delta_t$), $d_{\mathrm{floor}}(\delta)$ with $\delta=2\times10^{-3}$ (the CIFAR-10 $\dlat=220$ floor is reached only to $1.3\times10^{-3}$; to $\le10^{-4}$ for CIFAR-10 $\dlat\ge240$ and CelebA $\dlat\ge180$), $\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_0$, and $q[\rho_{\dlat}]$; and plotting memorization against $d_{\mathrm{eff}}$ and $q$ as well as against $\dlat$. On the present sweeps the points with $\dlat\ge160$ (CelebA) and $\dlat\ge220$ (CIFAR-10) differ from one another in $q$ and $d_{\mathrm{floor}}$ only, so any effect of $\dlat$ there is a $q$ effect, a $c_+$ effect, or is outside the theory.
\end{remark}
```



---

# Piece 8: Regime boundaries and scope: where the four-bulk theory applies, and what it predicts at the edges (revised after referee reports)

## Statements

### `def:scope-ratios` (definition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Data model of Section 2 (k centers of norm s in a d_int subspace, xi ~ N(0, diag(sig_sig^2 I, sig_perp^2 I)), random rotation Q); Diffused x_t = e^{-t} x + sqrt(Delta_t) eta; U is the eta-averaged feature second moment (code: compute_U, 50 Monte Carlo draws)

```latex
\begin{definition}[Aspect ratios, null-data fraction, the two feature second moments, onset statistic]\label{def:scope-ratios}
Let $D:=\dlat-\dint$, $\psi_n:=\nsamp/\dlat$, $\psi_p:=\pwidth/\dlat$, $\psi:=D/\nsamp$, $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$, and write $\alpha_t^2=e^{-2t}(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)+\Delta_t$ (mean signal eigenvalue of $M_t$) and $\beta_t^2=e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t$ (null eigenvalue of $M_t$).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] The \emph{null-data fraction} $\rho_\perp(t):=e^{-2t}\signoise^2/\beta_t^2\in[0,1)$ is the share of null-block variance that comes from data rather than from diffusion noise.
\item[(ii)] For the population second moment $M_t$ the pre-activation variance obeys the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-q}
q=\frac{\operatorname{tr}M_t}{\dlat}=\beta_t^2+\frac{\dint(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)}{\dlat},\qquad q_\infty:=\lim_{\dlat\to\infty}q=\beta_t^2 .
\end{equation}
The empirical trace differs by $O(\nsamp^{-1/2})$, and the per-neuron variance $w_i^\top M_tw_i/\dlat$ fluctuates around $q$ ($\chi^2$-type, relative width $O(\dlat^{-1/2})$); by Jensen on the concave $q\mapsto\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)$ this makes measured traces slightly low at small $\dlat$ (3\% at $\dlat=10$).
\item[(iii)] \emph{Two feature second moments.} $\Umat:=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\eta[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ is the $\eta$-averaged object of the notation; it governs the gradient-flow ODE and is what the saved spectra estimate (Monte Carlo over $50$ draws of $\eta$). $\Umat^{(1)}:=\Phi\Phi^\top/\nsamp$, with $\Phi=[\phi(x_t^1),\dots,\phi(x_t^\nsamp)]$ for a \emph{single} draw of $\eta$, is the single-draw Gram. All spectral statements below are about $\Umat$ unless $\Umat^{(1)}$ is named.
\item[(iv)] $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ is the largest eigenvalue of $\Umat$ outside the span of the $\dlat$ Gaussian-equivalent linear modes, i.e.\ the top of the residual (``sample'') bulk; $\taumem^{\rm onset}:=1/\lambda_{\rm onset}$ and $\taugen:=1/\lambda^{\rm sig}_{\min}$. For the RFNN these are spectral proxies: the RFNN runs log no sample-based memorization.
\item[(v)] For the null block, with $\hat C_\perp=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\xi^\mu_\perp\xi^{\mu\top}_\perp$ (rank $\min(D,\nsamp)$), $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=e^{-2t}\hat C_\perp+\Delta_t I_D$ and $\Sigma_{t,\perp}=\beta_t^2I_D$, the \emph{sample-specificity} of the converged linear null score is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-epsdef}
\varepsilon_\perp^2:=\frac{\E\|\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2}{\|\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2}.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Identity \eqref{eq:scope-q}: $\operatorname{tr}M_t=e^{-2t}(s^2+\dint\sigsig^2+D\signoise^2)+\dlat\Delta_t=\dint\alpha_t^2+D\beta_t^2$, since the $k$ centers of norm $s$ contribute $s^2$ to the trace of $\hat C$ (population mixture second moment) and $Q$ preserves traces. Divide by $\dlat$. The $O(\nsamp^{-1/2})$ empirical correction is the CLT for $\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\|x^\mu\|^2$; the neuron-variance statement is $w_i\sim N(0,I_{\dlat})$ and $\operatorname{Var}(w^\top Mw)=2\operatorname{tr}(M^2)$.
```

### `asm:scope-standing` (assumption, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Stated hypotheses H1-H5

```latex
\begin{assumption}[Four-bulk regime]\label{asm:scope-standing}
The spectral statements of this section are made under:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(H1)] \emph{Frozen features.} $W$ is fixed at initialization; only $A$ is trained, from $A(0)=0$, by gradient flow on score matching at fixed $t$, so each eigenmode of $\Umat$ decays independently at rate $\lambda_i$.
\item[(H2)] \emph{Ordering.} $\dint<\dlat<\nsamp<\pwidth$.
\item[(H3)] \emph{Spiked-plus-atom spectrum with data-dominated null block.} $\rho_\Sigma$ consists of $\dint$ finite-rank spikes of mean $\alpha_0^2$ (the $k$ centers in the $\dint$-subspace give distinct spike locations; measured spread $\approx2.3\times$) and one atom $\signoise^2$ of weight $D/\dlat$; and $\rho_\perp(t)\ge\tfrac12$, i.e.\ $e^{-2t}\signoise^2\ge\Delta_t$.
\item[(H4)] \emph{Gaussian equivalence at leading order} \citep{goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality}: $\phi(x)=a_1(q)\,Wx/\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}+a_*(q)\,g(x)$ with $g$ uncorrelated with $x$, $a_1(q)=\E[\tanh'(\sqrt q z)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\tanh(\sqrt q z)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$; the cross-block part of the empirical $M_t$ is neglected (block-diagonal approximation).
\item[(H5)] \emph{Separation.} The noise-dim block lies at least one decade above $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ (quantified in Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}).
\end{itemize}
Under (H1)--(H5) the leading-order block positions (block \emph{means}) are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-edges}
\lambda^{\rm sig}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\lambda^{\rm nd}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\bar\lambda^{\rm samp}:=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp}\ \text{(residual mass per training point)},
\end{equation}
with the trace check, valid for block means to leading order in $\dlat/\nsamp$ and $1/\nsamp$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-trace}
\dint\lambda^{\rm sig}+D\lambda^{\rm nd}+a_*(q)^2\approx\E[\tanh(\sqrt q z)^2].
\end{equation}
The residual mass $a_*(q)^2$ is shared between the residual bulk (measured share $0.5$--$0.7$ on the repo spectra) and $O(a_*^2/\nsamp)$ per linear mode; the residual bulk is heavy-tailed, so its median is not a location statistic and the check must be done with means.
\end{assumption}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Not a claim; \eqref{eq:scope-trace} follows from \eqref{eq:scope-q}: $\dint a_1^2\alpha_t^2/\dlat+Da_1^2\beta_t^2/\dlat=a_1^2q$ and $a_1^2q+a_*^2=\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)$ by definition of $a_*$. Measured (block means, $\signoise=0.5$, code units$/\pwidth$): $0.456$ vs $0.470$ ($\dlat=10$), $0.2915$ vs $0.2974$ ($40$), $0.2092$ vs $0.2093$ ($200$); noise-dim and signal totals match $a_1^2\beta_t^2D/\dlat$ and $a_1^2\alpha_t^2\dint/\dlat$ to $1$--$2\%$.
```

### `prop:scope-counts` (proposition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: H1 frozen features; H4 Gaussian equivalence (block-diagonal); p > n; Fact F1 (cliff at n) is a property of the single-draw Gram; the saved spectra are 50-draw averages and show no cliff (verified on results_rfnn_exp2v3 eigenvalues_pre.npy)

```latex
\begin{proposition}[Block counts for the two feature second moments]\label{prop:scope-counts}
Assume (H1), (H4) and $\pwidth>\nsamp$.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] \emph{Single-draw Gram.} $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}=\nsamp$ a.s., and its non-zero spectrum splits as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-counts}
N_{\rm sig}=\dint,\qquad N_{\rm nd}=\min(\dlat,\nsamp)-\dint,\qquad N_{\rm samp}=(\nsamp-\dlat)_+,\qquad N_{\rm null}=\pwidth-\nsamp ,
\end{equation}
with a hard cliff at index $\nsamp$; the residual bulk is empty for $\psi_n\le1$.
\item[(b)] \emph{$\eta$-averaged $\Umat$.} The linear part is $a_1(q)^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\dlat\pwidth)$ with $\hat M_t=e^{-2t}\hat C+\Delta_tI_{\dlat}$ of full rank $\dlat$ for every $\dlat$. Hence $N_{\rm sig}=\dint$ and $N_{\rm nd}=D$ for all $\dlat$: for $D\le\nsamp$ all $D$ noise-dim modes lie in the data-broadened band of \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin}, and for $D>\nsamp$ exactly $\nsamp$ of them do while $D-\nsamp$ sit on the diffusion floor $a_1(q)^2\Delta_t(1\pm\psi_p^{-1/2})^2/\dlat$. The residual part $\frac{a_*^2}{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[g(x_t^\mu)g(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ has rank $>\nsamp$: there is no cliff at index $\nsamp$ and no rank-null block, only a soft residual tail (measured $e_{\nsamp-1}/e_{\nsamp}=1.32$ at $\dlat=40$, $1.73$ at $\dlat=200$, $0.3\%$ of the trace beyond index $\nsamp$).
\item[(c)] \emph{Eigenvectors.} In both cases the noise-dim eigenvectors are $W_\perp v/\|W_\perp v\|+O(\psi_p^{-1/2})$ with $v$ ranging over the eigenvectors of the empirical noised null second moment; for $\Umat$ that matrix is $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$, whose non-floor eigenvectors are those of $\hat C_\perp$ (rank $\min(D,\nsamp)$), so they are determined by the training noise vectors $\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) Under (H4), $\Phi=a_1(q)WX_t/\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}+a_*(q)G$ with $X_t\in\R^{\dlat\times\nsamp}$ a single draw; $\operatorname{rank}(WX_t)=\operatorname{rank}X_t=\min(\dlat,\nsamp)$ a.s.\ (the $\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$ term makes $X_t$ generic; $W$ has full column rank since $\pwidth>\dlat$), of which $\dint$ directions carry the signal spikes. $\Phi$ has $\nsamp$ columns, so $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}\le\nsamp$, with equality a.s.\ because $G$ is generic; the residual therefore adds $\nsamp-\min(\dlat,\nsamp)$ directions and $\pwidth-\nsamp$ are annihilated. (b) $\E_\eta[x_tx_t^\top]=e^{-2t}xx^\top+\Delta_tI$, so the $\eta$-average of the linear Gram is $a_1^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\dlat\pwidth)$ with $\hat M_t\succeq\Delta_tI$ of full rank; its null block is $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=e^{-2t}\signoise^2\Xi\Xi^\top/\nsamp+\Delta_tI_D$ with $\Xi\in\R^{D\times\nsamp}$ standard Gaussian, whose $\min(D,\nsamp)$ non-trivial eigenvalues are $e^{-2t}\signoise^2m_i+\Delta_t$ with $m_i$ Wishart eigenvalues and whose remaining $(D-\nsamp)_+$ eigenvalues equal $\Delta_t$ exactly. The residual $\eta$-average is a sum of $\nsamp$ PSD matrices each of rank $>1$ (exact $\E_\eta$: full; $50$-draw MC: $\le50$), so no cliff occurs. (c) With $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth=I_D+O(\psi_p^{-1/2})$ the eigenpairs of $W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top$ are $(\lambda_i\pwidth,\,W_\perp v_i)$ to that order; the isotropic $\Delta_t$ shift does not change the eigenvectors of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ relative to $\hat C_\perp$.
```

### `prop:scope-nullspec` (proposition, **proved**)

Hypotheses: Linear null-block score model s_perp(x) = B x_perp (exact for the population null score, fact F12); Denoising score matching at fixed t over the n training points, gradient flow; Block-diagonal approximation of the empirical hat M_t (signal-null cross covariance of order alpha beta / sqrt n neglected); Null noise xi_perp ~ N(0, sig_perp^2 I_D), cluster means with zero null component; Proportional limit D, n -> infinity with D/n -> psi for the MP form; finite-size version holds with the empirical spectral measure

```latex
\begin{proposition}[Sample-specificity of the learned null score]\label{prop:scope-nullspec}
Model the null block of the score linearly, $s_\perp(x)=B_\perp x_\perp$, trained by gradient flow on denoising score matching over the $\nsamp$ training points at fixed $t$, with the block-diagonal approximation of the empirical $\hat M_t$. Then $B_\perp(T)\to-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}$ (population target $-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}=-I_D/\beta_t^2$), and in the proportional limit $D,\nsamp\to\infty$, $D/\nsamp\to\psi$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-eps}
\varepsilon_\perp^2\;\to\;\int\frac{\rho_\perp^2\,(m-1)^2}{\big(1+\rho_\perp(m-1)\big)^2}\,d\mu_{\psi}(m),
\end{equation}
$\mu_\psi$ the Marchenko--Pastur law of ratio $\psi$ with unit mean \citep{marchenko1967}. Consequently:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] $\varepsilon_\perp^2=\rho_\perp^2\psi\,(1-2\rho_\perp\psi+O(\rho_\perp^2\psi))$ for $\rho_\perp\psi\to0$; $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ depends on $(\dlat,\nsamp)$ only through $\psi=D/\nsamp$ and on $\signoise$ only through $\rho_\perp$. (The expansion is poor at $\rho_\perp\approx1$, $\psi\gtrsim0.1$; use \eqref{eq:scope-eps} numerically there.)
\item[(ii)] For $\psi>1$ the atom of $\mu_\psi$ at $0$ gives $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge(1-\psi^{-1})\rho_\perp^2/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$: on the $D-\nsamp$ directions orthogonal to $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ the learned score is $-x_\perp/\Delta_t$ instead of $-x_\perp/\beta_t^2$, a contraction stronger by $(1-\rho_\perp)^{-1}$, so the reverse dynamics confine generated null components to the span of the training noise vectors.
\item[(iii)] $\varepsilon_\perp^2\to0$ as $\rho_\perp\to0$ at fixed $\psi$: at $\signoise=0.01$, $t=0.01$, $\rho_\perp=4.9\times10^{-3}$ and $\varepsilon_\perp^2<10^{-4}$ for all $\dlat\le1000$ ($4.7\times10^{-5}$ at $\dlat=1000$).
\end{itemize}
The neglected signal--null cross covariance $\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu(m_{c(\mu)}+\xi^\mu_{\rm sig})\xi^{\mu\top}_\perp$ has entries of order $\alpha_t\beta_t/\sqrt\nsamp$ and contributes a sample-specific error of the same order as $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ when $\psi$ is small; it is not included.
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
\emph{Closed form.} With DSM target $y=-\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$ and $x_t=e^{-t}x+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$, the null-block loss over the empirical measure is $\E\|B_\perp x_{t,\perp}-y_\perp\|^2$, whose gradient flow is $\dot B_\perp=-2(B_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}+I_D)$ because $\E[x_{t,\perp}x_{t,\perp}^\top]=\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ over the training points (cluster means have zero null component) and $\E[y_\perp x_{t,\perp}^\top]=-I_D$. Hence $B_\perp(T)=-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}+(B_\perp(0)+\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1})e^{-2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}T}$ (fact F12 with the empirical second moment); the population version replaces $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ by $\beta_t^2I_D$.

\emph{Spectral form.} $\hat C_\perp=\signoise^2\Xi\Xi^\top/\nsamp$, so its eigenvalues are $\signoise^2m_i$ with $\{m_i\}$ Wishart eigenvalues of ratio $\psi$ whose empirical law converges weakly to $\mu_\psi$. Using $e^{-2t}\signoise^2m+\Delta_t=\beta_t^2(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))$,
$$\|\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2=\frac1{\beta_t^4}\sum_i\Big(\frac{1}{1+\rho_\perp(m_i-1)}-1\Big)^2=\frac1{\beta_t^4}\sum_i\frac{\rho_\perp^2(m_i-1)^2}{(1+\rho_\perp(m_i-1))^2}.$$
Dividing by $\|\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2=D/\beta_t^4$ gives the normalized trace of a bounded continuous function of $\hat C_\perp$ (denominator $\ge1-\rho_\perp>0$ on $m\ge0$), so weak convergence yields \eqref{eq:scope-eps}.
(i) Expand $(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))^{-2}=1-2\rho_\perp(m-1)+3\rho_\perp^2(m-1)^2-\dots$ and use the MP central moments $\int(m-1)^2d\mu_\psi=\psi$, $\int(m-1)^3d\mu_\psi=\psi^2$, $\int(m-1)^4d\mu_\psi=2\psi^2+\psi^3$.
(ii) $\mu_\psi=(1-\psi^{-1})\delta_0+\psi^{-1}\tilde\mu$ for $\psi>1$; the integrand at $m=0$ is $\rho_\perp^2/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$; the $m_i=0$ eigenvectors are the complement of $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$, on which $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=\Delta_tI$.
(iii) The integrand is $O(\rho_\perp^2)$ uniformly on the support once $\rho_\perp\le\tfrac12$; $\rho_\perp=e^{-0.02}10^{-4}/(e^{-0.02}10^{-4}+0.0198)=4.9\times10^{-3}$.
\emph{Numerics} (Gauss--Hermite/MP quadrature, $t=0.01$): $\signoise=0.5$ ($\rho_\perp=0.925$, $\nsamp=500$): $\varepsilon_\perp^2=0.028,0.103,0.302,0.638,1.19$ at $\dlat=20,50,100,150,200$; first order $\rho_\perp^2\psi=0.026,0.077,0.163,0.248,0.334$. $\signoise=1$ ($\rho_\perp=0.98$): $0.032,0.122,0.379,0.867,1.80$.
```

### `prop:scope-rebound` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: H1, H3, H4, p > n; eta-averaged U; Isotropic-residual approximation for the residual-bulk top (accurate for d_lat >= 150, off by up to 4.6x at d_lat = 10 on clustered data); W_perp^T W_perp / p = I + O(psi_p^{-1/2}); product-of-edges treatment of the two MP broadenings

```latex
\begin{proposition}[Onset statistics across the $\dlat\to\nsamp$ boundary]\label{prop:scope-rebound}
Assume (H1), (H3), (H4) and $\pwidth>\nsamp$; $\Umat$ is the $\eta$-averaged second moment.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] \emph{Residual-bulk onset.} In the isotropic-residual approximation (columns $g(x^\mu_t)$ with uncorrelated entries of variance $1/\pwidth$),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-onset-iso}
\lambda_{\rm onset}^{\rm iso}=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp}\Big(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}\Big)^2,\qquad \taumem^{\rm onset,iso}=\frac{\nsamp}{a_*(q)^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2},
\end{equation}
which depends on $\dlat$ only through $q\downarrow\beta_t^2$ in $a_*(q)^2$. On the repo spectra ($\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$) the measured onset exceeds \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} by $4.6,4.2,2.8,2.2,1.7,1.5,1.1,1.0$ at $\dlat=10,20,40,60,80,100,150,200$: the excess is a small-$\dlat$ effect of cluster-level residual spikes (residual features of the $\nsamp/k$ points in a cluster are correlated) and disappears at $\dlat\ge150$, where \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} is quantitatively right ($0.173$ vs $0.171$ in code units at $\dlat=200$). Because the excess factor itself decays, \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} under-predicts the $\dlat$-trend: the measured onset falls $107\times$ from $\dlat=10$ to $200$ ($221\times$ from $\dlat=5$), the formula predicts $24\times$ ($\approx60\times$).
\item[(b)] \emph{Noise-dim top for $D>\nsamp$.} The residual bulk does not empty at $\psi_n=1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-counts}(b)); its top stays at \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso}. The noise-dim block keeps $\nsamp$ data-broadened modes with top
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-onset-rd}
\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\max}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\big(1+\sqrt{\psi}\big)^2+\Delta_t\Big]\big(1+\psi_p^{-1/2}\big)^2\;\xrightarrow{\psi_n\to0}\;\frac{a_1(\beta_t^2)^2\,e^{-2t}\signoise^2\,(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2}{\nsamp},
\end{equation}
so $1/\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\max}\to\nsamp/(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2)$: $\dlat$-independent and linear in $\nsamp$. Its ratio to the large-$\dlat$ limit of $\taumem^{\rm onset,iso}$ is $\frac{a_*(\beta_t^2)^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2}{a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2}$, which tends to $a_*(\beta_t^2)^2/(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2)=0.019$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $t=0.01$ as $\pwidth/\dlat,\pwidth/\nsamp\to\infty$ ($0.015$ at $\psi_p=64$). These modes are sample-specific in the sense of \eqref{eq:scope-epsdef}: their eigenvectors are fixed by $\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ and the learned content deviates from the population score by $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge\rho_\perp^2(1-\psi^{-1})/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(ii)). In this regime the earliest sample-specific modes are therefore the noise-dim modes, learned $\approx50\times$ faster than the residual bulk: the population/sample distinction of the noise-dim ``buffer'' dissolves.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(a) Under (H4) the residual part of $\Umat$ is $\frac{a_*^2}{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[gg^\top]$; treating its columns as uncorrelated with variance $1/\pwidth$ ($\|g\|^2\to1$) makes it $a_*^2/\nsamp$ times a Wishart matrix of ratio $\nsamp/\pwidth$ with top edge $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$ \citep{marchenko1967}; the residual bulk is this spectrum projected onto the complement of the linear span and by Cauchy interlacing its top is at most the unprojected edge. Measured/iso ratios and the trend numbers are computed from \texttt{bulk\_summary.json} sample-bulk tops ($0.93,0.374,0.198,0.173$ code units at $\dlat=10,40,100,200$) against $\pwidth\lambda^{\rm iso}_{\rm onset}=0.203,0.133,0.135,0.171$ with $a_*(q)^2=0.0447,0.0125,0.00644,0.00467$. (b) For the $\eta$-averaged $\Umat$ the null-block linear part is $a_1^2W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$, with non-zero eigenvalues equal to those of $a_1^2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}/\dlat$; the top edge of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ is $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t$ (for $\psi>1$ the non-zero Wishart eigenvalues lie in $[(\sqrt\psi-1)^2,(\sqrt\psi+1)^2]$) and that of $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$ is $(1+\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2\approx(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2$; the product of the two top edges is the top edge of the free multiplicative convolution to leading order in the broadening. As $\psi_n\to0$, $(1+\sqrt\psi)^2/\dlat\to1/\nsamp$ and $q\to\beta_t^2$ by \eqref{eq:scope-q}. Numerics: $a_*(\beta_t^2)^2=3.09\times10^{-3}$, $a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2=0.671\times0.245=0.164$, ratio $0.019$; $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2/(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2\to1/1.27$ at $\psi_p=64$, $\nsamp/\pwidth\to0$.
```

### `rem:scope-rebound-conj` (remark, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: Everything in prop:scope-rebound; Existence of a threshold epsilon_c (conjectured, metric-dependent); Nonlinear softmax mechanism (F15) is required for NN-ratio memorization; not derived here

```latex
\begin{remark}[Conjecture: return of memorization through the null block at large $\dlat$]\label{rem:scope-rebound-conj}
Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-rebound}(b) shows that for $\psi_n\lesssim1$ the fast noise-dim modes are already sample-specific. We \emph{conjecture} that this is the channel through which memorization returns at large $\dlat$, and we state what the conjecture does and does not contain.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] \emph{What a linear null score cannot do.} The converged null score $-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}x_\perp$ is linear; under the reverse dynamics it generates null components with the empirical covariance $\hat C_\perp$ (confined to $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ when $D>\nsamp$), i.e.\ $x_\perp\approx\Xi c/\sqrt\nsamp$ with $c\sim N(0,I_\nsamp)$, whose squared distance to the nearest training point is $\approx D\signoise^2(2-O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/\nsamp}))$ and whose NN-ratio is $\approx1$. A linear null score therefore \emph{cannot} by itself produce NN-ratio memorization (ratio $<1/3$); that requires the softmax concentration of the empirical score \citep{biroli2024dynamical,scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization}, which lives in the nonlinear (residual) part of the features. $\varepsilon_\perp$ is proposed only as a \emph{necessary-condition proxy}: it measures how much sample-specific null-block information the fast modes carry.
\item[(ii)] \emph{Conditional functional form.} If a metric-dependent threshold $\varepsilon_c$ exists such that memorization appears once $\varepsilon_\perp\ge\varepsilon_c$, then, since $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ depends on $(\dlat,\nsamp)$ only through $\psi=D/\nsamp$ and on $\signoise$ only through $\rho_\perp$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-rebound}
\dlat^{\rm reb}=\dint+\nsamp\,\psi^{\rm reb}(\rho_\perp),\qquad \varepsilon_\perp^2\big(\rho_\perp,\psi^{\rm reb}\big)=\varepsilon_c^2 ,
\end{equation}
linear in $\nsamp$ at fixed $(\signoise,t)$, absent as $\rho_\perp\to0$, and earlier for larger $\rho_\perp$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{Evidence and confounds.} In the scaled-width MLP sweep (hidden $8\dlat=800,1200,1600$; $\signoise=0.5$, $\nsamp=500$, $3\times10^5$ steps) the memorization fraction is $0.0000,0.0022,0.0144$ at $\dlat=100,150,200$ and still rising at the last evaluations ($0.004\to0.0144$ over the final $5\times10^4$ steps at $\dlat=200$), with the mean NN-ratio falling $0.755\to0.713$; the matching $\signoise=0.01$ scaled sweep gives $0$ at all three with NN-ratio rising $1.98\to2.59$. In the paper's \emph{fixed-width} sweep (hidden $256$) at $\signoise=0.5$ the fraction is $0.0000$ at every evaluation for $\dlat=100,150,200$. Hence the rebound requires \emph{both} $\rho_\perp\approx1$ and capacity growing with $\dlat$; the $\signoise$ contrast at equal capacity rules out capacity as the sole cause, the fixed-width null rules out $\rho_\perp$ as the sole cause. Calibrating $\varepsilon_c$ on the scaled sweep gives $\varepsilon_c^2\in(0.30,0.64)$ (the values at $\dlat=100$ and $150$), $\psi^{\rm reb}(0.925)\in(0.19,0.29)$; this is a fit to the same two points, not a test. The discriminating tests are the $\nsamp$-scaling of \eqref{eq:scope-rebound} and the $\signoise$ contrast at fixed large width.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Not proved. (i): for $x_\perp=\Xi c/\sqrt\nsamp$ and training null vectors $\xi^\mu_\perp=\signoise\Xi e_\mu$, $\|x_\perp-\xi^\mu_\perp\|^2\approx D\signoise^2(1+1)-2\signoise^2\,\xi^{\mu\top}_\perp\Xi c/(\signoise\sqrt\nsamp)$, and the cross term is $O(\signoise^2\sqrt{D}\,c_\mu)$ with $\max_\mu c_\mu\approx\sqrt{2\log\nsamp}$, negligible against $2D\signoise^2$ for $D\gg\log\nsamp$; the NN and second-NN distances then agree to relative $O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/D})$, so the NN-ratio is $\approx1$. (ii) is a restatement of Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(i). (iii): numbers from \texttt{results\_mlp\_exp2\_scaled\_sn05}, \texttt{results\_mlp\_exp2\_sn05} and the $\signoise=0.01$ scaled sweep, final \texttt{metrics.jsonl} rows.
```

### `prop:scope-sigperp` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: H1, H2, H4; eta-averaged U; Leading-order (product-of-edges) treatment of the two MP broadenings (under-estimates the measured lower edge by ~25%); lambda_onset taken from measured spectra because eq:scope-onset-iso under-estimates it for clustered data at small d_lat

```latex
\begin{proposition}[The $\signoise\to0$ limit and the merge condition]\label{prop:scope-sigperp}
Assume (H1), (H2), (H4); $\Umat$ is the $\eta$-averaged second moment.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] As $\signoise\to0$: $\beta_t^2\to\Delta_t$, $\rho_\perp\to0$, $q\to\Delta_t+\dint(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)/\dlat$, all eigenvalues of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ tend to $\Delta_t$ (no data-side broadening), and the noise-dim block sits on the diffusion floor
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-floor}
\lambda^{\rm nd}\to\frac{a_1(q)^2\Delta_t}{\dlat}\big(1\pm\psi_p^{-1/2}\big)^2,
\end{equation}
broadened only by $W$ (factor $((1+\psi_p^{-1/2})/(1-\psi_p^{-1/2}))^2=1.65$ at $\psi_p=64$; measured $1.5$--$1.6$ at $\signoise=0.01$), with $N_{\rm nd}=D$. These modes carry no data variance, the learned null score equals the population one ($\varepsilon_\perp\to0$), and the data lie in the $\dint$-subspace as in \citet{george2025dsm}.
\item[(ii)] Including the data-side (ratio $\psi$) and width-side (ratio $D/\pwidth\approx\psi_p^{-1}$) Marchenko--Pastur broadenings, the lower edge of the noise-dim block is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ndmin}
\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1-\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t\Big](1-\psi_p^{-1/2})^2\qquad(\psi<1),
\end{equation}
and with $G:=\log_{10}(\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}/\lambda_{\rm onset})$ the one-decade criterion $G\ge1$ of (H5) holds iff
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-sigthr}
\signoise^2\;\ge\;\frac{e^{2t}}{(1-\sqrt\psi)^2}\Big[\frac{10\,\dlat\,\lambda_{\rm onset}}{a_1(q)^2(1-\psi_p^{-1/2})^2}-\Delta_t\Big]=:(\signoise^*)^2 .
\end{equation}
\item[(iii)] With the measured onsets of the repo spectra ($t=0.01$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $\signoise=0.5$), $\signoise^*=0.91,0.70,0.50,0.45,0.41,0.40,0.42,0.52$ at $\dlat=10,20,40,60,80,100,150,200$. Hence $\signoise=0.5$ is below threshold for $\dlat\le20$ and at or above it for $\dlat\ge40$, matching the measured $G<1$ for $\dlat\le20$ and $G\ge1$ for $\dlat\ge30$ ($G=0.64,0.89,1.12,1.19,1.27,1.26,1.26,1.09$; predicted from \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin}: $0.51,0.73,0.99,1.08,1.14,1.15,1.11,0.98$, the product-of-edges lower edge under-estimating the measured $\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}$ by $\approx25\%$). $\signoise=0.01$ is below the floor ($\signoise^2=5\times10^{-3}\Delta_t$) and the measured separation there is $G=0.004$--$0.056$ decades: a three-bulk spectrum. The four-bulk picture applies only for $\signoise\gtrsim\signoise^*$.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
(i) Substitute in Definition~\ref{def:scope-ratios} and \eqref{eq:scope-edges}; $\varepsilon_\perp^2\to0$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(iii). For the $\eta$-averaged $\Umat$ the null block of $\hat M_t$ is $e^{-2t}\hat C_\perp+\Delta_tI_D\to\Delta_tI_D$, so the only remaining broadening is that of $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$, with edges $(1\pm\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$. (ii) The non-zero eigenvalues of $a_1^2W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ equal those of $a_1^2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}/\dlat$; the lower edges of the factors are $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1-\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t$ and $(1-\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$, and their product is the lower edge of the free multiplicative convolution to leading order in the broadening (exact edges follow from the Silverstein equation, cf.\ \citealp{ELKaroui2010spectrum}). Solve $\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}\ge10\lambda_{\rm onset}$ for $\signoise^2$. (iii) Code units are $\pwidth\times$ theory units. At $\dlat=10$: $\lambda_{\rm onset}=0.93/640=1.45\times10^{-3}$, $a_1(q)^2=0.281$, $\psi=0.01$: $(\signoise^*)^2=e^{0.02}(0.9)^{-2}[100\cdot1.45\times10^{-3}/(0.281\cdot0.766)-0.0198]=0.83$. At $\dlat=40$: $\lambda_{\rm onset}=0.374/2560=1.46\times10^{-4}$, $a_1(q)^2=0.494$, $\psi=0.07$: $(\signoise^*)^2=1.0202\cdot1.849\cdot[400\cdot1.46\times10^{-4}/(0.494\cdot0.766)-0.0198]=0.256$, $\signoise^*=0.50$. The remaining entries are the same evaluation on the other $\dlat$. Measured $G$ from \texttt{bulk\_summary.json} (only its noise-dim minimum and its sample-bulk top, index $\dlat$, are used; the file's block partition follows the superseded cumulative count): $\signoise=0.5$: $0.669,0.644,0.784,0.888,0.996,1.12,1.19,1.27,1.25,1.26,1.09$ at $\dlat=8,10,15,20,30,40,60,80,100,150,200$; $\signoise=0.01$: $0.045,0.032,0.019,0.006,0.004,0.056$ at $\dlat=8,10,15,20,30,40$.
```

### `rem:scope-psip` (remark, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: H1, H4; Product-of-edges approximation for block widths; No d_lat sweep at this p exists in the repo; only exp3 at d_lat = 20

```latex
\begin{remark}[$\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$: $\psi_p$ decreasing toward $1$]\label{rem:scope-psip}
This configuration was run only at $\dlat=20$ (exp3, $\dint$ sweep); the statements for $\dlat\ge40$ are predictions. For $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$ one has $\pwidth>\nsamp$ for all $\dlat$ and $\psi_p=1+800/\dlat\ge4.3$ for $\dlat\le240$; the Bonnaire margin $\psi_p-1-\psi_n=300/\dlat$ stays positive but is not uniform in $\dlat$. The block \emph{means} \eqref{eq:scope-edges} are $\pwidth$-independent (a $5.8\times$ change of $\pwidth$ moves them by $<0.5\%$). What changes is the block \emph{width}: the factor $(1\pm\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$ in \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin} has $\sqrt{D/\pwidth}=0.22$ at $\dlat=40$ (noise-dim block spanning a factor $2.4$) and $0.44$ at $\dlat=200$ (factor $6.8$, i.e.\ $0.83$ decades from $W$ alone), against $0.125$ (factor $1.65$) at $\psi_p=64$. (H5) then requires $G\ge1$ \emph{after} this broadening, which the product-of-edges estimate predicts to fail at $\signoise=0.5$ for $\dlat\gtrsim100$. On the residual side $\nsamp/\pwidth\to0.5$ at $\dlat=200$ and the MP lower edge $(1-\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2=0.086$ pushes the slowest residual modes toward zero (the random-features interpolation peak, \citealp{mei2022generalization}); this stretches the time to \emph{complete} memorization but leaves $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ within $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2\le2.9$ of the residual mean.
\end{remark}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Follows from \eqref{eq:scope-counts}, \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin} and the MP edges of the residual Gram; numerical values by substitution: $(1.218/0.782)^2=2.43$, $(1.442/0.558)^2=6.7$--$6.85$, $(1.125/0.875)^2=1.65$, $(1-\sqrt{0.5})^2=0.086$, $(1+\sqrt{0.5})^2=2.91$.
```

### `prop:scope-control` (proposition, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: H1, H2, H4; sigma_perp^2 = c/(d_lat - d_int) with c fixed; D >> D_c = e^{-2t} c / Delta_t for the collapse statements

```latex
\begin{proposition}[Fixed-null-energy control $\signoise^2=c/D$]\label{prop:scope-control}
Assume (H1), (H2), (H4) and set $\signoise^2=c/D$ with $c>0$ fixed, so that the total null variance $D\signoise^2=c$ does not grow with $\dlat$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ctrl}
\beta_t^2=\Delta_t+\frac{e^{-2t}c}{D},\qquad \rho_\perp=\frac{e^{-2t}c}{e^{-2t}c+\Delta_tD},\qquad q=\Delta_t+\frac{\dint(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)+e^{-2t}c}{\dlat},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ctrl-edges}
\lambda^{\rm sig}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\lambda^{\rm nd}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big(\Delta_t+\frac{e^{-2t}c}{D}\Big),\qquad
\bar\lambda^{\rm samp}=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp},
\end{equation}
with crossover $D_c:=e^{-2t}c/\Delta_t\approx49.5\,c$ at $t=0.01$. For $D\gg D_c$ the control is spectrally the $\signoise\to0$ limit of Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}: (i) the noise-dim block collapses onto the diffusion floor and the four-bulk separation is lost; (ii) $\taugen=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2)$ keeps its linear growth in $\dlat$, with $a_1(q)^2\to a_1(\Delta_t)^2=0.96$ (vs.\ $0.63$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $\dlat=200$); (iii) $a_*(q)^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$ collapses faster than in the $\signoise=0.5$ sweep, so the frozen-feature delay of the residual bulk persists; (iv) $\varepsilon_\perp^2\approx\rho_\perp^2\psi\approx c^2e^{-4t}/(\Delta_t^2D\nsamp)\to0$, so no null-block rebound (Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj}) is predicted. The control separates the \emph{count} $D$ acting through $q$-dilution (retained) from the \emph{null data variance} acting through $\rho_\perp$ (removed): the frozen-feature theory predicts that the memorization delay survives the control while the four-bulk structure and the rebound do not.
\end{proposition}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Substitute $\signoise^2=c/D$ into Definition~\ref{def:scope-ratios} and \eqref{eq:scope-edges}; the trace check \eqref{eq:scope-trace} holds by \eqref{eq:scope-q}. (i) is Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i)--(ii) with $\signoise^2=c/D\to0$. (ii) $\alpha_t^2$ is $\signoise$-independent and $a_1$ is decreasing in $q$; $a_1(0.0198)^2=0.962$. (iii) $\tanh x=x-x^3/3+\tfrac{2}{15}x^5+O(x^7)$ gives $\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)=q-2q^2+\tfrac{17}{3}q^3+O(q^4)$ and $a_1(q)^2q=q-2q^2+5q^3+O(q^4)$, so $a_*(q)^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$ (numerically confirmed for $q\le0.02$). (iv) Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(i) with $\rho_\perp\approx e^{-2t}c/(\Delta_tD)$. The prediction that the delay persists is a frozen-feature statement, not a statement about the trainable MLP (Remark~\ref{rem:scope-feature}).
```

### `rem:scope-feature` (remark, **conjectured**)

Hypotheses: Trainable first layer; A pre-trained for T_0 before the W step; One gradient step of size eta (Ba et al. 2022 regime); H4 Gaussian equivalence for the post-step kernel

```latex
\begin{remark}[Conjecture: one-step feature learning]\label{rem:scope-feature}
Everything above is a frozen-feature (H1) result. For the trainable MLP we conjecture the following, modelled on the one-gradient-step analysis of \citet{ba2022onestep}. Let $A$ first be trained for a time $T_0$ (so that $A\neq0$; at $A(0)=0$ the $W$-gradient vanishes identically), then take one gradient step $W_1=W_0-\eta\,\nabla_WL$ with
$$\nabla_WL=\frac{1}{\nsamp\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}}\sum_\mu\big[(A^\top r_\mu)\odot\tanh'(W_0x^\mu_t/\sqrt{\dlat})\big]\,x_t^{\mu\top},\qquad r_\mu=s_A(x^\mu_t)-y_\mu .$$
The update is a sum of rank-one terms with row space in $\operatorname{span}\{x^\mu_t\}$ whose signal- and null-block energies scale as $\alpha_t^2$ and $\beta_t^2$ per dimension, so the spike it adds to the conjugate kernel $\Umat_1=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\eta\phi_1\phi_1^\top$ loads on the signal subspace with per-dimension null leakage $O(\beta_t^2/\alpha_t^2)$ ($0.096$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $0.007$ at $0.01$). Because $\E[rx_t^\top]=A\E[\phi x_t^\top]+I$ contains an isotropic term from the DSM target, the \emph{total} null energy of the spike, $D\beta_t^2$ against $\dint\alpha_t^2$, can be comparable at large $\dlat$. Two predictions: \textbf{(P-i)} after the step the signal-block eigenvalues increase by $O(\eta)$ (the cross term of $\Phi_1\Phi_1^\top$ is linear in the step; a rank-$\le\dint$ spike, BBP-type, \citealp{baik2005bbp}) while the noise-dim edges move by at most $O(\eta\beta_t^2/\alpha_t^2)$, so $\taugen$ decreases and the noise-dim timescale does not; \textbf{(P-ii)} under continued training the first layer acquires components aligned with individual training points (the nonlinear part of the score target is the softmax assignment of the empirical score, fact F15), which raises the top of the residual bulk and lowers $\taumem^{\rm onset}$; the buffer in absolute steps, $\taumem^{\rm onset}-\taugen$, is then \emph{shorter} than the frozen-feature value even though (P-i) alone would lengthen it in ratio. Status: conjecture. The saved RFNN \texttt{eigenvalues\_pre/post.npy} cannot test it: $W$ is frozen there and the pre/post block medians agree to $<0.5\%$ (\texttt{results\_rfnn\_exp2v3}, $\dlat=5$--$40$), which only confirms (H1). The test requires logging the first-layer conjugate-kernel spectrum of the MLP at initialization and during training (\texttt{compute\_U} applies verbatim to $W_1$); the MLP runs saved only \texttt{config.json} and \texttt{metrics.jsonl}.
\end{remark}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Not proved. The gradient formula is the chain rule for $s_A(x)=\pwidth^{-1/2}A\tanh(Wx/\sqrt{\dlat})$ and the DSM loss $\frac1{2\nsamp}\sum_\mu\|s_A(x^\mu_t)-y_\mu\|^2$. The block-energy scaling follows from $\E[x_{t,{\rm sig}}x_{t,{\rm sig}}^\top]=\alpha_t^2I$ (mean), $\E[x_{t,\perp}x_{t,\perp}^\top]=\beta_t^2I$. The $O(\eta)$ scaling is the first-order term of $\phi_1=\phi_0+\eta\,\partial_\eta\phi+O(\eta^2)$ in $\Phi_1\Phi_1^\top$; whether it is $\Theta(\eta)$ or cancels at first order depends on the alignment of $\partial_\eta\phi$ with $\phi_0$ and is part of what must be measured.
```

### `rem:scope-outside` (remark, **sketched**)

Hypotheses: Paper's experimental configurations as listed in sec-design.tex, sec-appendix.tex and the run configs (verified: hidden = 256 in results_mlp_exp2_sn05; hidden = 8 d_lat in results_mlp_exp2_scaled_sn05; no d_lat = 240 or sigma_perp = 1 synthetic run)

```latex
\begin{remark}[Configurations in the paper that fall outside (H1)--(H5)]\label{rem:scope-outside}
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\dlat=\dint=5$ (both sweeps): $N_{\rm nd}=0$; moreover $\pwidth=320<\nsamp$, so (H2) fails twice and the residual bulk has $\le\pwidth-\dlat=315$ modes. A baseline, not a four-bulk spectrum.
\item $\signoise=0.01$ at $t=0.01$ (RFNN exp2/exp3, MLP exp2/exp3): $\rho_\perp=4.9\times10^{-3}$, $\signoise^2=5\times10^{-3}\Delta_t$, measured noise-dim/residual gap $0.004$--$0.056$ decades. (H3), (H5) fail: three-bulk regime. Counts and $\taugen$ still apply; Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i) is the operative statement and predicts no rebound (observed in the scaled sweep: memorization $0$ for $20\le\dlat\le200$). Floor medians match $\pwidth a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$ for $\dlat\ge30$ ($0.67,0.77$ predicted vs $0.74,0.81$ measured at $\dlat=30,40$); at $\dlat=10$ the floor block is merged with the residual bulk ($G=0.03$) and the comparison is not meaningful.
\item $\dlat\ge150$ at $\nsamp=500$, $\signoise=0.5$: $\psi_n\le3.3$, $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge0.64$; regime of Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj}. (H5) also degrades (measured gap $1.26\to1.09$ decades from $\dlat=150$ to $200$). Memorization returns only in the scaled-width MLP sweep ($0.0022,0.0144$ at $\dlat=150,200$, hidden $8\dlat$) and not in the fixed-width sweep (hidden $256$: $0.0000$).
\item $\dint$ sweep at $\dlat=20$ (exp3): $\dint\in\{16,20\}$ gives $N_{\rm nd}\in\{4,0\}$, too few modes for a block; the signal block is a finite-rank spike for every $\dint$, so ``signal edges'' are spike locations, not bulk edges.
\item RFNN with $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$: run only at $\dlat=20$; Remark~\ref{rem:scope-psip} predicts that width-side broadening breaks (H5) for $\dlat\gtrsim100$ at $\signoise=0.5$.
\item Isotropic control $\signoise=\sigsig=1$ (no synthetic run at $\signoise=1$ exists in the repo; all statements are predictions): (H3) holds ($\rho_\perp=0.98$) and the $D$ noise-dim modes exist at $a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ between signal and residual modes, but the signal/noise-dim ratio is $\alpha_t^2/\beta_t^2=2.76$ ($0.44$ decades) for all $\dlat$: the two-atom \emph{separation} of the signal block is absent, not the block. The reported $10.7\times$ growth of the spectral gap under isotropy (fact F6) must then be a residual-bulk effect (fall of $a_*(q)^2$ and of the cluster-spike excess), not a noise-dim one. Under Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj} with $\varepsilon_c^2\in(0.30,0.64)$, $\psi^{\rm reb}(0.98)\in(0.166,0.251)$, i.e.\ $\dlat^{\rm reb}\in[88,130]$ at $\nsamp=500$, earlier than at $\signoise=0.5$.
\item All trainable-MLP runs (synthetic, hidden $256$ fixed or $8\dlat$ scaled; real, hidden $256$): (H1) fails; only Remark~\ref{rem:scope-feature} applies.
\item Real VAE latents (CelebA, CIFAR-10, $t=0.1$, $\nsamp=1000$): $\rho_\Sigma$ is continuous (fact F9), effective rank $\approx0.5\,\dlat$, and for $\dlat\ge140$/$160$ a set of coordinates has $\lambda=\Delta_t$ exactly (posterior collapse). (H3) fails as stated; the collapsed coordinates are the $\signoise=0$ case of Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i): free modes at the floor with $\varepsilon_\perp=0$, counted in $N_{\rm nd}$ but not contributing to $\rho_\perp$. The general-$\rho_\Sigma$ form of \citet[Thm.~3.1]{bonnaire2025} applies; the two-atom edges do not.
\item Time mismatch: spectra are computed at the single time $t=0.01$, while training and generation cover $t\in[0.01,3]$ (the reverse SDE is integrated from $t_{\max}=3$ to $t_{\min}=0.01$; $t_{\rm eval}=0.1$ in the code is used only for the score-error metric). $\rho_\perp(t)\ge\tfrac12$ only for $t\le\tfrac12\log(1+\signoise^2)$ ($0.11$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $5\times10^{-5}$ at $0.01$), so the null block is data-dominated only on a short final segment of the reverse trajectory.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}
```

Proof / derivation:
```latex
Each item is a direct evaluation of Definition~\ref{def:scope-ratios} and (H1)--(H5) on the configuration in question; numerical values from \texttt{bulk\_summary.json} (noise-dim minimum and sample-bulk top only), the run configs, and \texttt{experiment\_v2.py} (\texttt{generate\_samples}, \texttt{t\_eval}).
```

## Predictions

- **The large-d_lat return of memorization is a null-block effect that additionally requires capacity: it appears when rho_perp ~ 1 (sigma_perp = 0.5) with hidden = 8 d_lat, is absent at rho_perp ~ 0 (sigma_perp = 0.01) at the same scaled capacity, and is absent at rho_perp ~ 1 with fixed hidden = 256. Prediction: at fixed LARGE width (hidden = 1600 for all d_lat) the sigma_perp = 0.5 rebound persists at d_lat >= 150 and the sigma_perp = 0.01 one stays at zero.**  
  test: Rerun d_lat in {100,150,200} at hidden = 1600 fixed for sigma_perp in {0.01, 0.5, 1}; the isotropic sigma_perp = 1 run (rho_perp = 0.98) should rebound earlier (d_lat^reb in [88,130] at n = 500) and more strongly. Train beyond 300k steps since the d_lat = 200 fraction is still rising.  
  support: Scaled sweep results_mlp_exp2_scaled_sn05 (hidden 800/1200/1600, 300k steps, seed 42): mem = 0.0000, 0.0022, 0.0144 at d_lat = 100, 150, 200 (rising 0.004 -> 0.0144 over the last 50k steps at d = 200), mean_nn_ratio 0.755 -> 0.713; sigma_perp = 0.01 scaled: 0/0/0, nn_ratio 1.98 -> 2.59. Fixed-width results_mlp_exp2_sn05 (hidden 256, sigma_perp = 0.5): mem = 0.0000 at every evaluation for d_lat = 100, 150, 200 (nn_ratio 1.10, 1.22, 1.36). No sigma_perp = 1 run exists.

- **Conditional on the threshold conjecture (rem:scope-rebound-conj), the rebound point scales linearly with n at fixed sigma_perp, t and capacity schedule: d_lat^reb - d_int = n psi^reb(rho_perp), with psi^reb(0.925) in (0.19, 0.29).**  
  test: Rerun the sigma_perp = 0.5 scaled-width MLP sweep at n = 250 and n = 1000 (same hidden = 8 d_lat, same steps per sample); predicted rebound onset d_lat ~ 55-80 at n = 250 and ~ 200-300 at n = 1000. The null-block signature (null-block share of d(gen,NN1)^2 below its population value D beta^2/(D beta^2 + d_int alpha^2)) requires re-running with generated-sample dumps: no existing MLP run saves samples or checkpoints.  
  support: Only the n = 500 scaled sweep (rebound between d_lat = 100 and 150, where epsilon_perp^2 = 0.30 and 0.64); epsilon_c is calibrated on these same two points, so this is not yet a test.

- **Four-bulk separation threshold: at t = 0.01, p = 64 d_lat, n = 500 the noise-dim/residual gap crosses one decade at sigma_perp^* = 0.91 (d_lat = 10), 0.70 (20), 0.50 (40), 0.40-0.45 (60-100), 0.52 (200); below sqrt(Delta_t e^{2t}) = 0.14 the noise-dim block is diffusion-noise dominated (rho_perp < 1/2) and sits on the W-broadened floor a_1^2 Delta_t (1 +/- psi_p^{-1/2})^2 / d_lat.**  
  test: RFNN spectra at d_lat in {10, 40, 100}, sweep sigma_perp in {0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0}; plot G = log10(noise-dim min / sample-bulk top) vs sigma_perp; expect G = 1 crossing near 0.9 at d = 10 and near 0.5 at d = 40, and G ~ 0 with an unbroadened floor block (width factor ~1.65) below 0.14.  
  support: bulk_summary.json: G = 0.004-0.056 decades at sigma_perp = 0.01 (d_lat 8-40, floor block width 1.5-1.6 = W-side factor) and 0.64-1.27 at sigma_perp = 0.5, with G < 1 for d_lat <= 20 and >= 1 for d_lat >= 30; predicted G from eq:scope-ndmin 0.51-1.15 tracks the measured values with a ~25% under-estimate of nd_min.

- **Fixed-null-energy control sigma_perp^2 = c/D: for D >> 50c the spectrum coincides with the sigma_perp -> 0 spectrum (noise-dim block on the floor, three bulks), tau_gen keeps growing linearly in d_lat with a_1(q)^2 -> 0.96, the RFNN residual-bulk delay persists, and no rebound appears in the MLP.**  
  test: Run the RFNN spectral sweep and the scaled-width MLP sweep with sigma_perp^2 = 1/D (c = 1) for d_lat in {10,...,200}; compare block means to eq:scope-ctrl-edges and memorization fraction to the sigma_perp = 0.01 scaled sweep.  
  support: Indirectly by the sigma_perp = 0.01 sweep (its D >> D_c regime is spectrally the same): floor medians 0.74, 0.81 measured vs 0.67, 0.77 predicted (code units, d_lat = 30, 40); memorization 0 for 20 <= d_lat <= 200 in the scaled sweep; no rebound.

- **p = d_lat + n + 300: block means unchanged, but the noise-dim block width grows as ((1 + sqrt(D/p))/(1 - sqrt(D/p)))^2 (2.4x at d_lat = 40, 6.8x at d_lat = 200) and the four-bulk separation fails at sigma_perp = 0.5 for d_lat >~ 100.**  
  test: At d_lat in {40, 100, 200}, compute RFNN spectra for p in {d_lat + n + 300, 8 d_lat, 64 d_lat}; measure noise-dim min/max and mean. This configuration currently exists only at d_lat = 20 (exp3).  
  support: Fact F2 (means move < 0.5% under a 5.8x change of p); widths not measured; no d_lat sweep at this p exists.

- **Feature-learning conjecture: after one W-step following readout pre-training, signal-block eigenvalues of the first-layer conjugate kernel rise by O(eta) while noise-dim edges move by at most O(eta beta_t^2/alpha_t^2); with continued training the residual-bulk top rises and tau_mem^onset - tau_gen shrinks relative to the frozen-feature value.**  
  test: Log the conjugate-kernel spectrum of the MLP's first layer (compute_U applied to W_1) at init and every eval step for the sigma_perp = 0.5 exp2 sweep (fixed and scaled width); compare pre/post block means and the top of the residual bulk.  
  support: Nothing yet. The RFNN eigenvalues_pre/post.npy agree to < 0.5% (frozen W) and so only confirm H1; MLP runs saved no spectra or checkpoints.

## Empirical anchor
(1) Rebound (capacity-confounded): results_mlp_exp2_scaled_sn05/di5_d{100,150,200}_n500_s42 (hidden 800/1200/1600, 300k steps) final memorization_fraction = 0.0000, 0.0022, 0.0144 (mean_nn_ratio 0.755, 0.726, 0.713; d = 200 rising 0.004 -> 0.0144 over the last 50k steps); sigma_perp = 0.01 scaled sweep: 0, 0, 0 (nn_ratio 1.98, 2.34, 2.59); FIXED-width results_mlp_exp2_sn05/di5_d{100,150,200}_n500_s42 (hidden 256, sigma_perp = 0.5): 0.0000 at every evaluation (nn_ratio 1.10, 1.22, 1.36). Predicted epsilon_perp^2 (eq:scope-eps, rho_perp = 0.925, n = 500) = 0.302, 0.638, 1.19 at d = 100, 150, 200 (first order 0.163, 0.248, 0.334); sigma_perp = 0.01: rho_perp = 0.0049, epsilon_perp^2 < 1e-4. (2) Separation: bulk_summary.json (noise-dim min and sample-bulk top only; its partition uses the superseded count) gaps in decades, sigma_perp = 0.01: 0.045, 0.032, 0.019, 0.006, 0.004, 0.056 (d = 8..40); sigma_perp = 0.5: 0.669, 0.644, 0.784, 0.888, 0.996, 1.12, 1.19, 1.27, 1.25, 1.26, 1.09 (d = 8..200); predicted from eq:scope-ndmin: 0.51, 0.73, 0.99, 1.08, 1.14, 1.15, 1.11, 0.98 at d = 10..200; threshold eq:scope-sigthr with measured onsets: sigma_perp^* = 0.91, 0.70, 0.50, 0.45, 0.41, 0.40, 0.42, 0.52 at d = 10, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100, 150, 200. (3) q and coefficients (t = 0.01, Delta_t = 0.01980, alpha_t^2 = 2.7644): sigma_perp = 0.5: beta_t^2 = 0.2649, rho_perp = 0.925, q = 2.764, 1.515, 0.890, 0.577, 0.390, 0.348, 0.327 at d = 5, 10, 20, 40, 100, 150, 200; a_1^2 = 0.180, 0.281, 0.393, 0.494, 0.586, 0.611, 0.625; a_*^2 = 0.0785, 0.0447, 0.0235, 0.0125, 0.00644, 0.00524, 0.00467; a_1(beta^2)^2 = 0.671, a_*(beta^2)^2 = 3.09e-3; sigma_perp = 0.01: q(200) = 0.0885, a_*^2 = 2.6e-4; a_1(Delta_t)^2 = 0.962; a_*(1)^2 = 0.0274. (4) Onset vs isotropic-residual formula (code units, p = 64 d): measured sample-bulk tops 0.93, 0.374, 0.198, 0.173 at d = 10, 40, 100, 200 vs p lambda_onset^iso = 0.203, 0.133, 0.135, 0.171; ratio 4.6, 4.2, 2.8, 2.2, 1.7, 1.5, 1.1, 1.0 at d = 10, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100, 150, 200; measured onset falls 107x (d = 10 -> 200) vs 24x predicted. (5) Trace check with block means (code units / p): 0.456 vs E tanh^2 = 0.470 (d = 10), 0.2915 vs 0.2974 (40), 0.2092 vs 0.2093 (200); sample-block mass / a_*^2 = 0.63, 0.71, 0.51. (6) U is 50-draw eta-averaged: compute_U (code/v3/lib/eigenvalues.py) averages n_noise_samples = 50; results_rfnn_exp2v3 eigenvalues_pre.npy has e[499]/e[500] = 1.32 at d = 40 (6.81e-3 / 5.15e-3), 1.73 at d = 200, 0.3% of trace beyond index n: no cliff. Pre/post block medians (frozen W) agree to < 0.5% (signal 72.84/72.71, noise-dim 8.36/8.36, onset 0.3741/0.3728 at d = 40). (7) Width factors: (1.125/0.875)^2 = 1.65 (psi_p = 64), 2.43 (d = 40, p = d+n+300), 6.85 (d = 200); sigma_perp = 0.01 floor block measured width 1.5-1.6.

## Open gaps
1. Onset statistic vs isotropic-residual formula: the measured residual-bulk top exceeds eq:scope-onset-iso by 4.6x at d_lat = 10, decaying to 1.0x at d_lat = 200 (referee tables confirmed against bulk_summary.json). The excess is attributed to cluster-level residual spikes but this is not derived; consequently the formula captures the d_lat trend only for d_lat >= 150 (24x predicted vs 107x measured over d = 10..200). Whether the small-d top modes are cluster-level (mixture-population) or sample-specific is unresolved and affects what 'onset' means; a participation-ratio test on the top residual eigenvectors would settle it. This also covers the isotropic control (F6's 10.7x gap growth), which the frozen-feature iso-residual model does not reproduce.
2. The rebound mechanism (rem:scope-rebound-conj) is a conjecture, not a sketched result: a linear null score cannot produce NN-ratio < 1/3 (its samples have NN-ratio ~ 1); epsilon_perp is only a necessary-condition proxy and epsilon_c is calibrated on the same two scaled-sweep points (d = 100, 150) that were previously claimed as a match. Prediction 2 (n-scaling) is the only non-circular test and requires new runs with sample dumps.
3. Capacity confound (corrected from the previous draft, which wrongly said the fixed-width run did not exist): the paper's fixed-width (hidden 256) sigma_perp = 0.5 runs at d_lat = 100/150/200 show memorization 0.0000 throughout; the rebound exists only with hidden = 8 d_lat and is still rising at 300k steps. So the rebound requires both rho_perp ~ 1 and growing capacity; the theory only addresses the first ingredient. The main text must not say the theory 'matches the MLP sweep'.
4. Definition of U (corrected): fact F1's cliff at index n holds for the single-draw Gram U^(1); the saved spectra (compute_U, 50 eta draws) are the eta-averaged U, show no cliff (e[n-1]/e[n] = 1.32-1.73), and have N_nd = D for all d_lat with a soft residual tail. The piece now states every dynamical result for the eta-averaged U and the counts (F1) for U^(1). F1 as phrased in the brief ('machine-precision cliff') is not a property of the saved eigenvalues_pre.npy; if the paper quotes the cliff it must say it was verified on a separate single-draw computation. Also bulk_summary.py still partitions with the superseded cumulative count (sample = e[d:d+n], rank_null = e[d+n:]); only its noise-dim minimum and sample-bulk top (index d_lat) are used here, and the script should be fixed before any other statistic from it is quoted.
5. Block widths in eq:scope-ndmin use a product-of-edges approximation of the free multiplicative convolution of two MP laws; it under-estimates the measured noise-dim lower edge by ~25% (predicted G 0.51-1.15 vs measured 0.64-1.27). Exact edges need the Silverstein fixed-point equation. rem:scope-psip's numbers are leading order and predictions (no d_lat sweep at p = d + n + 300 exists; only exp3 at d_lat = 20).
6. The block-diagonal approximation in prop:scope-nullspec neglects the signal-null cross covariance of order alpha beta / sqrt(n), which contributes sample-specific error of the same order as epsilon_perp^2 at small psi; a full-matrix version would replace the MP integral by the spectrum of the empirical hat M_t^{-1} - M_t^{-1}.
7. Feature learning is entirely conjectural; no MLP spectra exist. The one-step scaling was corrected from Theta(eta^2) to O(eta) (cross terms in Phi_1 Phi_1^T are first order); whether the first-order term survives depends on alignment and is untested.
8. The isotropic control (sigma_perp = 1) and d_lat = 240 do not exist as runs; all statements about them are predictions (d_lat = 240 was removed). The no-conjecture-environment issue remains: rem:scope-feature and rem:scope-rebound-conj are typeset as remarks; adding \\newtheorem{conjecture} to main.tex would be cleaner.
9. Real-data regime (continuous rho_Sigma, posterior-collapsed coordinates) is placed outside (H3); the general-rho_Sigma reading of Bonnaire Thm 3.1 plus the free-mode observation (collapsed coordinates have epsilon_perp = 0) is stated but not developed.
10. Referee points not adopted: none. Both referees' arithmetic (sigma^* = 0.50 at d = 40, excess factor 4.6 -> 1.0, first-order correction -2 rho psi, a_*^2 = (2/3) q^3, rank(hat C_perp) = min(D,n)) was checked and is correct; the previous draft's 0.40/0.63, '3-10x', 'exact' trace identity with n sample modes, 'rank n - d_int', Theta(eta^2), t_eval = 0.1 for memorization, and 'fixed-width run does not exist' were all errors and have been replaced.
