## Main-text LaTeX (compact)
```latex
\paragraph{Scope and regime boundaries.}
The four-bulk picture is stated under explicit hypotheses (Assumption~\ref{asm:scope-standing}): frozen features (H1), the ordering $\dint<\dlat<\nsamp<\pwidth$ (H2), $\dint$ signal spikes of mean $\alpha_t^2$ plus one null atom whose data share $\rho_\perp(t):=e^{-2t}\signoise^2/\beta_t^2$ is at least $\tfrac12$ (H3), Gaussian equivalence (H4), and at least one decade between the noise-dim block and the top of the residual bulk (H5). Throughout, $\Umat$ is the $\eta$-averaged feature second moment (what the saved spectra estimate); the single-draw Gram $\Umat^{(1)}$ has rank $\nsamp$ and the counts $(\dint,\min(\dlat,\nsamp)-\dint,(\nsamp-\dlat)_+,\pwidth-\nsamp)$, whereas $\Umat$ has $N_{\rm nd}=D:=\dlat-\dint$ for every $\dlat$ and a soft residual tail (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-counts}). Write $\psi=D/\nsamp$, $\psi_p=\pwidth/\dlat$, and note the exact population identity $q=\beta_t^2+\dint(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)/\dlat$, so $q\to\beta_t^2$ as $\dlat\to\infty$.

\begin{proposition}[Regime boundaries; Appendix~\ref{app:scope}]\label{prop:scope-main}
Assume (H1), (H4) and $\pwidth>\nsamp$.
\textbf{(a) $\dlat\to\nsamp$.} The converged linear null score is $-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}$ rather than $-I/\beta_t^2$, with sample-specificity $\varepsilon_\perp^2=\int\rho_\perp^2(m-1)^2/(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))^2\,d\mu_{\psi}(m)=\rho_\perp^2\psi\,(1-2\rho_\perp\psi+O(\rho_\perp^2\psi))$ ($\mu_\psi$ the Marchenko--Pastur law), a function of $(\rho_\perp,\psi)$ alone; for $D>\nsamp$ the $D-\nsamp$ directions off $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ are contracted by $-x_\perp/\Delta_t$ instead of $-x_\perp/\beta_t^2$. These modes are learned on the fast timescale $\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2)$; for $\psi_n=\nsamp/\dlat\to0$ the noise-dim top gives $1/\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\max}\to\nsamp/(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2)$, $\dlat$-independent and $\approx50\times$ shorter than the large-$\dlat$ residual-bulk onset at $\signoise=0.5$.
\textbf{(b) $\signoise\to0$.} The noise-dim block sits on the $W$-broadened diffusion floor $a_1(q)^2\Delta_t(1\pm\psi_p^{-1/2})^2/\dlat$, carries no data variance ($\varepsilon_\perp\to0$), and the one-decade separation (H5) requires $\signoise\ge\signoise^*$ with $(\signoise^*)^2=e^{2t}(1-\sqrt{\psi})^{-2}\big[10\dlat\lambda_{\rm onset}/(a_1(q)^2(1-\psi_p^{-1/2})^2)-\Delta_t\big]$; on the measured onsets ($t=0.01$) $\signoise^*=0.91$ at $\dlat=10$ falling to $0.40$--$0.50$ for $\dlat\ge40$.
\textbf{(c) $\psi_p$ toward $1$.} Block means are $\pwidth$-independent but widths broaden by $(1\pm\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$; at $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$ this is a factor $6.8$ at $\dlat=200$ and (H5) is predicted to fail for $\dlat\gtrsim100$.
\end{proposition}

Consequences. (i) The $\signoise=0.01$ sweeps ($\rho_\perp=0.005$, measured noise-dim/residual gap $0.004$--$0.056$ decades) are a three-bulk regime: counts and $\taugen$ apply, the buffer separation does not, and no null-block rebound is predicted; the $\signoise=0.5$ sweep ($\rho_\perp=0.925$, gaps $0.64$--$1.27$ decades) is four-bulk for $\dlat\ge30$, consistent with $\signoise^*$. (ii) Memorization returns at large $\dlat$ only in the scaled-width MLP sweep (hidden $8\dlat$: fractions $0,0.0022,0.0144$ at $\dlat=100,150,200$ for $\signoise=0.5$, still rising at $3\times10^5$ steps; $0,0,0$ for $\signoise=0.01$ at equal capacity) and not in the fixed-width sweep (hidden $256$: $0$ at the same $\dlat$). We \emph{conjecture} (Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj}) that the null-block sample-specificity $\varepsilon_\perp$ is the necessary ingredient supplied by $\rho_\perp\approx1$; a linear null score alone cannot produce NN-ratio memorization, and capacity is a second necessary ingredient. Calibrated on the scaled sweep, $\varepsilon_c^2\in(0.30,0.64)$ predicts $\dlat^{\rm reb}=\dint+\nsamp\psi^{\rm reb}$ linear in $\nsamp$; the $\nsamp$-scaling and the $\signoise$ contrast at fixed large width are the tests. (iii) The fixed-null-energy control $\signoise^2=c/D$ has $\rho_\perp\to0$ for $D\gg50c$: it removes the null data variance while keeping the count $D$ and the $q$-dilution, so the frozen-feature theory predicts that memorization delay persists but the four-bulk structure and the rebound disappear (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-control}). (iv) Under isotropy $\signoise=\sigsig$ the $D$ noise-dim modes still lie $\alpha_t^2/\beta_t^2=2.76$ ($0.44$ decades) below the signal spikes for all $\dlat$; what is absent is the two-atom separation, not the block (no such run exists; prediction). (v) Feature learning is outside (H1); Remark~\ref{rem:scope-feature} states the one-step conjecture and Remark~\ref{rem:scope-outside} lists every configuration in the paper that violates (H1)--(H5).
```

## Appendix LaTeX (full)
```latex
\section{Regime boundaries and scope}
\label{app:scope}

This appendix delimits the four-bulk theory: the block counts and edges of Section~\ref{sec:theory} are leading-order statements under hypotheses (H1)--(H5) below, and several of the paper's configurations sit at or beyond their boundaries. We first fix which feature second moment is meant (the $\eta$-averaged $\Umat$ of the notation, as opposed to a single-draw Gram), then treat three boundaries ($\dlat\to\nsamp$, $\signoise\to0$, $\pwidth$ close to $\dlat+\nsamp$), the fixed-null-energy control, feature learning, and list the out-of-scope configurations explicitly.

\begin{definition}[Aspect ratios, null-data fraction, the two feature second moments, onset statistic]\label{def:scope-ratios}
Let $D:=\dlat-\dint$, $\psi_n:=\nsamp/\dlat$, $\psi_p:=\pwidth/\dlat$, $\psi:=D/\nsamp$, $\Delta_t=1-e^{-2t}$, and write $\alpha_t^2=e^{-2t}(s^2/\dint+\sigsig^2)+\Delta_t$ (mean signal eigenvalue of $M_t$) and $\beta_t^2=e^{-2t}\signoise^2+\Delta_t$ (null eigenvalue of $M_t$).
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] The \emph{null-data fraction} $\rho_\perp(t):=e^{-2t}\signoise^2/\beta_t^2\in[0,1)$ is the share of null-block variance that comes from data rather than from diffusion noise.
\item[(ii)] For the population second moment $M_t$ the pre-activation variance obeys the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-q}
q=\frac{\operatorname{tr}M_t}{\dlat}=\beta_t^2+\frac{\dint(\alpha_t^2-\beta_t^2)}{\dlat},\qquad q_\infty:=\lim_{\dlat\to\infty}q=\beta_t^2 .
\end{equation}
The empirical trace differs by $O(\nsamp^{-1/2})$, and the per-neuron variance $w_i^\top M_tw_i/\dlat$ fluctuates around $q$ with relative width $O(\dlat^{-1/2})$; by Jensen on the concave $q\mapsto\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)$ this makes measured traces slightly low at small $\dlat$ ($3\%$ at $\dlat=10$).
\item[(iii)] \emph{Two feature second moments.} $\Umat:=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\eta[\phi(x_t^\mu)\phi(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ is the $\eta$-averaged object of the notation; it governs the gradient-flow ODE and is what the saved spectra estimate (Monte Carlo over $50$ draws of $\eta$). $\Umat^{(1)}:=\Phi\Phi^\top/\nsamp$, with $\Phi=[\phi(x_t^1),\dots,\phi(x_t^\nsamp)]$ for a \emph{single} draw of $\eta$, is the single-draw Gram. All spectral statements below concern $\Umat$ unless $\Umat^{(1)}$ is named.
\item[(iv)] $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ is the largest eigenvalue of $\Umat$ outside the span of the $\dlat$ Gaussian-equivalent linear modes, i.e.\ the top of the residual (``sample'') bulk; $\taumem^{\rm onset}:=1/\lambda_{\rm onset}$ and $\taugen:=1/\lambda^{\rm sig}_{\min}$. For the RFNN these are spectral proxies: the RFNN runs log no sample-based memorization.
\item[(v)] For the null block, with $\hat C_\perp=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\xi^\mu_\perp\xi^{\mu\top}_\perp$ (rank $\min(D,\nsamp)$), $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=e^{-2t}\hat C_\perp+\Delta_t I_D$ and $\Sigma_{t,\perp}=\beta_t^2I_D$, the \emph{sample-specificity} of the converged linear null score is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-epsdef}
\varepsilon_\perp^2:=\frac{\E\|\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2}{\|\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2}.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}
\emph{Proof of \eqref{eq:scope-q}.} $\operatorname{tr}M_t=e^{-2t}(s^2+\dint\sigsig^2+D\signoise^2)+\dlat\Delta_t=\dint\alpha_t^2+D\beta_t^2$, since the $k$ centers of norm $s$ contribute $s^2$ to the trace of the mixture second moment and $Q$ preserves traces. \qed

\begin{assumption}[Four-bulk regime]\label{asm:scope-standing}
\begin{itemize}
\item[(H1)] \emph{Frozen features.} $W$ is fixed at initialization; only $A$ is trained, from $A(0)=0$, by gradient flow on score matching at fixed $t$, so each eigenmode of $\Umat$ decays independently at rate $\lambda_i$.
\item[(H2)] \emph{Ordering.} $\dint<\dlat<\nsamp<\pwidth$.
\item[(H3)] \emph{Spiked-plus-atom spectrum with data-dominated null block.} $\rho_\Sigma$ consists of $\dint$ finite-rank spikes of mean $\alpha_0^2$ (the $k$ centers give distinct spike locations; measured spread $\approx2.3\times$) and one atom $\signoise^2$ of weight $D/\dlat$; and $\rho_\perp(t)\ge\tfrac12$, i.e.\ $e^{-2t}\signoise^2\ge\Delta_t$.
\item[(H4)] \emph{Gaussian equivalence at leading order} \citep{goldt2022gaussian,hu2022universality}: $\phi(x)=a_1(q)\,Wx/\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}+a_*(q)\,g(x)$ with $g$ uncorrelated with $x$, $a_1(q)=\E[\tanh'(\sqrt q z)]$, $a_*(q)^2=\E[\tanh(\sqrt q z)^2]-a_1(q)^2q$; the signal--null cross covariance of the empirical $\hat M_t$ is neglected (block-diagonal approximation).
\item[(H5)] \emph{Separation.} The noise-dim block lies at least one decade above $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}).
\end{itemize}
Under (H1)--(H5) the leading-order block positions (block \emph{means}) are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-edges}
\lambda^{\rm sig}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\lambda^{\rm nd}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\bar\lambda^{\rm samp}:=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp}\ \text{(residual mass per training point)},
\end{equation}
with the trace check, valid for block means to leading order in $\dlat/\nsamp$ and $1/\nsamp$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-trace}
\dint\lambda^{\rm sig}+D\lambda^{\rm nd}+a_*(q)^2\approx\E[\tanh(\sqrt q z)^2].
\end{equation}
The residual mass $a_*(q)^2$ is shared between the residual bulk (measured share $0.5$--$0.7$) and $O(a_*^2/\nsamp)$ per linear mode; the residual bulk is heavy-tailed, so its median is not a location statistic and the check must use means. Measured ($\signoise=0.5$, code units$/\pwidth$): $0.456$ vs $\E\tanh^2=0.470$ ($\dlat=10$), $0.2915$ vs $0.2974$ ($40$), $0.2092$ vs $0.2093$ ($200$).
\end{assumption}

\subsection{Which $\Umat$: block counts for the two feature second moments}

\begin{proposition}[Block counts]\label{prop:scope-counts}
Assume (H1), (H4) and $\pwidth>\nsamp$.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] \emph{Single-draw Gram.} $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}=\nsamp$ a.s., and its non-zero spectrum splits as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-counts}
N_{\rm sig}=\dint,\qquad N_{\rm nd}=\min(\dlat,\nsamp)-\dint,\qquad N_{\rm samp}=(\nsamp-\dlat)_+,\qquad N_{\rm null}=\pwidth-\nsamp ,
\end{equation}
with a hard cliff at index $\nsamp$; the residual bulk is empty for $\psi_n\le1$.
\item[(b)] \emph{$\eta$-averaged $\Umat$.} The linear part is $a_1(q)^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\dlat\pwidth)$ with $\hat M_t=e^{-2t}\hat C+\Delta_tI_{\dlat}$ of full rank $\dlat$ for every $\dlat$. Hence $N_{\rm sig}=\dint$ and $N_{\rm nd}=D$ for all $\dlat$: for $D\le\nsamp$ all $D$ noise-dim modes lie in the data-broadened band of \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin}, and for $D>\nsamp$ exactly $\nsamp$ of them do while $D-\nsamp$ sit on the diffusion floor $a_1(q)^2\Delta_t(1\pm\psi_p^{-1/2})^2/\dlat$. The residual part $\frac{a_*^2}{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[g(x_t^\mu)g(x_t^\mu)^\top]$ has rank $>\nsamp$: there is no cliff at index $\nsamp$ and no rank-null block, only a soft residual tail (measured $e_{\nsamp-1}/e_{\nsamp}=1.32$ at $\dlat=40$, $1.73$ at $\dlat=200$; $0.3\%$ of the trace beyond index $\nsamp$).
\item[(c)] \emph{Eigenvectors.} In both cases the noise-dim eigenvectors are $W_\perp v/\|W_\perp v\|+O(\psi_p^{-1/2})$ with $v$ ranging over eigenvectors of the empirical noised null second moment; for $\Umat$ that matrix is $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$, whose non-floor eigenvectors are those of $\hat C_\perp$, so they are determined by the training noise vectors $\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) Under (H4), $\Phi=a_1(q)WX_t/\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}+a_*(q)G$ with $X_t\in\R^{\dlat\times\nsamp}$ a single draw; $\operatorname{rank}(WX_t)=\operatorname{rank}X_t=\min(\dlat,\nsamp)$ a.s.\ (the $\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$ term makes $X_t$ generic; $W$ has full column rank since $\pwidth>\dlat$), of which $\dint$ directions carry the signal spikes. $\Phi$ has $\nsamp$ columns, so $\operatorname{rank}\Umat^{(1)}\le\nsamp$, with equality a.s.\ since $G$ is generic; the residual therefore adds $\nsamp-\min(\dlat,\nsamp)$ directions and $\pwidth-\nsamp$ are annihilated. (b) $\E_\eta[x_tx_t^\top]=e^{-2t}xx^\top+\Delta_tI$, so the $\eta$-average of the linear Gram is $a_1^2W\hat M_tW^\top/(\dlat\pwidth)$ with $\hat M_t\succeq\Delta_tI$ of full rank; its null block is $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=e^{-2t}\signoise^2\Xi\Xi^\top/\nsamp+\Delta_tI_D$ with $\Xi\in\R^{D\times\nsamp}$ standard Gaussian, whose $\min(D,\nsamp)$ non-trivial eigenvalues are $e^{-2t}\signoise^2m_i+\Delta_t$ ($m_i$ Wishart eigenvalues) and whose remaining $(D-\nsamp)_+$ eigenvalues equal $\Delta_t$ exactly. The residual $\eta$-average is a sum of $\nsamp$ PSD matrices each of rank $>1$, so no cliff occurs. (c) With $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth=I_D+O(\psi_p^{-1/2})$ the eigenpairs of $W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top$ are $(\lambda_i\pwidth,W_\perp v_i)$ to that order; the isotropic $\Delta_t$ shift does not change the eigenvectors of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ relative to $\hat C_\perp$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The boundary $\dlat\to\nsamp$}

\begin{proposition}[Sample-specificity of the learned null score]\label{prop:scope-nullspec}
Model the null block of the score linearly, $s_\perp(x)=B_\perp x_\perp$, trained by gradient flow on denoising score matching over the $\nsamp$ training points at fixed $t$, in the block-diagonal approximation. Then $B_\perp(T)\to-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}$ (population target $-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}=-I_D/\beta_t^2$), and in the proportional limit $D,\nsamp\to\infty$, $D/\nsamp\to\psi$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-eps}
\varepsilon_\perp^2\;\to\;\int\frac{\rho_\perp^2\,(m-1)^2}{\big(1+\rho_\perp(m-1)\big)^2}\,d\mu_{\psi}(m),
\end{equation}
$\mu_\psi$ the Marchenko--Pastur law of ratio $\psi$ with unit mean \citep{marchenko1967}. Consequently:
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] $\varepsilon_\perp^2=\rho_\perp^2\psi\,(1-2\rho_\perp\psi+O(\rho_\perp^2\psi))$ for $\rho_\perp\psi\to0$; $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ depends on $(\dlat,\nsamp)$ only through $\psi=D/\nsamp$ and on $\signoise$ only through $\rho_\perp$. (The expansion is poor for $\rho_\perp\approx1$, $\psi\gtrsim0.1$; use \eqref{eq:scope-eps} numerically there.)
\item[(ii)] For $\psi>1$ the atom of $\mu_\psi$ at $0$ gives $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge(1-\psi^{-1})\rho_\perp^2/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$: on the $D-\nsamp$ directions orthogonal to $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ the learned score is $-x_\perp/\Delta_t$ instead of $-x_\perp/\beta_t^2$, a contraction stronger by $(1-\rho_\perp)^{-1}$; the reverse dynamics confine generated null components to the span of the training noise vectors.
\item[(iii)] $\varepsilon_\perp^2\to0$ as $\rho_\perp\to0$ at fixed $\psi$: at $\signoise=0.01$, $t=0.01$, $\rho_\perp=4.9\times10^{-3}$ and $\varepsilon_\perp^2<10^{-4}$ for all $\dlat\le1000$.
\end{itemize}
The neglected signal--null cross covariance $\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu(m_{c(\mu)}+\xi^\mu_{\rm sig})\xi^{\mu\top}_\perp$ has entries of order $\alpha_t\beta_t/\sqrt\nsamp$ and contributes a sample-specific error of the same order as $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ when $\psi$ is small.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
With DSM target $y=-\eta/\sqrt{\Delta_t}$ and $x_t=e^{-t}x+\sqrt{\Delta_t}\eta$, the null-block loss over the empirical measure is $\E\|B_\perp x_{t,\perp}-y_\perp\|^2$; its gradient flow is $\dot B_\perp=-2(B_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}+I_D)$ because $\E[x_{t,\perp}x_{t,\perp}^\top]=\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ over the training points (cluster means have zero null component) and $\E[y_\perp x_{t,\perp}^\top]=-I_D$. Hence $B_\perp(T)=-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}+(B_\perp(0)+\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1})e^{-2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}T}$; the population version replaces $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ by $\beta_t^2I_D$.

$\hat C_\perp=\signoise^2\,\Xi\Xi^\top/\nsamp$, so its eigenvalues are $\signoise^2m_i$ with $\{m_i\}$ Wishart eigenvalues of ratio $\psi$ whose empirical law converges weakly to $\mu_\psi$. Since $e^{-2t}\signoise^2m+\Delta_t=\beta_t^2(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))$,
$$\|\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2=\frac1{\beta_t^4}\sum_i\Big(\frac{1}{1+\rho_\perp(m_i-1)}-1\Big)^2=\frac1{\beta_t^4}\sum_i\frac{\rho_\perp^2(m_i-1)^2}{(1+\rho_\perp(m_i-1))^2}.$$
Dividing by $\|\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}\|_F^2=D/\beta_t^4$ gives the normalized trace of a bounded continuous function of $\hat C_\perp$ (denominator $\ge1-\rho_\perp>0$ on $m\ge0$), so weak convergence yields \eqref{eq:scope-eps}. (i): expand $(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))^{-2}=1-2\rho_\perp(m-1)+3\rho_\perp^2(m-1)^2-\dots$ and use the MP central moments $\int(m-1)^2d\mu_\psi=\psi$, $\int(m-1)^3d\mu_\psi=\psi^2$, $\int(m-1)^4d\mu_\psi=2\psi^2+\psi^3$. (ii): $\mu_\psi=(1-\psi^{-1})\delta_0+\psi^{-1}\tilde\mu$ for $\psi>1$, and the integrand at $m=0$ equals $\rho_\perp^2/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$; the $m_i=0$ eigenvectors are the complement of $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$, on which $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}=\Delta_tI$. (iii): the integrand is $O(\rho_\perp^2)$ uniformly on the support once $\rho_\perp\le\tfrac12$.
\end{proof}

Numerically, at $\signoise=0.5$, $t=0.01$ ($\rho_\perp=0.925$, $\nsamp=500$), \eqref{eq:scope-eps} gives $\varepsilon_\perp^2=0.028,\,0.103,\,0.302,\,0.638,\,1.19$ at $\dlat=20,50,100,150,200$; the first-order form $\rho_\perp^2\psi$ gives $0.026,0.077,0.163,0.248,0.334$ and under-estimates from $\dlat\approx100$ on because small eigenvalues of $\hat C_\perp$ are amplified by $(1+\rho_\perp(m-1))^{-2}$. At $\signoise=1$ ($\rho_\perp=0.98$): $0.032,0.122,0.379,0.867,1.80$.

\begin{proposition}[Onset statistics across the $\dlat\to\nsamp$ boundary]\label{prop:scope-rebound}
Assume (H1), (H3), (H4) and $\pwidth>\nsamp$; $\Umat$ is the $\eta$-averaged second moment.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(a)] \emph{Residual-bulk onset.} In the isotropic-residual approximation (columns $g(x^\mu_t)$ with uncorrelated entries of variance $1/\pwidth$),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-onset-iso}
\lambda_{\rm onset}^{\rm iso}=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp}\Big(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth}\Big)^2,\qquad \taumem^{\rm onset,iso}=\frac{\nsamp}{a_*(q)^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2},
\end{equation}
which depends on $\dlat$ only through $q\downarrow\beta_t^2$ in $a_*(q)^2$. On the repo spectra ($\signoise=0.5$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$) the measured onset exceeds \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} by $4.6,4.2,2.8,2.2,1.7,1.5,1.1,1.0$ at $\dlat=10,20,40,60,80,100,150,200$: the excess is a small-$\dlat$ effect of cluster-level residual spikes (residual features of the $\nsamp/k$ points in a cluster are correlated) and disappears at $\dlat\ge150$, where \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} is quantitatively right ($0.173$ vs $0.171$ code units at $\dlat=200$). Because the excess itself decays, \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso} under-predicts the $\dlat$-trend: the measured onset falls $107\times$ from $\dlat=10$ to $200$ ($221\times$ from $\dlat=5$), the formula predicts $24\times$ ($\approx60\times$).
\item[(b)] \emph{Noise-dim top for $D>\nsamp$.} The residual bulk does not empty at $\psi_n=1$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-counts}(b)); its top stays at \eqref{eq:scope-onset-iso}. The noise-dim block keeps $\nsamp$ data-broadened modes with top
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-onset-rd}
\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\max}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big[e^{-2t}\signoise^2\big(1+\sqrt{\psi}\big)^2+\Delta_t\Big]\big(1+\psi_p^{-1/2}\big)^2\;\xrightarrow{\psi_n\to0}\;\frac{a_1(\beta_t^2)^2\,e^{-2t}\signoise^2\,(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2}{\nsamp},
\end{equation}
so $1/\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\max}\to\nsamp/(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2)$: $\dlat$-independent and linear in $\nsamp$. Its ratio to the large-$\dlat$ limit of $\taumem^{\rm onset,iso}$ is $a_*(\beta_t^2)^2(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2/\big(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\psi_p^{-1/2})^2\big)$, which tends to $a_*(\beta_t^2)^2/(a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2)=0.019$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $t=0.01$ as $\pwidth/\dlat,\pwidth/\nsamp\to\infty$ ($0.015$ at $\psi_p=64$). These modes are sample-specific in the sense of \eqref{eq:scope-epsdef}: their eigenvectors are fixed by $\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ and their learned content deviates from the population score by $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge\rho_\perp^2(1-\psi^{-1})/(1-\rho_\perp)^2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(ii)). In this regime the earliest sample-specific modes are the noise-dim modes, learned $\approx50\times$ faster than the residual bulk: the population/sample distinction of the noise-dim ``buffer'' dissolves.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(a) Under (H4) the residual part of $\Umat$ is $\frac{a_*^2}{\nsamp}\sum_\mu\E_\eta[gg^\top]$; with uncorrelated columns of variance $1/\pwidth$ it is $a_*^2/\nsamp$ times a Wishart matrix of ratio $\nsamp/\pwidth$ with top edge $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2$; the residual bulk is this spectrum projected onto the complement of the linear span and by interlacing its top is at most the unprojected edge. The measured/iso ratios use the \texttt{bulk\_summary.json} sample-bulk tops $0.93,0.374,0.198,0.173$ (code units, $\dlat=10,40,100,200$) against $\pwidth\lambda^{\rm iso}_{\rm onset}=0.203,0.133,0.135,0.171$ with $a_*(q)^2=0.0447,0.0125,0.00644,0.00467$. (b) The null-block linear part $a_1^2W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ has non-zero eigenvalues equal to those of $a_1^2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}/\dlat$; the top edge of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ is $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1+\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t$ (for $\psi>1$ the non-zero Wishart eigenvalues lie in $[(\sqrt\psi-1)^2,(\sqrt\psi+1)^2]$) and that of $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$ is $(1+\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$; the product of top edges is the top edge of the free multiplicative convolution to leading order in the broadening. As $\psi_n\to0$, $(1+\sqrt\psi)^2/\dlat\to1/\nsamp$ and $q\to\beta_t^2$. Numerics: $a_*(\beta_t^2)^2=3.09\times10^{-3}$, $a_1(\beta_t^2)^2e^{-2t}\signoise^2=0.671\times0.245=0.164$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Conjecture: return of memorization through the null block at large $\dlat$]\label{rem:scope-rebound-conj}
Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-rebound}(b) shows that for $\psi_n\lesssim1$ the fast noise-dim modes are already sample-specific. We \emph{conjecture} that this is the channel through which memorization returns at large $\dlat$, and state what the conjecture does and does not contain.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] \emph{What a linear null score cannot do.} The converged null score $-\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{-1}x_\perp$ is linear; under the reverse dynamics it generates null components with the empirical covariance $\hat C_\perp$ (confined to $\operatorname{span}\{\xi^\mu_\perp\}$ when $D>\nsamp$), i.e.\ $x_\perp\approx\Xi c/\sqrt\nsamp$ with $c\sim N(0,I_\nsamp)$, whose squared distance to the nearest training point is $\approx2D\signoise^2\,(1-O(\sqrt{\log\nsamp/D}))$ and whose NN-ratio is $\approx1$. A linear null score therefore \emph{cannot} by itself produce NN-ratio memorization (ratio $<1/3$); that requires the softmax concentration of the empirical score \citep{biroli2024dynamical,scarvelis2023closedform,gu2023memorization}, which lives in the nonlinear (residual) part of the features. $\varepsilon_\perp$ is proposed only as a \emph{necessary-condition proxy}: it measures how much sample-specific null-block information the fast modes carry.
\item[(ii)] \emph{Conditional functional form.} If a metric-dependent threshold $\varepsilon_c$ exists such that memorization appears once $\varepsilon_\perp\ge\varepsilon_c$, then, since $\varepsilon_\perp^2$ depends on $(\dlat,\nsamp)$ only through $\psi$ and on $\signoise$ only through $\rho_\perp$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-rebound}
\dlat^{\rm reb}=\dint+\nsamp\,\psi^{\rm reb}(\rho_\perp),\qquad \varepsilon_\perp^2\big(\rho_\perp,\psi^{\rm reb}\big)=\varepsilon_c^2 ,
\end{equation}
linear in $\nsamp$ at fixed $(\signoise,t)$, absent as $\rho_\perp\to0$, and earlier for larger $\rho_\perp$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{Evidence and confounds.} In the scaled-width MLP sweep (hidden $8\dlat=800,1200,1600$; $\signoise=0.5$, $\nsamp=500$, $3\times10^5$ steps) the memorization fraction is $0.0000,0.0022,0.0144$ at $\dlat=100,150,200$ and still rising ($0.004\to0.0144$ over the final $5\times10^4$ steps at $\dlat=200$), with the mean NN-ratio falling $0.755\to0.713$; the matching $\signoise=0.01$ scaled sweep gives $0$ at all three with the NN-ratio rising $1.98\to2.59$ (distance concentration). In the paper's \emph{fixed-width} sweep (hidden $256$) at $\signoise=0.5$ the fraction is $0.0000$ at every evaluation for $\dlat=100,150,200$. Hence the rebound requires \emph{both} $\rho_\perp\approx1$ and capacity growing with $\dlat$: the $\signoise$ contrast at equal capacity rules out capacity as the sole cause, the fixed-width null rules out $\rho_\perp$ as the sole cause. Calibrating on the scaled sweep gives $\varepsilon_c^2\in(0.30,0.64)$ (the values at $\dlat=100$ and $150$) and $\psi^{\rm reb}(0.925)\in(0.19,0.29)$; this is a fit to those two points, not a test. Under \eqref{eq:scope-rebound} the discriminating predictions are $\dlat^{\rm reb}\approx55$--$80$ at $\nsamp=250$ and $200$--$300$ at $\nsamp=1000$ (same capacity schedule), and $\dlat^{\rm reb}\in[88,130]$ at $\signoise=1$, $\nsamp=500$.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\subsection{The boundary $\signoise\to0$ and the merge condition}

\begin{proposition}[$\signoise\to0$; separation threshold]\label{prop:scope-sigperp}
Assume (H1), (H2), (H4); $\Umat$ is the $\eta$-averaged second moment.
\begin{itemize}
\item[(i)] As $\signoise\to0$: $\beta_t^2\to\Delta_t$, $\rho_\perp\to0$, $q\to\Delta_t+\dint(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)/\dlat$, all eigenvalues of $\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}$ tend to $\Delta_t$ (no data-side broadening), and the noise-dim block sits on the diffusion floor
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-floor}
\lambda^{\rm nd}\to\frac{a_1(q)^2\Delta_t}{\dlat}\big(1\pm\psi_p^{-1/2}\big)^2,
\end{equation}
broadened only by $W$ (factor $1.65$ at $\psi_p=64$; measured $1.5$--$1.6$ at $\signoise=0.01$), with $N_{\rm nd}=D$. These modes carry no data variance, the learned null score equals the population one ($\varepsilon_\perp\to0$), and the data lie in the $\dint$-subspace, the setting of the linear pencil of \citet{george2025dsm}.
\item[(ii)] Including the data-side (ratio $\psi$) and width-side (ratio $D/\pwidth\approx\psi_p^{-1}$) Marchenko--Pastur broadenings, the lower edge of the noise-dim block is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ndmin}
\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big[e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1-\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t\Big](1-\psi_p^{-1/2})^2\qquad(\psi<1),
\end{equation}
and with $G:=\log_{10}(\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}/\lambda_{\rm onset})$ the one-decade criterion $G\ge1$ of (H5) holds iff
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-sigthr}
\signoise^2\;\ge\;\frac{e^{2t}}{(1-\sqrt\psi)^2}\Big[\frac{10\,\dlat\,\lambda_{\rm onset}}{a_1(q)^2(1-\psi_p^{-1/2})^2}-\Delta_t\Big]=:(\signoise^*)^2 .
\end{equation}
\item[(iii)] With the measured onsets of the repo spectra ($t=0.01$, $\pwidth=64\dlat$, $\nsamp=500$, $\signoise=0.5$): $\signoise^*=0.91,0.70,0.50,0.45,0.41,0.40,0.42,0.52$ at $\dlat=10,20,40,60,80,100,150,200$. Hence $\signoise=0.5$ is below threshold for $\dlat\le20$ and at or above it for $\dlat\ge40$, matching the measured $G<1$ for $\dlat\le20$ and $G\ge1$ for $\dlat\ge30$ (measured $G=0.64,0.89,1.12,1.19,1.27,1.26,1.26,1.09$; predicted from \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin}: $0.51,0.73,0.99,1.08,1.14,1.15,1.11,0.98$, the product-of-edges lower edge under-estimating $\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}$ by $\approx25\%$). $\signoise=0.01$ lies below the floor ($\signoise^2=5\times10^{-3}\Delta_t$) and the measured separation there is $G=0.004$--$0.056$ decades: a three-bulk spectrum. The four-bulk picture applies only for $\signoise\gtrsim\signoise^*$.
\end{itemize}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) Substitute in Definition~\ref{def:scope-ratios} and \eqref{eq:scope-edges}; $\varepsilon_\perp^2\to0$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(iii); the null block of $\hat M_t$ is $e^{-2t}\hat C_\perp+\Delta_tI_D\to\Delta_tI_D$, so the only remaining broadening is that of $W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth$, with edges $(1\pm\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$. (ii) The non-zero eigenvalues of $a_1^2W_\perp\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}W_\perp^\top/(\pwidth\dlat)$ equal those of $a_1^2\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}(W_\perp^\top W_\perp/\pwidth)\hat\Sigma_{t,\perp}^{1/2}/\dlat$; the lower edges of the two factors are $e^{-2t}\signoise^2(1-\sqrt\psi)^2+\Delta_t$ and $(1-\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$, and their product is the lower edge of the free multiplicative convolution to leading order in the broadening (exact edges from the Silverstein equation, cf.\ \citealp{ELKaroui2010spectrum}). Solve $\lambda^{\rm nd}_{\min}\ge10\lambda_{\rm onset}$ for $\signoise^2$. (iii) Code units are $\pwidth\times$ theory units. $\dlat=10$: $\lambda_{\rm onset}=0.93/640=1.45\times10^{-3}$, $a_1(q)^2=0.281$, $\psi=0.01$, giving $(\signoise^*)^2=e^{0.02}(0.9)^{-2}[100\cdot1.45\times10^{-3}/(0.281\cdot0.766)-0.0198]=0.83$. $\dlat=40$: $\lambda_{\rm onset}=0.374/2560=1.46\times10^{-4}$, $a_1(q)^2=0.494$, $\psi=0.07$, giving $(\signoise^*)^2=1.0202\cdot1.849\cdot[400\cdot1.46\times10^{-4}/(0.494\cdot0.766)-0.0198]=0.256$, $\signoise^*=0.50$. The remaining entries are the same evaluation at the other $\dlat$. Measured $G$ from \texttt{bulk\_summary.json}, of which only the noise-dim minimum and the sample-bulk top (index $\dlat$) are used (the file's block partition follows the superseded cumulative count): $\signoise=0.5$: $0.669,0.644,0.784,0.888,0.996,1.12,1.19,1.27,1.25,1.26,1.09$ at $\dlat=8,10,15,20,30,40,60,80,100,150,200$; $\signoise=0.01$: $0.045,0.032,0.019,0.006,0.004,0.056$ at $\dlat=8,\dots,40$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{$\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$: $\psi_p$ decreasing toward $1$}

\begin{remark}[$\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$]\label{rem:scope-psip}
This configuration was run only at $\dlat=20$ (exp3, $\dint$ sweep); the statements for $\dlat\ge40$ are predictions. One has $\pwidth>\nsamp$ for all $\dlat$ and $\psi_p=1+800/\dlat\ge4.3$ for $\dlat\le240$; the margin $\psi_p-1-\psi_n=300/\dlat$ of the proportional asymptotics of \citet{bonnaire2025} stays positive but is not uniform in $\dlat$. The block \emph{means} \eqref{eq:scope-edges} are $\pwidth$-independent (a $5.8\times$ change of $\pwidth$ moves them by $<0.5\%$). What changes is the block \emph{width}: the factor $(1\pm\sqrt{D/\pwidth})^2$ in \eqref{eq:scope-ndmin} has $\sqrt{D/\pwidth}=0.22$ at $\dlat=40$ (noise-dim block spanning a factor $2.4$) and $0.44$ at $\dlat=200$ (factor $6.8$, i.e.\ $0.83$ decades from $W$ alone), against $0.125$ (factor $1.65$) at $\psi_p=64$. (H5) then requires $G\ge1$ \emph{after} this broadening, which the product-of-edges estimate predicts to fail at $\signoise=0.5$ for $\dlat\gtrsim100$. On the residual side $\nsamp/\pwidth\to0.5$ at $\dlat=200$ and the MP lower edge $(1-\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2=0.086$ pushes the slowest residual modes toward zero (the random-features interpolation peak of \citealp{mei2022generalization}); this stretches the time to \emph{complete} memorization but leaves $\lambda_{\rm onset}$ within $(1+\sqrt{\nsamp/\pwidth})^2\le2.9$ of the residual mean.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The fixed-null-energy control}

\begin{proposition}[$\signoise^2=c/D$]\label{prop:scope-control}
Assume (H1), (H2), (H4) and $\signoise^2=c/D$ with $c>0$ fixed. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ctrl}
\beta_t^2=\Delta_t+\frac{e^{-2t}c}{D},\qquad \rho_\perp=\frac{e^{-2t}c}{e^{-2t}c+\Delta_tD},\qquad q=\Delta_t+\frac{\dint(\alpha_t^2-\Delta_t)+e^{-2t}c}{\dlat},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scope-ctrl-edges}
\lambda^{\rm sig}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2}{\dlat},\qquad
\lambda^{\rm nd}\asymp\frac{a_1(q)^2}{\dlat}\Big(\Delta_t+\frac{e^{-2t}c}{D}\Big),\qquad
\bar\lambda^{\rm samp}=\frac{a_*(q)^2}{\nsamp},
\end{equation}
with crossover $D_c:=e^{-2t}c/\Delta_t\approx49.5\,c$ at $t=0.01$. For $D\gg D_c$ the control is spectrally the $\signoise\to0$ limit of Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}: (i) the noise-dim block collapses onto the diffusion floor and the four-bulk separation is lost; (ii) $\taugen=\dlat/(a_1(q)^2\alpha_t^2)$ keeps its linear growth, with $a_1(q)^2\to a_1(\Delta_t)^2=0.96$ (vs.\ $0.63$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $\dlat=200$); (iii) $a_*(q)^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$ collapses faster than in the $\signoise=0.5$ sweep, so the frozen-feature delay of the residual bulk persists; (iv) $\varepsilon_\perp^2\approx\rho_\perp^2\psi\approx c^2e^{-4t}/(\Delta_t^2D\nsamp)\to0$, so no null-block rebound (Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj}) is predicted. The control thus separates the \emph{count} $D$ acting through $q$-dilution (retained) from the \emph{null data variance} acting through $\rho_\perp$ (removed): the frozen-feature theory predicts that the memorization delay survives the control while the four-bulk structure and the rebound do not.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Substitute $\signoise^2=c/D$ into Definition~\ref{def:scope-ratios} and \eqref{eq:scope-edges}; the trace check \eqref{eq:scope-trace} holds by \eqref{eq:scope-q}. (i) is Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i)--(ii). (ii) $\alpha_t^2$ is $\signoise$-independent and $a_1$ is decreasing in $q$; $a_1(0.0198)^2=0.962$. (iii) $\tanh x=x-x^3/3+\tfrac2{15}x^5+O(x^7)$ gives $\E\tanh^2(\sqrt qz)=q-2q^2+\tfrac{17}3q^3+O(q^4)$ and $a_1(q)^2q=q-2q^2+5q^3+O(q^4)$, so $a_*(q)^2=\tfrac23q^3+O(q^4)$. (iv) is Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-nullspec}(i). The prediction that the delay persists is a frozen-feature statement, not a statement about the trainable MLP.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Feature learning}

\begin{remark}[Conjecture: one-step feature learning]\label{rem:scope-feature}
Everything above is a frozen-feature (H1) result. For the trainable MLP we conjecture the following, modelled on the one-gradient-step analysis of \citet{ba2022onestep}. Let $A$ first be trained for a time $T_0$ (so that $A\neq0$; at $A(0)=0$ the $W$-gradient vanishes identically), then take one step $W_1=W_0-\eta\nabla_WL$ with
$$\nabla_WL=\frac{1}{\nsamp\sqrt{\dlat\pwidth}}\sum_\mu\big[(A^\top r_\mu)\odot\tanh'(W_0x^\mu_t/\sqrt{\dlat})\big]\,x_t^{\mu\top},\qquad r_\mu=s_A(x^\mu_t)-y_\mu .$$
The update is a sum of rank-one terms with row space in $\operatorname{span}\{x^\mu_t\}$ whose signal- and null-block energies scale as $\alpha_t^2$ and $\beta_t^2$ per dimension, so the spike it adds to the conjugate kernel $\Umat_1=\frac1\nsamp\sum_\mu\E_\eta\phi_1\phi_1^\top$ loads on the signal subspace with per-dimension null leakage $O(\beta_t^2/\alpha_t^2)$ ($0.096$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $0.007$ at $0.01$). Because $\E[rx_t^\top]=A\E[\phi x_t^\top]+I$ contains an isotropic term from the DSM target, the \emph{total} null energy of the spike, $D\beta_t^2$ against $\dint\alpha_t^2$, can be comparable at large $\dlat$; whether this shifts the noise-dim block is what (P-i) asks to be measured.
\textbf{(P-i)} After the step the signal-block eigenvalues increase by $O(\eta)$ (the cross term of $\Phi_1\Phi_1^\top$ is first order in the step; a rank-$\le\dint$ spike, BBP-type, \citealp{baik2005bbp}) while the noise-dim edges move by at most $O(\eta\beta_t^2/\alpha_t^2)$: $\taugen$ decreases, the noise-dim timescale does not.
\textbf{(P-ii)} Under continued training the first layer acquires components aligned with individual training points (the nonlinear part of the score target is the softmax assignment of the empirical score, \citealp{biroli2024dynamical,scarvelis2023closedform}), raising the top of the residual bulk and lowering $\taumem^{\rm onset}$; the buffer in absolute steps, $\taumem^{\rm onset}-\taugen$, is then \emph{shorter} than the frozen-feature value even though (P-i) alone lengthens it in ratio.
Status: conjecture. The saved RFNN \texttt{eigenvalues\_pre/post.npy} cannot test it: $W$ is frozen there and the pre/post block medians agree to $<0.5\%$ (\texttt{results\_rfnn\_exp2v3}, $\dlat=5$--$40$), which only confirms (H1). The test requires logging the first-layer conjugate-kernel spectrum of the MLP at initialization and during training (\texttt{compute\_U} applies verbatim to $W_1$); the MLP runs saved only \texttt{config.json} and \texttt{metrics.jsonl}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Configurations outside (H1)--(H5)}

\begin{remark}[Out-of-scope configurations]\label{rem:scope-outside}
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\dlat=\dint=5$ (both sweeps): $N_{\rm nd}=0$ and $\pwidth=320<\nsamp$, so (H2) fails twice; the residual bulk has $\le\pwidth-\dlat=315$ modes. A baseline, not a four-bulk spectrum.
\item $\signoise=0.01$ at $t=0.01$ (RFNN and MLP, exp2 and exp3): $\rho_\perp=4.9\times10^{-3}$, $\signoise^2=5\times10^{-3}\Delta_t$, measured noise-dim/residual gap $0.004$--$0.056$ decades. (H3), (H5) fail: three-bulk regime. Counts and $\taugen$ still apply; Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i) is the operative statement and predicts no rebound (observed in the scaled sweep: memorization $0$ for $20\le\dlat\le200$). Floor medians match $\pwidth a_1(q)^2\Delta_t/\dlat$ for $\dlat\ge30$ ($0.67,0.77$ predicted vs $0.74,0.81$ measured at $\dlat=30,40$); at $\dlat=10$ the floor block is merged with the residual bulk ($G=0.03$) and the comparison is not meaningful.
\item $\dlat\ge150$ at $\nsamp=500$, $\signoise=0.5$: $\psi_n\le3.3$, $\varepsilon_\perp^2\ge0.64$; regime of Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj}. (H5) also degrades (gap $1.26\to1.09$ decades from $\dlat=150$ to $200$). Memorization returns only in the scaled-width MLP sweep ($0.0022,0.0144$ at $\dlat=150,200$, hidden $8\dlat$) and not in the fixed-width sweep (hidden $256$: $0.0000$).
\item $\dint$ sweep at $\dlat=20$ (exp3): $\dint\in\{16,20\}$ gives $N_{\rm nd}\in\{4,0\}$; the signal block is a finite-rank spike for every $\dint$, so ``signal edges'' are spike locations, not bulk edges.
\item RFNN with $\pwidth=\dlat+\nsamp+300$: run only at $\dlat=20$; Remark~\ref{rem:scope-psip} predicts that width-side broadening breaks (H5) for $\dlat\gtrsim100$ at $\signoise=0.5$.
\item Isotropic control $\signoise=\sigsig=1$ (no synthetic run at $\signoise=1$ exists; all statements are predictions): (H3) holds ($\rho_\perp=0.98$) and the $D$ noise-dim modes exist at $a_1(q)^2\beta_t^2/\dlat$ between signal and residual modes, but the signal/noise-dim ratio is $\alpha_t^2/\beta_t^2=2.76$ ($0.44$ decades) for all $\dlat$: the two-atom \emph{separation} of the signal block is absent, not the block. The reported $10.7\times$ growth of the spectral gap under isotropy must then be a residual-bulk effect (fall of $a_*(q)^2$ and of the cluster-spike excess), not a noise-dim one. Remark~\ref{rem:scope-rebound-conj} with $\varepsilon_c^2\in(0.30,0.64)$ gives $\psi^{\rm reb}(0.98)\in(0.166,0.251)$, i.e.\ $\dlat^{\rm reb}\in[88,130]$ at $\nsamp=500$, earlier than at $\signoise=0.5$.
\item All trainable-MLP runs (synthetic, hidden $256$ fixed or $8\dlat$ scaled; real, hidden $256$): (H1) fails; only Remark~\ref{rem:scope-feature} applies.
\item Real VAE latents ($t=0.1$, $\nsamp=1000$): $\rho_\Sigma$ is continuous, effective rank $\approx0.5\,\dlat$, and for $\dlat\ge140$ (CelebA) / $160$ (CIFAR-10) a set of coordinates has $\lambda=\Delta_t$ exactly (posterior collapse). (H3) fails as stated; the collapsed coordinates are the $\signoise=0$ case of Proposition~\ref{prop:scope-sigperp}(i): free modes at the floor with $\varepsilon_\perp=0$, counted in $N_{\rm nd}$ but not contributing to $\rho_\perp$. The general-$\rho_\Sigma$ form of \citet[Thm.~3.1]{bonnaire2025} applies; the two-atom edges do not.
\item Time mismatch: spectra are computed at the single time $t=0.01$, while training and generation cover $t\in[0.01,3]$ (the reverse SDE is integrated from $t_{\max}=3$ to $t_{\min}=0.01$; the code's $t_{\rm eval}=0.1$ enters only the score-error metric). $\rho_\perp(t)\ge\tfrac12$ only for $t\le\tfrac12\log(1+\signoise^2)$ ($0.11$ at $\signoise=0.5$, $5\times10^{-5}$ at $0.01$), so the null block is data-dominated only on a short final segment of the reverse trajectory.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}
```

### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Reframe the posterior-collapse material: delete the claim that collapsed coordinates 'do not affect memorisation timing' and that the released predictor's large-d trend is 'an artefact of posterior collapse'. Collapsed coordinates are exactly the sigma_perp->0 buffer of the paper's synthetic thesis and enter the piece's own clock through q-dilution (Cor. (iii)); the only defensible test is 'drop them while holding the first-layer pre-activation variance fixed'. Alongside this, redo the synthetic onset arithmetic in code units (predicted top edge 0.254 -> 0.174, a 1.5x drop vs observed 221x), and add the p>n hypothesis (p=320<n=500 at d_lat=5 on the p=64 d_lat sweep).

Independent check: Gauss-Hermite (80 nodes, tanh): a_1^2 = 0.623/0.367/0.320/0.246/0.180 and a_*^2 = 0.0047/0.0274/0.0359/0.0544/0.0784 at q = 0.33/1.0/1.247/1.834/2.76 (piece's table correct); a_*^2/q^3 -> 0.661 at q=1e-3 (2/3 confirmed analytically: E tanh^2 = q-2q^2+17q^3/3, a_1^2 q = q-2q^2+5q^3). Ratios: CelebA 50->200 1.51x, 100->200 1.49x, CIFAR 100->260 1.70x, synthetic 2.76->0.33 16.5x (all as stated). CSV checks: lambda_mean CelebA 2.575/1.834/1.808/1.247, CIFAR 2.271/1.783/1.108; cv of ||z||^2/d CelebA 0.479->0.223, CIFAR 0.349->0.168; tau_5 CelebA 300k->1160k (3.87x), 400k->1160k (2.9x); CIFAR 720k->2520k (3.5x, but sd 1.39M at d=260; d=240 gives 2.08x); tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14 (CelebA d=200), 3.0 (CIFAR d=200). One-constant refit: the piece's 0.347/0.449, 0.246/0.308, 0.161/0.176, 0.158/0.165 and the tau∝d baseline 0.125/0.083 reproduce exactly with CelebA d>=50, CIFAR d>=60, median-C rule; released in-sample 0.339/0.480, 0.169/0.345, 0.146/0.327, 0.143/0.562 reproduce with the same sets (with the paper's d_ID thresholds 70/140: released 0.311 vs a_* 0.335/0.377 LOO CelebA 5%; CIFAR d>=140 only 7/4 points). Fitted C at 5%: CelebA 21.5 vs CIFAR 49.9 (25%: 63 vs 105) with identical training configs (hidden 1024, depth 5, GELU, SGD lr 1e-3, m 0.8). Gaussian-moment 10-90 a_*^2 ratio at CelebA d=200 = 1.806 (reproduces the piece's 1.81; per-sample latents are not in the repo); final fractions at 5M steps: CelebA d=200 0.448, d=100 0.782; CIFAR d=200 0.346. Code-unit onset: p a_*^2/n (1+sqrt(n/p))^2 = 0.254 (d=5, p=320) vs 0.174 (d=200, p=12800): 1.46x, vs F5's 221x; MP edge factor 5.06/1.43 = 3.5x as stated. Raw RFNN data: di5_d5 has p=320 < n=500, 320 nonzero eigenvalues (no rank-null bulk); di5_d200 has 12800 eigenvalues > 1e-8 with n_noise_samples=50, t_fixed=0.01; only eigenvalues saved. Real-data first layer: nn.Linear(d+64, 1024)+GELU on concat(x_t, 32 sin/cos time features), PyTorch default init; deployed q_mu ≈ (d m + 32)/(3(d+64)) ≈ 0.39 (d=50) vs 0.37 (d=200). GELU at q=1.247: mean^2 0.110, a_1^2 q 0.312, centred residual 0.125. Released calibrate(): 121-point log10 grid on [-12,-4], PAVA over 6 thresholds, fit_df = all uncensored rows (includes d=20,30 CelebA, d=40 CIFAR). nn1_idx returned by the metric but not logged. references.bib has bonnaire2025, biroli2024dynamical, somepalli2023diffusion, pennington2017nonlinear, benigni2021eigenvalue, ELKaroui2010spectrum; no gu2023memorization, scarvelis, george, hu, mei, goldt, ba, baik, marchenko keys.

- [fatal] predictions[2] (collapsed coordinates do not affect timing), rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred ('artefact of posterior collapse'), cor:shape-from-qmu-pred(iii), appendix 'Falsification of the released clock': The claim that dropping collapsed coordinates (lambda_i(M_t)=Delta_t exactly) 'leaves the fraction curve unchanged' contradicts (a) the paper's own headline synthetic result: the sigma_perp=0.01 sweep is precisely 'excess coordinates with ~zero data variance' and there memorization IS delayed with d_lat (F13: sigma_perp->0 puts the noise-dim bulk on the diffusion floor Delta_t, i.e. collapsed coords are the synthetic buffer), and (b) the piece's own Corollary (iii): q_mu = (e^{-2t}||z_active||^2 + d_lat Delta_t)/d_lat changes when the denominator d_lat and the Delta_t share are removed, so by the piece's own clock the curve moves. Calling the released predictor's large-d trend 'an artefact of posterior collapse' is therefore not defensible as written: the count of collapsed coordinates dilutes q_d, which is the piece's own mechanism.  
  FIX: Restate: collapsed coordinates enter the sample-bulk clock only through q-dilution (their share of Delta_t in q_mu and the 1/d_lat in the first-layer scaling), which is the same channel as the synthetic sigma_perp->0 buffer; the released clock instead couples to them through lambda_i=Delta_t modes averaged in P_d(s), an unmotivated channel. Correct testable prediction: drop the collapsed coordinates AND rescale the input gain so that the first-layer pre-activation variance q_mu is unchanged; then the fraction curve is unchanged while P_d(s) moves. Delete 'cannot affect memorisation timing' and 'artefact'.
- [major] rem:collective-pred (statement and appendix), predictions[5] 'already_supported_by', open_gaps (2), empirical_anchor (4): Unit bookkeeping for the synthetic onset is inconsistent. In the median comparison the piece correctly converts to code units (code = p x theory, p = 64 d_lat, giving 40x/16.5x = 2.4x), but for the onset it says the per-sample clock explains '16.5x (x3.5 MP edge) of the 221x'. In code units the predicted top edge is p a_*^2/n (1+sqrt(n/p))^2: d=5: 320*0.0784/500*5.06 = 0.254; d=200: 12800*0.0047/500*1.43 = 0.174, i.e. a 1.46x drop against the observed 221x (F5). So the per-sample+MP clock explains essentially nothing of the onset growth (unexplained ~150x, not ~4x), and the rho_c^3 factor (~110x) of the conjecture carries the whole effect rather than a residual.  
  FIX: Redo the arithmetic in code units throughout: state predicted onset-rate change 0.254 -> 0.174 (1.5x) vs observed 221x; say the conjectured collective block must supply ~150x; drop '16.5x (x3.5 MP edge) of the 221x' everywhere including open_gaps (2).
- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred proof (MP half-width '≈1.25 at d_lat=5'), rem:collective-pred median-rate estimate, use of F1 counts on the p=64 d_lat sweep: On the sweep F5 uses (p = 64 d_lat, n=500), p < n for d_lat <= 7: at d_lat=5 the saved spectrum (sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn/raw_data/di5_d5_n500_s42/eigenvalues_pre.npy) has exactly 320 eigenvalues, all nonzero, so there is no rank-null bulk, the sample bulk has p - d_lat = 315 modes (not n - d_lat = 495), 'one eigenvalue per training point' is impossible (320 directions for 500 points), 'relative half-width 2 sqrt(n/p) = 1.25' is meaningless (>1), and the sample-bulk MEAN in theory units is a_*^2/p, not a_*^2/n (code units: a_*^2 = 0.078 at d=5 vs 12800*0.0047/500 = 0.121 at d=200, ratio 1.55x, not 2.4x). Also the raw runs use n_noise_samples=50 so rank(U) <= 50 n at finite xi-sampling; at d=200 all 12800 eigenvalues exceed 1e-8, so 'n - d_lat sample modes' must be read as a cliff, per F1, not an exact rank.  
  FIX: Add hypothesis p > n (in fact p >> n) to prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred and note it fails for d_lat <= 7 on the p=64 d_lat sweep; give the p<n branch (mean a_*^2/p, edges symmetric in n,p); recompute the median comparison (1.55x predicted vs ~1.04x observed); state that small-d_lat onset behaviour on that sweep is confounded by p<n before invoking cluster-collective modes.
- [major] predictions[6] (cross-dataset transfer) and its 'already_supported_by': The repo's own tables already contradict the claim. CelebA and CIFAR-10 runs use identical training hyperparameters (FlexibleMLPScore hidden 1024, depth 5, GELU, SGD lr 0.001, momentum 0.80), so no eta/width rescaling applies, yet the fitted C = exp(median log(tau_5 a_*^2(q_d)/n)) is 21.5 (CelebA) vs 49.9 (CIFAR) at 5% and 63 vs 105 at 25%: a 2.3x / 1.7x mismatch. The 'released kappa differ by only 1.36x' support is irrelevant to C, and kappa sits on a grid of step 10^{1/15} = 1.166x (1.36 = two grid steps) and is jointly fit with six thetas, so it is poorly identified.  
  FIX: Report the 2.3x C mismatch as the current status (transfer fails at the one-constant level) and drop the kappa-based support; or reframe as 'C is dataset-specific by a factor ~2 with identical eta, so an unmodelled dataset-dependent factor (e.g. |D_{t_c}|/n, decoder distortion of d_NN) is required'.
- [major] def:two-source-predictor-pred (gamma, sigma), practitioner recipe step 1, empirical_anchor (2)-(5), open_gaps (5)-(6): The deployed real-data first layer is nn.Linear(d_lat + 64 -> 1024) with GELU acting on concat([x_t, 32 sin + 32 cos time features]) with PyTorch default init (var = 1/(3 fan_in)). So for the deployed network q_mu = (e^{-2t}||z||^2 + d Delta_t + 32)/(3 (d+64)), which is ~0.39 at d=50 and ~0.37 at d=200 (ratio 1.05), not lambda_mean = 1.83 -> 1.25; and GELU is not odd, so Lemma (iii) fails (k=0 and k=2 Hermite terms: mean^2 = 0.11-0.19, k=2 gives O(rho^2) off-diagonals and a rank-one spike). The anchored numbers (1.5x/1.7x explained slowdown, LOO 0.449/0.176) therefore come from the tanh/gamma=1/q=lambda_mean surrogate, not from the recipe as defined; applying the piece's own recipe to the deployed network would shrink the predicted a_*^2 slowdown to ~1.05x and widen the unexplained gap to ~3x.  
  FIX: State explicitly that all real-data numbers use the tanh surrogate with gamma=1 and no time-embedding, and that the deployed network's first layer (GELU, fan-in d+64 with 64 fixed-norm time inputs, default init) gives a nearly d_lat-independent q_mu; either add the time-embedding dilution term and GELU Hermite coefficients to the recipe, or downgrade the real-data section to 'surrogate clock' and say the derived predictor has not yet been evaluated with the deployed network's own q_mu.
- [major] rem:param-count-pred, empirical_anchor (1) and (5), main text 'post-threshold dims': (a) The released code (make_celeba_spectral_mem_predictor_figures.py, calibrate()) fits kappa and the PAVA thetas on ALL uncensored rows, including pre-threshold d=20,30 (CelebA) and d=40 (CIFAR); it does not restrict to post-threshold dims, contrary to the remark (and to the paper's own caption). (b) The piece's 'post-threshold' set is undeclared; its numbers (0.339/0.480, 0.169/0.345, 0.146/0.327, 0.143/0.562 and the one-constant 0.347/0.449 etc.) reproduce exactly only with CelebA d>=50, CIFAR d>=60 and a median-C rule, whereas the paper defines the identity threshold as d_ID(0.95) ~ 70 (CelebA) / ~140 (CIFAR). With the paper's thresholds the comparison changes (CelebA d>=70: released 0.311 vs a_* 0.335 in / 0.377 LOO at 5%; CIFAR d>=140 leaves 7 points at 5% and 4 at 25%).  
  FIX: State the dim sets and the C rule (median of log residual) explicitly; correct the remark to 'fitted on all uncensored dims, including pre-threshold ones'; either adopt the paper's d_ID(0.95) sets or justify d>=50/60; flag to the authors that the released code and the caption disagree on both the response function ((kappa,a,b) sigmoid vs PAVA) and the fit set.
- [major] predictions[3] and appendix 'Validation protocol' (10-90 span 1.81 vs tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14): (a) Per-sample latent norms are not in the repo (latent_norms.npz / spectra.npz are not present; only mean/std columns), and the 1.81 is reproduced exactly (1.806) by assuming ||z||^2/d Gaussian with the CSV mean 1.302 and sd 0.2905, so 'computed from the 1k latents' is false. (b) The comparison is not like-for-like: at CelebA d=200 the final fraction is 0.448, so the 5% and 25% absolute levels are 11% and 56% of the asymptote; the corresponding a_*^2 quantile ratio under the same Gaussian assumption is ~1.4x, not 1.8x, against the observed 2.14x.  
  FIX: Say the quantile ratio is a Gaussian-moment estimate from the CSV mean/std, and compare quantiles matched to the observed asymptote (11%/56% at d=200 CelebA: ~1.4x vs 2.14x); the qualitative conclusion (learning-side spread too narrow) survives.
- [minor] empirical_anchor (4) and main text/appendix 'observed 3.5x' CIFAR d=100->260: The CIFAR d=260 5% hitting time (2,520k) has sd 1,392k and CI ±1.73M across 5 seeds; d=240 gives 1,500k (2.08x vs d=100). The 3.5x anchor rests on the noisiest point in the table.  
  FIX: Quote d=100->240 (2.1x) or give the CI; state the a_*^2 ratio 1.70x accordingly.
- [minor] empirical_anchor (7) vs rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred and F9: The anchor says lambda_min = Delta_t 'for d>=160 (CelebA CSV), d>=200 (CIFAR CSV)', but the CSVs give CelebA d=140: 0.181454 and CIFAR d=160: 0.183274 vs Delta_t = 0.181269, i.e. within 1e-4 / 2e-3 of the floor, consistent with F9's >=140 / >=160 which the remark uses. Internal inconsistency.  
  FIX: Use F9's thresholds (140/160) and give the tolerance (excess < 2e-3).
- [minor] predictions[0], [1], [4], [5] 'how_to_test': Not testable from the repo's local data: nn1_idx is computed by remote_metrics.nn_ratio_memorization but the run script logs only memorization_fraction_pixel and mean_nn_ratio_pixel (no NN1 identities); the real-data results directories and the VAE d-sweep checkpoints are not in the local repo (only the clean CSV summaries); RFNN raw_data saves eigenvalues only (no eigenvectors or W), so the IPR test requires re-running experiment_v2_rfnn.py. Only predictions 3, 4 (with the caveat above) and 7 are testable from existing files, and 7 is already contradicted.  
  FIX: Mark predictions 1, 2, 5, 6 as requiring new runs (log nn1_idx; save W/A or U eigenvectors; re-encode latents), and say so in the validation protocol.
- [minor] predictions[4] (kappa_d ∝ a_1(q_d)^2/d_lat): By the piece's own Remark, the released clock runs on the data-side (generalising) modes, so refitting kappa_d to memorisation times tests the wrong object; the prediction is a consistency check on the data-side rate, not a memorisation prediction. Also F3 supports a_1(q)^2 alpha_t^2 psi_p for the RFNN only.  
  FIX: Rephrase as a check of the data-side rate (measure lambda_i(U) on the synthetic RFNN vs a_1(q_d)^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d_lat), not as a memorisation-time regression.
- [minor] citations_needed: hu2022universality: Hu & Lu, IEEE Trans. Inf. Theory vol. 69 no. 3 pp. 1932-1964 is the 2023 issue (arXiv 2009.07669, 2020); the entry says year 2022.  
  FIX: Set year={2023} (or cite the arXiv version with 2020).
- [minor] def:two-source-predictor-pred (d_NN in latent space) vs F10: Memorisation is measured in pixel space after decoding (F10), while D_{t_c} is defined with latent-space d_NN; decoder distortion of neighbour distances and the NN-ratio concentration drift (mean ratio 0.52->0.87 on synthetic data) are not in the predictor, though open_gaps mentions the drift.  
  FIX: State the latent-vs-pixel mismatch as a hypothesis ('decoder approximately preserves NN ordering') and list it in open_gaps.


### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Replace the per-sample sample-bulk eigenvalue Lambda_mu = a_*(q_mu)^2/n by the noise-averaged one, Lambda_mu = (1/n) sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q_mu)^2 (1-Delta_t/q_mu)^k/k! = r(q_mu) a_*(q_mu)^2/n (2-D Gauss-Hermite: E[tanh(sqrt q z)tanh(sqrt q z')] - a_1^2 q varrho, corr = varrho = 1 - Delta_t/q), state explicitly that a_*^2/n is only the single-noise-draw statement, and fix the Gram identity in prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred(iii) which is false for the E_xi-averaged U that the proposition, the training loss and the repo's compute_U (50 draws) all use; then propagate the strongly d_lat-dependent factor r(q) (0.086 -> 0.755 across the synthetic sweep) through every ratio and fit quoted.

Independent check: (1) Lemma algebra: Stein gives E[sigma z] = sqrt q a_1; tanh x = x - x^3/3 + 2x^5/15 gives E sigma^2 = q - 2q^2 + (17/3) q^3, a_1 = 1 - q + 2q^2 - (17/3)q^3, a_1^2 q = q - 2q^2 + 5q^3, so a_*^2 = (2/3) q^3: confirmed; numerically a_*^2/q^3 = 0.617 at q = 0.01. 120-node Gauss-Hermite: a_1^2 = 0.623, 0.367, 0.345, 0.320, 0.251, 0.246, 0.180 and a_*^2 = 0.0047, 0.0274, 0.0312, 0.0359, 0.0529, 0.0544, 0.0784 at q = 0.33, 1, 1.108, 1.247, 1.783, 1.834, 2.76; a_*^2 monotone increasing on [0.05, 3.5]; ratios a_*^2: CelebA 1.834/1.247 = 1.51, CIFAR 1.783/1.108 = 1.70, synthetic 2.76/0.33 = 16.5 (all match the piece). (2) Gradient-flow ODE: grad_A L = A U - V, a_i-dot = -lambda_i(a_i - a_i^*), L - L^* = (1/2) tr[(A-A^*) U (A-A^*)^T]: confirmed; V u_i = 0 whenever lambda_i = 0 so A^* = V U^+ is a minimiser. Trace check of the stated edges: d_int a_1^2 alpha^2/d + (d-d_int) a_1^2 beta^2/d + a_*^2 = a_1^2 q + a_*^2 = E tanh^2: passes by construction. (3) Noise-averaged sample mode: two noise draws of the same training point have normalised overlap varrho = e^{-2t}||x||^2/(d q) = 1 - Delta_t/q; by Mehler, ||E_xi rho||^2 = E[tanh tanh'] - a_1^2 q varrho. Fraction r = that/a_*^2 at t = 0.1: 0.086 (q=0.33), 0.538 (1.108), 0.574 (1.247), 0.675 (1.834), 0.755 (2.76). Corrected d-slopes: synthetic 145x (vs 16.5x; observed onset 221x), CelebA 1.78x (vs 1.51x; obs 2.9x), CIFAR 2.11x (vs 1.70x; obs 3.5x). (4) Direct simulation (tanh RFNN, synthetic clustered data, k=10, s=3, sigma_perp=0.5, t=0.1): d=20,n=100,p=1280: one draw -> ev[n] = 1.5e-17 (cliff at n), sample-bulk median 1.45e-4 (a_*^2/n = 2.46e-4); 50 draws (repo default compute_U) -> ev[n-1] = 4.70e-5, ev[n] = 4.66e-5, ev[n+50] = 2.9e-5, sum of ev beyond n = 7.6e-3 = 31% of a_*^2 (no cliff), median 8.75e-5 vs corrected r a_*^2/n = 1.17e-4. d=50,n=200,p=3200: one draw cliff at n, median 5.1e-5 (a_*^2/n = 6.2e-5); 50 draws median 1.95e-5 vs corrected 1.85e-5 (piece's formula 6.2e-5, 3x off). (5) Repo code: code/v3/lib/eigenvalues.py compute_U averages over n_noise_samples=50 draws and uses phi = tanh(x_t W^T) with no p^{-1/2}; make_celeba_spectral_mem_predictor_figures.py calibrate() fits kappa on a 121-point log grid plus PAVA thresholds over THRESHOLDS -- the piece's 7-parameter count is correct. (6) CSV checks: lambda_mean = e^{-2t} z_normsq_over_d_mean + Delta_t exactly for every row; CelebA lambda_min = 0.1815 from d=140, CIFAR from d=160; CelebA effective rank 89.5/90.3/87.9 at 160/180/200; cv of ||z||^2/d 0.48 -> 0.22 (CelebA), 0.35 -> 0.17 (CIFAR); tau_5 CelebA 300k (d=50) -> 1160k (d=200) = 3.87x; tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14 at CelebA d=200, 3.0 at CIFAR d=200. (7) One-constant fits reproduced from the tau tables (C = mean log-residual): CelebA 5% d>=50: a_*^2 clock 0.361/0.397 (in-sample/LOO), corrected r a_*^2 clock 0.319/0.351, tau ∝ d 0.126/0.139, released 0.339; CelebA 25% d>=50: 0.249/0.274, 0.213/0.235, 0.086/0.094, released 0.169; CIFAR 5% d>=100: 0.123/0.138, 0.122/0.137, 0.146/0.164, released 0.116; on all uncensored dims (d>=20) CelebA 5%: 0.385/0.415 and tau ∝ d 0.488. (8) MP numbers for p = 64 d_lat, n = 500: d=5: n/p = 1.56 (>1), sqrt = 1.25, 2 sqrt = 2.5, (1+sqrt)^2 = 5.06; d=200: n/p = 0.039, sqrt = 0.198, 2 sqrt = 0.395, (1+sqrt)^2 = 1.43. (9) Collective-block numerology with e^{-2t}: varrho_c = 0.534 (d=5), 0.112 (d=200); block eigenvalue estimate a_*^2/n + (n/k-1) g/n = 1.0e-3 vs 1.0e-5, ~100x.

- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred (iii), proof step 'The nonzero eigenvalues of U_* are those of the n x n Gram G_*/n with (G_*)_{mu nu} = E_xi rho(x_t^mu)^T rho(x_t^nu)': This identity is false for the U the piece itself defines (and the U that prop:mode-clocks-pred uses in the training loss). With E_xi inside, U_* = (1/n) sum_mu E_xi[rho_mu rho_mu^T] is not Phi Phi^T for any p x n matrix Phi, so it has no n x n Gram; its rank is up to p, not n. The correct decomposition is U_* = (1/n) sum_mu [rhobar_mu rhobar_mu^T + Cov_xi(rho_mu)] with rhobar_mu = E_xi rho(x_t^mu). The rank-<=n localized part has Gram (rhobar_mu^T rhobar_nu), whose diagonal is NOT a_*(q_mu)^2 but ||rhobar_mu||^2 = sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q_mu)^2 varrho_mu^k / k! with varrho_mu = e^{-2t}||x^mu||^2/(d q_mu) = 1 - Delta_t/q_mu (Mehler between two independent noise draws of the same training point; this is exactly the piece's own part (ii) applied to x=y with different xi). The remaining mass (1-r(q_mu)) a_*(q_mu)^2 is a diffuse full-rank cloud. I verified this by simulation (d=50, n=200, p=3200, 50 noise draws as in the repo's compute_U): sample-bulk median 1.95e-5 vs the corrected formula r a_*^2/n = 1.85e-5 vs the piece's a_*^2/n = 6.2e-5 (3x off); and with 50 draws ev[n]/ev[n-1] = 1.0, i.e. there is NO cliff at index n (sum of eigenvalues beyond n = 31% of a_*^2). With a single draw the cliff at n is at machine precision and a_*^2/n is roughly right (median 5.1e-5 vs 6.2e-5). So the piece's Lambda_mu = a_*(q_mu)^2/n is only the single-draw statement, F1's count is only the single-draw count, but the repo's default compute_U(n_noise_samples=50) and the actual training loss (fresh noise every step) are the averaged object. The factor r(q) = ||rhobar||^2/a_*^2 is strongly d_lat-dependent: 0.086 (q=0.33), 0.538 (1.108), 0.574 (1.247), 0.675 (1.834), 0.755 (2.76). This changes the piece's headline slopes: synthetic 2.76->0.33 ratio becomes 145x instead of 16.5x (observed onset 221x); CelebA 1.834->1.247 becomes 1.78x (was 1.51x; observed 2.9x); CIFAR 1.783->1.108 becomes 2.11x (was 1.70x; observed 3.5x). It also partially closes the 'missing slope' the piece lists as open gap (2).  
  FIX: State two objects explicitly. (a) Single-draw U (one x_t^mu per point; this is what F1/F2 describe): Lambda_mu = a_*(q-hat_mu)^2/n with q-hat_mu the realised norm; Gram argument valid. (b) Noise-averaged U (training loss; compute_U with 50 draws): sample-specific eigenvalue Lambda_mu = (1/n) sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q_mu)^2 (1-Delta_t/q_mu)^k / k! = r(q_mu) a_*(q_mu)^2 / n, localized on rhobar_mu, plus a diffuse cloud of mass (1-r) a_*^2 spread over up to p directions (so rank(U) is not <= n; the 'n-d_lat sample bulk' of F1 becomes the localized modes only). Use (b) in def:two-source-predictor-pred and in every ratio quoted (replace a_*(q)^2 by r(q) a_*(q)^2, i.e. by the 2-D Gauss-Hermite integral E[tanh(sqrt q z) tanh(sqrt q z')] - a_1^2 q varrho with corr(z,z') = varrho = 1 - Delta_t/q). Recompute the anchor numbers: with the corrected clock the one-constant fits are CelebA 5% 0.319 in-sample / 0.351 LOO (d>=50), 25% 0.213/0.235; CIFAR 5% 0.122/0.137 (d>=100). Reconcile with F1 by saying which n_noise_samples the F1 spectra used; if it was 50, F1's machine-precision cliff at n needs re-checking (my simulation shows none).
- [major] predictions[1] ('dropping collapsed coordinates leaves the fraction curve unchanged') vs cor:shape-from-qmu-pred(iii) and def:two-source-predictor-pred: The prediction contradicts the piece's own theory. q_mu = gamma(e^{-2t}||z^mu||^2/d_lat + Delta_t) has an explicit 1/d_lat: removing zero-variance coordinates leaves ||z^mu||^2 unchanged but lowers d_lat to the effective rank, so q_mu RISES and a_*(q_mu)^2 rises. At CelebA d=200 (q_d = 1.247, e^{-2t}mean||z||^2/d = 1.066, effective rank ~88): q_d -> 1.066*200/88 + 0.181 = 2.60, a_*^2 goes 0.036 -> 0.075, so the predictor says memorisation becomes ~2x FASTER (more with the r(q) factor), not 'unchanged'. Corollary (iii)'s phrase 'contribute only through their share of Delta_t in q_mu' is also incomplete: they contribute through the 1/d_lat denominator, which is precisely the fan-in scaling of the first layer.  
  FIX: Restate (iii): collapsed coordinates enter the sample-bulk clock only through q_mu, i.e. through the pre-activation variance, via both the Delta_t share and the 1/d_lat fan-in normalisation. Restate the prediction as a quantitative test: dropping the collapsed coordinates should shift the fraction curve by exactly the factor r(q')a_*(q')^2 / (r(q)a_*(q)^2) with q' computed at d_eff (a ~2x speed-up at CelebA d=200 under fan-in init), whereas the released P_d(s) shifts for an unrelated reason (loss of its slowest modes). If instead the network's first-layer gain is not fan-in scaled, say so and recompute q'.
- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred and rem:collective-pred: 'relative half-width ~2 sqrt(n/p): ~1.25 at d_lat=5, 0.2 at d_lat=200 for p=64 d_lat, n=500'; 'MP edge factor (1+sqrt(n/p))^2 adds 5.1/1.4': (a) The quoted numbers are sqrt(n/p) (1.25, 0.198), not 2 sqrt(n/p) (2.5, 0.395); the formula and the numbers disagree. (b) At d_lat=5 with p=64 d_lat=320 < n=500 the aspect ratio is n/p=1.56>1: the residual Gram is rank-deficient, there are only p-d_lat (not n-d_lat) nonzero sample modes and no rank-null block, so F1's counts and the 'MP spread around Lambda_mu' picture do not apply for d_lat < n/64 ~ 8. The piece uses d_lat=5 numbers throughout rem:collective-pred without noting this. (c) For the smoothing width varsigma in def:two-source-predictor-pred the relevant quantity is the standard deviation of log-eigenvalue under Marchenko-Pastur, ~ sqrt(n/p) (the MP variance with ratio gamma is gamma), not the half-width 2 sqrt(n/p).  
  FIX: Use varsigma = sqrt(n/p) (state it as the MP standard deviation), correct the quoted numbers to match whichever formula is kept, and add the hypothesis p > n (so n/p < 1) to prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred; flag that in the p=64 d_lat sweep this fails for d_lat <= 7 and that rem:collective-pred's d_lat=5 point is outside the proposition's regime for this reason as well.
- [minor] rem:collective-pred: 'observed: tau_mem on the median statistic changes by ~8.1/7.8 ~ 1.04x': This is inferred by dividing two F5 ratios, but F5 does not say whether the denominator of the median-statistic ratio is tau_gen = 1/lambda_min^signal (grows 8.1x) or a median signal statistic (F3's medians RISE 3.4x in code units, so 1/median falls). Under the second reading the median sample eigenvalue would rise ~26x, not fall 1.04x. Also the direction is not stated: the per-sample clock in code units 64 d_lat a_*(q_d)^2/n predicts the median sample eigenvalue INCREASES 2.4x with d_lat (i.e. tau_mem(median) falls), which is the opposite sign to the '1.04x growth' inferred. Independently, the overlap varrho_c = s^2/(d_lat q_d) omits the e^{-2t} factor on the data-data overlap of noised points: correct values are 0.53 (d=5) and 0.11 (d=200), not 0.65/0.14; and cross-cluster overlaps in a 5-dimensional signal subspace are also O(1) at d_lat=5 (|m_c . m_c'| ~ 9/sqrt5), so the residual Gram is not a clean rank-k block. Numerically the block estimate a_*^2/n + (n/k - 1) g(q_d, varrho_c)/n with g = E[tanh tanh'] - a_1^2 q varrho gives 1.0e-3 (d=5) vs 1.0e-5 (d=200), a ~100x ratio, which is the right order for 221x; but the phrase 'scale like varrho_c^3 propto d_lat^{-3}' is wrong since q_d is not constant (varrho_c changes 4.8x, not 40x) and the c_j(q)^2 factors change ~16x as well.  
  FIX: Compute the median sample-bulk eigenvalue directly from the repo's spectra at d_lat=5 and 200 rather than inferring it from F5's ratios; state the sign of the predicted vs observed change. Put e^{-2t} into varrho_c, replace 'propto d_lat^{-3}' by the explicit block estimate with numbers, and note that cross-cluster overlaps are also O(1) at small d_lat.
- [minor] rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred, last sentence: 'which no clock reading only spec(M_t) can reproduce': Contradicts the preceding sentence: the collapsed coordinates DO change spec(M_t) (they add eigenvalues at Delta_t and enter the mean over i), so a clock reading only spec(M_t) does reproduce a trend -- the remark's own point is that it does so through an artefact. 'Effective rank saturates' and 'spec(M_t) unchanged' are not the same thing.  
  FIX: Replace with: 'which a clock reading spec(M_t) reproduces only through the growing number of collapsed coordinates, i.e. through an artefact of posterior collapse rather than through data-carrying modes'.
- [minor] lem:ge-coeff-pred (iv) and appendix numbers: 120-node Gauss-Hermite gives a_*^2 = 0.0047, 0.0274, 0.0359, 0.0544, 0.0784 at q = 0.33, 1, 1.247, 1.834, 2.76 and a_1^2 = 0.623, 0.367, 0.320, 0.246, 0.180. The piece's 0.0270, 0.0354, 0.0538 are 1-2% low; the integrand is smooth so 80 nodes should agree to many digits -- the discrepancy suggests a different q or a coarser rule. The 2/3 q^3 coefficient checks (expansion: E sigma^2 = q - 2q^2 + (17/3)q^3, a_1^2 q = q - 2q^2 + 5q^3; numerically a_*^2/q^3 = 0.62 at q=0.01, tending to 0.667). Monotonicity on [0.05, 3.5] confirmed on a 70-point grid.  
  FIX: Recompute with the stated rule and quote consistent numbers (the ratios 1.51x, 1.70x, 16.5x are unaffected to 2 digits).
- [minor] prop:mode-clocks-pred, 'T = eta s' and prop:data-side-rates-pred, 'kappa_d = eta a_1(q_d)^2/d_lat': The repo's phi is tanh(x_t W^T) with no p^{-1/2} (code normalisation = p x theory, F3), so in code units the gradient-flow time is T = eta_code p s and the data-side rate is eta_code p a_1^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d_lat = 64 eta_code a_1(q_d)^2 lambda_i for p = 64 d_lat; the 1/d_lat cancels. Prediction 5 ('kappa refit per d scales as a_1^2/d_lat') is therefore normalisation-dependent and, for the repo's own sweep, should read 'scales as a_1(q_d)^2 with p=64 d_lat'.  
  FIX: State the conversion between theory and code units once and express kappa_d in both; adjust Prediction 5 accordingly.
- [minor] def:two-source-predictor-pred and rem:param-count-pred: 'theta is the absorbed-amplitude threshold at which the sample-specific score component is strong enough': The definition conflates 'fraction of the optimum learned' with 'strength of the learned component'; a referee will ask why a fraction rather than an absolute displacement is the right threshold, since a_mu^* = V u_mu / Lambda_mu grows as Lambda_mu shrinks. The piece has the ingredients to answer this but does not: with u_mu ~ rhobar_mu/||rhobar_mu||, V u_mu ~ (1/n) y_mu ||rhobar_mu|| and Lambda_mu = ||rhobar_mu||^2/n, so a_mu^* = y_mu/||rhobar_mu|| and the model's OUTPUT along the sample mode at its own training point is A(T) phi_mu . (component) = y_mu (1 - e^{-Lambda_mu T}). Hence absorbed fraction = fraction of the sample-specific score output learned, an absolute statement in output space; the fraction clock is well-founded, not a convention.  
  FIX: Add this two-line derivation to prop:mode-clocks-pred (or the definition) so theta has a concrete meaning, and move open gap (3) to 'what remains: relating theta to the softmax condensation basin'.
- [minor] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred (iii), Weyl bound '||E||_op <= max_mu sum_nu |E_{mu nu}| = O(n d^{-3/2})' and hypothesis 'n << d^{3/2} a_*^2': The row-sum bound is very loose: for entries of random sign of size d^{-3/2}, ||E||_op = O(sqrt(n) d^{-3/2}) (semicircle), giving the condition n << d_lat^3 a_*^4. On the synthetic data at d_lat=200 (a_*^2 = 0.0047) this is d^3 a_*^4 = 177 < n = 500, so even the sharper condition fails there; on real latents (d=200, a_*^2 ~ 0.036) it holds by an order of magnitude. Also, 'The d_lat data-side directions remove d_lat of these n residual directions' is not an argument; the correct statement is rank(Phi) <= n for the single-draw U so U_lin + U_* has at most n nonzero eigenvalues, the top d_lat of which are data-side because a_1^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d_lat >> a_*^2/n (6e-4 vs 9e-6 at d=200; 1.4e-2 vs 1.6e-4 at d=5).  
  FIX: Use the semicircle scaling for ||E||_op, restate the hypothesis as n << d_lat^3 a_*^4 (and give the numbers for both datasets), and replace the 'remove d_lat directions' sentence by the rank/separation argument.
- [minor] cor:shape-from-qmu-pred (ii): 's_f = C n / a_*(q_{(f)})^2 with q_{(f)} the upper f-quantile of {q_mu}': F-hat is normalised by n but only sums over mu in D_{t_c}, so the level f is reached when the upper f-quantile of {q_mu : mu in D_{t_c}} (with f <= |D|/n) crosses the threshold; the corollary silently assumes D = [n] or independence. Also D_{t_c} uses latent-space d_NN whereas the memorisation statistic is computed in pixel space after decoding (F10); the two neighbour structures need not coincide.  
  FIX: Define q_{(f)} as the quantile within D_{t_c} and restrict f <= |D_{t_c}|/n; add the latent-vs-pixel neighbour mismatch to the hypotheses of the definition.
- [minor] main_text and appendix validation numbers: 'one-constant clock ... 0.347 in-sample / 0.449 LOO on CelebA'; 'post-threshold dims': The set of 'post-threshold dims' is never stated. Reproducing from the released tau tables: on all uncensored dims (d>=20) CelebA 5% gives 0.385/0.415 and the tau ∝ d baseline 0.488; on d>=50 it gives 0.361/0.397 and the baseline 0.126; CIFAR 5% on d>=100 gives 0.123/0.138 (baseline 0.146), on d>=80 0.142/0.158. So the piece's numbers are reproducible only for a specific subset, and the claim that a d-linear baseline 'fits CelebA better' is true only on that subset (it is the worst model on d>=20). Also 'comparable to the 7-parameter in-sample fit' is generous for CelebA 25% (0.25-0.31 vs 0.17).  
  FIX: List the dims used in each fit, report both the d>=20 and d>=50 numbers, use the mean of log-residuals for C consistently (or say median), and temper 'comparable'.
- [minor] prop:data-side-rates-pred statement: 'more generally eta a_1(gamma q_d)^2 gamma times the first-layer initialisation variance': Dimensionally muddled: the argument of a_1 should be the pre-activation variance gamma q_d and the rate factor is gamma a_1(gamma q_d)^2 / d_lat (fan-in) or gamma a_1(gamma q_d)^2 sigma_w^2 (unnormalised) -- 'gamma times the initialisation variance' double counts if gamma already is that variance.  
  FIX: Define gamma once as the pre-activation gain (gamma = d_lat sigma_w^2 for W_ij ~ N(0, sigma_w^2)) and write the rate as eta gamma a_1(gamma q_d)^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d_lat.

(1) d_NN quantiles q10/q50/q90, sn=0.5: 0.74/1.05/1.69 (d=5), 2.13/2.57/3.04 (20), 3.46/3.84/4.27 (40), 8.77/9.22/9.60 (200); sn=0.01: 0.74/1.05/1.69 flat; q90/q10 2.30 -> 1.10 (0.5), 2.3 flat (0.01) — agrees with anchor (2). (5) Crossing times seed 42 from metrics.jsonl: T(1%)/T(5%)/T(10%) = 150k/350k/550k (d=5), 250k/550k/1.0M (8), 300k/700k/1.7M (10), 400k/1.0M/2.65M (12), 600k/2.1M/- (15), 1.05M (20), 2.55M (25); ratios 2.33/2.2/2.33/2.5/3.5 — exactly as anchored; final fractions 0.2896/0.2236/0.1684/0.1274/0.079/0.0438/0.017/0.006/0.0038/0.0018 confirmed; config confirms t_min=0.01, t_max=3, 500 E-M steps, hidden 256, 5000 gen samples, no terminal denoising. (6) E-M terminal variance for the condensed linear score from the recursion v_{k+1}=a_k^2 v_k+2dt: 0.0269 (ratio 1.358 to Delta_{t_min}; last-step a=0.626, t_last=0.016); closed-form s_emp sampler in the code's loop (2000 samples): per-coordinate displacement variance 0.027 at all d and both sigma_perp; reported fractions sn=0.5: 0.540 (d=5), 0.664 (10), 0.802 (20), 0.965 (40), mean ratio 0.347 -> 0.264; sn=0.01: 0.531, 0.235, 0.041, 0.001. (7) Metric definition: experiment_v2.py evaluate() uses topk(cdist(generated,train),2) -> nn1/nn2 both from the generated point; git -S history shows the earlier version used nn2_dists = train_dists[nn1_idx].min (piece's definition); old sn=0.01 raw_data mean_nn_ratio 1.06-2.59 (>1) confirms those runs used the old definition; config there: hidden=160 at d=20 (8 d_lat), 300k steps. (8) No score-model checkpoints exist: find *.pt returns only VAE checkpoints and encoded datasets.
