- [major] def:bridge-objects (rho definition), lem:endpoint eq:ceiling, prop:bridge eq:mem-criterion/eq:Fmem, all '9' constants, main text para 2: The piece defines the NN statistic as ||x_hat-NN1|| / ||NN1-NN2|| and attributes it to Somepalli. The released code that produced the anchored 5M sweep (experiment_v2.py lines 363-371, comment 'd(gen, NN1_train(gen)) / d(gen, NN2_train(gen))') computes nn1/nn2 with BOTH distances taken from the generated point (topk of cdist(generated, train)). The git history shows the old sigma_perp=0.01 sweep (raw_data mean_nn_ratio = 1.06..2.59, impossible for a gen-based ratio) used the piece's definition (nn2_dists = train_dists[nn1_idx].min). So the two empirical anchors use two different statistics and the piece's criterion constant is wrong for the run it anchors on: with the code's ratio and delta nearly orthogonal to x^mu-x^nu (high d), rho<1/3 <=> 9||delta||^2 < d_NN^2 + ||delta||^2, i.e. 8 d_lat Delta_eff < d_NN^2 (not 9), and at low d the constant lies between 4 and 16. MC ceilings differ (d=5, sn=0.5: 0.686 code vs 0.675 piece-def; d=20, sn=0.01: 0.099 vs 0.078).  
  FIX: Define rho(x_hat) = ||x_hat-NN1(x_hat)|| / ||x_hat-NN2(x_hat)|| (as in the code; attribute to the code and cite Somepalli/Bonnaire for the 1/3 threshold only). Replace eq:mem-criterion by 8 d_lat Delta_eff,mu < d_NN(mu)^2 (state the orthogonality approximation and the [1/16,1/4] range at small d), replace 3 sqrt(.) by sqrt(8 .) in eq:ceiling, eq:Fmem, eq:taumem-q, eq:taumem-spread, and the per-coordinate condition becomes Delta_eff < sigma_perp^2/4. State explicitly that the old sigma_perp=0.01 300k-step sweep used the ||NN1-NN2|| denominator and is not directly comparable.
- [major] lem:endpoint (proof, last sentence 'Euler-Maruyama ... only lowers the ceiling'), eq:ceiling numbers, appendix preamble 'Delta_{t_min}=0.0198', empirical anchor (1), predictions 2 and 5, main text 'equal to 0.68 at d=5 ... 0.03 by d=20': For the released sampler (500 E-M steps on [0.01,3], dt=0.00598) the terminal per-coordinate variance of the condensed linear score is NOT Delta_{t_min}=0.0198 and E-M does not lower it: the offset recursion v_{k+1}=a_k^2 v_k + 2dt with a_k = 1-(1+e^{-2t_k})dt/Delta_{t_k} gives v_EM = 0.0269 (last-step contraction a=0.626, i.e. dt/Delta_t=0.19, near the stability edge), 36% ABOVE Delta_{t_min}. I ran the closed-form s_emp sampler in the code's own loop: measured per-coordinate displacement variance 0.027 at every d and both sigma_perp; reported fractions 0.540/0.664/0.802/0.965 (sn=0.5, d=5/10/20/40) versus the piece's ceilings 0.676/0.870/0.979/~1, and 0.531/0.235/0.041/0.001 (sn=0.01) versus 0.668/0.250/0.034/0.002. Prediction 5's 'fraction = ceiling ~1 for d>=25' and prediction 2's 'must vanish for d>=20 regardless of training time' are therefore overstated; at sn=0.5, d=5 the exact empirical sampler's mean ratio is 0.347, i.e. the metric sits at the knife edge.  
  FIX: Replace Delta_{t_min} by v_EM = 0.0269 (give the recursion) everywhere the sampler's terminal variance enters; reword to 'the coarse E-M discretization raises the terminal variance by 36%'; recompute anchor (1) with the s_emp sampler run (numbers above) and quote those as the ceiling; rewrite prediction 2 as 'the exact-empirical-score ceiling is <=0.05 for d_lat>=20 at sigma_perp=0.01'; add the hard floor for an arbitrary learned score, 2dt=0.012 per coordinate from the last noise injection (ceiling with that floor: 0.25 at d=20, sn=0.01), and state that the Delta/v_EM ceiling applies to a score that is at best s_emp-accurate, not 'regardless of training time'.
- [major] main_text_latex \begin{proposition}...\label{prop:bridge} and appendix_latex same label: \label{prop:bridge} is issued twice (main text and appendix), giving a multiply-defined label and two differently numbered Propositions; the appendix also re-issues def:bridge-objects/lem:* only once (fine) but the proposition collides.  
  FIX: Keep the numbered statement and label in the main text; in the appendix use \begin{proposition}[Restatement of Proposition~\ref{prop:bridge}] with no \label (or the thmtools restatable mechanism).
- [major] rem:pixel and main text consequence (c): 'the pixel-space fraction is a lower bound on the latent-space fraction whenever L/ell>1' does not follow from bi-Lipschitz bounds: rho_pix <= (L/ell) rho_lat and rho_lat <= (L/ell) rho_pix give F_pix(1/3) >= F_lat(ell/(3L)) and F_pix(1/3) <= F_lat(L/(3ell)) — neither fraction bounds the other at the same threshold. Also the pixel-space NN of D(z_hat) need not be D(NN1_lat).  
  FIX: Replace with the two-sided statement F_lat(ell/(3L)) <= F_pix(1/3) <= F_lat(L/(3ell)) under the added hypothesis that pixel-space and latent-space nearest neighbours coincide (or bound the NN mismatch by the same L/ell), and delete 'lower bound'.
- [major] cor:dnn-scaling eq:dnn-scaling and proof: d_NN(mu)^2 = min_nu (S_nu + N_nu) is written as (signal min) + (null min); that is only a lower bound. Numerically the 'null part' alone, 2 sigma_perp^2 (d_lat-d_int) = 17.5 at d=40 (97.5 at d=200), already EXCEEDS the measured median d_NN^2 = 14.7 (85.0), so the stated (1+O(.)) correction has the wrong sign (proof says 1-O(.)) and is O(1) in the sweep (sqrt(log n/(d-d_int)) = 0.42 at d=40). Also labels are drawn uniformly at random (not n/k per cluster) and with s=3, sigma_sig=1 clusters overlap, so the n/k-in-d_int Poisson argument for C_{d_int} is heuristic.  
  FIX: State eq:dnn-scaling as an approximation with an explicit negative extreme-value correction, d_NN^2 ~ 2 sigma_perp^2 (d-d_int) - c sigma_perp^2 sqrt((d-d_int) log n) + O(sigma_sig^2) in regime (ii), and d_NN ~ n^{-1/d_int} in regime (i) supported by anchor (3); drop the equality and the 'n/k points each' clause; mark the corollary 'sketched/empirical'.
- [major] predictions[0] how_to_test ('at every eval checkpoint compute E_{t_min}(T)'): Not testable with the repo's data: multiseed_runs/*/di5_d*_n500_s*/ contain only config.json and metrics.jsonl; no score-model checkpoints exist anywhere (the only .pt files are VAE checkpoints and encoded datasets). The zero-parameter test requires re-running training.

- [major] prop:bridge last sentence and cor:taumem-fraction ('for every d_lat>=2 ... t<=0.1'): The hypothesis t<=0.1 contradicts the piece's own attachment times t_c = 0.21 (d=20), 0.39 (40), 1.02 (200) at sigma_perp=0.5. The implication metric=>condensation requires d_lat > (2/9) e^{2t} log n, which at t_c(200)=1.02 is d_lat > 10.6 — still satisfied, but the stated condition is wrong as written.  
  FIX: State the condition as d_lat > (2/9) e^{2 t_c} log n and verify it along the sweep (worst case d_lat=200: 10.6 < 200; d_lat=40, t_c=0.39: 3.0 < 40).
- [minor] cor:dnn-scaling (i), main text and appendix ('acquires the additive term -1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem)'): Sign error: threshold ∝ n^{-2/d_int} and E(T)=E_0 e^{-2 lambda T} give tau = log(E_0/thr)/(2 lambda), so d tau/d log n = +1/(d_int lambda_samp) and d log tau/d log n = +1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem): denser data (larger n) memorizes LATER, not earlier.  
  FIX: Flip the sign to +1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem) in both main text and appendix.
- [minor] lem:condensation proof (union bound): Two-sided Gaussian tail P(|<eta,u>| > D sqrt(2 log(n/delta))) <= 2 delta/n, so the event has probability >= 1-2delta, not 1-delta. Also 'O(t_min ||x^mu||)' in ||x_{t_min}-x^mu||^2 should be the cross term O(t_min ||x^mu|| sqrt(d Delta)).  
  FIX: Use sqrt(2 log(2n/delta)) in eq:cond-rigorous (constant 32 unchanged) or state 1-2delta; fix the shrinkage term.
- [minor] empirical_anchor (1) sigma_perp=0.01 ceilings and predictions[1] already_supported_by: Tail ceilings are seed-sensitive (seed-42 formula gives 0.060/0.026/0.010 at d=20/25/30 vs the quoted 0.034/0.015/0.008), and the quoted old sigma_perp=0.01 runs (300k steps, hidden 8 d_lat, confirmed in raw_data configs) used the other NN-ratio definition, so 'consistent with the ceiling' is not a like-for-like comparison. The parenthetical about the d=150/200 reappearance belongs to the sigma_perp=0.5 300k run (raw fractions 0.0022, 0.0144), not the sigma_perp=0.01 list.  
  FIX: Quote ceilings with replicate spread; state the metric mismatch for the old runs; move the reappearance remark to the sigma_perp=0.5 run.
- [minor] citations_needed: scarvelis2023closedform, gu2023memorization, kadkhodaie2023learning already exist in references.bib (TMLR 2025 versions of the first two); adding them again duplicates keys. efron2011tweedie is genuinely missing. main.tex inputs a pre-generated main.bbl, so new entries need the bbl regenerated.  
  FIX: Drop the three existing keys from citations_needed; keep efron2011tweedie (and george2025dsm if used elsewhere); regenerate main.bbl.
- [minor] lem:endpoint status 'proved': The transport claim is proved for the continuous-time sampler, but the ceiling's numerical content depends on the (false) E-M remark and on the '1/n on average' attachment frequency, which is only exact for exact initialization at t_max (the code initializes at N(0,I), t_max=3; negligible but unstated).

### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Re-anchor the bridge to what the released code actually does: (i) use the code's NN-ratio d(gen,NN1)/d(gen,NN2), which turns the criterion into d_lat*Delta_eff < d_NN^2/8 (not /9) at leading order; (ii) replace Delta_{t_min}=0.0198 by the EM sampler's terminal variance Delta_EM = 1.36*Delta_{t_min} = 0.0269 (exact recursion + simulation; the exact-s_emp EM sampler gives 0.51/0.67/0.81/0.87/0.96 at d=5/10/20/25/40, sigma_perp=0.5, not 0.68/0.87/0.98/1/1); (iii) commit to t = t_min in Proposition bridge and state that the SDE-integrated constant is 1.2-3.6x the Tweedie one -- the current 't in [t_min,t_c]' leaves c_t undetermined by a factor up to 340 and makes the 'zero-parameter' prediction meaningless.

Independent check: Code inspection (experiment_v2.py): metric is nn1/nn2 with both distances from the generated point (lines 362-371); sampler is 500 EM steps t_max=3 -> t_min=0.01, noise added on the final step, no denoising (lines 284-296). Lemma condensation algebra re-derived (log-ratio, union bound, constants 4 sqrt(2 log(n/delta)), -8 log(n/delta), delta^8 n^{-7}): correct except one-sided tail (2 delta). Tweedie/c_t algebra and offset-ODE contraction sqrt(Delta_{t_min}/Delta_{t_c}) re-derived: correct. Simulations on a replica of generate_data (n=500,k=10,d_int=5,s=3,sigma_sig=1,seed 42): d_NN quantiles q10/q50/q90 = 0.74/1.05/1.69 (d=5), 2.13/2.57/3.04 (20), 3.46/3.84/4.27 (40) at sigma_perp=0.5, flat 0.74/1.05/1.69 at 0.01 (agree with anchors); median d_NN*n^{1/5} = 3.90/3.66/3.81/3.70 for n=100/500/1000/5000 (flat, agrees). Ceilings, exact endpoint law + code metric + Delta_{t_min}: 0.680/0.761/0.820/0.866/0.900/0.945/0.975/0.998 (sigma_perp=0.5, d=5..40) and 0.681/0.478/0.349/0.259/0.183/0.095/0.050/0.008 (sigma_perp=0.01) versus the piece's concentrated-form 0.676/.../1 and 0.668/0.364/0.250/0.165/0.100/0.034/0.015/0.002. EM terminal variance on the condensed linear SDE: 0.02684 (simulation, 4e5 paths) and 0.02690 (exact recursion) = 1.36 Delta_{t_min}; 1.17x at 1000 steps, 1.03x at 5000. Exact-s_emp EM sampler fractions (code metric): 0.508/0.597/0.667/0.715/0.757/0.808/0.873/0.960 (sigma_perp=0.5), 0.508/0.321/0.231/0.163/0.091/0.050/0.018/0.002 (sigma_perp=0.01); these match the endpoint-law ceiling recomputed with Delta_EM (0.561/0.669/0.818/0.872/0.964 at d=5/10/20/25/40). d_NN^2 decomposition: measured medians 6.60/14.72/40.0/85.0 vs formula 8.61/18.61/48.61/98.61 at d=20/40/100/200. Softmax off-attachment mass at the quoted t_c (median over typical eta): 0.023 (d=5), 0.11 (d=20), 0.83 (d=200); typical-eta t_c (median 1-w_mu<0.05): 0.053/0.19/0.315/0.757 (d=5/20/40/200) and 0.053 flat at sigma_perp=0.01; rigorous-lemma LHS at t_min, d=5: 7.4 vs 16.5 required. Error-accumulation constants (units Delta_{t_min} r^2): Tweedie@t_min 1.02; Tweedie@t_c 4.9/26.4/338; ODE-integrated 0.61/2.64/8.42; SDE-integrated 1.18/2.58/3.57 for t_c=0.046/0.21/1.02. Sign of the n-derivative term in cor:dnn-scaling(i) re-derived: +1/(d_int lambda tau). Bi-Lipschitz sandwich (l/L) varrho_lat <= varrho_pix <= (L/l) varrho_lat re-derived: no fraction ordering follows.

- [major] def:bridge-objects (NN-ratio), lem:endpoint eq:ceiling, prop:bridge eq:mem-criterion, all anchor numbers: The piece defines varrho = ||xhat-NN1|| / ||NN1-NN2||, but the released code (experiment_v2.py lines 362-371) computes d(gen,NN1)/d(gen,NN2) (brief item F10 is itself wrong about this). With xhat = x^mu + delta and delta nearly orthogonal to x^mu - x^nu (high d), d(gen,NN2)^2 = d_NN^2 + ||delta||^2, so varrho<1/3 <=> ||delta||^2 < d_NN^2/8, not d_NN^2/9; at low d the exact criterion is only sandwiched: ||delta|| < d_NN/4 => memorized => ||delta|| < d_NN/2. The 'iff' in lem:endpoint and eq:mem-criterion is therefore not exact for either metric, and every displayed '9 d_lat Delta' should be '8 d_lat Delta' (leading order).  
  FIX: Define varrho as in the code. Replace d_NN^2/9 by d_NN^2/8 in eq:ceiling, eq:mem-criterion, eq:Fmem, eq:taumem-q, cor:dnn-scaling(ii) (2 sigma_perp^2/9 -> sigma_perp^2/4), and state the two-sided sandwich instead of 'iff'. Recompute all anchors with the code metric (my Monte Carlo of the exact endpoint law with the code metric: 0.680/0.761/0.820/0.866/0.900/0.945/0.975/0.998 for d=5..40 at sigma_perp=0.5 with Delta_{t_min}; see next item for the sampler-corrected values).
- [major] lem:endpoint proof last sentence ('Euler--Maruyama adds O(dt) variance, which only lowers the ceiling'), appendix preamble Delta_{t_min}=0.0198, anchors (1), predictions 2 and 5: The released sampler (500 EM steps, dt=0.00598, terminal drift coefficient 1-2/Delta ~ -100) does not reach the continuous endpoint variance. Exact variance recursion and a 4e5-sample simulation of the condensed linear reverse SDE both give terminal variance 0.0269 = 1.36 Delta_{t_min} (1.17x at 1000 steps, 1.03x at 5000). Running the exact s_emp sampler with the code's EM loop and metric gives fractions 0.508/0.597/0.667/0.715/0.757/0.808/0.873/0.960 for d=5/8/10/12/15/20/25/40 (sigma_perp=0.5), versus the quoted ceilings 0.676/0.790/0.870/0.924/0.950/0.979/0.998/~1. Prediction 5's expected outcome ('fraction = metric ceiling ~1 for d>=25') is wrong by 0.13 at d=25; the 'within a factor 2.3 of the ceiling' comparison at d=5 becomes 1.75.  
  FIX: Replace Delta_{t_min} by the sampler's terminal variance Delta_EM = 1.36 Delta_{t_min} = 0.0269 throughout (state it as a computed property of the released schedule, give the recursion V_{k+1}=(1+a_k dt)^2 V_k + 2dt), and recompute ceilings: with Delta_EM and the code metric I get 0.561/0.669/0.818/0.872/0.964 (d=5/10/20/25/40, sigma_perp=0.5) and 0.558/0.331/0.233/0.043/0.002 (d=5/8/10/20/40, sigma_perp=0.01), matching the EM-sampled fractions to within 0.05. Reword the EM sentence: it is a 36% effect, not a footnote.
- [major] eq:ceiling and eq:Fmem ('d_lat >> 1', chi^2 concentration), anchors (1), prediction 2: At d_lat = 5..40 the chi^2_d fluctuation of ||delta||^2 (relative std sqrt(2/d) = 0.63 at d=5) is not negligible, yet ceilings are quoted to three digits from the concentrated form 1 - G_NN(3 sqrt(d Delta)). Exact endpoint-law Monte Carlo (code metric, Delta_{t_min}) gives 0.478 vs quoted 0.364 (d=8, sigma_perp=0.01), 0.349 vs 0.250 (d=10), 0.095 vs 0.034 (d=20), 0.050 vs 0.015 (d=25), 0.008 vs 0.002 (d=40). Prediction 2 ('fraction must vanish for d_lat >= 20 regardless of training time') is false as stated: the ceiling at d=20 is 0.04-0.10 depending on the terminal variance. The chi^2 spread is also a third source of fraction-curve width that cor:taumem-fraction omits.  
  FIX: Write the ceiling and eq:Fmem as an average over mu of P(chi^2_d < d_NN(mu)^2 / (8 Delta_eff,mu)) (or its Monte Carlo), keep the concentrated form only as the d_lat->infinity limit, quote the exact numbers, and restate prediction 2 as 'ceiling <= 0.1 for d>=20 and <= 0.01 for d>=40 at sigma_perp=0.01'.
- [major] prop:bridge statement 'Fix a diffusion time t in [t_min,t_c]' and c_t = e^{2t} Delta_t^2 / d_lat; open gap G2; prediction 1 ('zero-parameter'): c_t is not determined by the proposition: e^{2t}Delta_t spans 0.020 (t=t_min) to 6.7 (t=t_c=1.02, d=200), so the predicted threshold on E moves by a factor 340 with the unstated choice of t. Numerical integration of the offset equation with a persistent relative error r^2 = E_s Delta_s/d gives per-coordinate displacement variance (units Delta_{t_min} r^2): Tweedie@t_min 1.02, Tweedie@t_c 4.9/26/338 (t_c = 0.046/0.21/1.02), ODE-integrated 0.61/2.64/8.42, SDE-integrated (the sampler actually used, propagator sinh(t_min)/sinh(s), factor 2 on the score) 1.18/2.58/3.57. So even at t=t_min the accumulated constant is 1.2-3.6x the Tweedie one and, through t_c, d_lat-dependent. G2 analyses the ODE, but the code runs the SDE, and 'accurate to a logarithmic factor' understates the spread.  
  FIX: Commit to t = t_min in the proposition (c = e^{2t_min} Delta_{t_min}^2 / d_lat), state explicitly that the SDE-integrated version multiplies c by a factor between 1.2 and 3.6 across the sweep (give the propagator sinh(t_min)/sinh(s) formula), and downgrade prediction 1 from 'zero-parameter' to 'one O(1) constant in [1,4] fixed a priori by the persistence assumption'. Delete the latitude t in [t_min,t_c]; if attachment near t_c must be discussed, do it with the integrated constant, never with Tweedie at t_c.
- [major] rem:pixel claim (a) and main-text consequence (c) 'the pixel-space fraction lower-bounds the latent one': Bi-Lipschitz D gives the two-sided sandwich (l/L) varrho_lat <= varrho_pix <= (L/l) varrho_lat (apply the same argument to D^{-1}); it imposes no ordering between varrho_pix and varrho_lat and hence none between the fractions at the common threshold 1/3. From varrho_pix <= (L/l) varrho_lat one gets {varrho_lat < l/(3L)} subset {varrho_pix < 1/3}, i.e. F_pix(1/3) >= F_lat(l/(3L)) -- an upper bound on the latent fraction at a stricter threshold, the opposite of what is claimed. In addition, the pixel-space NN1/NN2 need not be the decoded latent NN1/NN2, so the denominator bound needs a global min over training pairs.  
  FIX: Replace (a) by: F_lat(l/(3L)) <= F_pix(1/3) <= F_lat(L/(3l)), with the caveat on NN identity; delete 'lower bound' from the main text; keep (b).
- [major] cor:dnn-scaling (i), main-text statement and appendix statement of the n-derivative term: Sign error. The threshold on E in eq:taumem-q is proportional to (d_NN^{(1-q)})^2 ~ n^{-2/d_int}; with E(T) = E_0 e^{-2 lambda T}, T(q) = (1/2lambda) log(E_0 / thr), so a larger n lowers thr and delays tau_mem: d log tau / d log n = + 1/(d_int lambda tau_mem), not '-(2/d_int) 1/(2 lambda tau)' (main statement) or '-1/(d_int lambda tau)' (appendix). Prediction 3's phrasing 'shift log tau_mem(q) by (2/d_int)(log n)/(2 lambda)' also mixes tau and log tau.  
  FIX: Write the term as +1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem) for d log tau/d log n, equivalently d tau/d log n = +1/(d_int lambda_samp), and fix prediction 3 accordingly.
- [major] cor:dnn-scaling eq:dnn-scaling and its proof ('the two parts add because signal and null coordinates are independent'); regime (ii) 'per-coordinate condition Delta_eff < 2 sigma_perp^2/9': d_NN^2 = min_nu (S_nu + N_nu) is not (min S) + (min N); the decomposition is only a lower bound and the extreme-value correction has a definite negative sign and is large. On the repo's generator (seed 42, sigma_perp=0.5) median d_NN^2 = 6.60/14.72/40.0/85.0 at d=20/40/100/200 versus the formula's 8.61/18.61/48.61/98.61 (signal-only NN^2 = 1.11 plus 2 sigma_perp^2 (d-5)): 15-30% too high. The appendix writes (1+O(...)) in the statement and (1-O(...)) in the proof. Consequently the regime-(ii) 'per-coordinate' threshold is off by 15-30% across the sweep, and the t_c values computed from it inherit the error.  
  FIX: State d_NN^2 = 2 sigma_perp^2 (d-d_int) - 2 sigma_perp^2 sqrt(2(d-d_int)) c_n + S_{nu*} with c_n ~ sqrt(2 log n) (explicitly negative), or simply write 'approx, 15-30% below 2 sigma_perp^2 d_lat in the sweep' and use measured quantiles wherever numbers are displayed; replace 'the two parts add' by the inequality min S + min N <= d_NN^2 <= S_{nu*} + N_{nu*}.
- [major] lem:condensation (rigorous constant 32 log(n/delta) versus the eta=0 form), prop:bridge proof 'By (B1) and Lemma condensation ... up to the negligible r', anchor (4) and prediction 5 t_c values: (1) The rigorous condition is never met on the sampler interval where memorization is largest: at d=5, sigma_perp=0.5, t=t_min one has e^{-t} d_NN/sqrt(Delta) = 7.4 versus the required 4 sqrt(2 log(n/delta)) = 16.5 (delta=0.1); so the sketch's step '(B1) + Lemma => r negligible' is a non-sequitur, since (B1) is the 2 log n form. (2) The eta=0 evaluation is not a typical-eta condensation criterion: E_eta[w_nu/w_mu] = 1 identically (exponential tilt), and with n competitors at concentrated distances the typical max fluctuation 2 sqrt(M log n) must be beaten by the mean M = e^{-2t}d_NN^2/(2 Delta), i.e. M >~ 5.8 log n. Simulation at the quoted t_c: median off-attachment mass 1-w_mu = 0.023 (d=5), 0.11 (d=20), 0.83 (d=200) -- at d=200 the quoted t_c=1.02 lies in the non-condensed phase. Typical-eta condensation times (median 1-w_mu < 0.05): 0.053/0.19/0.315/0.757 for d=5/20/40/200 (0.053 flat at sigma_perp=0.01). (3) The union bound uses a one-sided tail; two-sided gives 2 delta.  
  FIX: State (B1) as the typical-eta condition (or a per-neighbour sum condition sum_nu exp(-e^{-2t}D_nu^2/(2Delta) + e^{-t}D_nu sqrt(2 log n)/sqrt(Delta)) < eps, which avoids the crude union over n), replace anchor (4) and prediction 5 with the typical-eta numbers (the qualitative claim -- t_c grows with d_lat at sigma_perp=0.5 -- survives), use sqrt(2 log(2n/delta)) in the proof, and note that mean-based statements about w are vacuous because E[w_nu/w_mu]=1.
- [minor] lem:endpoint hypotheses and proof: The condensation hypothesis is superfluous for the first claim and for the ceiling: the reverse SDE (and the probability-flow ODE) with the exact score of rho^emp transports rho^emp_{t_c} to rho^emp_{t_min} = (1/n) sum_mu N(e^{-t_min} x^mu, Delta_{t_min} I) with no condensation at all (only the N(0,I) initialization at t_max=3 is approximate). The mixture endpoint law, the uniform 1/n attachment frequency and the ceiling are therefore unconditional. Also 'O(t_min ||x^mu||)' should be O(t_min^2 ||x^mu||^2) in the squared norm (plus a cross term of std 2 t_min sqrt(Delta) ||x^mu||).  
  FIX: Restate the lemma as unconditional (drop condensation), which also removes the G8 dependence for the ceiling; keep the ODE contraction y_{t_min} = sqrt(Delta_{t_min}/Delta_{t_c}) y_{t_c} as a remark. Fix the O() term.
- [minor] prop:bridge (B2) and open gap G1: (B2) as written is self-contradictory: zeta_T(x_t,t) is a deterministic function of x_t, so it cannot be independent of eta; and Gaussianity/isotropy are not needed for eq:mem-criterion. The Tweedie identity delta = e^t Delta_t zeta is exact, so ||delta||^2 = e^{2t} Delta_t^2 ||zeta_T(x_t)||^2 identically; all that is used is concentration of ||zeta_T(x_t)||^2 at E_{t,mu}(T) over eta and negligible cross term with the terminal noise. In the blend model of G1 this identity also holds (I checked the null block: c_t E_{t,mu} = (1-m)^2 Delta_t sigma_perp^2/beta_t^2 per coordinate, the same as ||delta||^2/d computed directly), so 'blend vs isotropic' is not where the models differ -- they differ only through the choice of t and the multi-step accumulation (G2/G3).  
  FIX: Weaken (B2) to the concentration statement, and rewrite G1 to say the two models agree at fixed t and differ only in c (G2/G3).
- [minor] cor:taumem-fraction eq:taumem-spread, text 'width of the fraction curve in log T', prediction 4, anchor (6): eq:taumem-spread is a difference in linear T, not in log T; the anchor compares ratios T(5%)/T(1%) = 2.33 with a difference formula. Under the single-exponential + data-spread model, T(5%)/T(1%) = 1 + log(d^{(.99)}/d^{(.95)})/(lambda T(1%)) ~ 1 + 0.15/(lambda T(1%)), so a ratio 2.33 would need lambda T(1%) ~ 0.1, i.e. the residual already within one tenth of an e-fold of threshold at the first crossing -- impossible; the observed ratios therefore already refute the data-level-only width and the text should say so rather than 'both spreads are needed'. The chi^2_d spread (item 3) is a further width source, dominant at d=5.  
  FIX: State the width in T, add the chi^2 term, and reword prediction 4/anchor (6) as a comparison of differences normalized by 1/lambda_samp.
- [minor] prop:bridge 'companion condition' and cor:taumem-fraction tau_mem^cond; '(n=500, t <= 0.1)': 'Condensation at the effective level' is conceptually muddled: Delta_eff is a terminal displacement variance, not a diffusion time at which softmax weights are evaluated; (B1) already assumes condensation at t. The comparison is between two candidate definitions of tau_mem, not a hypothesis. Also 't <= 0.1' contradicts 't in [t_min, t_c]' with t_c up to 1.02 (the inequality 9d > 2 e^{2t} log n needs d > 10.6 at t = 1.02).  
  FIX: Move to a remark: 'a Biroli-type threshold Delta_eff < e^{-2t} d_NN^2/(2 log n) is implied by the metric threshold for d_lat >= 2 at t = t_min', and delete the effective-level condensation language.
- [minor] def:bridge-objects and main text ('rank-null floor'); eta-union 'iff' with n vs n-1; song2021scoresde/levina/facco citations: E_t(infinity) is the L^2(rho_t^emp) projection error onto the feature span, which has nothing to do with the rank-null (lambda=0) modes; calling it the rank-null floor is misleading. In the eta=0 form, sum <= (n-1) e^{-...} < 1 iff ... uses log n for log(n-1) so 'iff' should be 'if'. Citation keys song2021scoresde, levina2004maximum, facco2017twonn are present in references.bib (fine); efron2011tweedie is correctly listed as needed; F10 in the brief should be corrected to the code's d(gen,NN1)/d(gen,NN2).  
  FIX: Rename to 'feature-span floor', change 'iff' to 'if', and flag the F10/code mismatch to the orchestrator.


### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Replace the per-sample attachment Lambda_mu = a_*(q_mu)^2/n (and everything built on it: cor:shape (i)-(ii), the 'shape from the q_mu distribution with no response function' claim, and the norm-ordering prediction) by the correct statement: the residual block is a Wishart matrix with xi-averaged variance profile K_*(q_mu, 1-Delta_t/q_mu) = sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q_mu)^2 (1-Delta_t/q_mu)^k/k! (not a_*(q_mu)^2; 0.54-0.68 of it on real data at t=0.1), whose spectrum is a generalised MP bulk of relative width 2sqrt(n/p); per-sample attachment requires n/p << 1 and p > n, which fails on both the synthetic sweep at d_lat <= 50 (n/p = 1.56 -> 0.16) and the real runs (hidden=1024, n=1000, n/p ~ 1). Also fix the recipe for the deployed GELU/PyTorch-init network (subtract a_0^2; gamma = 1/3; fan_in = d+64 makes q_real flat in d, so the literal recipe predicts no slowdown).

Independent check: (1) Gauss-Hermite (120 nodes, tanh): a_1^2 = 0.6234, 0.3669, 0.3197, 0.2455, 0.1801 and a_*^2 = 0.00474, 0.02742, 0.03593, 0.05439, 0.07840 at q = 0.33, 1, 1.247, 1.834, 2.76 -- all of the piece's tabulated values confirmed. a_*^2 is increasing on [0.3,3] (and up to q=6). Small-q: a_*^2/q^3 = 0.617 (q=0.01), 0.573 (0.02), consistent with 2/3 - O(q); by hand, E tanh^2 = q - 2q^2 + (17/3)q^3, a_1^2 q = q - 2q^2 + 5q^3, difference (2/3)q^3: confirmed. (2) Mehler kernel K_*(q,rho) = sum_{k>=3 odd} c_k^2 rho^k/k! checked at rho=1: 0.07831 vs a_*^2 = 0.07840. Same-cluster (q=2.76, rho=0.652): K_* = 0.0165 = 0.21 a_*^2; x n/k=50 -> 10.5x per-sample; at (0.33, 0.136): 0.002 a_*^2 -> 0.1x. Within-noise-cloud rho_w = 1-Delta_t/q: per-point mode/a_*^2 = 0.969 (synthetic d=5, t=0.01), 0.822 (d=200), 0.675/0.671/0.574 (CelebA d=50/100/200, t=0.1), 0.668/0.538 (CIFAR 100/260); corrected d-slopes 1.78x (CelebA 50->200), 2.1x (CIFAR 100->260). (3) MP: n=500, p=64d: n/p = 1.56 (d=5), 1.12 (7), 0.78 (10), 0.39 (20), 0.16 (50), 0.039 (200); 2sqrt(n/p) = 2.50, 2.11, 1.77, 1.25, 0.79, 0.40; edge (1+sqrt(n/p))^2 = 5.06 (d=5), 1.43 (d=200), ratio 3.5 (matches piece). Code-unit top sample eigenvalue p a_*^2 edge/n: 0.254 (d=5) -> 0.174 (d=200), ratio 1.46 (piece implies 58x). Code-unit median 64 d a_*^2/n: 0.050 -> 0.121 (rises 2.4x). (4) Repo facts: compute_U averages n_noise_samples=50 draws; RFNN t_fixed=0.01 (my inference from q_d=2.76/0.33 gives e^{-2t}=0.978, t≈0.011, consistent); real net = GELU MLP, PyTorch default init (var 1/(3 fan_in)), fan_in = d+64 (n_freq=32 sin+cos), hidden 1024, depth 5, SGD lr 1e-3 momentum 0.8, t~U[0.01,3]. GELU a_0 = 0.43 at q=1.83; proper a_*^2 (minus a_0^2) 0.191 vs the recipe's formula 0.380. q_real = (d q_d+33)/(3(d+64)) = 0.365/0.435/0.357 (CelebA 50/100/200), GELU a_*^2 = 0.0238/0.0310/0.0230 (non-monotone). (5) CSVs: CelebA lambda_min = 0.18145 from d=140, CIFAR 0.18327 from d=160 (piece's anchor says 160/200). One-constant clock fit on CelebA dims>=40 (median-C): 5% in-sample 0.332 / LOO 0.334 vs released 0.340; 25% 0.239/0.320 vs 0.156; tau∝d baseline 0.158 (5%), 0.138 (25%). (6) Prop mode-clocks algebra: grad L = A U - V, a_i' = -lambda_i(a_i - V u_i/lambda_i), L - L* = (1/2) tr[(A-A*)U(A-A*)^T] valid since V u_i = 0 whenever lambda_i = 0: correct.

- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred (iii), def:two-source-predictor-pred, cor:shape-from-qmu-pred (i)-(ii), main text 'up to a Marchenko--Pastur spread of relative width 2sqrt(n/p)': The 'one eigenvalue per training point, Lambda_mu = a_*(q_mu)^2/n' claim is a statement about a Wishart Gram with a variance profile, and it is only true when the MP spread 2sqrt(n/p) is small compared with the heterogeneity of a_*(q_mu)^2 across mu (cv(q_mu) 0.17-0.48, i.e. a_*^2 spread of order +-30-80%). In the repo's regimes it is not: synthetic p=64 d_lat, n=500 gives n/p = 1.56 (d=5, p<n: the residual Gram is rank-deficient, 'n-d sample modes' do not even exist), 0.78 (d=10), 0.16 (d=50), 0.039 (d=200); real data runs use hidden=1024 with n=1000 (run_celeba_diverse_bigmlp_sgd_lr001_m08_*.sh: --hidden 1024 --depth 5), so n/p = 0.98 and 2sqrt(n/p) = 2.0: the sample bulk spans ~[0, 4] x a_*^2/n with fully delocalised eigenvectors. Then the fraction-curve shape is an (generalised) MP density, NOT the empirical distribution of log a_*(q_mu)^2, and 'points are memorised in decreasing order of latent norm' has no support. The main-text sentence presents the spread as a small correction; it is O(1).  
  FIX: State n/p -> 0 (and p > n) as an explicit hypothesis of (iii). Replace the per-sample attachment by: 'the residual block is a Wishart matrix with variance profile {K_*(q_mu)}; its spectrum is the generalised MP law with that profile; eigenvalues attach to individual training points only when 2sqrt(n/p) << spread of log K_*(q_mu)'. Downgrade cor:shape (i)-(ii) and the 'no response function' claim to a conjecture valid for p >> n; keep (iii). In the predictor, replace the indicator 1[Lambda_mu s >= C] by the MP-smoothed version with sigma_varsigma = 2sqrt(n/p) computed from the actual width (=2.0 on real data), and say explicitly that at n/p ~ 1 the q_mu-ordering prediction is untestable/unsupported.
- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred (i),(iii); eq:Lambda-mu-pred; def; recipe step 2: With E_xi inside U (the piece's own definition, and the code's compute_U which averages 50 noise draws, code/v3/lib/eigenvalues.py), E_xi[rho rho^T] = m m^T + Cov_xi(rho) with m = E_xi rho(x_t^mu). By the piece's own Mehler formula (ii), ||m||^2 = sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q_mu)^2 rho_mu^k / k! with the within-cloud overlap rho_mu = 1 - Delta_t/q_mu, NOT a_*(q_mu)^2; the remainder a_*^2 - ||m||^2 is spread over a within-cloud sub-bulk of rank >> n (49 n with 50 MC draws), so 'rank(U) <= n' is not what the code computes either. Numerically (120-node Gauss-Hermite): the per-point mode is 0.969 a_*^2 (synthetic d=5, t=0.01), 0.822 (d=200), but only 0.675 (CelebA d=50, t=0.1), 0.671 (d=100), 0.574 (d=200), 0.668/0.538 (CIFAR d=100/260). So the real-data Lambda_mu is overstated by up to 46%, and the d-slope changes: CelebA 50->200 ratio becomes 0.0367/0.0206 = 1.78x (piece: 1.52x), CIFAR 100->260 becomes 2.1x (piece: 1.7x).  
  FIX: Replace Lambda_mu = a_*(q_mu)^2/n by Lambda_mu = K_*(q_mu, rho_mu)/n with K_*(q,rho) = sum_{k odd>=3} c_k(q)^2 rho^k/k!, rho_mu = e^{-2t}||x^mu||^2/(d_lat q_mu) = 1 - Delta_t/q_mu; state that the remaining mass a_*(q_mu)^2 - K_* forms a within-noise-cloud sub-bulk; state precisely what xi-average the U in F1/F5 uses. Update all anchor ratios (1.52->1.78, 1.7->2.1) and the trace check (data-side + per-point + within-cloud = E tanh^2).
- [major] def:two-source-predictor-pred, Practitioner recipe (steps 1-2, 'gamma=1 for LeCun/Kaiming'), prediction 3, empirical anchor (4)-(5), open gaps (5)-(6): The recipe as written, applied to the deployed real-data network, does not reproduce the trend the anchor attributes to it. The deployed net (run_celeba_diverse_diffusion.py FlexibleMLPScore) is GELU, PyTorch default init (weight var 1/(3 fan_in), bias var 1/(3 fan_in)), fan_in = d_lat + 64 (32 sin + 32 cos time-embedding inputs contributing exactly 32 to ||input||^2), trained with t ~ U[0.01, 3] and SGD momentum 0.8. Consequences: (a) GELU is not odd, a_0 = E sigma != 0, Lemma (iii) does not apply, and the recipe's formula a_*^2 = E sigma^2 - a_1^2 q is wrong (must subtract a_0^2): at q=1.834, GELU proper a_*^2 = 0.191 vs recipe 0.380 (2x). (b) The first-layer pre-activation variance is q_real = (d q_d + 33)/(3(d+64)) = 0.365 (CelebA d=50), 0.435 (d=100), 0.357 (d=200), 0.429/0.330 (CIFAR 100/260): essentially FLAT and non-monotone in d, so the GELU a_*^2 is 0.0238, 0.0310, 0.0230 -> the derived clock predicts NO monotone slowdown for the real runs. The anchor's 1.5x-1.7x uses tanh at gamma=1, which is not the deployed network. (c) 'gamma = 1 for LeCun/Kaiming' is wrong: Kaiming is gamma=2, PyTorch default is gamma=1/3. (d) eta must be eta/(1-momentum) = 0.005 for the SGD runs; the fixed-t proposition is a proxy for a network trained over all t.  
  FIX: Either (i) restrict the predictor's derivation to the tanh RFNN and present the real-data application explicitly as a heuristic that uses q_d = lambda_mean as a de-saturation proxy (and say that the literal recipe on the deployed GELU/PyTorch-init network gives a flat clock), or (ii) do the computation for the deployed first layer (GELU with a_0 removed, gamma=1/3, fan_in d+64, shifted pre-activation from the embedding+bias, t-averaged) and confront the result honestly. Fix the recipe formula (subtract a_0^2), the gamma table, and eta_eff. Remove the claim that gamma is 'read from the initialisation (0 fitted)' unless the result is actually used.
- [major] rem:collective-pred; empirical anchor (4); open gaps (2); prediction 6: Unit bookkeeping is inconsistent and the conjecture cannot do what is claimed. The median estimate is made in code units (includes p = 64 d_lat: 64 d a_*^2/n rises 2.4x, i.e. the predicted median eigenvalue RISES with d so tau_mem(median) should FALL 2.4x, whereas F5 says it rises 1.04x -- a sign discrepancy the piece does not mention), while the onset estimate drops the p factor (16.5x, theory units). In consistent code units the per-sample+MP prediction for the largest sample-bulk eigenvalue is p a_*^2 (1+sqrt(n/p))^2/n = 0.254 (d=5) -> 0.174 (d=200), a 1.46x change against the observed 221x; the unexplained factor is ~150x, not ~4x. The conjectured collective block: with the actual Hermite coefficients, sum_{k odd>=3} c_k^2 rho_c^k/k! = 0.0165 = 0.21 a_*^2 at (q=2.76, rho_c=0.652), times n/k = 50 gives a collective eigenvalue ~10.5x the per-sample one at d=5 and ~0.1x at d=200 -- it can contribute at most ~10x. Also 'scale like rho_c^3 ∝ d^-3' is wrong: rho_c = e^{-2t} s^2/(d q_d) with q_d itself falling 8.4x over the sweep (rho_c changes 4.8x, not 40x).  
  FIX: Rewrite with one unit convention (state whether F5's tau's are 1/lambda_code or 1/lambda_theory; with the A/sqrt(p) readout the dynamics rate is lambda_theory = lambda_code/p, which matters because p ∝ d). Report the maximal collective contribution (~10x at d=5) and state that even with it the 221x onset growth is unexplained; add the possibility that the index-partitioned 'sample bulk' of bulk_indices() at small d_lat is contaminated by data-side/BBP-spike modes and that the IPR test must be run before F5's onset statistic is used in the buffer claim. Replace 'd^-3' by '(d q_d)^-3'.
- [major] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred (iii) proof sketch and hypotheses; open gaps (1): The stated per-eigenvalue attachment condition n << d_lat^{3/2} a_*(q_d)^2 is said to be 'marginal' on real latents; it fails by an order of magnitude. At CelebA d=200: d^{3/2} a_*^2 = 2828 x 0.036 = 101 << n = 1000; at d=50: 354 x 0.054 = 19. Even the sign-cancelling estimate ||E||_op ~ sqrt(n) rho^3 with real-latent cosines ~0.1-0.14 (effective rank ~0.5 d) gives 0.03-0.09, comparable to a_*^2 = 0.02-0.05. On the synthetic data same-cluster overlaps are O(1) at every d_lat (rho_c = 0.14 even at d=200 gives n rho_c^3/k per cluster ~ 0.27 vs a_*^2 = 0.0047).  
  FIX: State that the hypothesis fails on both datasets, and that only the bulk/quantile-level statement can be claimed -- and (per issue 1) even that requires n/p << 1. Do not call the condition 'marginal'.
- [minor] prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred proof sketch, parenthetical MP widths: 'relative half-width ≈ 2sqrt(n/p): ≈1.25 at d_lat=5, 0.2 at d_lat=200' -- those are sqrt(n/p); 2sqrt(n/p) = 2.50 and 0.40. Also at d_lat <= 7, p = 64 d < n = 500.  
  FIX: Correct to 2.5 and 0.40; add the p > n requirement and note the rank-deficient regime d_lat <= 7.
- [minor] def:two-source-predictor-pred (set D_{t_c}); cor:shape (ii): Every training point condenses once Delta_t < d_NN(mu)^2/(2 log n), so as the reverse trajectory reaches t -> 0 all points enter D; a hard filter at a single t_c fixes the asymptotic fraction |D|/n by the choice of t_c, making t_c an effectively fitted parameter (rem:param-count admits '+1'). Biroli et al.'s threshold is stated for the typical pairwise distance, not per-point d_NN. Also, when |D| < n, level f corresponds to the upper f n/|D| quantile of q_mu, not the f-quantile as cor (ii) states.  
  FIX: Say that t_c is the sampler's effective stopping/condensation time and is a parameter unless Piece 5 fixes it; state the per-point reinterpretation of Biroli as an assumption; correct the quantile in cor (ii).
- [minor] rem:spec-Mt-misses-mem-pred; empirical anchor (7): The anchor says the CSV floor lambda_min = Delta_t starts at d>=160 (CelebA) / d>=200 (CIFAR); the CSVs actually show 0.18145 at d=140 (CelebA) and 0.18327 at d=160 (CIFAR), matching F9. 'These dominate the late-time growth of P_d(s)' needs the count of exact-Delta_t coordinates, which is not given.  
  FIX: Correct the CSV dims to 140/160; quote the number of collapsed coordinates per d (or the fraction of eigenvalues within 1e-3 of Delta_t).
- [minor] cor:shape-from-qmu-pred (i); rem:square-pred: The norm-ordering follows only because the criterion is on the absorbed FRACTION 1-e^{-eta Lambda s}. The absolute learned sample-specific amplitude is a_mu^*(1-e^{-eta Lambda_mu s}) with a_mu^* = V u_mu/Lambda_mu; if the sampler responds to the absolute amplitude (as the softmax-weight picture of F15 suggests), larger Lambda_mu does not obviously imply earlier memorisation. theta is admittedly underived, so (i) is conditional on an undischarged choice.  
  FIX: State (i) as conditional on the fractional-absorption criterion, and note the alternative.
- [minor] Validation section, empirical anchor (5): Which dims are 'post-threshold' and how C is fit (mean vs median log residual) are unstated. My reproduction on dims >= 40: 5%: one-constant 0.332 in-sample / 0.334 LOO vs released 0.340; 25%: 0.239/0.320 vs 0.156; tau ∝ d baseline 0.158/0.138 (piece: 0.125/0.083). Numbers are consistent in kind but not in value with the piece's 0.347/0.449 and 0.125/0.083.  
  FIX: State the dim set and the estimator for C; regenerate the table with the corrected Lambda_mu (issue 2).
- [minor] prop:mode-clocks-pred; prop:data-side-rates-pred: Both are correct; the only omissions are that T = eta s/(1-m) for heavy-ball SGD (m=0.8 in the real runs) and that for the deployed net the data-side rate carries fan_in = d_lat+64 rather than d_lat. The Gaussian-equivalence step is invoked for clustered synthetic data where GE is known to fail at O(1) overlaps (same failure the piece itself exploits in rem:collective-pred).  
  FIX: Add eta_eff and fan_in remarks; state in prop:data-side hypotheses that GE is assumed for clustered data even though its residual is not Gaussian-equivalent at O(1) same-cluster overlap.


### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Proposition bridge / Prediction 1 cannot be instantiated at t = t_min. With t = t_min the formula F = 1 - G_NN(3 sqrt(d Delta_{t_min} + e^{2t} Delta_t^2 E_t)) applied to a model that has learned the EXACT POPULATION score predicts F_mem = 0.24 (d=5), 0.30 (10), 0.39 (20), 0.64 (40) -- growing with d_lat -- while the released Euler-Maruyama sampler driven by s_pop reports 0.004, 0.000, 0.000, 0.000. The claim in the proof sketch that "early ... E_t is far above the threshold and F_mem = 0 regardless" is false (E_{t_min}(s_pop) = 247 vs median threshold 61 at d=5, but 1886 vs 2107 at d=40). Root cause: at t_min the Tweedie displacement of any non-empirical score is essentially the un-removed forward noise e^t sqrt(Delta_t) eta, which is then added in quadrature to a second independent terminal N(0, Delta_{t_min}) -- (B3) double counts, and (B1) is a condition on s_emp when attachment is a property of the LEARNED score. Fix: (i) make the attachment time the (learned-score) condensation time t_c(mu), not t_min; (ii) rewrite the criterion in relative-error form r_mu^2(T) := E_{t_c,mu}(T) Delta_{t_c}/d_lat < (2 log n / 9 d_lat)(1 - 9 d_lat Delta_{t_min}/d_NN(mu)^2), which correctly gives F = 0 for s_pop (r^2 = O(1) at t_c by definition of t_c) and matches the O(1) blend threshold of (G1); (iii) restate Prediction 1 with E measured at t_c(mu), and downgrade prop:bridge from "sketched" to "heuristic model / conjecture" since its key hypothesis is shown to fail in a regime the paper's own sweep passes through.

Independent check: All numerics run on the repo's own generator (code/v3/lib/data_synthetic.py, seed 42, n=500, k=10, d_int=5, s=3, sig_sig=1) and the released metric (code/v3/lib/metrics.py, threshold 1/3; NOTE the code's denominator is d(NN1, nearest-train-neighbour-of-NN1), not x-hat's second-nearest) and sampler (code/v3/lib/diffusion.py: EM, 500 steps, t_max=3, t_min=0.01, dt=0.00598, no terminal denoising; confirmed by run config.json).

(1) Lemma condensation algebra: log(w_nu/w_mu) = -e^{-2t}D^2/(2Delta) - e^{-t}<eta, x^mu-x^nu>/sqrt(Delta) -- correct. Bracket bound, -8 log(n/delta), sum <= delta^8 n^{-7}, and the r bound all check. Union bound with two-sided |.| gives 1-2delta, not 1-delta; only the lower tail matters, so one-sided suffices. Eta=0 form: n e^{-e^{-2t}d^2/2Delta}<1 iff e^{2t}Delta<d^2/(2 log n) -- correct, but it only certifies w_mu>1/2 at the noise-free point. Simulated at the quoted eta=0 t_c (median d_NN): P_eta(w_mu>0.9) = 0.73, 0.59, 0.48, 0.30 for d=5,10,20,40 (sig_perp=0.5) -- not condensed for most points at d>=20. The time at which P(w_mu>0.9)>=0.9 is 0.025, 0.060, 0.118, 0.211 vs quoted 0.046, 0.105, 0.21, 0.39 (fluctuation term e^{-t}D/sqrt(Delta) has std a with a^2 = 2 log n at the eta=0 threshold; sharp large-deviation threshold is e^{2t}Delta <~ d_NN^2/(8 log n_eff)).

(2) Lemma endpoint: ODE offset dy/dtau = -(e^{-2t}/Delta_t) y, integral (1/2) log(Delta_c/Delta_min): correct. NN1 = mu when ||delta||<d_NN/3: correct. But the exact reverse SDE with s_emp samples rho^emp_{t_min} = (1/n) sum N(e^{-t_min} x^mu, Delta_min I) EXACTLY, so the ceiling needs no condensation hypothesis at all. Ceiling numbers: concentrated formula 1-G(3 sqrt(d Delta_min)) gives 0.656/0.778/0.882/0.920/0.956/0.984/0.996/1.000 (d=5,8,10,12,15,20,25,40; piece quotes 0.676/0.790/0.870/0.924/0.950/0.979/0.998/~1, consistent across replicates); exact law of rho^emp_{t_min} through the code metric gives 0.672/0.751/0.823/0.857/0.895/0.939/0.970/0.997; the ACTUAL released EM sampler with the exact s_emp gives 0.533/0.588/0.632/0.686/0.736/0.801/0.863/0.947 with measured terminal variance 1.35-1.43 x Delta_min (dt/Delta_min = 0.30; EM on a linear SDE with rate a inflates variance by 1/(1 - a dt/2), a dt ~ 0.38 at the last step). At sig_perp=0.01: concentrated 0.656/0.350/0.236/0.170/0.106/0.060/0.026/0.000, exact law 0.671/0.446/0.329/0.243/0.156/0.078/0.042/0.004, EM 0.526/0.301/0.219/0.133/0.085/0.037/0.016/0.000 (piece quotes 0.668/0.364/0.250/0.165/0.100/0.034/0.015/0.002). "O(dt) which only lowers the ceiling" hides a 25-45% effect.

(3) Prop bridge instantiated at t=t_min on the exact population score (code/v3/lib/true_score.py with Q): E_{t_min}(s_pop vs s_emp) mean = 247, 483, 949, 1886 for d=5,10,20,40; median threshold (d_NN^2 - 9d Delta_min)/(9 e^{2t} Delta_min^2) = 61, 299, 844, 2107; predicted F_mem (per-sample indicator / mean form) = 0.242/0.236, 0.296/0.298, 0.386/0.396, 0.640/0.632. EM sampler with s_pop: fraction 0.004, 0.000, 0.000, 0.000, mean NN-ratio 1.07-1.12. Fresh population samples through the metric: 0.004, 0.001, 0.000 (d=5,8,>=10) -- so the metric has no false-positive floor; the failure is the proposition's.

(4) Tweedie algebra: x-hat = e^t(x_t + Delta_t s) = x^mu + e^t Delta_t zeta; c_t = e^{2t} Delta_t^2/d; criterion algebra; threshold comparison 9d > 2 e^{2t} log n (d>=1.7 at n=500, t=0.1): all correct. Note c_t ~ 4t^2, so c_{t_c}/c_{t_min} = 18 (d=5) to ~1500 (d=40): the constant is not "known up to a logarithm" unless E_s ~ 1/Delta_s is assumed; with t-persistent zeta the ODE accumulation gives 2 sqrt(t_min t_c) zeta vs one-step 2 t zeta.

(5) Cor taumem-fraction: tau(q) = (1/lambda)[log(3 e^t Delta_t sqrt(E_0)) - (1/2) log((d^{(1-q)})^2 - 9 d Delta_min)], difference (1/lambda) log(d^{(1-q1)}/d^{(1-q2)}) + O(...) -- correct, but this is a width in T, not log T. n-scaling: d^{(1-q)} ~ n^{-1/d_int} so d tau/d log n = +1/(lambda d_int) > 0 (smaller d_NN => later memorization); the piece writes -(2/d_int)(1/(2 lambda tau)) (main) and -1/(d_int lambda tau) (appendix): SIGN WRONG. Crossing times from metrics.jsonl d=5: 150k/350k/550k match anchor (6); predicted spacing ratio log(2.27/1.92)/log(1.92/1.69) = 1.3 vs measured 1.0 (50k granularity) -- not decisive.

(6) Cor dnn-scaling: min over nu of (signal+null) != min signal + min null; at d=40 the formula gives 2*0.25*35 + 1.07^2 = 18.6 vs measured median d_NN^2 = 14.7 (-22%); at d=200, 97.5 vs 83.9 (-14%), consistent with a NEGATIVE correction of order sqrt(log n/(d-d_int)). Quantiles reproduced: d=5 0.74/1.05/1.69, d=20 2.13/2.57/3.04, d=40 3.46/3.84/4.27 (piece: 0.72/1.10/1.63, 2.13/2.56/3.00, 3.41/3.81/4.25).

(7) Remark pixel: from (l/L) rho_lat <= rho_pix <= (L/l) rho_lat one gets F_lat(l/3L) <= F_pix <= F_lat(L/3l), a sandwich; "pixel fraction lower-bounds the latent fraction" does not follow. Also rho_pix <= (L/l) rho_lat needs pixel-NN1 = latent-NN1.

(8) Bib: song2021scoresde, somepalli2023diffusion, biroli2024dynamical, levina2004maximum, facco2017twonn, scarvelis2023closedform, gu2023memorization, kadkhodaie2023learning all present; efron2011tweedie absent (correctly listed under citations_needed). Definition E_t(T) = sum_i lambda_i ||a_i*||^2 e^{-2 lambda_i T} + E_t(inf) is consistent with a_i(T)-a_i* = -a_i* e^{-lambda_i T} and Pythagoras for the L^2(rho_emp,t) projection.

- [fatal] prop:bridge, proof sketch ('When (B2) is justified', failure mode (i)) and Prediction 1 ('E measured ... at t = t_min'): Instantiated at t = t_min the proposition predicts F_mem = 0.24, 0.30, 0.39, 0.64 (d_lat = 5, 10, 20, 40) for a model that has learned the exact POPULATION score, increasing with d_lat; the released EM sampler driven by s_pop reports 0.004, 0, 0, 0. The sketch's assertion that early residuals are 'far above the threshold' is numerically false (E_{t_min}(s_pop) = 1886 vs threshold 2107 at d=40). Mechanism: at t_min the Tweedie displacement of any non-empirical score is the un-removed forward noise e^t sqrt(Delta_t) eta, which (B3) then adds in quadrature to a second independent terminal N(0, Delta_{t_min}); and (B1) conditions on s_emp condensing, whereas attachment is a property of the learned score s_T. The 'zero-parameter prediction' is therefore falsified before it is run.  
  FIX: Take the attachment time to be the condensation time of the LEARNED score (approximated by t_c(mu) of s_emp only once s_T is close to s_emp there), never t_min. State the criterion in relative-error form: r_mu^2(T) := E_{t_c,mu}(T) Delta_{t_c}/d_lat < (2 log n/(9 d_lat)) (1 - 9 d_lat Delta_{t_min}/d_NN(mu)^2), which yields F = 0 for s_pop (r^2 = O(1) at t_c) and coincides with the O(1) blend threshold of (G1). Rewrite Prediction 1 with E evaluated at t_c(mu). Relabel prop:bridge as a heuristic model/conjecture; in the main text say explicitly that (B3) is known to fail at t = t_min.
- [major] lem:endpoint eq:ceiling, its proof ('Euler--Maruyama adds O(dt) variance ... which only lowers the ceiling'), empirical anchor (1), and main-text numbers 0.68 / ->1 for d>=25 / 0.03 at d=20: The released sampler has dt = 0.00598 = 0.30 Delta_{t_min}; simulating it with the exact s_emp gives terminal variance 1.35-1.43 x Delta_{t_min} and ceilings 0.533, 0.588, 0.632, 0.686, 0.736, 0.801, 0.863, 0.947 (d=5,8,10,12,15,20,25,40, sig_perp=0.5) and 0.526, 0.301, 0.219, 0.133, 0.085, 0.037, 0.016, 0.000 (sig_perp=0.01), versus the quoted 0.676 ... ~1 and 0.668 ... 0.002. The d_lat >> 1 chi-square concentration is also inaccurate at small d (exact-law ceiling 0.446 vs 0.364 quoted at sig_perp=0.01, d=8). The measured d=5 fraction 0.29 is within 1.8x of the true ceiling, not 2.3x.  
  FIX: Define the ceiling as the fraction reported by the released sampler driven by the exact s_emp (Monte Carlo, no d>>1 approximation) or, if a formula is wanted, replace Delta_{t_min} by Delta_EM = Delta_{t_min}(1 + O(dt/Delta_{t_min})) with the measured factor ~1.4 and state it. Also note the ceiling lemma needs NO condensation hypothesis: the exact reverse SDE with s_emp samples rho^emp_{t_min} exactly, so drop 'Suppose condensation holds' from the ceiling part.
- [major] cor:dnn-scaling (i), main text (b): '\partial\log\taumem/\partial\log\nsamp acquires the additive term -\frac{2}{\dint}\cdot\frac{1}{2\lambda_{\rm samp}\taumem}' (appendix: '-\frac{1}{\dint\lambda_{\rm samp}\taumem}'): Sign error. tau(q) = (1/lambda)[log(3 e^t Delta_t sqrt(E_0)) - log d_NN^{(1-q)} + ...] and d_NN^{(1-q)} ~ n^{-1/d_int}, so larger n makes d_NN smaller and memorization LATER: d tau/d log n = +1/(lambda d_int), i.e. the term is +1/(d_int lambda tau).  
  FIX: Replace the sign in both the main text and appendix; state the direction in words (denser training sets memorize later at fixed spectrum).
- [major] rem:pixel (a) and main text (c): 'the pixel-space fraction is a lower bound on the latent-space fraction whenever L/ell>1': Does not follow. From (ell/L) rho_lat <= rho_pix <= (L/ell) rho_lat one gets only the sandwich F_lat(threshold ell/3L) <= F_pix <= F_lat(threshold L/3ell); the pixel fraction at threshold 1/3 can be above or below the latent fraction at threshold 1/3. Moreover rho_pix <= (L/ell) rho_lat requires the pixel-space NN_1 to coincide with the latent NN_1, which bi-Lipschitzness does not guarantee.  
  FIX: State the sandwich, add the NN_1-coincidence hypothesis (or restrict to the memorized event ||delta|| < ell d_NN/(2L) where it holds), and delete the 'lower bound' claim from the main text.
- [major] lem:condensation ('gives the sufficient condition e^{2t}Delta_t < d_NN^2/(2 log n)'), prop:bridge (B1), empirical anchor (4), Prediction 5: The eta=0 form certifies only w_mu > 1/2 at the noise-free point; the fluctuation term has std a with a^2 = 2 log n exactly at that threshold, so it is not a condensation condition with high probability over eta. Simulated at the quoted t_c: P(w_mu > 0.9) = 0.73, 0.59, 0.48, 0.30 for d = 5, 10, 20, 40. The time at which P(w_mu>0.9) >= 0.9 is 0.025, 0.060, 0.118, 0.211 vs quoted 0.046, 0.105, 0.21, 0.39; the large-deviation threshold is e^{2t}Delta_t <~ d_NN^2/(8 log n_eff), n_eff = number of competitors at distance ~d_NN.  
  FIX: Call the eta=0 form a mean-field estimate, not a sufficient condition; replace the anchor t_c values by the high-probability ones (or report both); keep the qualitative trend (t_c grows with d_lat at sig_perp=0.5, flat at 0.01), which survives.
- [minor] lem:condensation proof, union bound 'with probability >= 1-delta simultaneously |<eta, x^mu - x^nu>| <= D_nu sqrt(2 log(n/delta))': Two-sided Gaussian tail gives failure probability 2 delta/n per index, so the event has probability >= 1-2delta, not 1-delta.  
  FIX: Use the one-sided bound <eta, x^mu-x^nu> >= -D_nu sqrt(2 log(n/delta)) (only the lower tail increases w_nu), which gives 1-delta.
- [minor] def:bridge-objects: 'nearest and second-nearest training points NN_1, NN_2' and rho = ||x-hat - NN_1||/||NN_1 - NN_2||: The released metric (code/v3/lib/metrics.py) uses nn2 = nearest training neighbour OF NN_1 (train_dists[nn1_idx].min), not x-hat's second-nearest training point. Under the code's definition the denominator equals d_NN(NN_1) identically, which is what every proof in the piece uses.  
  FIX: Define NN_2 as the nearest training neighbour of NN_1 (Bonnaire-style), note it matches the code, and drop the 'valid when ||delta|| < d_NN/2' caveats for the denominator (only NN_1 = mu needs ||delta|| < d_NN/3).
- [minor] prop:bridge hypothesis (B2): 'conditionally on x_t ..., zeta_T(x_t,t) is approximately N(0,(E/d) I) and independent of eta': zeta_T(x_t,t) is a deterministic function of x_t; conditioning on x_t leaves nothing random. Also the identity E||delta||^2 = d Delta_{t_min} + e^{2t}Delta_t^2 E_{t,mu} needs only (B3) (zeta independent of the terminal Brownian noise), not (B2); (B2) is needed only for chi-square concentration.  
  FIX: Rephrase: 'as a function of the forward noise eta (and the random features), zeta_T(x_t,t) is approximately isotropic Gaussian with E||zeta||^2 = E_{t,mu} and asymptotically uncorrelated with eta'. Separate the mean identity (from B3) from the concentration step (from B2).
- [minor] prop:bridge c_t = e^{2t}Delta_t^2/d_lat and open gap (G2) 'accurate to a logarithmic factor in t_c/t_min': c_t ~ 4t^2 varies by a factor (t_c/t_min)^2 = 18 (d=5) to ~1500 (d=40) over the admissible t in [t_min, t_c]; the logarithmic-factor claim holds only under the extra scaling assumption E_s ~ 1/Delta_s, and for a t-persistent zeta the ODE accumulation gives displacement 2 sqrt(t_min t_c) zeta vs the one-step 2 t zeta.  
  FIX: State the scaling assumption explicitly in the proposition (or use the relative-error form r^2 = E_t Delta_t/d, which removes the t^2 prefactor), and fix t = t_c(mu).
- [minor] cor:dnn-scaling eq:dnn-scaling and proof ('the two parts add because signal and null coordinates are independent'); '(1+O(...))' in statement vs '(1-O(...))' in proof: min_nu(a_nu + b_nu) != min_nu a_nu + min_nu b_nu; the decomposition is a lower bound, and the correction has a definite negative sign. Numerically the formula gives 18.6 vs measured median d_NN^2 = 14.7 at d=40 (-22%) and 97.5 vs 83.9 at d=200 (-14%).  
  FIX: Write d_NN^2 = 2 sig_perp^2 (d-d_int)(1 - c sqrt(log n/(d-d_int))) + O(signal part), label the two-term split as leading-order/heuristic, and make the sign consistent between statement and proof.
- [minor] cor:taumem-fraction: 'the width of the fraction curve in log T is set by ...': eq:taumem-spread gives tau(q2)-tau(q1) = (1/lambda) log(d^{(1-q1)}/d^{(1-q2)}), an additive width in T; the width in log T depends on the absolute offset log(3 e^t Delta_t sqrt(E_0)) and is not determined by the d_NN spread alone.  
  FIX: Say 'width in T (in units of 1/lambda_samp)'; if a log-T statement is wanted, include the offset term.
- [minor] lem:endpoint proof: '||x_{t_min}-x^mu||^2 = Delta_{t_min}||eta||^2 + O(t_min ||x^mu||)': The shrinkage contributes (1-e^{-t_min})^2||x^mu||^2 + 2(1-e^{-t_min}) sqrt(Delta_{t_min}) <x^mu, eta>, i.e. O(t_min^2||x^mu||^2) + O(t_min sqrt(Delta) ||x^mu||), not O(t_min||x^mu||); numerically <= 2% of d Delta_{t_min}, so harmless but mis-stated.  
  FIX: Correct the order symbol.
- [minor] Prediction 4 and anchor (6): crossing-time ratios T(5%)/T(1%) 'grow with d_lat, which the data-level term alone does not produce': Under the single-exponential model the ratio T(q2)/T(q1) = 1 + lambda-independent-offset ... depends on the absolute offset, so a growing ratio is not evidence for the eigenvalue-spread term without first checking the additive width T(5%)-T(1%) in units of 1/lambda_samp; at d=5 the measured additive spacings (200k, 200k) versus predicted log-quantile spacings (0.167, 0.128) are consistent only to the 50k eval granularity.  
  FIX: Test the additive form (differences, not ratios) and quote the RFNN 1/lambda_samp used; soften the attribution.


### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Rewrite the metric to what the code computes -- varrho = d(gen,NN1(gen))/d(gen,NN2(gen)) < 1/3 -- and propagate it: every 3*sqrt(d_lat Delta) becomes sqrt(8 d_lat Delta), every d_NN^2/9 becomes d_NN^2/8, the per-coordinate criterion becomes Delta_eff < sigma_perp^2/4; then replace the analytic ceiling table by the direct simulation of the Gaussian endpoint law with the released sampler's actual terminal variance 0.0269 = 1.36 Delta_tmin and no chi^2 concentration (sigma_perp=0.5: 0.56, 0.64, 0.68, 0.71, 0.77, 0.83, 0.88, 0.91, 0.96 for d=5..40; sigma_perp=0.01: 0.56, 0.35, 0.24, 0.17, 0.10, 0.04, 0.02, 0.01, 0.001), and drop '->1 for d_lat>=25' and 'must vanish for d_lat>=20'.

Independent check: Repo facts verified: multiseed_runs/exp2_mlp_dlat_sn05_5m_bonnaire_hidden256/di5_d{5..40}_n500_s{42..46} exist with config hidden=256, total_steps=5e6, t_min=0.01, t_max=3.0, n_sde_steps=500, eval_interval=50000, n_gen_samples=5000; metrics.jsonl has 101 rows and no checkpoints are stored (no .pt/.pth/.ckpt anywhere). Seed-42 final fractions 0.2896, 0.2236, 0.1684, 0.1274, 0.0790, 0.0438, 0.0170, 0.0060, 0.0038, 0.0018 and mean_nn_ratio 0.524 -> 0.868 match the piece. Level-crossing steps (first row with fraction >= q) for q=1%/2%/5%/10%: d=5 150k/200k/350k/550k; d=8 250k/350k/550k/1.0M; d=10 300k/450k/700k/1.7M; d=12 400k/500k/1.0M/2.65M; d=15 600k/850k/2.1M/-; d=20 1.05M/1.8M/-/-; d=25 2.55M/-/-/- -- all match the piece (ratios T5/T1 = 2.33, 2.2, 2.33, 2.5, 3.5). Old sigma_noise_0.01/exp2_mlp/raw_data (300k steps, hidden = 8 d_lat): final fractions 0.0062, 0.0010, 0.0014, 0.0002, 0 (d=5,8,10,15,20..200) match; old sigma_noise_0.5: 0.0022 at d=150, 0.0144 at d=200 (F13 return effect). Code (experiment_v2.py): sampler is EM x <- x + (x+2s)dt + sqrt(2dt) xi from t=3 to t=0.01 with dt=0.00598, last score at t=0.016, no denoising; metric is topk(k=2) distances from each GENERATED point to the training set, ratio nn1/nn2 < 1/3 (not d(NN1,NN2)); generator: 5 orthogonal + 5 random centers of norm 3 in d_int=5, multinomial labels, sigma_signal=1, sigma_noise in null dims, random QR rotation. Numerics with a reimplementation of that generator (n=500, 5 replicate seeds): d_NN q10/q50/q90 at sigma_perp=0.5: 0.71/1.10/1.66 (d=5), 1.31/1.70/2.21 (10), 2.16/2.58/3.03 (20), 3.43/3.83/4.26 (40), 8.77/9.19/9.59 (200), spread q90/q10 2.34 -> 1.09; at sigma_perp=0.01 flat 0.71/1.10/1.66 -- agrees with the piece. Condensation times (eta=0 form, median d_NN): 0.046, 0.105, 0.214, 0.390, 0.718, 1.026 at sigma_perp=0.5 and 0.046-0.048 flat at 0.01 -- agrees. Analytic ceilings with the piece's constant 3 and Delta_tmin=0.019801: 0.685, 0.818, 0.884, 0.916, 0.955, 0.983, 0.992, 0.998 (d=5..30, sigma_perp=0.5) -- agrees within replicate noise. Direct simulation of x_hat = e^{-0.01} x^mu + sqrt(V) eta scored with the code's metric: V=Delta_tmin gives 0.685/0.781/0.832/0.869/0.911/0.951/0.975/0.986/0.998 (sigma_perp=0.5) and 0.685/0.483/0.362/0.278/0.178/0.087/0.046/0.024/0.006 (sigma_perp=0.01) for d=5,8,10,12,15,20,25,30,40; EM endpoint variance recursion v <- (1+dt-2dt/Delta_t)^2 v + 2dt from t_c to t_min gives v = 0.0269 = 1.36 Delta_tmin (independent of t_c), and with V=0.0269 the ceilings are 0.560/0.640/0.680/0.710/0.766/0.830/0.880/0.909/0.956 (0.5) and 0.560/0.345/0.238/0.167/0.095/0.038/0.017/0.008/0.001 (0.01). n-scaling at d=20, sigma_perp=0.01, 3 seeds: median d_NN * n^{1/5} = 4.05, 3.79, 3.80, 3.79 for n=100,500,1000,5000. Algebra checked: lem:condensation constants (bracket >= e^{-t}D/(2 sqrt Delta), log ratio <= -8 log(n/delta), 1-w_mu <= delta^8 n^{-7}) correct; probability-flow offset ODE dy/dtau = -(e^{-2t}/Delta_t) y and the factor sqrt(Delta_tmin/Delta_tc) correct; Tweedie step x_hat = x^mu + e^t Delta_t zeta correct; threshold comparison 9 d > 2 e^{2t} log n correct as written (becomes 8 d with the code's metric); cor:dnn-scaling(i) derivative d log tau/d log n = +1/(d_int lambda tau), piece has the wrong sign. Bib: gu2023memorization, scarvelis2023closedform, kadkhodaie2023learning, song2021scoresde, levina2004maximum, facco2017twonn present; efron2011tweedie absent. Section labels sec:rfnn, sec:design, sec:real-data exist; macros \\dlat, \\taugen, \\taumem, \\signoise exist in main.tex.

- [major] def:bridge-objects, lem:endpoint (eq:ceiling and its proof), prop:bridge (eq:mem-criterion, eq:Fmem), cor:taumem-fraction (eq:taumem-q), cor:dnn-scaling (ii), main text paragraph 2 and (a),(b): The piece defines the Somepalli statistic as varrho = ||x_hat - NN1|| / ||NN1 - NN2|| and then uses ||NN1-NN2|| = d_NN(mu) to get every constant (3*sqrt(d Delta), d_NN^2/9, 9 d Delta, 2 sigma^2/9). The released code (experiment_v2.py lines 362-371, used by all synthetic runs in multiseed_runs/exp2_mlp_dlat_sn05_5m_bonnaire_hidden256) computes nn_ratio = d(gen,NN1_train(gen)) / d(gen,NN2_train(gen)), i.e. the SECOND-NEAREST TRAINING POINT TO THE GENERATED SAMPLE, and thresholds at 1/3. For x_hat = x^mu + delta with isotropic delta, d(gen,NN2)^2 ~ d_NN^2 + ||delta||^2, so varrho<1/3 iff ||delta||^2 < d_NN^2/8, not /9. All '3' constants become sqrt(8)=2.83, all '/9' become '/8', the per-coordinate criterion in cor:dnn-scaling(ii) becomes Delta_eff < sigma_perp^2/4, and the threshold-comparison inequality becomes 8 d_lat > 2 e^{2t} log n (still d_lat >= 2). The proof step 'NN_1 = x^mu and ||NN_1-NN_2|| = d_NN(mu)' is simply not what the metric measures.  
  FIX: Rewrite def:bridge-objects to the code's definition: varrho(x_hat) = d(x_hat, NN1(x_hat)) / d(x_hat, NN2(x_hat)), citing Bonnaire 2025 for the 1/3 threshold. In lem:endpoint and prop:bridge replace the identification ||NN1-NN2|| = d_NN(mu) by d(x_hat,NN2)^2 = d_NN(mu)^2 + ||delta||^2 + 2<delta, x^mu - x^nu> (cross term O(||delta|| d_NN / sqrt(d)) drops under (B2)), giving the criterion ||delta||^2 < d_NN(mu)^2/8. Propagate: eq:ceiling -> 1 - G_NN(sqrt(8 d_lat Delta_tmin)); eq:mem-criterion -> E_{t,mu} < (d_NN^2 - 8 d_lat Delta_tmin)/(8 e^{2t} Delta_t^2); eq:Fmem, eq:taumem-q, eq:taumem-spread, cor:dnn-scaling(ii) (Delta_eff < sigma_perp^2/4) and main-text (a),(b) accordingly.
- [major] lem:endpoint last sentence ('Euler-Maruyama adds a discretization variance of order dt ... which only lowers the ceiling'); appendix preamble; empirical_anchor (1); prediction 2; main text 'equal to 0.68 at d_lat=5 and ->1 for d_lat>=25': The ceiling table is computed for the continuous-time endpoint variance Delta_tmin = 0.0198, but the released sampler (500 EM steps, dt = 2.99/500 = 0.00598, last score evaluated at t = 0.016, no denoising) has endpoint offset variance 0.0269 = 1.36 Delta_tmin (exact recursion v <- (1+dt-2dt/Delta_t)^2 v + 2dt for the single-Gaussian score, independent of t_c). That is not 'of order dt' relative to Delta_tmin, it is a 36% inflation and shifts the ceilings materially. Direct simulation of x_hat = e^{-t_min} x^mu + sqrt(V) eta with the code's NN-ratio over 5 replicate training sets: at V = Delta_tmin the ceilings at sigma_perp=0.5 are 0.685/0.781/0.832/0.869/0.911/0.951/0.975/0.986/0.998 (d=5..40), at V = 0.0269 they are 0.560/0.640/0.680/0.710/0.766/0.830/0.880/0.909/0.956 -- so the claim '->1 for d_lat>=25' is false for the actual sampler (0.88 at d=25) and the d=5 run is within a factor 1.9, not 2.3, of its ceiling. At sigma_perp=0.01 the EM-variance ceilings are 0.560/0.345/0.238/0.167/0.095/0.038/0.017/0.008/0.001; the piece's table (0.668/0.364/0.250/0.165/0.100/0.034/0.015/0.008/0.002) happens to be numerically close only because two errors (wrong constant 3 vs sqrt8, and ignoring chi^2 fluctuation + EM inflation) partly cancel. Also, at sigma_perp=0.01 and d<=40 the chi^2_d fluctuation of ||delta||^2 is NOT negligible: with V=Delta_tmin the concentration formula gives 0.044 at d=20 while the exact expectation E_mu P_eta gives 0.087 (factor 2).  
  FIX: (i) Replace 'O(dt), only lowers the ceiling' by the explicit statement: for the released sampler the endpoint offset variance is Delta_EM = 0.0269 = 1.36 Delta_tmin (give the one-line recursion), and use Delta_EM everywhere Delta_tmin appears as terminal noise, or better define Delta_term := Delta_EM. (ii) State the ceiling without the chi^2 concentration step, as F_max = (1/n) sum_mu P_eta[ Delta_term ||eta||^2 < d_NN(mu)^2/8 ] (exact under the Gaussian endpoint law), and quote the direct-simulation numbers above. (iii) Change main text to 'about 0.56 at d_lat=5, rising to about 0.83 at d_lat=20 and 0.96 at d_lat=40 at sigma_perp=0.5; collapsing to 0.04 at d_lat=20 at sigma_perp=0.01'. (iv) In prediction 2 replace 'must vanish for d_lat>=20' by 'is capped at 0.04 (d=20), 0.017 (25), 0.008 (30), 0.001 (40)'; the old sigma_perp=0.01 runs (0, 0, 0, 0 at d=20,30,40,50) are consistent with the cap but cannot distinguish it from undertraining.
- [major] predictions[0] ('Zero-parameter fraction prediction'), prop:bridge hypothesis 'Fix a diffusion time t in [t_min,t_c]', open_gaps G2/G3: The prediction is labelled zero-parameter but c_t = e^{2t} Delta_t^2 / d_lat varies by 4 orders of magnitude over the admissible attachment window: at d_lat=200, sigma_perp=0.5 the piece's own t_c = 1.02 gives e^{2t}Delta_t^2 = 5.8 versus 4.0e-4 at t=t_min (ratio ~1.5e4); at d_lat=5 (t_c=0.046) the ratio is ~20. Even in the relative-error form (E_t = d r_t^2/Delta_t) the additive term is e^{2t} Delta_t r_t^2, i.e. Delta_{t_c} r^2 versus Delta_{t_min} r^2. The choice t = t_min in the test is therefore a discrete free choice that decides the prediction, and G2 admits a further 'logarithmic factor 1.5-4.6'. A referee will not accept 'zero-parameter' or 'curves collapse without fitting'.  
  FIX: Rename the test 'one-choice test (t = t_min) with the t = t_c alternative as the competing candidate', state explicitly that the two candidates differ in c_t by e^{2t_c}(Delta_{t_c}/Delta_{t_min})^2 (20 at d=5, 1.5e4 at d=200), and present the comparison as discriminating between (a) t_min-Tweedie, (b) t_c-Tweedie, and (c) the blend model of G1, rather than as a parameter-free prediction. Say in prop:bridge that t is not determined by the argument.
- [major] predictions[0], predictions[1](b), predictions[3], predictions[4]; how_to_test fields: None of the proposed tests can be run on the repo's existing data: (1) no checkpoints are saved anywhere (find over multiseed_runs, sigma_noise_0.5, sigma_noise_0.01 returns no .pt/.pth/.ckpt), and metrics.jsonl only stores train/test loss, gen_gap, score_error, mean_nn_ratio, memorization_fraction every 50k steps -- so E_t(T) 'at every eval checkpoint' requires re-running the 5M-step sweep (~4.7 h per (d, seed) at the logged 168 s / 50k steps) with either checkpointing or an online residual computation; (2) there is no empirical-score implementation in the repo (grep for logsumexp / s_emp / empirical score over *.py finds nothing; code/v3/lib/true_score.py has only the GMM population score), so s_emp must be written (trivial, but say so); (3) the Tweedie-denoise / t_min=1e-3 sampler variant in prediction 2(b) is a code change plus new runs; (4) the sigma_perp=0.01 5M/hidden-256 sweep in prediction 2(a) does not exist (only the 300k-step hidden=8 d_lat runs in sigma_noise_0.01/exp2_mlp/raw_data and results_mlp_exp2*_sn001). The piece's 'already_supported_by' fields are honest, but the how_to_test fields read as if the data were on disk.  
  FIX: Add one sentence per prediction stating what must be produced: 'requires re-running the sweep with an online residual E_{t_min}(T) computed against a (to-be-written) closed-form s_emp, since no checkpoints are stored' etc. Prediction 5 (s_emp sampler alone) is the only one runnable today and should be run before submission: its endpoint law has already been simulated here (numbers in the ceiling issue) and matches the piece's qualitative claim (fraction = ceiling at every d_lat).
- [major] cor:taumem-fraction (mu-independent E_t(T)), eq:taumem-q, relation to F5: Under the mu-independent reduction E_t(T) = sum_i w_i e^{-2 lambda_i T}, a level crossing far below E_t(0) is controlled by the SLOWEST sample-bulk modes with appreciable weight, so tau_mem(q) would track the lower part of the sample bulk. F5 (ground truth) says the memorization onset on the repo's own spectra tracks the TOP of the sample bulk (largest sample-bulk eigenvalue) and only that statistic gives the monotone tau_mem/tau_gen; on the median statistic the ratio shrinks 7.8x. The piece only recovers the F5 convention through the per-sample form E_{t,mu} (G6, explicitly not derived). As written, the corollary and the 'single lambda_samp' formula eq:taumem-spread are in tension with F5, and the piece never says which sample-bulk eigenvalue lambda_samp is.  
  FIX: State in cor:taumem-fraction that (i) with a mu-independent residual the crossing is governed by the slow end of the bulk, (ii) the onset statistic used in the paper (largest sample-bulk eigenvalue, F5) corresponds to the per-sample picture in which the first-memorized points are those whose sample modes have the largest lambda_i, and (iii) therefore eq:taumem-spread with lambda_samp := lambda_max^{samp} is the version consistent with the paper's tau_mem(onset), while the median version is not expected to be monotone. Move G6 from 'open gap' to a stated caveat next to the corollary.
- [major] cor:dnn-scaling (i) and main text (b); prediction 3; positioning vs Bonnaire 2025: Two problems. (1) Sign error: with E(T) = E_0 e^{-2 lambda T} and threshold ∝ n^{-2/d_int}, tau = (1/2 lambda) log(E_0/thr) so d tau / d log n = +1/(d_int lambda), i.e. d log tau / d log n = +1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem) (larger n -> smaller d_NN -> longer wait). The piece writes '-(2/d_int) 1/(2 lambda_samp tau_mem)' (main statement) = '-1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem)' (appendix): wrong sign. (2) Magnitude/positioning: lambda_samp tau_mem = (1/2) log(E_0/thr) is O(few), so this data-side term is ~0.03-0.1 in the log-log slope, against the spectral term ~1 coming from the sample-bulk mean eigenvalue a_*(q)^2/n (F2), which is exactly Bonnaire 2025's tau_mem ∝ n law. The piece never says that its framework reproduces Bonnaire's linear-in-n scaling through lambda_samp ∝ 1/n, and prediction 3 ('check that the ordering of tau_mem(1%) across n has the extra n^{-2/d_int} term') would require resolving a ~5% slope correction on 50k-step-gridded crossing times across 4 values of n -- not realistic as stated.  
  FIX: Fix the sign to +1/(d_int lambda_samp tau_mem). Add a sentence: 'The dominant n-dependence is spectral: the sample-bulk eigenvalues scale as a_*(q)^2/n (Section rfnn), reproducing the tau_mem ∝ n law of Bonnaire et al. 2025; the data-side term is a subleading correction of relative size 1/(d_int log(E_0/thr)) ≈ 0.05 and is d_int-, not d_lat-, indexed.' Downgrade prediction 3 to the directly testable part (median d_NN * n^{1/d_int} flat: my replicate check at d=20, sigma_perp=0.01 gives 4.05, 3.79, 3.80, 3.79 for n=100,500,1000,5000 -- flat within 7%, not 3%, because the n=100 median is noisy) and drop the claim that the MLP sweep can isolate the extra term.
- [minor] main_text_latex Proposition (\label{prop:bridge}) and appendix_latex Proposition (\label{prop:bridge}); likewise def:bridge-objects/lem:condensation/lem:endpoint if the main text ever restates them: The same \label{prop:bridge} is issued twice (main text and appendix). LaTeX will warn 'multiply defined labels' and every \ref{prop:bridge} resolves to the last definition; theorem numbering will also show two different numbers for the 'same' proposition.  
  FIX: Label the main-text statement prop:bridge-main (or use an unnumbered \begin{proposition*}) and make the appendix restatement 'Proposition~\ref{prop:bridge-main} (restated)' without a new label, or vice versa. Check none of the piece's labels collide with ICLR_2026/sec-appendix.tex (existing lem:Udiag, lem:Ulin, app:* labels -- currently no collision; app:bridge is new).
- [minor] cor:dnn-scaling, eq:dnn-scaling and its proof: (a) Statement has null part '(1+O(sqrt(log n/(d-d_int))))', proof says '(1-O(...))'; the correction is a reduction and is not small in the sweep: measured median d_NN^2 vs 2 sigma_perp^2 (d-d_int) is 6.7 vs 7.5 (d=20), 14.7 vs 17.5 (d=40), 84 vs 97.5 (d=200), i.e. 11-16% below. (b) The decomposition 'signal part + null part' with the signal part equal to the d_int-dimensional NN distance is only valid in regime (i); in regime (ii) the argmin is chosen jointly, so eq:dnn-scaling should be '≈' with the regime stated. (c) 'n/k points per cluster, i.i.d. within cluster' is not the generator: labels are multinomial (rng.integers), the 10 centers (5 orthogonal + 5 random on the radius-3 sphere in d_int=5) overlap at sigma_sig=1, and the NN can be cross-cluster; the n^{-1/d_int} scaling holds for the mixture density, which is all that is needed.  
  FIX: Write d_NN^2 ≈ signal part + 2 sigma_perp^2 (d-d_int)(1 - c sqrt(log n/(d-d_int))) with the minus sign, replace '=' by '≈' and say the split is exact only when one part dominates; describe the generator as it is (mixture density; scaling m^{-1/d_int} for the local density) and cite levina2004maximum/facco2017twonn as already done.
- [minor] prop:bridge last sentence ('for every d_lat>=2 in our sweeps (n=500, t<=0.1)'), cor:taumem-fraction ('weaker for all d_lat>=2'): The attachment time t is allowed to be up to t_c, and the piece's own t_c values reach 1.02 at d_lat=200 (0.39 at d=40), so 't<=0.1' contradicts the rest of the piece. With the code's metric the condition is 8 d_lat > 2 e^{2t} log n; at t=t_c(d) it is satisfied throughout the sweep (d=5: 1.7; d=200: 96 < 1600) but not 'for every d_lat>=2' when t~1.  
  FIX: Replace by: 'implied by the metric condition whenever 8 d_lat > 2 e^{2t} log n, which holds at t = t_c(d_lat) for every d_lat in the sweeps because t_c grows with d_lat (d_lat=5: 1.7 < 40; d_lat=200: 96 < 1600)'.
- [minor] empirical_anchor (6), predictions[3] already_supported_by, cor:taumem-fraction discussion of curve width: Crossing times are read off a 50k-step evaluation grid (eval_interval=50000 in config.json), so T(1%) at d=5 is only known to lie in (100k,150k]; the ratio T(5%)/T(1%) = 2.33 carries ~±30% grid uncertainty and the 'grows with d_lat' claim (2.33, 2.2, 2.33, 2.5, 3.5) rests on one seed (42) while seeds 43-46 exist in the same directory. The numbers themselves are correct (verified: 150k/350k/550k, 250k/550k/1.0M, 300k/700k/1.7M, 400k/1.0M/2.65M, 600k/2.1M, 1.05M, 2.55M).  
  FIX: State the grid resolution, and either average the crossing times over seeds 42-46 (all present in multiseed_runs/exp2_mlp_dlat_sn05_5m_bonnaire_hidden256) or interpolate in log T between grid points before quoting ratios.
- [minor] predictions[1] already_supported_by ('except the unexplained d=150/200 reappearance in the sigma_perp=0.5 run'); open_gaps G5: The reappearance (old sigma_perp=0.5, hidden=8 d_lat, 300k-step run: 0.0022 at d=150, 0.0144 at d=200; verified) is F13's 'memorization returns as d_lat -> n because the sample bulk n-d_lat empties', not 'unexplained'; the sentence is also placed inside the sigma_perp=0.01 support where all old runs give exactly 0 for d>=20 (verified: 0.0062, 0.0010, 0.0014, 0.0002, 0, ... for d=5,8,10,15,20..200).  
  FIX: Move the remark to G5 and phrase it as F13 does (sample bulk n - d_lat vanishes as d_lat -> n; the bridge as stated does not cover that regime).
- [minor] def:bridge-objects and main text: attribution of the 1/3 NN-ratio test to \citet{somepalli2023diffusion}: Somepalli et al. 2023 detect copies with SSCD feature similarity; the distance-ratio test d(gen,NN1)/d(gen,NN2) < 1/3 used by Bonnaire et al. 2025 (and by this repo, see the code comment 'Bonnaire/Somepalli NN ratio') is the criterion of Yoon et al. 2023 ('Diffusion probabilistic models generalize when they fail to memorize'). A referee who has read Bonnaire will notice. (Medium confidence on the exact origin; check Bonnaire's citation.)  
  FIX: Cite bonnaire2025 for the threshold as sec-mlp.tex already does, and add Yoon et al. 2023 (placeholder key yoon2023generalize, to be added to references.bib) for the ratio criterion; keep somepalli2023diffusion for the general near-duplicate methodology.
- [minor] empirical_anchor (1) sigma_perp=0.01 ceiling at d=5 (0.668) vs sigma_perp=0.5 (0.676): At d_lat = d_int = 5 there are no null coordinates, so the two data-generating processes are identical in law and the ceilings must coincide; quoting different values exposes that they come from different replicate draws.  
  FIX: Quote one value at d=5 (direct simulation with EM variance: 0.56) and note it is common to both sigma_perp.
- [minor] citations_needed / bib: scarvelis2023closedform, gu2023memorization, kadkhodaie2023learning, song2021scoresde, levina2004maximum, facco2017twonn are already in ICLR_2026/references.bib (verified by grep); only efron2011tweedie is missing. The citations_needed list re-supplies three existing entries, which would create duplicate keys if pasted.
