### Referee verdict: **FIXABLE**
Must change: Re-base every real-data statement on the network that was actually trained (5-layer GELU MLP, hidden 1024, PyTorch-default init var 1/(3(d+64)), 64 time-embedding inputs): under that first layer q_eff=(q_d d+32)/(3(d+64)) is flat in d (0.36/0.43/0.36 at CelebA d=50/100/200) and a_1=1/2 exactly, so the 'de-saturation' mechanism explains ~0.9x, not 1.5x, of the 2.9x slowdown; present the tanh/gamma=1 clock only as a declared surrogate. In the same pass fix the sample-bulk spread model and its units: use the full Marchenko-Pastur law at gamma=n/p (1.56->0.039 on the synthetic sweep, ~1 for the real network), report that a_*(q_d)^2/n matches the bulk MEAN at all d and the TOP only for n/p<=0.08, and that the per-sample median prediction (16.7x in theory units) is contradicted by the observed ~1.04-1.3x.

Independent check: (1) 80-node Gauss-Hermite for tanh: a_1^2 = 0.625 (q=0.327), 0.320 (1.247), 0.246 (1.834), 0.180 (2.76); a_*^2 = 0.00466, 0.0359, 0.0544, 0.0784 -- matches the piece and F3; a_*^2 monotone on [0.05,6]; algebra gives a_*^2 = (2/3)q^3 + O(q^4) but quadrature/(2/3 q^3) = 0.72 at q=0.05, 0.52 at q=0.1. (2) CSV anchors verified: lambda_mean = e^{-0.2} z_normsq_over_d_mean + 0.1813 exactly; CelebA q_d 2.575/1.834/1.808/1.247, CIFAR 2.271/1.783/1.108; cv 0.48->0.22 and 0.35->0.17; kappa 1.3594e-6 / 1e-6; theta_5%=0.4366, theta_25%=0.7423 (CelebA), 0.5864/0.8715 (CIFAR); observed tau_5% CelebA 300k (d=50) -> 1160k (d=200) = 3.87x, 400k (d=100) -> 2.9x; CIFAR 720k (d=100) -> 2520k (d=260) = 3.5x. (3) Released in-sample |log res| reproduces the piece's 0.340/0.480 (CelebA 5%) only with d>=40 and 0.146/0.327 (CIFAR 5%) with d>=60; over all uncensored rows it is 0.365/0.194. One-constant tanh clock: CelebA 5% 0.354 in-sample / 0.386 LOO, 25% 0.239/0.261; CIFAR 5% 0.164/0.180, 25% 0.186/0.213; 1/d baseline CelebA 0.165 (5%), 0.155 (25%), CIFAR 0.156/0.294. (4) Deployed network from run_celeba_diverse_diffusion.py + shell scripts: GELU, hidden 1024, depth 5, fan_in d+64 (32 sinusoid pairs), SGD lr 1e-3 momentum 0.8 for both datasets, unnormalised encoder means. q_eff=(q_d d+32)/(3(d+64)): 0.362/0.433/0.355 at d=50/100/200 (CelebA); GELU a_1=1/2 for all q; GELU mean-removed a_*^2 = 0.0235/0.0308/0.0228, ratio d=50->200 = 0.91; GELU one-constant clock CelebA 5% 0.473/0.516. (5) Synthetic spectra (sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn/raw_data/di5_d{5..200}_n500_s42/eigenvalues_pre.npy; config t=0.01, p=64d, lr auto 0.01 d/Delta_t; code U = phi^T phi/n without 1/p so code = p x theory): signal medians 29.7 -> 99.8 and noise-dim medians 5.22 -> 9.22 reproduce F3; top sample eigenvalue/p goes 3.0e-3 -> 1.35e-5 = 222x (reproduces F5's 221x, hence F5 is in theory units); sample median/p 5.6e-6 -> 7.3e-6 (1.3x); mean/(a_*^2/n) = 0.89, 0.65, 0.72, 0.77, 0.80, 0.81 at d=5,10,20,40,100,200. Wishart MP(gamma=n/p) simulation (n=500, seed 0): (top, median, mean)/mean = (5.01,0.449,1.01) d=5; (3.47,0.754,1.02) d=10; (2.63,0.909,1.03) d=20; (2.05,0.989,1.05) d=40; (1.64,1.062,1.09) d=100; (1.44,1.118,1.13) d=200, versus measured/(a_*^2/n) = (19.0,0.036,0.89), (16.3,0.069,0.65), (11.1,0.26,0.72), (5.9,0.51,0.77), (2.4,0.72,0.80), (1.45,0.78,0.81). Piece's hypothesis d^{3/2} a_*(q_d)^2 = 0.87 (d=5) ... 13.3 (d=200) vs n=500. (6) Cross-dataset: mean log C on the tau tables gives C_CIFAR/C_CelebA = 1.97 (5%) and 1.86 (25%) with identical eta, width and depth; released kappa ratio 1.36 = 2 grid steps of spacing 10^{8/120}. (7) CelebA d=200 10-90 quantile ratio of a_*^2 under Gaussian norms from CSV mean/std = 1.806 (reproduces the piece's 1.81, so it is not from the raw latents); latent_norms.npz/spectra.npz, sweep VAE checkpoints, per-sample NN1 identities and U eigenvectors are absent from the repo. (8) Bib: references.bib lacks gu2023memorization, scarvelis2023closedform, kadkhodaie2023learning; \\label{sec:theory-mem} absent from the draft; main text describes a (kappa,a,b) sigmoid fit on d>=70/>=140 while calibrate() fits kappa on a 121-point log grid plus PAVA thetas on all uncensored rows with no sigmoid and no dimension restriction.

- [fatal] def:two-source-predictor-pred, Practitioner recipe step 1, prop:data-side-rates-pred (kappa_d = eta a_1^2/d_lat), main-text paragraph 'The missing term', predictions 3 and 5, empirical_anchor (2)-(5): Every real-data number is computed for a tanh first layer reading the raw latent with gain gamma=1, and the recipe asserts 'gamma=1 for LeCun/Kaiming fan-in scaling on unnormalised latents'. The network actually trained (run_celeba_diverse_diffusion.py, FlexibleMLPScore; scripts use --hidden 1024 --depth 5 --optimizer sgd --lr 0.001 momentum 0.8 for BOTH datasets) is a 5-layer GELU MLP with PyTorch-default nn.Linear init (weight variance 1/(3 fan_in)) whose input is [x_t, 64 sinusoidal time features] (fan_in = d_lat+64, time features contribute exactly 32 to the squared input norm). In the RFNN convention tanh(Wx/sqrt(d)) with W~N(0,1) the 1/sqrt(d) IS fan-in scaling, so gamma is not 1 but d/(3(d+64)) (0.15 at d=50, 0.25 at d=200), and the effective pre-activation variance is q_eff=(q_d d+32)/(3(d+64)). With the CSV lambda_mean this gives q_eff = 0.362 (d=50), 0.433 (100), 0.355 (200) on CelebA: FLAT in d. GELU also has a_1 = E[GELU'(sqrt(q) z)] = 1/2 exactly for every q (E[Phi(sz)]=1/2, E[z phi(sz)]=0), so a_1(q_d)^2 carries no d-dependence either. Hence for the deployed network the 'de-saturation' mechanism the main text credits with 1.5x of the 2.9x CelebA slowdown predicts 0.91x (a_*^2 ratio d=50->200 with GELU+actual init), and my one-constant GELU clock scores worse than the tanh surrogate (CelebA 5%: in-sample 0.473 / LOO 0.516 vs tanh 0.354/0.386). The piece's 'gamma read from the initialisation, 0 fitted' and 'total 1 (+1) versus 7' parameter counts are therefore not what the numbers use: gamma=1 and sigma=tanh are undeclared modelling choices.  
  FIX: Either (a) present the a_* clock explicitly as an RFNN-surrogate (sigma=tanh, gamma=1, one extra un-fitted modelling choice), delete the sentence 'gamma=1 for LeCun/Kaiming fan-in scaling', and state that under the deployed first layer (GELU, var 1/(3(d+64)), 32 from the time embedding) q_eff is flat in d so the learning-side mechanism does NOT explain the real-data d-slope; or (b) redo the numbers with GELU and the actual init (q_mu=(e^{-2t}||z^mu||^2+Delta_t d+32)/(3(d+64)); a_* with the mean removed since GELU is not odd) and report honestly that the a_* clock then predicts ~no slowdown, moving the whole 3x to the dynamics side / detection drift. In prop:data-side-rates-pred replace 'kappa_d = eta a_1(q_d)^2/d_lat' for the real network by 'kappa_d = eta a_1^2 * (first-layer weight variance) = eta/(4*3(d+64))', i.e. pure fan-in scaling, and rewrite prediction 5 accordingly. Update rem:param-count-pred: sigma and gamma are choices, not inputs.
- [fatal] rem:collective-pred (appendix and statements), empirical_anchor (4), open_gaps (2), prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred(iii) and its 'relative half-width 2 sqrt(n/p)': Units are mixed: the 'predicted sample-bulk median rate in code units ∝ p a_*^2/n = 64 d a_*^2/n changes 2.4x' is compared with an observed ~1.04x that (per F5 and my recomputation from sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn/raw_data/*/eigenvalues_pre.npy, code U = p x theory U, t=0.01) is in THEORY units. In theory units the per-sample clock predicts the median falls 16.7x (a_*^2: 0.0784 -> 0.0047) while the measured sample-bulk median (lambda/p) goes 5.6e-6 -> 7.3e-6 (rises 1.3x). So the per-sample median prediction FAILS by a factor ~20 at small d, and the piece hides this behind the units mismatch. Moreover the MP spread model is wrong: the residual Gram is a Wishart with aspect ratio gamma = n/p = 500/(64 d) = 1.56 (d=5), 0.78 (10), 0.39 (20), 0.20 (40), 0.08 (100), 0.039 (200) -- NOT a small 'relative half-width 2 sqrt(n/p)' (which at d=5 gives 1.25, i.e. a negative lower edge). Direct Wishart simulation vs measured, all divided by a_*(q_d)^2/n: d=5 measured (top, median, mean) = (19.0, 0.036, 0.89) vs MP (5.0, 0.45, 1.01); d=10 (16.3, 0.069, 0.65) vs (3.5, 0.75, 1.02); d=20 (11.1, 0.26, 0.72) vs (2.6, 0.91, 1.03); d=40 (5.9, 0.51, 0.77) vs (2.05, 0.99, 1.05); d=100 (2.4, 0.72, 0.80) vs (1.64, 1.06, 1.09); d=200 (1.45, 0.78, 0.81) vs (1.44, 1.12, 1.13). So: the MEAN a_*^2/n is verified at every d (0.65-0.9); the TOP is exactly a_*^2/n x MP edge at d=200 and exceeds it by 3.8-4.7x at d<=20 (supports the collective-mode conjecture quantitatively: excess 3.8, 4.7, 4.2, 2.9, 1.5, 1.0); the MEDIAN is 12x BELOW the MP prediction at d=5 and only within 30% at d=200. The piece's own hypothesis n << d^{3/2} a_*(q_d)^2 evaluates to n=500 vs 0.87 (d=5) ... 13 (d=200): it fails at every d in the sweep.

State the verified content precisely: mean = a_*^2/n at all d; top = a_*^2/n (1+sqrt(n/p))^2 for d>=100 (n/p<=0.08); per-sample attachment Lambda_mu <-> mu is CONJECTURED and contradicted at d<=40 (median 2-12x below MP). Fix the units in rem:collective-pred: 'in theory units the per-sample clock predicts the median slows 16.7x; observed ~1.04-1.3x; MP at gamma=n/p accounts for part (median/mean 0.45 at d=5) but not all'. Include the six-row table above as the evidence for the conjecture. Downgrade prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred(iii) from 'sketched' to 'conjectured (verified for the mean; for the top only at n/p<=0.08)'.
- [major] cor:shape-from-qmu-pred(ii), def:two-source-predictor-pred (varsigma = 2 sqrt(n/p) 'not fitted'), prediction 4, empirical_anchor (6), main text 'the curve's shape in log s is the empirical distribution of log a_*(q_mu)^2 (no response function)': For the real-data network p = hidden = 1024 and n = 1000, so gamma = n/p ≈ 0.98: the MP support is [0, 4] x mean and the Wishart spread alone spans >100x in eigenvalue, dwarfing the 1.8x spread from q_mu. A log-normal smoothing with varsigma=2 is not 'the Marchenko-Pastur spread' and the claim that the fraction-curve width is set by the a_*(q_mu)^2 distribution cannot survive at gamma≈1. In addition, the '10-90 quantile ratio 1.81 computed from the 1k latents' is NOT from the latents: the per-sample norms (latent_norms.npz / spectra.npz written by compute_*_spectral_predictor_data.py) are not in the repo; I reproduce 1.806 exactly by assuming Gaussian norms with the CSV mean/std (1.302 +/- 0.2905). The observed tau_25/tau_5 = 2.14 at d=200 (CelebA table: 2.48M/1.16M) is correct.

Say the 1.81 ratio assumes Gaussian per-sample norms from the CSV summary stats; mark prediction 4 as requiring re-encoding with the sweep VAEs (not in the repo).

The 'released kappa values differ by only 1.36x' is two steps of the 121-point grid 10^(-12..-4) (spacing 10^{8/120}=1.166), not evidence of transfer.

- [major] prop:data-side-rates-pred and prop:sample-bulk-persample-pred proof sketches (positioning), citations: A referee who knows Bonnaire 2025 will note that Lambda = a_*^2/n x MP(n/p) is exactly Bonnaire's sample bulk rho_1 (the eta_* term) and the data-side law a_1^2 lambda_i(M_t)/d is their theorem on a general rho_Sigma; the only new content is the per-sample reweighting diag(a_*(q_mu)^2), which the repo's spectra do not support at small d (issue 2). Also hu2022universality (Hu-Lu) is a proportional-regime (p ∝ d) result; here p/d -> infinity with n fixed, so it is the wrong citation for the stated limit.

- [minor] lem:ge-coeff-pred hypotheses and def:two-source-predictor-pred ('a_* computed for sigma'): For a non-odd activation (GELU) the k=0 Hermite coefficient is nonzero; a_*^2 := E sigma^2 - a_1^2 q then includes (E sigma)^2, a rank-one all-ones direction, not sample-bulk mass. Lemma (iv)'s expansion a_*^2=(2/3)q^3 is correct (I verified 17/3-5=2/3) but already 28% off at q=0.05 and 48% off at q=0.1, so it is useless on the paper's q range.  
  FIX: Define a_*^2 = E sigma^2 - (E sigma)^2 - a_1^2 q in the recipe for general sigma; either drop the (2/3)q^3 statement or add 'valid only for q << 0.05'.
- [minor] main text 'synthetic: 2.76 -> 0.33', empirical anchors, appendix header: The synthetic RFNN spectra (sigma_noise_0.5/exp2_rfnn, config t_fixed=0.01, Delta_t=0.0198, p_ratio 64, momentum 0) are at t=0.01 while the real-data predictor uses t=0.1 (Delta_t=0.181); the piece never states t for the synthetic numbers although Delta_t enters q. The exp2 lr is 0.01 d_lat/Delta_t (auto-scaled), so any conversion of eigenvalues to steps is d-dependent; the piece's 'T = eta s' hides this.  
  FIX: State t=0.01 for the synthetic q values and t=0.1 for real data; note lr ∝ d_lat in the RFNN runs when converting rates to steps.
